# 7. Sūrat al-Aʿrāf

**Two hundred and six verses.** It is widely reported that it came down in the Meccan period; some of its verses (particularly 163-171) are also said to have come down in Medina — **I adopt no firm preference.** It is the second longest sūra in the Qurʾān: by number of verses it comes after al-Baqara and al-Shuʿarāʾ, but **in volume of words it is second only to al-Baqara.** The verses of al-Shuʿarāʾ were short (`026-suara.md`); those of al-Aʿrāf are long.

It takes its name from a word that occurs **in verses 46, 47 and 48**:

> **وَعَلَى ٱلْأَعْرَافِ رِجَالٌ يَعْرِفُونَ كُلًّۢا بِسِيمَىٰهُمْ**
>
> *Wa ʿala'l-aʿrāfi rijālun yaʿrifūna kullan bi-sīmāhum* (46)

**ٱلْأَعْرَاف** — *al-aʿrāf*, from the root **ع-ر-ف**, the plural of *ʿurf*. The concrete sense of the root is **height and ridge**: the *ʿurf* of a horse is the ridge of the neck where the mane ends, the *ʿurf* of a cockerel is its comb. From this comes the sense **"a raised place, a ridge, a height"**. **The same root also carries the sense "to know, to recognise" (*maʿrifa*)** — and that these two senses stand side by side in a single verse (*ʿala'l-**aʿrāf**i rijālun **yaʿrifūna*** kullan bi-sīmāhum) **is the first thing to record about the name of the sūra.** This passage will be treated in detail at verses 46-49, and **because both what the word denotes and who those upon it are remain disputed, no preference will be imposed.**

**A note on naming:** in the Qurʾān the names of sūras are most often not a summary of the subject but **a distinctive word that occurs in the sūra** — this was recorded in `026-suara.md`. **Al-Aʿrāf is the same:** a name taken from a scene of four verses is set at the head of a sūra of two hundred and six.

---

## The structure of the sūra

| Block | Verses | Subject | What closes the block |
|---|---|---|---|
| **I** | 1-10 | The Book, the towns destroyed, the questioning and **the weighing** | *Qalīlan mā tashkurūn* (10) |
| **II** | 11-25 | **Adam (Ādam) and Iblīs** — the fullest account in the Qurʾān | *Wa minhā tukhrajūn* (25) |
| **III** | 26-36 | **Four addresses of *yā banī Ādam***: clothing, temptation, adornment, messengers | *Hum fīhā khālidūn* (36) |
| **IV** | 37-53 | The two sides meet: the people of the Fire, the people of the Garden, **the people of al-Aʿrāf** | *Ḍalla ʿanhum mā kānū yaftarūn* (53) |
| **V** | 54-58 | Creation, supplication and **the likeness of the two soils** | *Li-qawmin yashkurūn* (58) |
| **VI** | 59-102 | **The sequence of prophets**: Noah (Nūḥ), Hūd, Ṣāliḥ, Lot (Lūṭ), Shuʿayb + the law of destruction | *Wa in wajadnā aktharahum la-fāsiqīn* (102) |
| **VII** | 103-171 | **The Moses (Mūsā) block** — the longest section of the sūra (69 verses) | *Laʿallakum tattaqūn* (171) |
| **VIII** | 172-186 | **The covenant of *alast***, the man who slipped out of the signs, *dharaʾnā li-jahannam*, the names | *Fī ṭughyānihim yaʿmahūn* (186) |
| **IX** | 187-206 | The Hour, the single soul, the helplessness of those called upon, commands to the messenger, **the prostration** | *Wa lahū yasjudūn* (206) — **a verse of prostration** |

### The backbone of the sūra: one question and its three answers

**From beginning to end the sūra works on a single question: what does man do with the knowledge given to him?** And it asks that question from three different places.

| From where | Which verses | What it states |
|---|---|---|
| **From the beginning** | 11-25, 172-174 | Man was given a knowledge and a word **at the very outset** |
| **From history** | 59-171 | The messengers came; each time the same two attitudes appeared |
| **From the present** | 26-36, 175-206 | The same two attitudes are being lived **right now** |

**And the three cornerstones of the sūra — 11-25 (Adam), 172-174 (*alast*), 175-177 (the man who slipped out of the signs) — are three moments of the same matter:**

| Verse | What was given | What happened |
|---|---|---|
| **7/19-22** | A **prohibition** and a **warning** | The prohibition was broken; then it was confessed (23) |
| **7/172** | A **testimony** | The testimony was taken; then it was forgotten |
| **7/175-176** | **Signs** | The signs were **slipped off and cast away** like a worn skin |

**I record this as my own reading and it is not binding; its ground is the verbs of the three sections themselves:** *nahā* (he forbade), *ashhada* (he called to witness), *ātaynāhu āyātinā* (We gave him Our signs). **In all three there is first a giving, and then the passing of what was given out of the hand.** What is commanded at the end of the sūra is exactly the reverse of this: *wa'dhkur rabbaka fī nafsika* (205) — **to hold, to keep in hand.**

### The second backbone: ملأ — "the notables"

**The word repeated through the sequence of prophets in this sūra is *al-malaʾ***, and it takes the place held in al-Shuʿarāʾ by the seal *kadhdhabat … al-mursalīn*.

| Prophet | Verse where *malaʾ* occurs | How it is qualified |
|---|---|---|
| **Noah** | 60 | *al-malaʾu min qawmih* — without qualifier |
| **Hūd** | 66 | *al-malaʾu'lladhīna **kafarū** min qawmih* |
| **Ṣāliḥ** | 75 | *al-malaʾu'lladhīna'**stakbarū** min qawmih* |
| **Shuʿayb** | 88 | *al-malaʾu'lladhīna'**stakbarū** min qawmih* |
| **Shuʿayb (a second time)** | 90 | *al-malaʾu'lladhīna **kafarū** min qawmih* |
| **Moses / Pharaoh** | 103, 109, 127 | *Firʿawna wa **malaʾih*** |

**And *malaʾ* does not occur in the story of Lot** (80-84) — there the addressee is directly *qawmuh*. **This is a countable datum.**

The root **م-ل-أ**: **to fill.** *Malaʾ* means "those who fill an assembly", that is, **the body that holds the word and takes the decisions.** The idea of "filling" in the root was also treated in `067-mulk.md` and `038-sad.md` in the pattern *la-amlaʾanna jahannama*; **I rely on that treatment.**

**I record this as an observation:** in the sequence of prophets in al-Aʿrāf the opposing side is never "the people" but **the deciding body within the people.** And in verse 75 that body is set against *alladhīna'stuḍʿifū* (those made weak) — **the sūra presents the opposition along a line of class.**

### The third backbone: countable repetitions

| Repetition | Where it occurs | How many |
|---|---|---|
| ***Yā qawmi'budū'llāha mā lakum min ilāhin ghayruh*** | 59 (Noah), 65 (Hūd), 73 (Ṣāliḥ), 85 (Shuʿayb) | **4** |
| ***Yā banī Ādam*** | 26, 27, 31, 35 | **4** |
| ***Fa-akhadhathumu'r-rajfatu fa-aṣbaḥū fī dārihim jāthimīn*** | 78 (Thamūd), 91 (Madyan) | **2** |
| The pattern ***Lā khawfun ʿalayhim wa lā hum yaḥzanūn*** | 35, 49 | **2** |
| ***Alladhīna kadhdhabū bi-āyātinā*** | 36, 40, 64, 72, 92 (as a verb), 136, 146, 147, 177, 182 | **many** |
| ***Wa lā tufsidū fi'l-arḍi baʿda iṣlāḥihā*** | 56, 85 | **2** |
| ***Wa yabghūnahā ʿiwajā*** | 45, 86 | **2** |
| ***Ummatun yahdūna bi'l-ḥaqqi wa bihī yaʿdilūn*** | 159 (of the people of Moses), 181 (of those We created) | **2** |
| ***Sīmā*** | 46, 48 | **2** |
| Announcements of destruction of the type ***Fa-kullan akhadhnā bi-dhanbih*** | 64, 72, 78, 84, 91, 96, 136 | **7** |

**Two lines must be recorded separately.**

**One — *wa lā tufsidū fi'l-arḍi baʿda iṣlāḥihā* (56) and *wa lā tufsidū fi'l-arḍi baʿda iṣlāḥihā* (85) are the same sentence.** The first is said directly to the reader, the second **in the mouth of Shuʿayb to Madyan.** **That is, the sūra puts its own command into the mouth of a prophet.** In `011-hud.md` Table 4 it was tabulated that Sūrat Hūd does the same thing (*istaghfirū rabbakum thumma tūbū ilayh* → the third verse of the sūra and the mouths of three prophets). **I rely on that treatment; al-Aʿrāf applies the same technique once.**

**Two — the sentence *ummatun yahdūna bi'l-ḥaqqi wa bihī yaʿdilūn* occurs twice word for word** (159 and 181). **The first is about the people of Moses, the second about all created beings.** **I record this as an observation:** the sūra shows that it issues no blanket judgement about a community **by widening its own sentence.** The rule of the method that "no blanket judgement is issued" (`STYLE.md`) finds support here from within the text itself.

### The verse-ending

**The dominant verse-ending of the sūra is ‑ūn / ‑īn** (*tashkurūn, yadhdhakkarūn, yaʿlamūn, muʾminīn, khālidīn*). **But the verse-ending of al-Aʿrāf is not single-toned as that of al-Shuʿarāʾ is:** among the long verses, closings such as *al-ghāfirīn* (155), *al-rāḥimīn* (151), *ḥakīm* are scattered. **The sūra takes its length from the length of its sentences; its sound pattern loosens accordingly.** This is a record of fact; no reckoning of letters or numbers is entered into (`002-bakara.md`).

### Revelation and historical setting

It is widely reported that the sūra came down in the last years of Mecca. **The addressees appear in three rings:**

| Ring | Where | What is said |
|---|---|---|
| **The notables of Mecca** | 2-10, 175-206 | Follow the Book, not the forefathers; the weighing is real |
| **The peoples of the past** | 59-102 | The same call, the same body, the same end |
| **The community given a Book** | 103-171 | What was done with the Book given |

**And the last section of the sūra (172-206) draws the three rings together at a single point:** the scene of *alast* looks to all mankind, the example of *insilākh* to the man given a Book, the command *khudhi'l-ʿafw* to the messenger himself.

The basmala was treated in earlier sections; the verses are numbered without it.

---

## 7/1 — الٓمٓصٓ

*Alif-Lām-Mīm-Ṣād*

**This is one of the two disconnected-letter sequences in the Qurʾān made up of four letters** (the other being *al-Mr* — al-Raʿd 13/1). **And the sequence *al-Mṣ* occurs only here in the Qurʾān.**

**The record concerning the disconnected letters was set down in `002-bakara.md` at 2/1 and in `020-taha.md` at 20/1: what the letters mean is not known with certainty; explanations are transmitted, none of them carrying certainty.** **I rely on that treatment and do not repeat it here.** **Claims resting on the reckoning of letters and numbers are not used in this commentary** (`STYLE.md`).

**The one thing that belongs here is the placement of the sequence, and that is a countable datum:**

| Sūra | Sequence | What comes after it |
|---|---|---|
| al-Baqara 2/1-2 | *Alif-Lām-Mīm* | *Dhālika'l-**kitāb*** |
| **al-Aʿrāf 7/1-2** | ***Alif-Lām-Mīm-Ṣād*** | ***Kitābun** unzila ilayk* |
| Yūnus 10/1 | *Alif-Lām-Rā* | *Tilka āyātu'l-**kitābi**'l-ḥakīm* |
| Hūd 11/1 | *Alif-Lām-Rā* | ***Kitābun** uḥkimat āyātuh* (`011-hud.md`) |
| al-Raʿd 13/1 | *Alif-Lām-Mīm-Rā* | *Tilka āyātu'l-**kitāb*** |

**In all five of these sūras the "Book" is named immediately after the disconnected letters.** This is a verifiable pattern; **I make no claim about what it means.**

---

## 7/2-3 — كِتَٰبٌ أُنزِلَ إِلَيْكَ فَلَا يَكُن فِى صَدْرِكَ حَرَجٌ مِّنْهُ لِتُنذِرَ بِهِۦ وَذِكْرَىٰ لِلْمُؤْمِنِينَ · ٱتَّبِعُوا۟ مَآ أُنزِلَ إِلَيْكُم مِّن رَّبِّكُمْ وَلَا تَتَّبِعُوا۟ مِن دُونِهِۦٓ أَوْلِيَآءَ قَلِيلًا مَّا تَذَكَّرُونَ

*Kitābun unzila ilayka fa-lā yakun fī ṣadrika ḥarajun minhu li-tundhira bihī wa dhikrā li'l-muʾminīn · Ittabiʿū mā unzila ilaykum min rabbikum wa lā tattabiʿū min dūnihī awliyāʾ, qalīlan mā tadhakkarūn*

"**It is a Book sent down to you** — let there be no constriction in your breast on account of it — **that you may warn by it, and as a reminder to the believers. · Follow what has been sent down to you from your Lord; do not follow other guardians beside Him. How little you take to heart!**"

### حَرَج — the root ح-ر-ج

**The concrete sense of the root: tightness, a narrow place.** The lexicographers describe *ḥaraj* as "a place where the trees are so tangled together that one cannot get in". From this comes the sense of **narrowness, distress, the tightening of the breast.**

**The root occurs in the sūra `006-enam.md` and at 22/78 in `022-hac.md` (*wa mā jaʿala ʿalaykum fi'd-dīni min ḥaraj*);** **the use in al-Aʿrāf is different, and the difference should be recorded: there *ḥaraj* is a weight not laid upon the one addressed by the religion, here it is the constriction in the breast of the messenger.**

### The sentence that cuts in

**A point of word order should be recorded.** The main construction of the verse is this:

> *Kitābun unzila ilayka* — **li-tundhira bihī wa dhikrā li'l-muʾminīn**

**That is, "it is a Book sent down to you, that you may warn by it." But a sentence comes in between the two parts: *fa-lā yakun fī ṣadrika ḥarajun minh*.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the position of the sentence:** before the purpose of the Book's descent is stated, a note is entered about the **inner state** of the messenger. **Before the task of warning is assigned, the distress that task will cause is named.**

**And this finds its answer at the end of the sūra:** *khudhi'l-ʿafwa wa'mur bi'l-ʿurfi wa aʿriḍ ʿani'l-jāhilīn* (199) — there too the messenger is told what to do in the face of the addressee's response. **The sūra opens by telling the messenger "do not be distressed" and closes by telling him "turn away".**

**A comparison:** in `026-suara.md` at 26/3 the same place was held by *laʿallaka bākhiʿun nafsaka allā yakūnū muʾminīn* — there the messenger's grief was described with the words **destroying himself.** **The same sentence was also treated in `018-kehf.md` at 18/6.** **Three sūras look at the same state of the messenger with three different words:**

| Sūra | Word | What it states |
|---|---|---|
| al-Shuʿarāʾ 26/3 · al-Kahf 18/6 | *Bākhiʿun nafsak* | **Do not grieve to the point of wearing yourself away** |
| **al-Aʿrāf 7/2** | ***Ḥaraj fī'ṣ-ṣadr*** | **A constriction of the breast** |
| al-Ḥijr 15/97 | *Yaḍīqu ṣadruk* | **The narrowing of the breast** |

### ٱتَّبِعُوا۟ … وَلَا تَتَّبِعُوا۟ — the same verb, two directions

**The third verse builds the whole matter of the sūra out of two verbs: *ittabiʿū* and *lā tattabiʿū*.** The same root (ت-ب-ع), one an imperative and one a prohibition.

**And the object of the prohibition should be recorded: *awliyāʾ* — "guardians", in the plural.** **The verse does not say "do not follow other gods"; it says "do not follow guardians".**

The root **و-ل-ي**: **nearness, adjacency.** *Walī* — the one who is near, who takes charge of another's affair. **The root was treated in `045-casiye.md` and in `002-bakara.md` at 2/257 (*Allāhu waliyyu'lladhīna āmanū*); I rely on that treatment.**

**I record this as an observation:** the choice of the word overlaps with two later sentences of the sūra — *innā jaʿalna'sh-shayāṭīna **awliyāʾa** lilladhīna lā yuʾminūn* (27) and *innahumu'ttakhadhu'sh-shayāṭīna **awliyāʾa** min dūni'llāh* (30). **That is, the prohibition of the third verse receives its ground in the twenty-seventh and thirtieth verses.** This is a link that can be verified within the sūra.

---

## 7/4-5 — وَكَم مِّن قَرْيَةٍ أَهْلَكْنَٰهَا فَجَآءَهَا بَأْسُنَا بَيَٰتًا أَوْ هُمْ قَآئِلُونَ · فَمَا كَانَ دَعْوَىٰهُمْ إِذْ جَآءَهُم بَأْسُنَآ إِلَّآ أَن قَالُوٓا۟ إِنَّا كُنَّا ظَٰلِمِينَ

*Wa kam min qaryatin ahlaknāhā fa-jāʾahā baʾsunā bayātan aw hum qāʾilūn · Fa-mā kāna daʿwāhum idh jāʾahum baʾsunā illā an qālū innā kunnā ẓālimīn*

"**How many a town We destroyed; Our violence came upon them by night, or while they slept at noon. · When Our violence came upon them, they had nothing to say but, "We were indeed wrongdoers."**"

### Two times of day: بَيَاتًا and قَآئِلُونَ

**Both words state sleep, and each is a different time.**

| Word | Root | Time |
|---|---|---|
| *Bayātan* | **ب-ي-ت** — to pass the night | **The sleep of night** |
| *Qāʾilūn* | **ق-ي-ل** — *qaylūla* | **Sleep in the middle of the day** (the noon rest) |

The root **ق-ي-ل**: *qaylūla*, the short sleep taken at midday in hot regions. **The word occurs also at 25/24 in al-Furqān** (*wa aḥsanu **maqīlā***) — there it is the resting place of the people of the Garden. **I rely on `025-furkan.md`.**

**I record this as an observation:** by naming two times of sleep the verse covers **the two ends of the day** — and both are moments of **defencelessness**. **The same pair will be repeated in verses 97-98 of this sūra** (*baʾsunā **bayātan** wa hum nāʾimūn* / *baʾsunā **ḍuḥan** wa hum yalʿabūn*). **But there the second time will not be sleep but play.** The comparison of the two places will be given at 97-98.

### دَعْوَىٰهُمْ — the collapse of the plea

The root **د-ع-و**: to call, to claim. ***Daʿwā* is "the word put forward, the claim".**

**A point of word order:** the sentence is built as *fa-mā kāna daʿwāhum … illā an qālū* — **a *qaṣr* (restriction, *ḥaṣr*) construction: "their claim was nothing but this".**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the choice of the word:** *daʿwā* means a person's **defence, his assertion of a right.** **And the verse says that when the violence came, the content of the defence was "we were wrongdoers".** **That is, the defence becomes a confession.**

**And the sentence *innā kunnā ẓālimīn* is in the past tense:** "we **were** wrongdoers". **The admission is a diagnosis turned towards the past; it is not a sentence of repentance.** This distinction becomes visible when it is compared with verse 23 of the same sūra (*rabbanā ẓalamnā anfusanā* — there a request for forgiveness follows it). **This comparison will be completed at 7/23.**

---

## 7/6-7 — فَلَنَسْـَٔلَنَّ ٱلَّذِينَ أُرْسِلَ إِلَيْهِمْ وَلَنَسْـَٔلَنَّ ٱلْمُرْسَلِينَ · فَلَنَقُصَّنَّ عَلَيْهِم بِعِلْمٍ وَمَا كُنَّا غَآئِبِينَ

*Fa-la-nasʾalanna'lladhīna ursila ilayhim wa la-nasʾalanna'l-mursalīn · Fa-la-naquṣṣanna ʿalayhim bi-ʿilmin wa mā kunnā ghāʾibīn*

"**We shall certainly question those to whom messengers were sent, and We shall certainly question the messengers. · We shall certainly recount to them with knowledge; for We were never absent.**"

### That both sides are questioned

**The structure of the verse must be recorded: the same verb (*la-nasʾalanna*) is repeated twice, with two different objects.**

| Who | Expression |
|---|---|
| **The addressees** | *Alladhīna ursila ilayhim* — those to whom it was sent |
| **The messengers** | *Al-mursalīn* — those who were sent |

**I record this as an observation:** that the questioning takes in the messengers as well stands beside the question put to Jesus (ʿĪsā) in the sūra `005-maide.md` (5/116) and beside **the line of al-Shuʿarāʾ that "your task is delivery".** **But here there is no accusation; the verb is only *suʾāl*, questioning.**

**And the twofold repetition of *la-nasʾalanna* is not the same as lining up two objects in a single sentence.** It does not say "We shall question both these and those"; **the verb is built afresh for each of them.** This is an emphasis that can be read off the word order.

### وَمَا كُنَّا غَآئِبِينَ

The root **غ-ي-ب**: to disappear from sight, to be absent. ***Ghāʾib*: the one who is not present.***

**The construction of the sentence must be recorded: a negated nominal sentence.** It does not say "We were present"; **it says "We were not absent".**

**I record this as my own reading and it is not binding:** the word *ghayb* is generally used in the Qurʾān for **the region man cannot reach** (`002-bakara.md` 2/3, `072-cin.md` 72/26). **Here the same root is used, negated, of God:** man has a *ghayb*; He has none. **And the expression *fa-la-naquṣṣanna ʿalayhim bi-ʿilmin* looks to the same place: the recounting is grounded not on witnessing but on knowledge.**

---

## 7/8-9 — وَٱلْوَزْنُ يَوْمَئِذٍ ٱلْحَقُّ فَمَن ثَقُلَتْ مَوَٰزِينُهُۥ فَأُو۟لَٰٓئِكَ هُمُ ٱلْمُفْلِحُونَ · وَمَنْ خَفَّتْ مَوَٰزِينُهُۥ فَأُو۟لَٰٓئِكَ ٱلَّذِينَ خَسِرُوٓا۟ أَنفُسَهُم بِمَا كَانُوا۟ بِـَٔايَٰتِنَا يَظْلِمُونَ

*Wa'l-waznu yawmaʾidhini'l-ḥaqqu fa-man thaqulat mawāzīnuhū fa-ulāʾika humu'l-mufliḥūn · Wa man khaffat mawāzīnuhū fa-ulāʾika'lladhīna khasirū anfusahum bimā kānū bi-āyātinā yaẓlimūn*

"**The weighing on that day is the truth. Whoever's scales are heavy, those are the ones who prosper. · And whoever's scales are light, those are the ones who have lost themselves, because they used to wrong Our signs.**"

### The scales verses: the ring in the index

**These two verses are identical in structure with 101/6-9 in `101-karia.md` and 23/102-103 in `023-muminun.md`** (*fa-ammā man thaqulat mawāzīnuh… wa ammā man khaffat mawāzīnuh*). **The root was further treated at 21/47 in `021-enbiya.md` (*al-mawāzīna'l-qisṭ*) and at 42/17 in `042-sura.md` (*wa anzala … al-mīzān*).** **I rely on those treatments and do not undo the root و-ز-ن again here.**

**What I write is the difference that belongs here, and the table can be verified from the text:**

| Place | Sentence | The name of the outcome |
|---|---|---|
| al-Qāriʿa 101/6-9 | *Fa-huwa fī ʿīshatin rāḍiya* / *fa-ummuhū hāwiya* | **Place** — where he will go |
| al-Muʾminūn 23/102-103 | *Fa-ulāʾika humu'l-mufliḥūn* / *khālidūn* | **Duration** — permanence |
| **al-Aʿrāf 7/8-9** | *Humu'l-mufliḥūn* / ***khasirū anfusahum*** | **Loss** — what he has lost |

**The word al-Aʿrāf adds is *khasirū anfusahum*: "they have lost themselves".** **What answers to the measure is neither a place nor a duration, but the person himself.**

### وَٱلْوَزْنُ يَوْمَئِذٍ ٱلْحَقُّ — word order

**The sentence is a nominal sentence and its predicate is definite (*al-ḥaqq*).** In Arabic a definite predicate states **restriction**: **"the weighing on that day — that is the truth."**

**Two readings are transmitted, and both accord with the language:**

| Reading | What *al-ḥaqq* is | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **The predicate** | "The weighing on that day **is the truth**" — it will take place |
| 2 | **An attribute of *wazn* / "justice" as predicate of the subject** | "On that day there is a **true** weighing" — a measure without shortfall |

**Both are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.** The two readings do not exclude each other.

### بِمَا كَانُوا۟ بِـَٔايَٰتِنَا يَظْلِمُونَ — the work of the preposition

**A point of word order must be recorded: the verb *yaẓlimūn* comes here with the preposition *bi-*.**

**In Arabic *ẓalama* is most often directly transitive** (*ẓalamnā anfusanā*, 7/23). **Its coming with *bi-* gives the sense "to do wrong in the matter of something, not to put a thing in its proper place".**

The concrete sense of the root **ظ-ل-م** is **"to put a thing somewhere other than its own place"**; the root was treated at 31/13 in `031-lokman.md` (*inna'sh-shirka la-ẓulmun ʿaẓīm*). **I rely on that treatment.**

**I record this as my own reading:** the sentence does not say "they denied Our signs"; **it says "they did wrong in the matter of Our signs"** — that is, they put the sign somewhere other than where it stood. **And precisely this verb will be used of Pharaoh and his people in verse 103 of this sūra:** *fa-**ẓalamū bihā*** ("they did wrong in the matter of the signs"). **The same rare construction in two places.** This is a repetition that can be verified within the sūra.

---

## 7/10 — وَلَقَدْ مَكَّنَّٰكُمْ فِى ٱلْأَرْضِ وَجَعَلْنَا لَكُمْ فِيهَا مَعَٰيِشَ قَلِيلًا مَّا تَشْكُرُونَ

*Wa laqad makkannākum fi'l-arḍi wa jaʿalnā lakum fīhā maʿāyish, qalīlan mā tashkurūn*

"**We have established you on the earth and given you the means of livelihood in it. How little you give thanks!**"

### مَكَّنَّا — the root م-ك-ن

**The concrete sense of the root: to provide a place for something, to settle it firmly.** *Makān* is from the same root (some lexicographers count it from the root *kawn*; **both views are transmitted, and I do not impose a preference**). ***Tamkīn* is to give someone the power to stand and to dispose in a place.**

**The root was treated at 12/21 and 12/56 in `012-yusuf.md` (*wa kadhālika makkannā li-Yūsufa fi'l-arḍ*) and at 18/84 in `018-kehf.md` (*innā makkannā lahū fi'l-arḍ*).** **I rely on those treatments.** **I record the difference: there *tamkīn* was an authority given to one person; here it is a ground given to all mankind.**

### مَعَايِش — the root ع-ي-ش

***Maʿāyish*, the plural of *maʿīsha*: the things by which life is lived, the means of livelihood.** **The root ع-ي-ش: to live.** **The same root was treated at 20/124 in `020-taha.md` as *maʿīshatan ḍankā* (a straitened livelihood)** — there the **narrowing** of livelihood is described, here its **being given**.

**That the word comes in the plural must be recorded:** not "a livelihood" but **"livelihoods"**. **What is stated is that there is on the earth not one path of living but many.**

### قَلِيلًا مَّا تَشْكُرُونَ — the overlap with the third verse

**A point of word order and a link within the sūra must be recorded.** The third verse ended with *qalīlan mā **tadhakkarūn***; the tenth ends with *qalīlan mā **tashkurūn***.

| Verse | Closing | What is little |
|---|---|---|
| **7/3** | *Qalīlan mā **tadhakkarūn*** | The littleness of **taking to heart** |
| **7/10** | *Qalīlan mā **tashkurūn*** | The littleness of **thanks** |

**And the same closing was recorded at 32/9 in `032-secde.md` and at 23/78 in `023-muminun.md`** — there it came after the giving of hearing, sight and heart. **That triad will meet us again in verse 179 of al-Aʿrāf.** **A link can be drawn between the two places, and I give it as my own reading:** the tenth verse names **the ground that was given**, the hundred and seventy-ninth **the faculties that were given**; **the closing of both looks to the same place.**

**And two readings are transmitted of the pattern *qalīlan mā*:**

| Reading | What *mā* is | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Redundant** (*zāʾida*, for emphasis) | "You give very little thanks" |
| 2 | **Maṣdariyya** | "Your giving of thanks is little" |

**Both readings are transmitted, and they do not change the meaning in any essential way.**

### What this says to the present

**The verse separates the giving of a possibility from the noticing of that possibility.** *Makkannā* and *jaʿalnā* are in the past tense — the work is done. *Tashkurūn* is imperfect — it is a continuing state.

**That is, the text says with a tense that what was given is complete and the answer to it is deficient.** This is something that can be read off the word order; there is no need to add an interpretation to it.

---

# II. Adam and Iblīs — 7/11-25

**There are seven tellings of this story in the Qurʾān: al-Baqara 2/30-38, al-Aʿrāf 7/11-25, al-Ḥijr 15/28-44, al-Isrāʾ 17/61-65, al-Kahf 18/50, Ṭāhā 20/115-123, Ṣād 38/71-85.** **That of al-Aʿrāf is the longest and the most detailed.**

**In this index the story has been treated three times, and in each a different layer was undone:**

| File | What was treated | The name of the matter |
|---|---|---|
| `002-bakara.md` 2/30-38 | The vicegerent, the names, the question of the angels, *lā taqrabā*, *azallahumā*, repentance | ***Zalla*** — the slip |
| `038-sad.md` 38/71-85 | The logical form of the *anā khayrun minh* syllogism, the table of خ-ي-ر, threefold emphasis | ***Qiyās*** — the error in reasoning |
| `020-taha.md` 20/115-123 | *Fa-nasiya*, *lam najid lahū ʿazmā*, the single occurrence of *abā*, the words of the whispering | ***Nisyān*** — forgetting |

**I rely on all three and do not repeat here what was treated there.** **The following in particular were exhausted there and will not be undone again here:**

- **That the prostration is not a prostration of worship**, and that *istikbār* in Form X means "to reckon oneself great" (`002-bakara.md` 2/34).
- **The undoing of the syllogism *anā khayrun minhu khalaqtanī min nārin wa khalaqtahū min ṭīn*** — the premises true, the conclusion not following from the premises; the comparison "fire burns and consumes, earth bears and brings forth"; the two-directional use of the root خ-ي-ر within Ṣād (`038-sad.md` 38/76, `002-bakara.md` 2/34).
- **The question whether Iblīs was an angel** (`002-bakara.md`, `018-kehf.md` 18/50).
- **That the verbs come in the dual, and that the Qurʾān does not blame the woman separately** (`002-bakara.md` 2/35-36; as evidence `020-taha.md` 20/120-121).
- **The Qurʾān's silence about the kind of tree** (`002-bakara.md` 2/35). **This commentary will be silent too; no entry is made into the *Isrāʾīliyyāt*.**

**Now I write what belongs to al-Aʿrāf. Al-Aʿrāf has four differences, and all four are countable:**

| # | What is peculiar to al-Aʿrāf | Verse | Where else it occurs |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | ***Khalaqnākum thumma ṣawwarnākum*** — an opening in the plural address | **11** | **Nowhere** |
| 2 | ***Fa-bimā aghwaytanī la-aqʿudanna lahum ṣirāṭaka'l-mustaqīm*** | **16** | **Nowhere** |
| 3 | ***Min bayni aydīhim wa min khalfihim wa ʿan aymānihim wa ʿan shamāʾilihim*** — **four directions** | **17** | **Nowhere** |
| 4 | ***Mā nahākumā rabbukumā … illā an takūnā malakayni aw takūnā mina'l-khālidīn*** — the ground of the whispering | **20** | In Ṭāhā with a different sentence (20/120) |

**And two verbs are peculiar to al-Aʿrāf: *qāsamahumā* (21) and *dallāhumā* (22).** Both will be undone separately below.

---

## 7/11 — وَلَقَدْ خَلَقْنَٰكُمْ ثُمَّ صَوَّرْنَٰكُمْ ثُمَّ قُلْنَا لِلْمَلَٰٓئِكَةِ ٱسْجُدُوا۟ لِءَادَمَ فَسَجَدُوٓا۟ إِلَّآ إِبْلِيسَ لَمْ يَكُن مِّنَ ٱلسَّٰجِدِينَ

*Wa laqad khalaqnākum thumma ṣawwarnākum thumma qulnā li'l-malāʾikati'sjudū li-Ādama fa-sajadū illā Iblīsa lam yakun mina's-sājidīn*

"**We created you, then We gave you form, then We said to the angels, 'Prostrate yourselves to Adam'; and they prostrated themselves, all but Iblīs — he was not among those who prostrated.**"

### That the address is plural — the first difference of al-Aʿrāf

**This is the only instance among the seven tellings in the Qurʾān, and it must be recorded.**

| Sūra | Opening | To whom |
|---|---|---|
| al-Baqara 2/30 | *Innī jāʿilun fi'l-arḍi khalīfa* | **To the angels** |
| al-Ḥijr 15/28 | *Innī khāliqun basharan* | **To the angels** |
| Ṣād 38/71 | *Innī khāliqun basharan min ṭīn* | **To the angels** |
| Ṭāhā 20/115 | *Wa laqad ʿahidnā ilā Ādam* | **Narrative** |
| **al-Aʿrāf 7/11** | *Wa laqad khalaqnā**kum** thumma ṣawwarnā**kum*** | **Directly to the reader** |

**The verse does not say "We created Adam"; it says "We created you." Then within the same sentence it passes over to Adam.**

**Two readings are transmitted, and both have been defended by the lexicographers:**

| Reading | Ground |
|---|---|
| 1 | **The pronoun *kum* means Adam**, because the human species is gathered in him; it means "your forefather was created" |
| 2 | **The pronoun really does mean all mankind;** creation and forming look to the human kind, the command to prostrate to Adam |

**Both are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.** **But this is worth recording, and I give it as an observation:** whichever reading is taken, **the word order places the reader inside the story.** **And from the twenty-sixth verse on the sūra will call out *yā banī Ādam* — that is, the narrative begins with a pronoun that makes the reader an addressee and continues with a vocative that makes him an addressee.**

### صَوَّرْنَا — the root ص-و-ر

**The sense of the root: to give a thing form, shape, figure.** *Ṣūra* is from the same root; *muṣawwir* is among the names of God (al-Ḥashr 59/24).

**The root was treated at 82/7-8 in `082-infitar.md` (*fa-sawwāka fa-ʿadalaka fī ayyi ṣūratin mā shāʾa rakkabak*) and at 40/64 in `040-gafir.md` and 64/3 in `064-tegabun.md` (*wa ṣawwarakum fa-aḥsana ṣuwarakum*).** **I rely on those treatments.**

**What belongs here is that the two verbs are separated by *thumma*:**

> *khalaqnākum* — ***thumma*** — *ṣawwarnākum* — ***thumma*** — *qulnā li'l-malāʾika*

The particle **ثُمَّ** states sequence and **interval** (*fāʾ* states immediate sequence). **That is, the text counts three stages separately: creating, forming, the command to prostrate.**

**I record this as my own reading and it is not binding; its ground is the particle itself:** the command to prostrate comes **not immediately after the creating, but after the forming.** **And at 38/72 in `038-sad.md` the ground of the command was given as *fa-idhā sawwaytuhū wa nafakhtu fīhi min rūḥī*** — there too the *taswiya* (the setting in order) is named before the command. **The two sūras keep the same order.**

### لَمْ يَكُن مِّنَ ٱلسَّٰجِدِينَ — not an act, but a belonging

**The second difference of word order in al-Aʿrāf is here, and it must be recorded.**

| Sūra | The expression used of Iblīs | What it states |
|---|---|---|
| al-Baqara 2/34 | *Abā wa'stakbara **wa kāna mina'l-kāfirīn*** | **Three verbs** |
| Ṣād 38/74 | *Istakbara **wa kāna mina'l-kāfirīn*** | **Two verbs** |
| Ṭāhā 20/116 | ***Abā*** | **One verb** |
| **al-Aʿrāf 7/11** | ***Lam yakun mina's-sājidīn*** | **No verb — a not-belonging to a group** |

**Al-Aʿrāf states not something Iblīs *did* but something he *was not*: "he was not among those who prostrated."**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the comparison of the four sūras:** where the other three give the act (*abā*, *istakbara*), al-Aʿrāf gives the **position**. **And this accords with al-Aʿrāf's building the whole story as a *separation*:** in later sections too the sūra will always name people in **two groups** (*farīqan hadā wa farīqan ḥaqqa ʿalayhimu'ḍ-ḍalāla*, 30; *aṣḥābu'l-janna* / *aṣḥābu'n-nār*, 44). **The language of al-Aʿrāf is the language of groups.**

**Note:** the debate over whether the exception in *fa-sajadū illā Iblīsa* is connected (*muttaṣil*) or disconnected (*munqaṭiʿ*) — that is, whether Iblīs was counted among the angels — was treated in `002-bakara.md` and at 18/50 in `018-kehf.md`. **I rely on those treatments; I do not repeat them here.**

---

## 7/12 — قَالَ مَا مَنَعَكَ أَلَّا تَسْجُدَ إِذْ أَمَرْتُكَ قَالَ أَنَا۠ خَيْرٌ مِّنْهُ خَلَقْتَنِى مِن نَّارٍ وَخَلَقْتَهُۥ مِن طِينٍ

*Qāla mā manaʿaka allā tasjuda idh amartuk · Qāla anā khayrun minhu khalaqtanī min nārin wa khalaqtahū min ṭīn*

"**He said: 'What kept you from prostrating when I commanded you?' · He said: 'I am better than he; You created me from fire, and You created him from clay.'**"

### This sentence was treated twice in the index

**The undoing of the sentence *anā khayrun minhu khalaqtanī min nārin wa khalaqtahū min ṭīn* was carried out in full at 38/76 in `038-sad.md`, and its ground was laid at 2/34 in `002-bakara.md`.** **I rely on those treatments and do not repeat them here.** What was treated there:

- **The logical form of the syllogism:** both premises are true (*khalaqtanī min nār* and *khalaqtahū min ṭīn* are each statements of fact); **the error lies in a step of superiority that does not follow from the premises being added of its own accord.**
- **"Fire burns and consumes; earth bears and brings forth"** — that the comparison resting on the quality of the materials is itself faulty.
- **That Iblīs's answer says nothing at all about the command:** the question was about obedience, the answer about worth.
- **The table of the root خ-ي-ر:** *al-akhyār* (Ṣād 38/47) is an attribute that has been given, *anā khayrun minh* an attribute that is claimed.
- ***Mā manaʿaka*** — not "why did you not prostrate" but "**what kept you**": the word order that shows prostration was taken as a matter of course.

### The difference between al-Aʿrāf and Ṣād — what belongs here

**The two sūras give the same sentence but build the question differently. The difference is a single letter, and it must be recorded.**

| Sūra | Question | Difference |
|---|---|---|
| **Ṣād 38/75** | *Mā manaʿaka **an** tasjuda li-mā khalaqtu bi-yadayy* | ***An*** — affirmative |
| **al-Aʿrāf 7/12** | *Mā manaʿaka **allā** tasjuda idh amartuk* | ***Allā* (an lā)** — negative |
| al-Ḥijr 15/32 | *Mā laka **allā** takūna maʿa's-sājidīn* | ***Allā*** — negative |

**That is, in al-Aʿrāf and al-Ḥijr the question takes the form "what kept you from not prostrating?" This is a construction that has drawn notice in Arabic, and two explanations are transmitted:**

| Explanation | Ground |
|---|---|
| 1 | ***Lā* is redundant (for emphasis)**; the sense is the same as in Ṣād: "what kept you from prostrating" |
| 2 | ***Lā* is not redundant**; the verb *manaʿaka* has been given by *taḍmīn* the sense "what drove you to this": "what carried you to not prostrating" |

**Both explanations have been voiced by the classical lexicographers; I do not impose a preference.** Both arrive at the same result: **the question is asking for a ground.**

### إِذْ أَمَرْتُكَ — the note al-Aʿrāf adds

**In Ṣād the note added to the question was *li-mā khalaqtu bi-yadayy* (treated in `038-sad.md` with the table of *tafwīḍ*/*taʾwīl*).** **The note al-Aʿrāf adds is a different one: *idh amartuk* — "when I commanded you".**

**I record this as an observation, and its ground is the comparison of the two sūras:**

| Sūra | Added to the question | What it draws attention to |
|---|---|---|
| **Ṣād 38/75** | *Li-mā khalaqtu bi-yadayy* | **To the standing of the one prostrated to** |
| **al-Aʿrāf 7/12** | ***Idh amartuk*** | **To the command itself** |

**And Iblīs's answer is the same in both: he speaks of the material.** **That is, in al-Aʿrāf the question expressly recalls the command, and the answer again passes over the command.** This makes the observation recorded in `038-sad.md` — "the question was about obedience, the answer about worth" — **sharper in al-Aʿrāf**, because in al-Aʿrāf the command is **expressly named** in the question.

### طِين — the root ط-ي-ن

***Ṭīn*: earth mixed with water, clay.** **The root was treated at 38/71 in `038-sad.md`** (*khāliqun basharan min ṭīn*). **I rely on that treatment.**

**The Qurʾān names the substance from which man was created with more than one word, and this is a countable datum:**

| Word | Meaning | Where |
|---|---|---|
| *Turāb* | Dry earth | 3/59, 22/5, 30/20 |
| ***Ṭīn*** | **Earth mixed with water** | **7/12**, 38/71, 6/2 |
| *Ṭīn lāzib* | Sticky clay | 37/11 |
| *Ḥamaʾ masnūn* | Altered, foul-smelling mud | 15/26, 28, 33 |
| *Ṣalṣāl ka'l-fakhkhār* | Dry clay like baked pottery | 55/14 |
| *Sulāla min ṭīn* | An extract drawn from clay | 23/12 |

**These words were treated separately at 55/14 in `055-rahman.md` and at 23/12 in `023-muminun.md`.** **The one note that belongs here is this, and I give it as an observation:** **the word that comes out of Iblīs's mouth is *ṭīn* — not dry earth but clay.** **And with the *nār* (fire) he sets against it, this word forms two extremes: the most mobile and the most inert.** **Why the comparison is itself faulty was treated in `002-bakara.md` and `038-sad.md`; I rely on those treatments.**

---

## 7/13 — قَالَ فَٱهْبِطْ مِنْهَا فَمَا يَكُونُ لَكَ أَن تَتَكَبَّرَ فِيهَا فَٱخْرُجْ إِنَّكَ مِنَ ٱلصَّٰغِرِينَ

*Qāla fa'hbiṭ minhā fa-mā yakūnu laka an tatakabbara fīhā fa'khruj innaka mina'ṣ-ṣāghirīn*

"**He said: 'Then go down from it! It is not for you to be great in it. Go out! You are among the lowly.'**"

### Three commands in succession

**A point of word order must be recorded: the verse has three commands, and two of them do the same work.**

| Order | Command | Root | What it states |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | *Fa'**hbiṭ*** | ه-ب-ط | **Go down** — movement downward |
| 2 | — | — | *Fa-mā yakūnu laka an tatakabbara fīhā* — **the ground** |
| 3 | *Fa'**khruj*** | خ-ر-ج | **Go out** — movement outward |

**The two verbs state two different directions: *hubūṭ* is vertical (downward), *khurūj* horizontal (outward).** **And the ground comes in between them.**

The root **ه-ب-ط**: to come down from a high place. **The root was treated at 2/36 and 2/61 in `002-bakara.md` (*ihbiṭū miṣran*) and at 11/48 in `011-hud.md` (*ihbiṭ bi-salāmin minnā*).** **I rely on those treatments.**

**I record this as my own reading and it is not binding:** the same verb (*ihbiṭū*) will be said to Adam too in the twenty-fourth verse. **That is, in al-Aʿrāf both Iblīs and Adam receive the command "go down"** — but alongside Iblīs's there stands *innaka mina'ṣ-ṣāghirīn*, and alongside Adam's *wa lakum fi'l-arḍi mustaqarrun wa matāʿun ilā ḥīn*. **The same verb, with two different endings.** This is a contrast that can be verified from the text.

### ٱلصَّٰغِرِين — the root ص-غ-ر

**The sense of the root: smallness.** ***Ṣaghār* is explained by the lexicographers as "abasement, being brought low" — that is, not smallness of size but smallness of position.***

The word will occur a second time in this sūra: *wa'nqalabū **ṣāghirīn*** (119) — **used of Pharaoh's side when the sorcerers are defeated.**

| Place | Who | Context |
|---|---|---|
| **7/13** | **Iblīs** | The answer to making oneself great |
| **7/119** | **Pharaoh's sorcerers / his side** | The answer to defeat |

**I record this as an observation:** the sūra uses the same word both at the beginning of the story and in the middle of it. **And in both places the word comes as the answer to *takabbur* (making oneself great)** — expressly in verse 13 (*an tatakabbara fīhā* → *mina'ṣ-ṣāghirīn*), indirectly in verse 119 (*fa-ghulibū hunālika wa'nqalabū ṣāghirīn*).

### فَمَا يَكُونُ لَكَ أَن تَتَكَبَّرَ فِيهَا — the form of the ground

**The sentence does not forbid by saying "do not be great"; it says "being great is not a thing that is to be yours".**

**The pattern *mā yakūnu laka an…* in Arabic gives the sense "this is not for you, this does not befit you".** The same pattern was treated at 19/35 in `019-meryem.md` (*mā kāna li'llāhi an yattakhidha min walad*) and at 33/36 in `033-ahzab.md`.

**I record this as my own reading:** the sentence is not a **prohibition** but a **statement of impossibility**. **What Iblīs did is named not as a thing forbidden to be done but as a thing that will not be.** **And the finding recorded in `002-bakara.md`, that pride is not a reality but a supposition, finds its counterpart here at the level of word order** — the "to reckon it so" sense of Form X / Form V and the "not a thing that will be" sense of the pattern *mā yakūnu laka* look to the same place.

---

## 7/14-15 — قَالَ أَنظِرْنِىٓ إِلَىٰ يَوْمِ يُبْعَثُونَ · قَالَ إِنَّكَ مِنَ ٱلْمُنظَرِينَ

*Qāla anẓirnī ilā yawmi yubʿathūn · Qāla innaka mina'l-munẓarīn*

"**He said: 'Give me respite until the day they are raised.' · He said: 'You are among those given respite.'**"

### The root ن-ظ-ر and *inẓār*

**The primary sense of the root is "to look"; in Form IV (*anẓara*) it means "to grant someone a span of looking", that is, to give a reprieve.** **The three occurrences of the root were tabulated at 38/79-80 in `038-sad.md`** (Ṣād 38/15, 79, 80). **I rely on that treatment.**

### The limit of the respite given — three sūras, two expressions

**This is a difference that must be recorded, and it was tabulated as a disagreement in `038-sad.md`. Here I only repeat the datum:**

| Sūra | What Iblīs asked | The answer given |
|---|---|---|
| **al-Aʿrāf 7/14-15** | *Ilā yawmi yubʿathūn* | ***Innaka mina'l-munẓarīn*** — the limit is **not stated** |
| al-Ḥijr 15/36-38 | *Ilā yawmi yubʿathūn* | *Innaka mina'l-munẓarīn* — ***ilā yawmi'l-waqti'l-maʿlūm*** |
| Ṣād 38/79-81 | *Ilā yawmi yubʿathūn* | *Innaka mina'l-munẓarīn* — ***ilā yawmi'l-waqti'l-maʿlūm*** |

**Al-Aʿrāf does not give the note (*ilā yawmi'l-waqti'l-maʿlūm*) found in the other two sūras.** **This is a countable absence, and the disagreement "are the two expressions the same" was tabulated in `038-sad.md`; I do not impose a preference.**

**I record this as an observation:** the answer does not say "as much as you asked", it says "you are among those given respite". **The request contains a date; the answer contents itself with including him in a group.** **And al-Aʿrāf, by not naming here the limit the other two sūras set, keeps this indefiniteness of the answer.**

---

## 7/16-17 — قَالَ فَبِمَآ أَغْوَيْتَنِى لَأَقْعُدَنَّ لَهُمْ صِرَٰطَكَ ٱلْمُسْتَقِيمَ · ثُمَّ لَءَاتِيَنَّهُم مِّنۢ بَيْنِ أَيْدِيهِمْ وَمِنْ خَلْفِهِمْ وَعَنْ أَيْمَٰنِهِمْ وَعَن شَمَآئِلِهِمْ وَلَا تَجِدُ أَكْثَرَهُمْ شَٰكِرِينَ

*Qāla fa-bimā aghwaytanī la-aqʿudanna lahum ṣirāṭaka'l-mustaqīm · Thumma la-ātiyannahum min bayni aydīhim wa min khalfihim wa ʿan aymānihim wa ʿan shamāʾilihim, wa lā tajidu aktharahum shākirīn*

"**He said: 'For Your having led me astray, I shall certainly sit for them upon Your straight path. · Then I shall come at them from before them and from behind them and from their right and from their left. And You will not find most of them thankful.'**"

### فَبِمَآ أَغْوَيْتَنِى — the direction of the blame

**This sentence is peculiar to al-Aʿrāf, and it is treated in this index here for the first time.**

The root **غ-و-ي**: **to stray from the right path, to go astray, to miss the mark.** The lexicographers define *ghayy* as the opposite of *rushd* (rightness). **The root was treated at 20/121 in `020-taha.md` (*wa ʿaṣā Ādamu rabbahū fa-ghawā*); I rely on that treatment.**

**And this root has four occurrences in this sūra:**

| Verse | Expression | Who |
|---|---|---|
| **7/16** | *Fa-bimā **aghwaytanī*** | **Iblīs** — about himself |
| **7/146** | *Wa in yaraw sabīla'l-**ghayyi** yattakhidhūhu sabīlā* | **Those turned away from the signs** |
| **7/175** | *Fa-kāna mina'l-**ghāwīn*** | **The man who slipped out of the signs** |
| **7/202** | *Yamuddūnahum fi'l-**ghayyi*** | **The brothers of the satans** |

**Four occurrences; the first in verse 16, the last in verse 202.** **The sūra uses the root both at its beginning and at its end.**

### The grammatical question of the sentence — the particle ب

**Two readings are transmitted of the *bāʾ* in the phrase *fa-bimā aghwaytanī*, and each changes the meaning:**

| Reading | The function of the *bāʾ* | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | ***Sababiyya*** (stating cause) | "**Because You led me astray**" — he gives a cause |
| 2 | ***Qasam*** (the *bāʾ* of the oath) | "**By Your having led me astray**" — he swears an oath |

**The ground of the second reading is that the answer comes as *la-aqʿudanna*, with the *lām* and the emphatic *nūn*; this is the pattern of the response to an oath in Arabic.** **Both readings have been voiced by the classical lexicographers; I do not impose a preference.**

**And the comparison recorded at 2/37 in `002-bakara.md` is completed here:** there Adam's prayer (*rabbanā ẓalamnā anfusanā*) was compared with Iblīs's sentence, and it was recorded that **"Iblīs too spoke, but he gave a ground and laid the blame on God (al-Aʿrāf 7/16)"**. **I rely on that treatment.**

**What belongs here is this, and I give it as an observation:** Iblīs's first sentence (12) put forward **his own superiority**; his second sentence (16) hands off **his own responsibility.** **There is an inconsistency between the two sentences, and the text leaves it without repair:** the superiority is his own, the going astray another's.

### لَأَقْعُدَنَّ لَهُمْ صِرَٰطَكَ ٱلْمُسْتَقِيمَ — the concreteness of the verb

The root **ق-ع-د**: to sit. **That this verb is the one chosen here must be recorded: not "I shall attack" or "I shall obstruct", but "I shall sit".**

**A point of word order:** *la-aqʿudanna lahum **ṣirāṭaka'l-mustaqīm*** — ***ṣirāṭ* comes in the accusative, that is, in the position of an adverbial: "upon the path / along the path".** In Arabic a noun of place put in the accusative without *fī* gives the sense "in that place, along that place".

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the choice of the verb and of the position:** sitting is an act of **waiting and of cutting off the road**; it is an act not of moving but of taking up a position. **And the place chosen is not a crooked road — it is *ṣirāṭaka'l-mustaqīm*, "Your straight path".**

**And this overlaps with verse 86 of the same sūra:** Shuʿayb says to his people *wa lā **taqʿudū** bi-kulli ṣirāṭin tūʿidūna wa taṣuddūna ʿan sabīli'llāh* — **word for word the same verb, word for word the same work.**

| Place | Who | Sentence |
|---|---|---|
| **7/16** | **Iblīs** | *La-**aqʿudanna** lahum **ṣirāṭaka'l-mustaqīm*** |
| **7/86** | **The notables of Madyan** | *Wa lā **taqʿudū** bi-kulli **ṣirāṭin** tūʿidūna wa taṣuddūna ʿan sabīli'llāh* |

**I record this as an observation, and the table can be verified from the text:** the sūra shows in the eighty-sixth verse the threat uttered in the sixteenth **carried out by human hands.** **There are seventy verses between the two; but the verb and the object are the same.**

### The four directions — 7/17

**This is the most distinctive sentence of al-Aʿrāf, and it is treated in full in this index here for the first time.**

| Direction | Expression | Particle |
|---|---|---|
| **Front** | *Min bayni aydīhim* | ***min*** |
| **Behind** | *Min khalfihim* | ***min*** |
| **Right** | *ʿAn aymānihim* | ***ʿan*** |
| **Left** | *ʿAn shamāʾilihim* | ***ʿan*** |

**Three things must be recorded.**

**One — the particle changes.** In the first two directions *min* comes, in the last two *ʿan*. **In Arabic *min* states a starting point, "out of / from the side of"; *ʿan* states moving away and lateral position, "over, alongside".** **This is a datum of word order.** The lexicographers have recorded this difference and given various explanations for it; **I am not in a position to transmit any of them as certain, so I record only the datum.**

**Two — the pair *bayna yadayh / khalfah* was treated earlier in the index.** **At 41/25 in `041-fussilet.md` this pair occurred as the extent of the adorning done by the *qarīn*s, and at 41/42 as the extent of the Book's being guarded, and it was recorded there: "in Arabic this pair is used to cover all the sides of a thing."** **I rely on that treatment.**

**And the difference of al-Aʿrāf becomes visible here:**

| Place | How many directions | For what |
|---|---|---|
| Fuṣṣilat 41/25 | **Two** — *mā bayna aydīhim wa mā khalfahum* | **The extent of the adorning** |
| Fuṣṣilat 41/42 | **Two** — *min bayni yadayhi wa lā min khalfih* | **The extent of the guarding** |
| Sabaʾ 34/9 · al-Baqara 2/255 | **Two** | Encompassing / knowledge |
| **al-Aʿrāf 7/17** | **Four** — front, behind, right, left | **The extent of the approach** |

**In two places two directions suffice for "all directions"; al-Aʿrāf counts four.**

**Three — and this must be recorded: up and down are not counted.**

**I record this as an observation. The text counts four directions and does not complete the six:** *fawq* (above) and *taḥt* (below) are not in the sentence. **And this is not a claim of deficiency; it is the datum of the text.**

**Explanations of this gap are transmitted in the classical commentaries** — it is widely said that "above was not counted because it is the direction from which mercy comes". **This is a transmitted view; I make no firm preference and do not use it as a proof.** **All I record is this: the text counts four directions, not six.**

**And two verses can be set side by side; this is a verifiable overlap:**

| Place | Expression | Directions |
|---|---|---|
| **al-Aʿrāf 7/17** | *Min bayni aydīhim… wa ʿan shamāʾilihim* | **Four** |
| al-Anʿām 6/65 | *Min **fawqikum** aw min **taḥti arjulikum*** | **Above and below** — but there what comes is **the punishment**, not Iblīs |
| al-Naḥl 16/26 | *Fa-ata'llāhu bunyānahum mina'l-**qawāʿid*** | **From the foundations** |

**I record this as my own reading and it is not binding; its ground is the comparison of the three verses:** the Qurʾān uses the directions above and below in other contexts — there the agent is God. **In al-Aʿrāf 7/17 the agent is Iblīs, and the directions he counts are man's horizontal surroundings.** This is an explanation, not a ruling.

### وَلَا تَجِدُ أَكْثَرَهُمْ شَٰكِرِينَ — and the tenth verse

**The last part of the sentence must be recorded, and it establishes a link that can be verified within the sūra.**

**The tenth verse ended with *qalīlan mā tashkurūn*.** **The seventeenth ends with *wa lā tajidu aktharahum shākirīn*.**

| Verse | Who says it | Sentence |
|---|---|---|
| **7/10** | **God** | *Qalīlan mā **tashkurūn*** |
| **7/17** | **Iblīs** | *Wa lā tajidu aktharahum **shākirīn*** |

**I record this as an observation:** there are seven verses between the two, and both end with the same root (ش-ك-ر). **One is a finding, the other a forecast.** **And the text does not correct Iblīs's forecast** — as was also recorded at 38/82-85 of Ṣād (`038-sad.md`), **the judgement is tied not to who Iblīs is but to who follows him.** **I rely on that treatment.**

**And the choice of the word is worth recording: Iblīs does not say "they will not believe", he says "they will not give thanks".** **I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the word itself:** *shukr* is the noticing of what has been given. **The tenth verse had counted what was given (the earth, the means of livelihood); the seventeenth says that what was given will not be noticed.**

---

## 7/18 — قَالَ ٱخْرُجْ مِنْهَا مَذْءُومًا مَّدْحُورًا لَّمَن تَبِعَكَ مِنْهُمْ لَأَمْلَأَنَّ جَهَنَّمَ مِنكُمْ أَجْمَعِينَ

*Qāla'khruj minhā madhʾūman madḥūrā, la-man tabiʿaka minhum la-amlaʾanna jahannama minkum ajmaʿīn*

"**He said: 'Go out from it, despised and driven away! Whoever of them follows you — I shall surely fill Hell with the whole lot of you.'**"

### مَذْءُومًا مَّدْحُورًا — two passive participles

**Both words are on the pattern of the passive participle (مَفْعُول) — that is, both state "the one to whom it is done", not "the one who does".**

| Word | Root | Concrete sense |
|---|---|---|
| **مَذْءُوم** | **ذ-ء-م** | **Faulted, despised** — the root carries "to name a thing's defect" |
| **مَّدْحُور** | **د-ح-ر** | **Pushed off and driven away, expelled** |

The root **د-ح-ر**: to push, to drive off, to send away. **The root was treated at 37/9 in `037-saffat.md` (*duḥūran*)** — there the driving off of those who would eavesdrop on heaven was described. **I rely on that treatment.**

The root **ذ-ء-م** occurs in this index here for the first time, and **it is found only in this verse in the Qurʾān.** The lexicographers explain *dhaʾama* as "to fault, to find defective" and record its kinship with the word *dhamm* (blame). **This kinship is a relation drawn by the lexicographers; I do not give it as a settled etymological ruling.**

**A point of word order must be recorded: both words stand in the position of a circumstantial accusative (*ḥāl*), and there is no conjunction between them.** *Madhʾūman madḥūrā* — "despised, driven away." **Sequence without a conjunction shows in Arabic that the two attributes join into a single state.**

**And the order of the two words is worth recording:** first **being despised** (loss of worth), then **being driven away** (loss of place). **I give this as my own reading:** the command to leave had already been given in the thirteenth verse (*fa'khruj*); **as it is repeated here, two attributes are added to it.** **That is, the second command to leave specifies the character of the first.**

### لَأَمْلَأَنَّ جَهَنَّمَ — the ring in the index

**This sentence occurs in four places in the Qurʾān, and it was treated at 38/85 in `038-sad.md`.** **I rely on that treatment.**

| Place | Sentence |
|---|---|
| **al-Aʿrāf 7/18** | *La-amlaʾanna jahannama **minkum** ajmaʿīn* |
| Hūd 11/119 | *La-amlaʾanna jahannama mina'l-jinnati wa'n-nāsi ajmaʿīn* (`011-hud.md`) |
| Ṣād 38/85 | *La-amlaʾanna jahannama minka wa mimman tabiʿaka minhum ajmaʿīn* |
| al-Sajda 32/13 | *La-amlaʾanna jahannama mina'l-jinnati wa'n-nāsi ajmaʿīn* (`032-secde.md`) |

**The difference of al-Aʿrāf is in the pronoun: *minkum* — plural, "of you".** **In Ṣād the two parts were counted separately, *minka wa mimman tabiʿaka* (of you and of those who follow you); al-Aʿrāf gathers both into a single pronoun.**

**And the construction of the sentence must be recorded: *la-man tabiʿaka minhum* is a conditional clause, and its answer is directly the announcement of Hell.** **That is, the judgement is tied not to Iblīs but to *those who follow him*.** **The same note was entered in `038-sad.md`** — there the link of the root ت-ب-ع between 38/26 and 38/85 was established. **I rely on that treatment.**

**What belongs here is this:** **in al-Aʿrāf the same root (ت-ب-ع) occurred in the third verse as a command** — *itta**bi**ʿū mā unzila ilaykum* / *wa lā tatta**bi**ʿū min dūnihī awliyāʾ*. **The eighteenth verse states what answers to that prohibition: *la-man tabiʿaka minhum*.** **The sūra brings its own command to its conclusion fifteen verses later.** This is a link that can be verified from the text.

---

## 7/19 — وَيَٰٓـَٔادَمُ ٱسْكُنْ أَنتَ وَزَوْجُكَ ٱلْجَنَّةَ فَكُلَا مِنْ حَيْثُ شِئْتُمَا وَلَا تَقْرَبَا هَٰذِهِ ٱلشَّجَرَةَ فَتَكُونَا مِنَ ٱلظَّٰلِمِينَ

*Wa yā Ādamu'skun anta wa zawjuka'l-jannata fa-kulā min ḥaythu shiʾtumā wa lā taqrabā hādhihi'sh-shajarata fa-takūnā mina'ẓ-ẓālimīn*

"**And, O Adam, dwell you and your wife in the Garden; eat from wherever you wish; but do not go near this tree, or you will be among the wrongdoers.**"

### وَيَٰٓـَٔادَمُ — the place of the conjunction

**A point of word order must be recorded: the verse begins with a *wāw*.** *Wa yā Ādam* — that is, this address is built as the continuation of the preceding speech.

**I record this as an observation:** what is said to Iblīs and what is said to Adam are given as **a single sequence of speech**. **In al-Baqara the two scenes were built separately (2/34 and 2/35); al-Aʿrāf joins them with a *wāw*.**

### لَا تَقْرَبَا — the pattern of the prohibition

**This pattern was treated at 2/35 in `002-bakara.md`, and it was recorded there: the prohibition is placed not on the act but on the distance that leads to the act; from this the principle of "closing the means" has been derived, and the parallel of al-Isrāʾ 17/32 (*wa lā taqrabu'z-zinā*) was given.** **I rely on that treatment and do not repeat it here.**

**And it was recorded at 2/187 in `002-bakara.md` that the same pattern returns in the form *tilka ḥudūdu'llāhi fa-lā taqrabūhā*.** **I rely on that treatment as well.**

**The difference that belongs here is this, and the table can be verified from the text:**

| Sūra | Prohibition | The name of the outcome |
|---|---|---|
| al-Baqara 2/35 | *Wa lā taqrabā hādhihi'sh-shajarata* | *Fa-takūnā mina'**ẓ-ẓālimīn*** |
| **al-Aʿrāf 7/19** | *Wa lā taqrabā hādhihi'sh-shajarata* | *Fa-takūnā mina'**ẓ-ẓālimīn*** |
| Ṭāhā 20/117 | *Fa-lā yukhrijannakumā mina'l-janna* | *Fa-**tashqā*** — you will fall into hardship |

**Al-Baqara and al-Aʿrāf are word for word identical at this point.** **Ṭāhā, however, builds the prohibition not upon the tree but upon Iblīs, and names the outcome *shaqāwa* (hardship) — this was treated in `020-taha.md` as the backbone of that sūra.**

**And the expression *fa-takūnā mina'ẓ-ẓālimīn* must be recorded:** the outcome is given as **belonging to a group** — exactly like *lam yakun mina's-sājidīn* in the eleventh verse. **The group language of al-Aʿrāf is at work here too.**

**And this expression overlaps with the confession in the twenty-third verse:** *rabbanā **ẓalamnā** anfusanā*. **The root used in the warning (ظ-ل-م) is used in the confession too.** **That is, the confession is made with the word of the warning.** This is an overlap that can be verified from the text.

### مِنْ حَيْثُ شِئْتُمَا — the breadth of the permission

**A point of word order: the permission comes as *min ḥaythu shiʾtumā* ("from wherever you wish"), the prohibition as *hādhihi'sh-shajarata* ("this tree") — the one open-ended, the other bounded by a demonstrative.**

**In al-Baqara 2/35 the permission was *raghadan ḥaythu shiʾtumā* ("in plenty, from wherever you wish").** **Al-Aʿrāf does not give the word *raghadan*.** This is a countable difference.

**I record this as an observation:** both texts give the permission **before** the prohibition, and the permission is **wider** than the prohibition. **The prohibition is one tree; the permission is all the rest.**

---

## 7/20-21 — فَوَسْوَسَ لَهُمَا ٱلشَّيْطَٰنُ لِيُبْدِىَ لَهُمَا مَا وُۥرِىَ عَنْهُمَا مِن سَوْءَٰتِهِمَا وَقَالَ مَا نَهَىٰكُمَا رَبُّكُمَا عَنْ هَٰذِهِ ٱلشَّجَرَةِ إِلَّآ أَن تَكُونَا مَلَكَيْنِ أَوْ تَكُونَا مِنَ ٱلْخَٰلِدِينَ · وَقَاسَمَهُمَآ إِنِّى لَكُمَا لَمِنَ ٱلنَّٰصِحِينَ

*Fa-waswasa lahumā'sh-shayṭānu li-yubdiya lahumā mā wūriya ʿanhumā min sawʾātihimā wa qāla mā nahākumā rabbukumā ʿan hādhihi'sh-shajarati illā an takūnā malakayni aw takūnā mina'l-khālidīn · Wa qāsamahumā innī lakumā la-mina'n-nāṣiḥīn*

"**Then Satan whispered to them, to make plain to them what had been covered from them of their shameful parts, and he said: 'Your Lord forbade you this tree only lest you become two angels or become of those who abide for ever.' · And he swore to them: 'I am indeed one of those who wish you well.'**"

### وَسْوَسَ — and the difference of the particle

The root **و-س-و-س**: **a very light, continuous sound, a sound at the level of a whisper.** The lexicographers explain *waswasa* by "the faint sound made by ornaments touching one another". **In respect of its pattern it counts as a sound-imitating word (onomatopoeia).** **The root was treated in `114-nas.md` (*al-waswāsi'l-khannās*) and at 20/120 in `020-taha.md`.** **I rely on those treatments.**

**What belongs here is a difference of particle, and it must be recorded:**

| Place | Verb + particle | To whom |
|---|---|---|
| Ṭāhā 20/120 | *Fa-waswasa **ilayhi** 'sh-shayṭān* | **To Adam** (singular) |
| **al-Aʿrāf 7/20** | *Fa-waswasa **lahumā** 'sh-shayṭān* | **To the two of them** (dual) |

**There are two differences: the number of those addressed, and the particle.**

***Ilā* states direction ("towards him"); the *lām* here means "for them, to them".** **The lexicographers record that the usage *waswasa lahū* carries the tone "he whispered for him, as though on his side".** **This is a transmitted report; I do not give it as a settled ruling.**

**And the number of those addressed must be recorded:** **in al-Aʿrāf the whispering is to the two of them at once.** **At 2/35-36 in `002-bakara.md` the coming of the verbs in the dual and the Qurʾān's not blaming the woman separately were treated; I rely on that treatment.** **Al-Aʿrāf strengthens that datum: the addressee of the whispering is dual as well.**

### سَوْءَات — the root س-و-أ

***Sawʾa* is the singular of *sawʾāt*; the root س-و-أ: to be bad, to be ugly.** *Sūʾ*, *sayyiʾa*, *aswaʾ* are from the same root. ***Sawʾa* is used in the sense of "the place whose exposure is not welcome"**, and this is a covering that comes from the word itself.

**A point of word order must be recorded: the word is not directly the name of an organ.** **That is, the Qurʾān names the place that is to be covered not by its name but by its quality.**

**I record this as an observation:** the word is itself a covering. **And the word occurs five times in this sūra** — 20, 22, 26, 27 (twice). **Three of the five occurrences are in the *yā banī Ādam* section.** **That is, what is named in the story is bound immediately afterwards to the reader.**

### مَّا وُۥرِىَ — the work of the passive verb

The root **و-ر-ي**: to cover, to conceal. **The same root will be repeated in verse 26 as *yuwārī sawʾātikum*.**

**A point of word order: *wūriya* is a passive verb — "that which had been covered".** **The agent is not stated.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the voice of the verb:** the text does not say "what they had covered" or "what God had covered"; **it says only "what had been covered".** **And in the twenty-sixth verse the same root will come back in the active: *libāsan yuwārī sawʾātikum* — there what covers is clothing, and that clothing has been sent down (*anzalnā*).** **A relation of answer can be drawn between the two verses; but I give this not as a certainty, only as a possible reading.**

### The content of the whispering — the second great difference of al-Aʿrāf

**The twentieth verse gives Iblīs's sentence in full, and this sentence is found nowhere else in the Qurʾān:**

> *Mā nahākumā rabbukumā ʿan hādhihi'sh-shajarati **illā an takūnā malakayni aw takūnā mina'l-khālidīn***

**The construction of the sentence is a restriction (*ḥaṣr*, *qaṣr*): *mā … illā* — "He did not forbid it, except for this".**

**That is, Iblīs alters the ground of the prohibition: the prohibition is presented not as protection but as obstruction.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the grammatical structure of the sentence:** **Iblīs does not deny the prohibition.** He accepts that it exists, and renames its **intention**. **And this is an operation of the same kind as the syllogism in the twelfth verse:** there a false conclusion was added to true premises; **here a false ground is added to a true piece of knowledge (that there is a prohibition).**

### مَلَكَيْنِ — a variant reading

**There is a variant reading in this word that changes the meaning, and it must be recorded:**

| Reading | Spelling | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| ***malakayni*** | مَلَكَيْنِ | **Two angels** |
| ***malikayni*** | مَلِكَيْنِ | **Two kings** |

**The first reading is the widespread one; the second is also transmitted.** **I do not impose a preference.**

**As ground for the second reading the sentence at 20/120 in Ṭāhā is cited: *hal adulluka ʿalā shajarati'l-khuldi wa mulkin lā yablā* — "the tree of immortality and a kingdom that does not decay".** **That is, the two things promised in Ṭāhā are immortality and kingship.**

**The two sūras can be compared, and the table can be verified from the text:**

| Sūra | The first thing promised | The second thing promised |
|---|---|---|
| **al-Aʿrāf 7/20** | ***Malakayn*** (or *malikayn*) | ***Mina'l-khālidīn*** — those who abide for ever |
| Ṭāhā 20/120 | ***Mulkin lā yablā*** — a kingdom that does not decay | ***Shajarati'l-khuld*** — the tree of immortality |

**In both sūras the second thing is the same: immortality.** **In the first thing the widespread reading of al-Aʿrāf says "angel", that of Ṭāhā says "kingdom".** **This promise was treated at 20/120-123 in `020-taha.md`; I rely on that treatment.**

**I record this as an observation:** both promises are promises of **crossing man's limit** — one of **kind** (to become an angel or a king), the other of **duration** (to become deathless). **And read together with the four directions of the seventeenth verse, it becomes visible that Iblīs's offers are always a promise of *widening*.** This is an explanation, not a ruling.

### وَقَاسَمَهُمَآ — the point of Form III

The root **ق-س-م**: to divide, to apportion; *qasam* is an oath. ***Qāsama* is a verb of Form III (فَاعَلَ), and this form in Arabic states reciprocity.**

**A point of word order must be recorded, and the lexicographers have recorded it:** *qāsamahumā* — "he exchanged oaths with them". **The verb is in a pattern that implies not merely that Iblīs swore, but as though there were a mutual swearing.**

**Two explanations are transmitted:**

| Explanation | Ground |
|---|---|
| 1 | **Form III is here for intensification**; it states a one-sided but **insistent** oath |
| 2 | **There really is reciprocity**: Iblīs swore, and they declared their trust in him |

**Both explanations are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.**

**I record this as my own reading and it is not binding:** **the choice of the verb shows that the deception is built not like a one-way assault but like an *exchange*.** **And the verb *dallāhumā* in the next verse will look to the same place.**

### ٱلنَّٰصِحِين — and the sequence of prophets in the sūra

The root **ن-ص-ح**: **to make a thing pure and unmixed** (honey is called *nāṣiḥ*, "unmixed") **and to sew up a tear** (*niṣāḥa* — sewing). **Both branches are recorded: counsel carries both the idea of sincerity and the idea of mending.**

**And this must be recorded — it is a pattern that can be verified within the sūra:** **the same root occurs four times in the sequence of prophets in al-Aʿrāf, and in all four it is used of true prophets.**

| Verse | Who | Expression |
|---|---|---|
| **7/21** | **Iblīs** | *Innī lakumā la-mina'**n-nāṣiḥīn*** |
| **7/62** | **Noah** | *Wa **anṣaḥu** lakum* |
| **7/68** | **Hūd** | *Wa anā lakum **nāṣiḥun** amīn* |
| **7/79** | **Ṣāliḥ** | *Wa **naṣaḥtu** lakum wa lākin lā tuḥibbūna'**n-nāṣiḥīn*** |
| **7/93** | **Shuʿayb** | *Wa **naṣaḥtu** lakum* |

**Five occurrences: one in the mouth of Iblīs, four in the mouths of prophets.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the five verses themselves:** **the sūra puts the same claim into the mouth of the liar and of the truthful alike** — and **shows what separates them not in the words but in the outcome.** **Ṣāliḥ's sentence in the seventy-ninth verse is the ground of this reading:** *wa lākin lā tuḥibbūna'n-nāṣiḥīn* — "but you do not love those who wish you well." **That is, the truth of the words is not measured by their being accepted.**

---

## 7/22 — فَدَلَّىٰهُمَا بِغُرُورٍ فَلَمَّا ذَاقَا ٱلشَّجَرَةَ بَدَتْ لَهُمَا سَوْءَٰتُهُمَا وَطَفِقَا يَخْصِفَانِ عَلَيْهِمَا مِن وَرَقِ ٱلْجَنَّةِ وَنَادَىٰهُمَا رَبُّهُمَآ أَلَمْ أَنْهَكُمَا عَن تِلْكُمَا ٱلشَّجَرَةِ وَأَقُل لَّكُمَآ إِنَّ ٱلشَّيْطَٰنَ لَكُمَا عَدُوٌّ مُّبِينٌ

*Fa-dallāhumā bi-ghurūr, fa-lammā dhāqā'sh-shajarata badat lahumā sawʾātuhumā wa ṭafiqā yakhṣifāni ʿalayhimā min waraqi'l-janna, wa nādāhumā rabbuhumā a-lam anhakumā ʿan tilkumā'sh-shajarati wa aqul lakumā inna'sh-shayṭāna lakumā ʿaduwwun mubīn*

"**So he lowered them down by deception. When they had tasted the tree, their shameful parts became apparent to them, and they set about patching over themselves with the leaves of the Garden. And their Lord called out to them: 'Did I not forbid you that tree, and say to you that Satan is an open enemy to you?'**"

### فَدَلَّىٰهُمَا — to let the bucket down

**This is the most striking verb in al-Aʿrāf, and that the deception is told with this verb must be recorded separately.**

The root **د-ل-و** was **undone in full** at 53/8 in `053-necm.md` (*thumma danā fa-tadallā*). **I rely on that treatment and do not undo the root again.** A summary of what was recorded there:

- ***Dalw* = bucket** — the vessel tied to a rope and let down to draw water from a well; **Yūsuf 12/19 (*fa-adlā dalwahū*)** was given as evidence.
- **Table of derivatives:** *adlā* (Form IV, transitive — **he let down**) / *tadallā* (Form V, reflexive — **he let himself down**).
- **The key image:** "the bucket is let down into the well; **what goes down goes down while remaining attached to what is above.**"
- **The relation *dalīl*–*dalāla*** ("evidence is the rope let down towards the unknown") was transmitted **with a note of caution.**

**And at 2/188 in `002-bakara.md` the same root occurred in the form *wa tudlū bihā ila'l-ḥukkām* — there, the letting down of property to a judge like a bucket.** **I rely on both places.**

**Now I write what belongs here.**

**The verb *dallā* is of Form II (فَعَّلَ) and is transitive: "he let them down".**

| Pattern | Verb | Who/what |
|---|---|---|
| **IV (*ifʿāl*)** | *Adlā* | Yūsuf 12/19 — **he let his bucket down** |
| **V (*tafaʿʿul*)** | *Tadallā* | al-Najm 53/8 — **he let himself down** |
| **II (*tafʿīl*)** | ***Dallā*** | **al-Aʿrāf 7/22 — he let the two of them down** |

**Three patterns, one root: one lowers an object, one lowers itself, one lowers two people.**

**I record this as my own reading and it is not binding; its ground is the concrete sense of the root:** **the deception is told here not as "he tricked" or "he misled" but as *he let them down*.** **And the picture of the bucket carries three things at once:**

| Element of the picture | Its counterpart in the verse |
|---|---|
| **The bucket goes down** | The deceived goes down |
| **What goes down is tied to a rope — it does not go down of itself** | The deceiver holds it from above |
| **The bucket is let down in order to take water** | The deception carries a promise of **gain** |

**The third line is especially worth recording, and it overlaps with the twentieth verse:** Iblīs's promise was a promise of **gain** (to become angels, to become deathless). **The bucket too does not go down empty; it goes down to take water.** **I give this as a reading, not as a certainty.**

**And a second sense recorded by the lexicographers is transmitted: the sense of *tadliya* as "to embolden someone, to egg him on".** **This is a transmitted view; the primary sense (to let down) is the more widespread and accords with the concrete sense of the root.**

### بِغُرُورٍ — the root غ-ر-ر

The concrete sense of the root: the surface of a thing, its outward look — and from this, **to be taken in by the look of a thing.** *Ghurra* is the bright white of the forehead, *gharīr* an inexperienced youth, *ghurūr* delusion.

**The root was treated at 31/33 in `031-lokman.md` and at 35/5 in `035-fatir.md` (*wa lā yaghurrannakum bi'llāhi'l-gharūr*); also at 57/20 in `057-hadid.md` (*matāʿu'l-ghurūr*).** **I rely on those treatments.**

**A point of word order: *bi-ghurūr* comes indefinite, and the *bāʾ* here states either instrument or state.** Both readings are possible: "he let them down **with a deception**" or "they went down **deluded**". **The first is the widespread one.**

**And the second occurrence of the same root in this sūra is in verse 51: *wa gharrathumu'l-ḥayātu'd-dunyā* — "the life of this world deluded them".** **I record this as an observation:** the sūra uses the same verb both at the beginning of the story (Satan's deception) and in the scene of the gathering (the world's deception).

### وَطَفِقَا يَخْصِفَانِ — the difference from Ṭāhā

The verb **ط-ف-ق** is an **auxiliary verb** in the sense "to set about a thing, to begin" (one of the verbs of inception, *afʿāl al-shurūʿ*). In Arabic the predicate of such verbs comes in the imperfect — and here *yakhṣifāni* is imperfect.

The root **خ-ص-ف**: **to add one thing to another, to patch.** The lexicographers explain *khaṣf* as the verb used for the mending of shoes: *khaṣafa'n-naʿl* — "he patched the sandal, he stitched layer on layer."

**And this sentence occurs in Ṭāhā as well. The two sūras can be compared:**

| Sūra | Expression | Difference |
|---|---|---|
| Ṭāhā 20/121 | *Wa ṭafiqā **yakhṣifāni** ʿalayhimā min waraqi'l-janna* | The same |
| **al-Aʿrāf 7/22** | *Wa ṭafiqā **yakhṣifāni** ʿalayhimā min waraqi'l-janna* | **Word for word the same** |

**The two sūras overlap word for word in this sentence.** **It was treated at 20/120-123 in `020-taha.md`; I rely on that treatment.**

**What belongs here is this, and I give it as an observation:** **the verb is not "they covered" but "they set about patching".** **Leaf on leaf, layer on layer — that is, not a ready covering but a covering in the making.** **And in the twenty-sixth verse its opposite will come: *qad anzalnā ʿalaykum libāsan* — a covering sent down, ready made.**

| Place | Covering | From where |
|---|---|---|
| **7/22** | *Waraqi'l-janna* — the leaves of the Garden | **By their own hands, by patching** |
| **7/26** | *Libāsan yuwārī sawʾātikum* | ***Anzalnā*** — by being sent down |

**There are four verses between the two, and both describe the same act (covering oneself); but their sources differ.** **I record this as my own reading and it is not binding.**

### وَنَادَىٰهُمَا رَبُّهُمَا — the form of the question

**A point of word order must be recorded: God's word comes as a question, not as an accusation.**

> *A-lam anhakumā ʿan tilkumā'sh-shajarati **wa aqul lakumā** inna'sh-shayṭāna lakumā ʿaduwwun mubīn*

**The question has two parts, and it recalls two different things:**

| Part | What is recalled | Where it had been said |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *A-lam **anhakumā** ʿan tilkumā'sh-shajarah* | **In verse 19** — *wa lā taqrabā hādhihi'sh-shajarah* |
| 2 | *Wa aqul lakumā inna'sh-shayṭāna lakumā **ʿaduwwun mubīn*** | **Not told in al-Aʿrāf** — it is in Ṭāhā 20/117 |

**The second part must be recorded: God recalls a word He had said earlier; but al-Aʿrāf had not told that word.** **The same warning occurs at 20/117 in Ṭāhā:** *yā Ādamu inna hādhā **ʿaduwwun laka wa li-zawjika***.

**I record this as an observation, and the table can be verified from the two texts:** **al-Aʿrāf gives the warning Ṭāhā tells only in the form of a *reminder*.** **That is, the two sūras complete one another** — and this is a countable datum about the way the Qurʾān tells its stories. The same phenomenon (different parts of the same story being given in different sūras) was tabulated in detail in `011-hud.md` and `026-suara.md`; **I rely on those treatments.**

**And the demonstrative must be recorded: *tilkumā'sh-shajarah* — "that tree".** **In the nineteenth verse it was *hādhihi'sh-shajarah* ("this tree").** ***Hādhā* points to what is near, *dhālika/tilka* to what is far.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the difference of the two demonstratives:** when the prohibition was laid down the tree was **near**; when the reckoning is asked for it is named as having **grown distant**. **This is an observation about the word order; I draw no further conclusion from it.**

---

## 7/23 — قَالَا رَبَّنَا ظَلَمْنَآ أَنفُسَنَا وَإِن لَّمْ تَغْفِرْ لَنَا وَتَرْحَمْنَا لَنَكُونَنَّ مِنَ ٱلْخَٰسِرِينَ

*Qālā rabbanā ẓalamnā anfusanā wa in lam taghfir lanā wa tarḥamnā la-nakūnanna mina'l-khāsirīn*

"**They said: 'Our Lord! We have wronged ourselves. If You do not forgive us and have mercy on us, we shall surely be among the lost.'**"

### The undoing of this prayer was done in the index

**The structure of this prayer was treated at 2/37 in `002-bakara.md`, and three elements of it were counted there:**

1. **Taking the fault on oneself** (without producing an excuse)
2. **Producing no excuse**
3. **An unconditional request**

**And it was compared there with Iblīs's sentence: "Iblīs too spoke, but he gave a ground and laid the blame on God (al-Aʿrāf 7/16)."** **I rely on that treatment and do not repeat it here.**

**Further, in al-Baqara the expression *talaqqā Ādamu min rabbihī kalimāt* was treated, and it was recorded that "Adam does not find the words himself, he receives them; the door of repentance had already been left open".** **Al-Aʿrāf does not give this piece of information; in al-Aʿrāf the prayer itself comes directly.** This is a countable difference between the two sūras.

### The same sentence in Moses's mouth — a comparison with 28/16 in `028-kasas.md`

**The same sentence occurs once more in the Qurʾān in the mouth of a prophet, and this comparison has been called for.**

| Place | Who | Sentence | What follows |
|---|---|---|---|
| **al-Aʿrāf 7/23** | **Adam and his wife** | *Rabbanā **ẓalamnā anfusanā*** | *Wa in lam **taghfir** lanā wa **tarḥamnā**…* |
| **al-Qaṣaṣ 28/16** | **Moses** | *Rabbi innī **ẓalamtu nafsī** fa'ghfir lī* | *Fa-**ghafara** lahū* |

**Three differences must be recorded.**

**One — number.** In al-Aʿrāf the sentence is **dual** (*ẓalamnā anfusanā*), in al-Qaṣaṣ **singular** (*ẓalamtu nafsī*). **At 2/35-36 in `002-bakara.md` it was recorded that the coming of the verbs in the dual distributes the responsibility; I rely on that treatment.**

**Two — the form of the request.** **Moses asks directly:** *fa'ghfir lī* (imperative). **Adam and his wife build a conditional clause:** *wa in lam taghfir lanā… la-nakūnanna mina'l-khāsirīn* — **the request comes indirectly, by saying what will happen if forgiveness is withheld.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the grammatical structure of the two sentences:** both prayers take the fault upon themselves; **but one asks in the imperative, the other asks by stating the outcome.** **The second is more indirect, and a condition stands in between.** This is an explanation, not a ruling.
