**Three — the answer.** **As was recorded at 28/16 in `028-kasas.md`, in al-Qaṣaṣ the answer is given at once in the same verse: *fa-ghafara lahū*.** **In al-Aʿrāf there is no such sentence.** **In al-Aʿrāf, in place of an answer, the command to go down comes (24).**

**I record this as an observation and rely on the table:** **al-Baqara 2/37 has an announcement of forgiveness that al-Aʿrāf does not have** (*fa-tāba ʿalayh*); **Ṭāhā 20/122 has one too** (*thumma'jtabāhu rabbuhū fa-tāba ʿalayhi wa hadā*). **Al-Aʿrāf does not give it.**

| Sūra | Is the acceptance of repentance announced |
|---|---|
| al-Baqara 2/37 | **Yes** — *fa-tāba ʿalayhi innahū huwa't-tawwābu'r-raḥīm* |
| Ṭāhā 20/122 | **Yes** — *thumma'jtabāhu rabbuhū fa-tāba ʿalayhi wa hadā* |
| **al-Aʿrāf 7/23-24** | **No** — there is no sentence between the prayer and the command to go down |

**This is a countable difference, and it does not break the frame recorded in `002-bakara.md` — "the fall has been forgiven, it is not inherited, it is not a loss of standing but the beginning of a task"** — because the announcement of that same event is expressly present in the other two sūras. **The telling of al-Aʿrāf breaks off here and passes straight to the earth.** **I make no claim about the reason for this; I record only the datum.**

### ٱلْخَٰسِرِين — a link within the sūra

The root **خ-س-ر**: diminution, loss. **The root was treated in detail at 103/2 in `103-asr.md` (*inna'l-insāna la-fī khusr*); I rely on that treatment.**

**And the same root had occurred in the ninth verse:** *fa-ulāʾika'lladhīna **khasirū anfusahum***. **There is an overlap between the two verses, and it must be recorded:**

| Verse | Sentence | Who |
|---|---|---|
| **7/9** | *Khasirū **anfusahum*** | **Those whose scales come out light** |
| **7/23** | *Ẓalamnā **anfusanā*** … *la-nakūnanna mina'l-**khāsirīn*** | **Adam and his wife** |

**In both verses the same two words stand side by side: *nafs* and *khusrān*.** **And in both, the loss is inflicted not on another but on the person himself.**

**I record this as an observation:** in the ninth verse this is a **verdict** (a finding delivered from outside); in the twenty-third it is a **fear** (from the person's own mouth). **The same word, in two different mouths.**

---

## 7/24-25 — قَالَ ٱهْبِطُوا۟ بَعْضُكُمْ لِبَعْضٍ عَدُوٌّ وَلَكُمْ فِى ٱلْأَرْضِ مُسْتَقَرٌّ وَمَتَٰعٌ إِلَىٰ حِينٍ · قَالَ فِيهَا تَحْيَوْنَ وَفِيهَا تَمُوتُونَ وَمِنْهَا تُخْرَجُونَ

*Qāla'hbiṭū baʿḍukum li-baʿḍin ʿaduww, wa lakum fi'l-arḍi mustaqarrun wa matāʿun ilā ḥīn · Qāla fīhā taḥyawna wa fīhā tamūtūna wa minhā tukhrajūn*

"**He said: 'Go down, some of you an enemy to others. On the earth there is for you a place of settlement and provision for a time.' · He said: 'There you will live, there you will die, and from it you will be brought forth.'**"

### Two *qāla*s — a point of word order

**Both the twenty-fourth and the twenty-fifth verses begin with *qāla*, and there is no conjunction between them.**

**In Arabic, two *qāla*s belonging to the same subject coming one after another without a conjunction is a construction worth recording.** The lexicographers generally explain it as showing that **the second speech is a separate, self-standing announcement, distinct from the first.**

**I record this as an observation:** the first speech states **the going down and the conditions of the going down**; the second states **the frame of life on the earth**. **The two are separate subjects, and the text too puts them into separate sentences.**

### Three verbs, three times — 7/25

**The twenty-fifth verse is built on three imperfect verbs:**

| Verb | Meaning | Voice |
|---|---|---|
| *Taḥyawna* | You will live | **Active** |
| *Tamūtūna* | You will die | **Active** |
| ***Tukhrajūna*** | **You will be brought forth** | **Passive** |

**A point of word order must be recorded: the first two verbs are active, the third passive.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the voice of the verbs:** living and dying are built as man's own act; **being brought forth as the act of another.** **That is, at the third step the subject changes.**

**And the same structure was built with three verbs at 20/55 in `020-taha.md`:** *minhā khalaqnākum wa fīhā nuʿīdukum wa minhā nukhrijukum tāratan ukhrā*. **The two verses can be compared:**

| Sūra | The three steps | Agent |
|---|---|---|
| Ṭāhā 20/55 | *Khalaqnākum · nuʿīdukum · nukhrijukum* | **God in all three** (the "We" form) |
| **al-Aʿrāf 7/25** | *Taḥyawna · tamūtūna · **tukhrajūn*** | **Two the human, the third passive** |

**I record this as an observation, and the table can be verified from the two texts:** **Ṭāhā tells the event from the side of the one who does it, al-Aʿrāf from the side of the one who lives it.** **And al-Aʿrāf, by making its third verb passive, joins the two tellings.**

### مُسْتَقَرّ and مَتَٰع — two words, two spans

| Word | Root | Meaning | Span |
|---|---|---|---|
| *Mustaqarr* | ق-ر-ر | **The place where one settles** | It states a place |
| *Matāʿ* | م-ت-ع | **What is to be enjoyed** | ***Ilā ḥīn*** — for a term |

The root **م-ت-ع** was undone at 47/12 in `047-muhammed.md`: **enjoyment + "stretching on, continuing"** (*mataʿa'n-nahāru*). **I rely on that treatment.**

***Ilā ḥīn* — "until a time".** The root **ح-ي-ن** states an indefinite span. **The same phrase occurs at 2/36 in al-Baqara as well** (*wa lakum fi'l-arḍi mustaqarrun wa matāʿun ilā ḥīn*) — **word for word the same sentence in the two sūras.**

**I record this as an observation:** the verse gives two things — a **place** and a **span**. **The limit of the place is not stated; the limit of the span is stated but its measure is not given.** **And the thirty-fourth verse will repeat the same matter for communities:** *wa li-kulli ummatin ajal*.

---

# III. The four *yā banī Ādam* — 7/26-36

**When the story ends the sūra turns directly to the reader and calls out four times, *yā banī Ādam*.** **This address occurs five times in the Qurʾān; four of them are in al-Aʿrāf** (26, 27, 31, 35), **the fifth is at Yā Sīn 36/60.**

| Verse | What comes after the address | Mood |
|---|---|---|
| **26** | *Qad anzalnā ʿalaykum libāsan…* | **Statement** — the announcement of what is given |
| **27** | *Lā yaftinannakumu'sh-shayṭān…* | **Prohibition** |
| **31** | *Khudhū zīnatakum ʿinda kulli masjid…* | **Command** |
| **35** | *Immā yaʾtiyannakum rusulun minkum…* | **Condition** |

**Four of them in four different moods: statement, prohibition, command, condition.** **This is a countable order.**

**And all four have a link with the story — I record this as an observation:**

| Address | Its counterpart in the story |
|---|---|
| 26 — clothing | **22** — covering oneself with leaves |
| 27 — Satan's temptation | **20-22** — the whispering and the deception |
| 31 — adornment, eating, drinking | **19** — *fa-kulā min ḥaythu shiʾtumā* |
| 35 — the coming of messengers | **19** — *wa lā taqrabā* (the first announcement) |

---

## 7/26 — يَٰبَنِىٓ ءَادَمَ قَدْ أَنزَلْنَا عَلَيْكُمْ لِبَاسًا يُوَٰرِى سَوْءَٰتِكُمْ وَرِيشًا وَلِبَاسُ ٱلتَّقْوَىٰ ذَٰلِكَ خَيْرٌ ذَٰلِكَ مِنْ ءَايَٰتِ ٱللَّهِ لَعَلَّهُمْ يَذَّكَّرُونَ

*Yā banī Ādama qad anzalnā ʿalaykum libāsan yuwārī sawʾātikum wa rīshā, wa libāsu't-taqwā dhālika khayr, dhālika min āyāti'llāhi laʿallahum yadhdhakkarūn*

"**O children of Adam! We have sent down upon you clothing to cover your shameful parts, and adornment as well. But the clothing of God-fearing is better. That is among the signs of God; perhaps they will take it to heart.**"

### لِبَاس — the ring in the index

The root **ل-ب-س** and the likeness of *libās* **were treated at 2/187 in `002-bakara.md`** (*hunna libāsun lakum wa antum libāsun lahunn*). **I rely on that treatment.** Five functions of clothing were counted there:

1. **It covers** — it does not show the flaw to the outside
2. **It protects**
3. **It is the nearest thing** — nothing stands in between
4. **It adorns**
5. **It takes shape according to the wearer** — it is not a ready-made mould

**And the negative branch of the same root was recorded there too: *talbīs* at 2/42 — "to dress falsehood in the clothing of truth".** **The root states both covering and confusing.**

**What belongs here is this: al-Aʿrāf uses the word *libās* in three different senses within three verses.**

| Verse | Expression | Which sense |
|---|---|---|
| **7/26 (a)** | *Libāsan **yuwārī sawʾātikum*** | **Physical clothing** — that which covers |
| **7/26 (b)** | ***Libāsu't-taqwā*** | **Figurative** — the clothing of God-fearing |
| **7/27** | *Yanziʿu ʿanhumā **libāsahumā*** | **The covering of Adam and his wife** — a return to the story |

**Three uses, two verses.** **And if the fourth use in al-Baqara (spouses are clothing to one another) is added to these three, the four distinct works of the root in the Qurʾān are complete.**

### أَنزَلْنَا — the choice of the verb

**A point of word order must be recorded, and this is the place the lexicographers have dwelt on: the verb used for clothing is *anzalnā* (We sent down).**

**Clothing does not come down from the sky. The Qurʾān uses the same verb elsewhere too for material things:**

| Place | For what | Sentence |
|---|---|---|
| al-Ḥadīd 57/25 | **Iron** | *Wa **anzalna'l-ḥadīd*** |
| al-Zumar 39/6 | **Livestock** | *Wa **anzala** lakum mina'l-anʿāmi thamāniyata azwāj* |
| **al-Aʿrāf 7/26** | **Clothing** | *Qad **anzalnā** ʿalaykum libāsā* |

**This usage was treated at 57/25 in `057-hadid.md`; I rely on that treatment.** A summary of the explanations recorded there: **the verb is also used in the sense "to be given as apportioned from a source", not "physical descent from above to below".** **The classical commentaries further transmit the explanation that "the raw material of clothing (plants that grow with the rain, the wool of animals) depends on water that comes from above".** **This is a transmitted explanation; I make no firm preference.**

**I record this as my own reading and it is not binding:** in the twenty-second verse the covering had been **patched together by their own hands** (*yakhṣifāni*); **here the covering is *sent down*.** **The choice of the verb carries the difference between the two verses.**

### رِيش — the root ر-ي-ش

The concrete sense of the root: **the feather of a bird.** *Rīsh* is the feather and the wing of a bird. From this the senses of **adornment, beauty, well-being, abundance of livelihood** derive; when the Arabs said *rāshahū* they meant "he equipped him, he bettered his condition".

**The word occurs only in this verse in the Qurʾān.**

**A variant reading must be recorded:**

| Reading | Spelling | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| ***rīshā*** | رِيشًا | **Feather, adornment** — the widespread reading |
| ***riyāshā*** | رِيَاشًا | **The plural of *rīsh*** or "clothing, goods" |

**The second reading is also transmitted; it does not change the meaning in any essential way** (a plural, or the sense "wealth and clothing"). **I do not impose a preference.**

**And the choice of the word is worth recording: a bird's feather covers, adorns and lifts into flight all at once.** **I give this as an observation and build no firm claim of meaning on it:** the verse counts two things — that which **covers** (*libāsan yuwārī*) and that which **adorns** (*rīshā*). **And in the thirty-second verse the lawfulness of adornment will be expressly defended** (*qul man ḥarrama zīnata'llāhi'llatī akhraja li-ʿibādih*). **That is, adornment is named in the twenty-sixth verse and defended in the thirty-second.**

### وَلِبَاسُ ٱلتَّقْوَىٰ ذَٰلِكَ خَيْرٌ

**The phrase occurs only here in the Qurʾān.**

The root **و-ق-ي**: **to guard, to be guarded.** ***Taqwā* is to take up a barrier that will guard a thing from harm.** **The root was treated at 2/2 in `002-bakara.md` and in `092-leyl.md`; I rely on those treatments.**

**I record this as an observation, and its ground is the root itself:** ***libās* covers and guards; *taqwā* guards too.** **That is, the likeness is built on the function the two words share.** This is a datum of word order showing at which point the likeness works.

**A point of word order:** *wa libāsu't-taqwā **dhālika** khayr* — **the subject is named again by a demonstrative.** In Arabic this serves for **emphasis and for drawing attention**: "the clothing of God-fearing — **that** is better."

**And *khayr* here is an elative ("better"), and the side of the comparison is not stated.** **The sentence does not say "better than that"; it says only "better".** **I record this as an observation; what is understood from the context is the two garments named in the preceding sentence.**

**One further note:** **two readings are transmitted** of the phrase *libāsu't-taqwā* — *libās* as nominative (subject) or as accusative (as the second object of *anzalnā*: "and We sent down the clothing of God-fearing too"). **The second reading is also reported.** **I do not impose a preference;** **but one consequence of the second reading is worth recording: on that reading the clothing of God-fearing enters among the things "sent down".**

### لَعَلَّهُمْ يَذَّكَّرُونَ — the change of pronoun

**A point of word order must be recorded:** the verse begins in the second person plural (*yā banī Ādam*, *ʿalaykum*, *sawʾātikum*) **and ends in the third person plural** (*laʿallahum yadhdhakkarūn*).

**This is *iltifāt* (a shift in the direction of address), and it was treated in many places in the index** (`001-fatiha.md`, the sections of `010-yunus.md`; `026-suara.md`). **I rely on those treatments.**

**I record this as an observation:** while the address is "you", the judgement is delivered about "them". **And the same shift was present in the third verse too** (*ittabiʿū… qalīlan mā tadhakkarūn* — there it remained in the second person). **Here it passes to the third person.**

### What this says to the present

**The verse divides dressing into two functions: covering and adorning. It counts both as lawful.** **Then it names a third thing and calls it "better".**

**What must be recorded is this: the verse does not exalt the third by rejecting the first two.** **All three are in the same sentence.** **And the thirty-second verse will expressly question the attitude that sets out to forbid adornment.** **When these two verses of the sūra are read together, what is given is not the rejection of adornment but its ordering.** This is something that can be read off the arrangement of the two verses themselves.

---

## 7/27 — يَٰبَنِىٓ ءَادَمَ لَا يَفْتِنَنَّكُمُ ٱلشَّيْطَٰنُ كَمَآ أَخْرَجَ أَبَوَيْكُم مِّنَ ٱلْجَنَّةِ يَنزِعُ عَنْهُمَا لِبَاسَهُمَا لِيُرِيَهُمَا سَوْءَٰتِهِمَآ إِنَّهُۥ يَرَىٰكُمْ هُوَ وَقَبِيلُهُۥ مِنْ حَيْثُ لَا تَرَوْنَهُمْ إِنَّا جَعَلْنَا ٱلشَّيَٰطِينَ أَوْلِيَآءَ لِلَّذِينَ لَا يُؤْمِنُونَ

*Yā banī Ādama lā yaftinannakumu'sh-shayṭānu kamā akhraja abawaykum mina'l-jannati yanziʿu ʿanhumā libāsahumā li-yuriyahumā sawʾātihimā, innahū yarākum huwa wa qabīluhū min ḥaythu lā tarawnahum, innā jaʿalna'sh-shayāṭīna awliyāʾa lilladhīna lā yuʾminūn*

"**O children of Adam! Let not Satan tempt you as he brought your parents out of the Garden, stripping from them their clothing to show them their shameful parts. He and his tribe see you from where you do not see them. We have made the satans the guardians of those who do not believe.**"

### يَنزِعُ — the work of the imperfect verb

The root **ن-ز-ع**: **to pull a thing out of the place where it stands, to strip it away.** **The root was treated at 79/1 in `079-naziat.md` (*wa'n-nāziʿāti gharqā*) and at 3/26 in `003-al-i-imran.md` (*wa tanziʿu'l-mulk*).** **I rely on those treatments.**

**A point of word order must be recorded: the sentence has two verbs, and their tenses differ.**

| Verb | Mood | What it states |
|---|---|---|
| *Akhraja* | **Perfect** | **He brought out** — a finished act |
| ***Yanziʿu*** | **Imperfect** | **Stripping** — a state (a circumstantial clause) |

**The imperfect coming here tells the event as though it were going on before one's eyes. In Arabic this is called *ḥikāyat al-ḥāl*: a past event is brought to life in the present tense.**

**I record this as an observation:** the verse puts an event that happened in the past inside a warning that looks to the present — and the mood of the verb does the same work.

**And the object of the verb must be recorded: *libāsahumā*.** **In the twenty-second verse the covering was told as being made *afterwards* (patching with leaves); here a *libās* is said to have been stripped away.**

**Two readings are transmitted, and both accord with the language:**

| Reading | What *libās* is |
|---|---|
| 1 | **An actual covering** — a garment that was upon them and was stripped away |
| 2 | **The state of the *sawʾāt* being unseen** — that is, "coveredness" is named as clothing |

**The Qurʾān gives no detail on this.** **Both are transmitted; I do not impose a preference, and I do not add the detail the Qurʾān does not give.**

### إِنَّهُۥ يَرَىٰكُمْ … مِنْ حَيْثُ لَا تَرَوْنَهُمْ

**The construction of the sentence must be recorded: the same verb (ر-أ-ي) twice, once affirmative and once negative, with the subjects exchanged.**

> *innahū **yarākum** huwa wa qabīluhū min ḥaythu **lā tarawnahum***

**And there is a disagreement of pronouns: *innahū yarākum* (singular) → *lā tarawnahum* (plural).** **This is explained by the intervening *huwa wa qabīluhū*: the sentence, beginning in the singular, passes to the plural once the tribe has been added.** This is a datum of word order.

**قَبِيل** — the root **ق-ب-ل**: to be opposite, to face towards. ***Qabīl* is "those who come together and face the same way" — a tribe, a company.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the one-sidedness of the verb of seeing:** the verse states an **asymmetry** — one side sees, the other does not. **And the thirtieth verse will state the consequence of this asymmetry: *wa yaḥsabūna annahum muhtadūn* — "and they suppose themselves to be rightly guided."** **Not seeing joins with not knowing that one does not see.**

**And this establishes a line with verses 179 and 198 of the sūra:**

| Verse | Seeing | Who |
|---|---|---|
| **7/27** | *Lā tarawnahum* | **Man — what he cannot see** |
| **7/179** | *Lahum aʿyunun **lā yubṣirūna** bihā* | **Man — what he does not see** |
| **7/198** | *Wa tarāhum **yanẓurūna** ilayka wa hum lā yubṣirūn* | **The idols — looking and not seeing** |

**Three verses, three different failures to see.** **The first an impossibility, the second a non-use, the third an absence.** I record this as an observation, and the table can be verified from the text.

### إِنَّا جَعَلْنَا ٱلشَّيَٰطِينَ أَوْلِيَآءَ لِلَّذِينَ لَا يُؤْمِنُونَ

**The construction of the sentence must be recorded, and it must be compared with the thirtieth verse.**

| Verse | Sentence | Agent |
|---|---|---|
| **7/27** | *Innā **jaʿalna'sh**-shayāṭīna awliyāʾa lilladhīna lā yuʾminūn* | **God** |
| **7/30** | *Innahumu'**ttakhadhu'sh**-shayāṭīna awliyāʾa min dūni'llāh* | **Human beings** |

**The same relation is tied in two verses to two different agents: in 27 "We made", in 30 "they took".**

**This is a place that falls within a debate of *kalām* (the divine decree, the creation of acts), and no polemic of *kalām* is entered into in this commentary** (`STYLE.md`). **All I record is this: the text builds both sentences and does not resolve the relation between them.** **The two verses stand three verses apart and are given to the reader together.**

**And this datum of word order must be recorded:** in the twenty-seventh verse the judgement is qualified as *lilladhīna **lā yuʾminūn*** (for those who do not believe) — **that is, it is not general, it is tied to an attribute.** **Per the method (`STYLE.md`) I record that this is an attribute and not an identity** (the same note was entered at 47/12 in `047-muhammed.md`).

---

## 7/28-30 — وَإِذَا فَعَلُوا۟ فَٰحِشَةً قَالُوا۟ وَجَدْنَا عَلَيْهَآ ءَابَآءَنَا وَٱللَّهُ أَمَرَنَا بِهَا قُلْ إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ لَا يَأْمُرُ بِٱلْفَحْشَآءِ أَتَقُولُونَ عَلَى ٱللَّهِ مَا لَا تَعْلَمُونَ · قُلْ أَمَرَ رَبِّى بِٱلْقِسْطِ وَأَقِيمُوا۟ وُجُوهَكُمْ عِندَ كُلِّ مَسْجِدٍ وَٱدْعُوهُ مُخْلِصِينَ لَهُ ٱلدِّينَ كَمَا بَدَأَكُمْ تَعُودُونَ · فَرِيقًا هَدَىٰ وَفَرِيقًا حَقَّ عَلَيْهِمُ ٱلضَّلَٰلَةُ إِنَّهُمُ ٱتَّخَذُوا۟ ٱلشَّيَٰطِينَ أَوْلِيَآءَ مِن دُونِ ٱللَّهِ وَيَحْسَبُونَ أَنَّهُم مُّهْتَدُونَ

*Wa idhā faʿalū fāḥishatan qālū wajadnā ʿalayhā ābāʾanā wa'llāhu amaranā bihā, qul inna'llāha lā yaʾmuru bi'l-faḥshāʾ, a-taqūlūna ʿala'llāhi mā lā taʿlamūn · Qul amara rabbī bi'l-qisṭ, wa aqīmū wujūhakum ʿinda kulli masjidin wa'dʿūhu mukhliṣīna lahu'd-dīn, kamā badaʾakum taʿūdūn · Farīqan hadā wa farīqan ḥaqqa ʿalayhimu'ḍ-ḍalāla, innahumu'ttakhadhu'sh-shayāṭīna awliyāʾa min dūni'llāhi wa yaḥsabūna annahum muhtadūn*

"**When they commit an indecency they say, 'We found our forefathers upon it, and God commanded it of us.' Say: God does not command indecency. Do you say about God what you do not know? · Say: My Lord commanded justice. Set your faces towards Him at every place of prayer, and call upon Him, making the religion His alone. As He brought you forth in the beginning, so you will return. · One party He guided, and upon another party going astray became due. For they took the satans as guardians instead of God, and they suppose themselves to be rightly guided.**"

### Two excuses, two answers

**The twenty-eighth verse gives the two grounds of the addressee side by side, and the word order must be recorded:**

| Order | Excuse | Its ground |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *Wajadnā ʿalayhā **ābāʾanā*** | **Tradition** — the forefathers |
| 2 | *Wa'llāhu **amaranā** bihā* | **Religion** — the claim of a divine command |

**And the answer is given only to the second:** *qul inna'llāha lā yaʾmuru bi'l-faḥshāʾ*.

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the position of the answer:** **the first excuse (the forefathers) is dealt with elsewhere in the sūra** (7/70 — *wa nadhara mā kāna yaʿbudu ābāʾunā*; 7/173 — *innamā ashraka ābāʾunā min qablu*). **What is answered here is the second excuse: the ascription of a behaviour to God.**

**And the answer has two steps:**

| Step | Sentence | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *Inna'llāha lā yaʾmuru bi'l-faḥshāʾ* | **It rejects the claim** |
| 2 | *A-taqūlūna ʿala'llāhi **mā lā taʿlamūn*** | **It names the kind of claim it is** |

**The expression in the second step must be recorded: *mā lā taʿlamūn* — "what you do not know".** **And the same expression will come back in the thirty-third verse as the last item in the list of what is forbidden:** *wa an taqūlū ʿala'llāhi **mā lā taʿlamūn***.

| Verse | Sentence | Mood |
|---|---|---|
| **7/28** | *A-taqūlūna ʿala'llāhi mā lā taʿlamūn* | **A question** |
| **7/33** | *Wa an taqūlū ʿala'llāhi mā lā taʿlamūn* | **An item in the list of what is forbidden** |

**The same expression five verses apart, one a question and one a ruling.** **This is a link that can be verified within the sūra, and it will be completed at verse 33.**

### فَٰحِشَة — the root ف-ح-ش

The sense of the root: **an indecency that exceeds the bound.** The lexicographers explain *fuḥsh* as "an ugliness that exceeds the measure, in word or in deed". **The word is not of sexual content alone; the root's own idea is the coming together of excess and ugliness.**

**The root was treated at 24/19 in `024-nur.md` and at 33/30 in `033-ahzab.md`; I rely on those treatments.**

**And in this sūra the root occurs three times:** 28 (*fāḥishatan*), 33 (*al-fawāḥisha*), 80 (*a-taʾtūna'l-fāḥisha* — Lot's question to his people).

### ٱلْقِسْط — the root ق-س-ط

**The root carries a curious double sense, and it must be recorded:**

| Pattern | Word | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| **Form I** | *Qasaṭa* | **To do wrong, to swerve from the right** |
| **Form IV (*ifʿāl*)** | *Aqsaṭa* | **To do justice** |
| Noun | ***Qisṭ*** | **Justice, a share** |
| Active participle | *Qāsiṭ* / *muqsiṭ* | **Wrongdoer** / **the just** |

**That is, the same root gives two opposed senses according to the form.** **The lexicographers explain this by "the privative sense of Form IV": *aqsaṭa* = "to remove wrong".** **The root was treated at 55/9 in `055-rahman.md` (*wa aqīmu'l-wazna bi'l-qisṭ*) and at 57/25 in `057-hadid.md` (*li-yaqūma'n-nāsu bi'l-qisṭ*).** **I rely on those treatments.**

**A point of word order must be recorded, and it is a contrast:**

| Verse | The command claimed | The real command |
|---|---|---|
| **7/28** | *Wa'llāhu **amaranā** bihā* — indecency | — |
| **7/29** | — | *Qul **amara** rabbī bi'l-**qisṭ*** |

**The same verb (أمر) stands facing itself in two verses: one a claim, the other an announcement.** **And the objects are opposites: *fāḥisha* (an indecency that exceeds the bound) ↔ *qisṭ* (that which is in its proper place).**

### وَأَقِيمُوا۟ وُجُوهَكُمْ عِندَ كُلِّ مَسْجِدٍ

***Aqīmū wujūhakum* — "set your faces straight".**

**A point of word order: *ʿinda kulli masjid* — "at every place of prayer".** **And the same phrase will be repeated in the thirty-first verse: *khudhū zīnatakum ʿinda kulli masjid*.**

| Verse | Command | Place |
|---|---|---|
| **7/29** | *Aqīmū wujūhakum* | ***ʿInda kulli masjid*** |
| **7/31** | *Khudhū zīnatakum* | ***ʿInda kulli masjid*** |

**The same adverbial two verses apart.** **I record this as an observation:** both commands concern the place of prayer — one a **turning towards**, the other a **preparing**.

**And the expression *mukhliṣīna lahu'd-dīn* must be recorded.** The root **خ-ل-ص**: **to be pure, unmixed.** **The distinction between *mukhliṣ* (active participle) and *mukhlaṣ* (passive participle) was tabulated at 38/83 in `038-sad.md`; I rely on that treatment.** **Here *mukhliṣīna* is the active participle: the work the person does himself.**

### كَمَا بَدَأَكُمْ تَعُودُونَ

**The sentence is short and dense: "as He began you, so you will return."**

**Two readings are transmitted:**

| Reading | The work of *kamā* | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **A likeness of manner** | "As your creation was, so your raising will be" |
| 2 | **A likeness of state** | "As you were created (naked, alone, with nothing in your hands), so you will return" |

**Both readings are transmitted and they do not exclude each other; I do not impose a preference.**

**And the making of the first creation into a proof of the raising was treated in many places in the index** (`036-yasin.md` 36/79, `075-kiyame.md` 75/40, `030-rum.md` 30/27, `021-enbiya.md` 21/104). **I rely on those treatments.**

### فَرِيقًا هَدَىٰ وَفَرِيقًا حَقَّ عَلَيْهِمُ ٱلضَّلَٰلَةُ — an asymmetry of word order

**The word order of this sentence must be recorded, and it is striking.**

| Side | Sentence | Agent |
|---|---|---|
| **The first party** | *Farīqan **hadā*** | **God** — expressly |
| **The second party** | *Wa farīqan **ḥaqqa ʿalayhimu'ḍ-ḍalāla*** | **The agent is not stated** — "going astray became due upon them" |

**The two sides are not built symmetrically.** In the first the verb is active and the agent plain (*hadā* — "He guided"); **in the second the verb is *ḥaqqa* (became due) and its subject is *al-ḍalāla*, that is, it does not say "He led astray".**

**I record this as an observation, and it is a new link in a pattern treated in the index.** **Similar asymmetries were recorded in `039-zumer.md` and `074-muddessir.md`.** **And no polemic of *kalām* is entered into in this commentary** (`STYLE.md`); **all I record is the datum of word order.**

**And the continuation of the verse shows that the condition of the second party is grounded in their own acts:** *inna**hum**u'ttakhadhu'sh-shayāṭīna awliyāʾa min dūni'llāh* — "**for they** took the satans as guardians."

### وَيَحْسَبُونَ أَنَّهُم مُّهْتَدُونَ — the sūra's most repeated observation

**The sentence must be recorded: "they suppose themselves to be rightly guided."**

**The same idea occurs elsewhere in the Qurʾān too and was treated in the index:**

| Place | Sentence |
|---|---|
| **al-Aʿrāf 7/30** | *Wa **yaḥsabūna** annahum muhtadūn* |
| al-Kahf 18/104 | *Wa hum **yaḥsabūna** annahum yuḥsinūna ṣunʿā* (`018-kehf.md`) |
| al-Mujādala 58/18 | *Wa **yaḥsabūna** annahum ʿalā shayʾ* (`058-mucadele.md`) |
| al-Zukhruf 43/37 | *Wa **yaḥsabūna** annahum muhtadūn* (`043-zuhruf.md`) |

**Four places, the same verb (ح-س-ب) and the same structure.** **I rely on those treatments and do not repeat them here.**

**What belongs here is this, and I give it as an observation:** **al-Aʿrāf and al-Zukhruf 43/37 are word for word the same sentence.** **And in al-Aʿrāf this sentence, read together with *min ḥaythu lā tarawnahum* ("from where you do not see them") in the twenty-seventh verse, forms a whole:** **not seeing, and supposing that one sees.**

---

## 7/31 — يَٰبَنِىٓ ءَادَمَ خُذُوا۟ زِينَتَكُمْ عِندَ كُلِّ مَسْجِدٍ وَكُلُوا۟ وَٱشْرَبُوا۟ وَلَا تُسْرِفُوٓا۟ إِنَّهُۥ لَا يُحِبُّ ٱلْمُسْرِفِينَ

*Yā banī Ādama khudhū zīnatakum ʿinda kulli masjidin wa kulū wa'shrabū wa lā tusrifū, innahū lā yuḥibbu'l-musrifīn*

"**O children of Adam! Take your adornment at every place of prayer; eat and drink, but do not go to excess. He does not love those who go to excess.**"

### زِينَة — the root ز-ي-ن

**The sense of the root: to adorn, to make beautiful.** ***Zīna* is what makes a thing look fair.** **The root was treated at 18/7 in `018-kehf.md` (*innā jaʿalnā mā ʿala'l-arḍi zīnatan lahā*) and at 20/59 in `020-taha.md` (*yawmu'z-zīna*).** **I rely on those treatments.**

**And in this sūra the root occurs three times:**

| Verse | Expression | What |
|---|---|---|
| **7/31** | *Khudhū **zīnat**akum* | **Command** |
| **7/32** | *Man ḥarrama **zīnata**'llāhi'llatī akhraja li-ʿibādih* | **Defence** |
| **7/26** | *Wa **rīshā*** | The same idea, with a different word |

### وَكُلُوا۟ وَٱشْرَبُوا۟ وَلَا تُسْرِفُوا۟ — the line of measure

**This sentence is a new link in a line of measure built up in the index.**

**A summary of what was treated at 25/67 in `025-furkan.md`** (I rely on it and do not repeat it):

- ***Isrāf* and *iqtār* (stinting) are both rejected as two extremes.**
- The root **ق-ت-ر**: to narrow, to stint.
- ***Qawām*** — the root **ق-و-م**: **"the point between two extremes".**
- The line had been tied to these verses: `035-fatir.md` 35/32, `031-lokman.md` 31/19 and 31/32 (*qaṣd*), `002-bakara.md` 2/219 (*quli'l-ʿafw*).

**Al-Aʿrāf 7/31 is added to this line as a new link, and its difference must be recorded:**

| Place | The subject of the measure | Are both extremes given |
|---|---|---|
| al-Furqān 25/67 | **Spending** (*anfaqū*) | **Yes** — *isrāf* and *iqtār* |
| Luqmān 31/19 | **Gait and voice** | **Yes** — *waqṣid* (the middle) |
| **al-Aʿrāf 7/31** | **Eating and drinking** | **No** — only one extreme: *lā tusrifū* |
| al-Isrāʾ 17/29 | **The hand** | **Yes** — *maghlūlatan* / *kulla'l-basṭ* |

**In al-Aʿrāf only one extreme is forbidden.** **And the reason for this becomes visible in the next verse: verse 32 will deal separately with the other extreme (setting out to forbid).** **That is, the two extremes have been divided between two verses.** **I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the arrangement of the two verses.**

The root **س-ر-ف**: **to exceed the bound, to lose the measure.** The lexicographers record that the word carries not only the sense "to spend much" but also **"not to use a thing in its place"**. **The root was treated at 20/127 in `020-taha.md` (*man asrafa wa lam yuʾmin*) and at 40/28 and 40/43 in `040-gafir.md`.** **I rely on those treatments.**

**And in this sūra the root occurs twice more, and both are striking:**

| Verse | Expression | About whom |
|---|---|---|
| **7/31** | *Wa lā tusrifū … al-**musrifīn*** | **The addressee** |
| **7/81** | *Bal antum qawmun **musrifūn*** | **The people of Lot** |

**I record this as an observation:** **the sūra uses the same word both for the measure of eating and drinking and for the conduct of Lot's people.** **The shared idea of the word is "exceeding the bound", and that idea is at work in both places.** **I draw no equivalence between the two behaviours from this; all I record is the shared root and the shared idea of the word.**

### إِنَّهُۥ لَا يُحِبُّ ٱلْمُسْرِفِينَ — the concealment of the pronoun

**A point of word order must be recorded: the pronoun *innahū* refers back to God, whose name is not expressly mentioned in the preceding sentences.**

**The name "God" does not occur in the verse.** **It will come in the thirty-second verse: *zīnata'llāh*.** **That is, the pronoun rests on the expression *amara rabbī* several verses earlier (29).**

**And the pattern *lā yuḥibbu* must be recorded.** **In the Qurʾān this pattern is a particular way of rejecting a behaviour, and it was treated in the index** (`002-bakara.md` 2/190 — *inna'llāha lā yuḥibbu'l-muʿtadīn*; `031-lokman.md` 31/18 — *lā yuḥibbu kulla mukhtālin fakhūr*). **I rely on those treatments.**

**And in this sūra the same pattern occurs twice:**

| Verse | Sentence |
|---|---|
| **7/31** | *Innahū lā yuḥibbu'l-**musrifīn*** |
| **7/55** | *Innahū lā yuḥibbu'l-**muʿtadīn*** |

**Both are in the same pattern, and both end with a word that carries the idea of "exceeding the bound"** (*isrāf* and *iʿtidāʾ*). **One concerns eating and drinking, the other supplication.** This is an overlap that can be verified from the text.

### What this says to the present

**The verse gives three commands and one prohibition: take, eat, drink — but do not exceed.** **The commands are three times the number of the prohibition.**

**What must be recorded is this, and it can be read off the word order:** the prohibition comes **after** the commands and sets a limit within them; it does not cancel them. **And the next verse will question the attitude that sets out to turn this limit into a prohibition.**

---

## 7/32 — قُلْ مَنْ حَرَّمَ زِينَةَ ٱللَّهِ ٱلَّتِىٓ أَخْرَجَ لِعِبَادِهِۦ وَٱلطَّيِّبَٰتِ مِنَ ٱلرِّزْقِ قُلْ هِىَ لِلَّذِينَ ءَامَنُوا۟ فِى ٱلْحَيَوٰةِ ٱلدُّنْيَا خَالِصَةً يَوْمَ ٱلْقِيَٰمَةِ كَذَٰلِكَ نُفَصِّلُ ٱلْءَايَٰتِ لِقَوْمٍ يَعْلَمُونَ

*Qul man ḥarrama zīnata'llāhi'llatī akhraja li-ʿibādihī wa'ṭ-ṭayyibāti mina'r-rizq, qul hiya lilladhīna āmanū fi'l-ḥayāti'd-dunyā khāliṣatan yawma'l-qiyāma, kadhālika nufaṣṣilu'l-āyāti li-qawmin yaʿlamūn*

"**Say: Who has forbidden the adornment God has brought forth for His servants, and the good things of provision? Say: These are for those who believe, in the life of this world; on the Day of Resurrection they will be theirs alone. Thus do We set out the signs in detail for a people who know.**"

### The form of the question

**A point of word order must be recorded: the sentence is a question opening with a command (*qul*), and the question is built with *man* (who).**

**In Arabic this pattern — *man ḥarrama…* — is a rhetorical question of denial (*istifhām inkārī*): no answer is expected, the question is itself a rejection.** "Who has forbidden it?" = **"No one has forbidden it."**

**And the verse contains two *qul*s: one for the question, one for the answer.** **That is, it makes the messenger utter both the question and the answer.**

**I record this as an observation:** in the twenty-eighth verse the other side ascribed something to God (*wa'llāhu amaranā bihā*); **here the other side ascribes a prohibition to God.** **The two verses show the same error in two directions: presenting something as God's command, and presenting something as God's prohibition.**

| Verse | What is ascribed | Answer |
|---|---|---|
| **7/28** | **A command** — to indecency | *Inna'llāha lā yaʾmuru bi'l-faḥshāʾ* |
| **7/32** | **A prohibition** — of adornment and good provision | *Man ḥarrama…* |
| **7/33** | — | ***Innamā ḥarrama rabbiya…*** — the real list |

**The three verses are a whole, and the thirty-third verse is the answer of the sequence.**

### ٱلطَّيِّبَٰت — the root ط-ي-ب

**The sense of the root: to be pleasant, clean, good.** ***Ṭayyib* is used in the sense "both agreeable to the senses and harmless".** **The root was treated at 2/168 in `002-bakara.md` (*kulū mimmā fi'l-arḍi ḥalālan ṭayyibā*); I rely on that treatment.**

**And the root will occur twice more in this sūra:**

| Verse | Expression |
|---|---|
| **7/32** | *Wa'ṭ-**ṭayyibāt**i mina'r-rizq* |
| **7/58** | *Wa'l-baladu'**ṭ-ṭayyib*** — the good land |
| **7/157** | *Wa yuḥillu lahumu'**ṭ-ṭayyibāt**i wa yuḥarrimu ʿalayhimu'l-**khabāʾith*** |

**Three occurrences: provision, land, what is made lawful.** **And in the hundred and fifty-seventh verse the word comes together with its opposite (*khabāʾith* — foul things), just as *ṭayyib* meets *khabutha* in verse 58.**

### خَالِصَةً يَوْمَ ٱلْقِيَٰمَةِ — the most disputed place in the sentence

**The answering sentence (*qul hiya lilladhīna āmanū fi'l-ḥayāti'd-dunyā khāliṣatan yawma'l-qiyāma*) has been read differently among the lexicographers. The disagreement is given in a table; I do not impose a preference.**

| Reading | The division of the sentence | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *Hiya lilladhīna āmanū **fi'l-ḥayāti'd-dunyā** · **khāliṣatan** yawma'l-qiyāma* | "These are **for those who believe** in the life of this world (others share in them too), and on the Day of Resurrection **theirs alone**" |
| 2 | *Hiya lilladhīna āmanū · fi'l-ḥayāti'd-dunyā **khāliṣatan** yawma'l-qiyāma* | "These are for those who believe: in this world (shared), and in the hereafter unmixed" |

**Both readings are transmitted and both come close to the same result: in this world the bounty is shared, in the hereafter it is not.** **I do not impose a preference.**

**A variant reading must also be recorded:**

| Reading | Spelling | Case |
|---|---|---|
| ***khāliṣatan*** | خَالِصَةً | **Accusative** — circumstantial |
| ***khāliṣatun*** | خَالِصَةٌ | **Nominative** — predicate |

**Both are transmitted; the meaning is not changed in any essential way.**

The root **خ-ل-ص** had occurred in the twenty-ninth verse as *mukhliṣīna lahu'd-dīn*. **The same root two verses apart: one the servant's making the religion unmixed, the other the bounty's being unmixed.** This is a repetition that can be verified within the sūra.

### What this says to the present

**The verse says that to hold a thing forbidden is also a claim.** **The twenty-eighth verse asked for the ground of saying "God commanded this"; the thirty-second asks for the ground of saying "God forbade this".**

**No legal ruling is issued in this commentary** (`STYLE.md`). **All I record is the verse's own construction: the question asks for the source of the prohibition.**

---

## 7/33 — قُلْ إِنَّمَا حَرَّمَ رَبِّىَ ٱلْفَوَٰحِشَ مَا ظَهَرَ مِنْهَا وَمَا بَطَنَ وَٱلْإِثْمَ وَٱلْبَغْىَ بِغَيْرِ ٱلْحَقِّ وَأَن تُشْرِكُوا۟ بِٱللَّهِ مَا لَمْ يُنَزِّلْ بِهِۦ سُلْطَٰنًا وَأَن تَقُولُوا۟ عَلَى ٱللَّهِ مَا لَا تَعْلَمُونَ

*Qul innamā ḥarrama rabbiya'l-fawāḥisha mā ẓahara minhā wa mā baṭan, wa'l-ithma wa'l-baghya bi-ghayri'l-ḥaqq, wa an tushrikū bi'llāhi mā lam yunazzil bihī sulṭānan, wa an taqūlū ʿala'llāhi mā lā taʿlamūn*

"**Say: My Lord has forbidden only these: indecencies, open and hidden; sin; wrongful transgression; that you associate with God that for which He has sent down no warrant; and that you say about God what you do not know.**"

### Undoing the list

**This verse gives a list, and per the method (`STYLE.md`) no legal ruling is built here.** **What I shall do is to work out how many items the list has and what those items are.**

**A point of word order must be recorded: the sentence begins with *innamā* — the particle of restriction (*ḥaṣr*) in Arabic: "He has forbidden only these."**

**The list has five items, and it falls into two groups:**

| # | Item | Arabic | Group |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Indecencies** — open and hidden | *Al-fawāḥisha mā ẓahara minhā wa mā baṭan* | **Deed** |
| 2 | **Sin** | *Al-ithm* | **Deed** |
| 3 | **Wrongful transgression** | *Al-baghya bi-ghayri'l-ḥaqq* | **Deed** |
| 4 | **Association** — associating without warrant | *An tushrikū bi'llāhi mā lam yunazzil bihī sulṭānā* | **Speech/belief** |
| 5 | **Ascribing to God what one does not know** | *An taqūlū ʿala'llāhi mā lā taʿlamūn* | **Speech** |

**The division of the two groups can be read off the word order and must be recorded:** the first three items come as **nouns** (with the definite article: *al-fawāḥish*, *al-ithm*, *al-baghy*); **the last two come with *an* + verb** (*an tushrikū*, *an taqūlū*). **That is, the first three are things, the last two are acts.**

**I record this as an observation, and its ground is the word order itself.**

### The roots of the items

**ف-ح-ش** — treated at verse 28; I rely on that treatment.

**أ-ث-م** — ***ithm*: the root carries the idea of "lagging behind, being late";** the lexicographers explain *ithm* as "an act that holds its doer back from good". **The root was treated at 2/219 in `002-bakara.md` (*fīhimā ithmun kabīr*), where the variant reading *kabīr/kathīr* was recorded.** **I rely on that treatment.**

**ب-غ-ي** — ***baghy*: the concrete sense of the root is "to exceed, to overflow";** a wound that festers is also said to *baghā'l-jurḥ*. **From this comes the sense "to exceed the bound, to do wrong".** **And the same root occurs twice more in this sūra:** at 45 and 86 (*wa **yabghūnahā** ʿiwajā* — "they seek to make it crooked").

**And the qualification *bi-ghayri'l-ḥaqq* must be recorded.** **A point of word order:** of the three items, a qualification is placed only on *baghy*. **The lexicographers give two explanations of this:**

| Explanation | Ground |
|---|---|
| 1 | **For emphasis** — *baghy* is already wrongdoing, and the qualification reinforces it |
| 2 | **To distinguish** — the firmness shown in recovering a right does not count as *baghy* |

**Both are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.**

### مَا لَمْ يُنَزِّلْ بِهِۦ سُلْطَٰنًا

The root **س-ل-ط**: **to prevail, to have mastery.** ***Sulṭān* is generally used in the Qurʾān in the sense "proof, evidence, warrant"** — because a proof is what gives mastery in a dispute.

**And the same phrase will occur in this sūra in the seventy-first verse, in the mouth of Hūd:** *a-tujādilūnanī fī asmāʾin sammaytumūhā antum wa ābāʾukum **mā nazzala'llāhu bihā min sulṭān***.

| Verse | Who says it | Sentence |
|---|---|---|
| **7/33** | **The command (*qul*)** | *Mā lam yunazzil bihī sulṭānā* |
| **7/71** | **Hūd** | *Mā nazzala'llāhu bihā min sulṭān* |

**I record this as an observation:** the sūra applies in the seventy-first verse, in the mouth of a prophet, the measure it laid down in the thirty-third. **The same technique was tabulated in `011-hud.md` Table 4; I rely on that treatment.**

### وَأَن تَقُولُوا۟ عَلَى ٱللَّهِ مَا لَا تَعْلَمُونَ — the end of the list

**The last item of the list is word for word the same expression as the question in the twenty-eighth verse.**

**And its being placed last in the list must be recorded.** **I record this as my own reading and it is not binding; its ground is the arrangement of the three verses:**

**In the twenty-eighth verse the other side ascribed an indecency to God; in the thirty-second it ascribed a prohibition to God.** **The thirty-third verse makes that very act the last item of the list.** **That is, the list ends with an item that takes in the list itself: if "saying what is forbidden on God's authority" enters the list, then adding to the list falls under that item too.**

**This is an explanation, not a ruling.** **And I draw no legal conclusion from it.**

---

## 7/34 — وَلِكُلِّ أُمَّةٍ أَجَلٌ فَإِذَا جَآءَ أَجَلُهُمْ لَا يَسْتَأْخِرُونَ سَاعَةً وَلَا يَسْتَقْدِمُونَ

*Wa li-kulli ummatin ajal, fa-idhā jāʾa ajaluhum lā yastaʾkhirūna sāʿatan wa lā yastaqdimūn*

"**Every community has its term. When their term comes, they cannot delay it by an hour, nor bring it forward.**"

### The position of the sentence

**A point of word order must be recorded: the verse comes immediately after the list (33) and immediately before the address *yā banī Ādam* (35).**

**I record this as an observation:** once the list of what is forbidden is finished, an announcement of a **term** comes directly. **And in the twenty-fourth verse the term of the individual was named (*wa matāʿun ilā ḥīn*); here that of the community is named.**

| Verse | Whose term | Expression |
|---|---|---|
| **7/24** | **Individuals** | *Matāʿun **ilā ḥīn*** |
| **7/34** | **Communities** | *Li-kulli ummatin **ajal*** |

### يَسْتَأْخِرُونَ / يَسْتَقْدِمُونَ — the point of Form X

**Both verbs are of Form X, and this form generally carries the sense of seeking.**

| Verb | Root | The sense of the form |
|---|---|---|
| *Yastaʾkhirūn* | أ-خ-ر | **To seek delay / to be delayed** |
| *Yastaqdimūn* | ق-د-م | **To seek to go forward / to go forward** |

**The lexicographers give two readings here:**

| Reading | Meaning |
|---|---|
| 1 | **Form X is here for seeking**: "even if they seek it, they are not delayed" |
| 2 | **Form X here has the sense of the bare verb**: "they neither lag behind nor go forward" |

**Both readings are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.**

**And the structure of the sentence must be recorded: both directions are closed at once — it neither goes back nor forward.** **This is a pattern repeated in the Qurʾān's announcements of the term** (Yūnus 10/49, al-Naḥl 16/61, al-Muʾminūn 23/43, al-Munāfiqūn 63/11). **It was treated in `016-nahl.md` and `063-munafikun.md`; I rely on those treatments.**

***Sāʿatan* — "an hour, a moment" — comes indefinite, and an indefinite in a context of negation covers the smallest quantity: "not even a moment".**

---

## 7/35-36 — يَٰبَنِىٓ ءَادَمَ إِمَّا يَأْتِيَنَّكُمْ رُسُلٌ مِّنكُمْ يَقُصُّونَ عَلَيْكُمْ ءَايَٰتِى فَمَنِ ٱتَّقَىٰ وَأَصْلَحَ فَلَا خَوْفٌ عَلَيْهِمْ وَلَا هُمْ يَحْزَنُونَ · وَٱلَّذِينَ كَذَّبُوا۟ بِـَٔايَٰتِنَا وَٱسْتَكْبَرُوا۟ عَنْهَآ أُو۟لَٰٓئِكَ أَصْحَٰبُ ٱلنَّارِ هُمْ فِيهَا خَٰلِدُونَ

*Yā banī Ādama immā yaʾtiyannakum rusulun minkum yaquṣṣūna ʿalaykum āyātī, fa-mani'ttaqā wa aṣlaḥa fa-lā khawfun ʿalayhim wa lā hum yaḥzanūn · Wa'lladhīna kadhdhabū bi-āyātinā wa'stakbarū ʿanhā ulāʾika aṣḥābu'n-nāri hum fīhā khālidūn*

"**O children of Adam! If messengers come to you from among yourselves, recounting My signs to you — whoever guards himself and sets himself right, no fear shall be upon them and they shall not grieve. · But those who called Our signs lies and were too great for them, those are the people of the Fire; they will remain in it.**"

### إِمَّا يَأْتِيَنَّكُمْ — the reinforcement of the conditional particle

**A point of word order must be recorded: *immā* is in fact *in* (the conditional) + *mā* (redundant), and the verb has taken the emphatic *nūn* (*yaʾtiyanna*).**

**In Arabic, adding *mā* to the conditional *in* and giving the verb the emphatic *nūn* is a construction implying that the condition will certainly come to pass.**

**And the same pattern occurred at al-Baqara 2/38:** *fa-**immā yaʾtiyannakum** minnī hudā*. **It was treated at 2/38 in `002-bakara.md`; I rely on that treatment.** **The ring closing back to al-Fātiḥa was recorded there.**

**The two verses can be compared:**

| Place | What comes | For whom |
|---|---|---|
| al-Baqara 2/38 | ***Hudā*** — guidance | **Adam and his offspring** |
| **al-Aʿrāf 7/35** | ***Rusulun minkum*** — messengers from among yourselves | **The children of Adam** |

**Al-Baqara says "if guidance comes", al-Aʿrāf "if messengers come".** **The same pattern, two different agents.** **I record this as an observation, and the table can be verified from the two texts.**

**And the qualification *minkum* must be recorded: the messengers will be "from among you".** **The same emphasis will be repeated twice in the sequence of prophets in this sūra:** *ʿalā **rajulin minkum*** (63, Noah) and *ʿalā **rajulin minkum*** (69, Hūd). **The same note in three places.**

### فَلَا خَوْفٌ عَلَيْهِمْ وَلَا هُمْ يَحْزَنُونَ

**This pattern was treated at 2/38 in `002-bakara.md`, and a table was drawn up there:**

| Word | Direction | What |
|---|---|---|
| ***Khawf*** | **The future** | What has not yet happened |
| ***Ḥuzn*** | **The past** | What cannot be taken back |

**I rely on that treatment and do not repeat it here.**

**What belongs here is this: the same pattern occurs twice in this sūra, and the second is in the section on the people of al-Aʿrāf.**

| Verse | Sentence | For whom |
|---|---|---|
| **7/35** | *Fa-lā khawfun ʿalayhim wa lā hum yaḥzanūn* | **Those who guard themselves and set themselves right** |
| **7/49** | *Lā khawfun ʿalaykum wa lā antum taḥzanūn* | **Those told to enter the Garden** |

**The first is in the third person, the second in the second person — that is, at 49 the word is said directly to the addressee.** This is an overlap that can be verified within the sūra, and it will be taken up again at 46-49.

### ٱتَّقَىٰ وَأَصْلَحَ — two verbs

**The protasis of the conditional clause is two verbs: *ittaqā* (he guarded himself) and *aṣlaḥa* (he set right).**

The root **ص-ل-ح**: the going away of a defect, becoming useful and fit. **The verb here is of Form IV (*aṣlaḥa*) and is transitive; its object is not stated.**

**I record this as an observation:** the verse does not say "he set himself right" or "he set his affair right"; **it leaves the object open.** **And the same root occurs seven times in this sūra**, the four most striking being these:

| Verse | Expression | Who |
|---|---|---|
| **7/56** | *Wa lā tufsidū fi'l-arḍi baʿda **iṣlāḥihā*** | **A command** |
| **7/85** | *Wa lā tufsidū fi'l-arḍi baʿda **iṣlāḥihā*** | **Shuʿayb** |
| **7/142** | *Ukhlufnī fī qawmī wa **aṣliḥ*** | **Moses → Aaron (Hārūn)** |
| **7/170** | *Innā lā nuḍīʿu ajra'l-**muṣliḥīn*** | **Those who hold fast to the Book** |

**The root is one of the words the sūra carries from beginning to end.**

---

# IV. The meeting of the two sides — 7/37-53

**This block is one of the most detailed dialogues of the gathering in the Qurʾān, and it contains five separate turns of speech:**

| Turn | Who → whom | Verse |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **The angels → those who are dying** | 37 |
| 2 | **Those who came later ↔ those who came first** | 38-39 |
| 3 | **The people of the Garden → the people of the Fire**, then a **crier** | 44-45 |
| 4 | **The people of *al-Aʿrāf* → both sides at once** | 46-49 |
| 5 | **The people of the Fire → the people of the Garden** | 50-51 |

**Five turns, six parties.** **And the sūra takes its name from the fourth turn.**

---

## 7/37 — فَمَنْ أَظْلَمُ مِمَّنِ ٱفْتَرَىٰ عَلَى ٱللَّهِ كَذِبًا أَوْ كَذَّبَ بِـَٔايَٰتِهِۦٓ أُو۟لَٰٓئِكَ يَنَالُهُمْ نَصِيبُهُم مِّنَ ٱلْكِتَٰبِ حَتَّىٰٓ إِذَا جَآءَتْهُمْ رُسُلُنَا يَتَوَفَّوْنَهُمْ قَالُوٓا۟ أَيْنَ مَا كُنتُمْ تَدْعُونَ مِن دُونِ ٱللَّهِ قَالُوا۟ ضَلُّوا۟ عَنَّا وَشَهِدُوا۟ عَلَىٰٓ أَنفُسِهِمْ أَنَّهُمْ كَانُوا۟ كَٰفِرِينَ

*Fa-man aẓlamu mimmani'ftarā ʿala'llāhi kadhiban aw kadhdhaba bi-āyātih, ulāʾika yanāluhum naṣībuhum mina'l-kitāb, ḥattā idhā jāʾathum rusulunā yatawaffawnahum qālū ayna mā kuntum tadʿūna min dūni'llāh, qālū ḍallū ʿannā wa shahidū ʿalā anfusihim annahum kānū kāfirīn*

"**Who is more wrongdoing than one who invents a lie against God or calls His signs lies? Their share of the Book reaches them. Then, when Our messengers come to take their souls, they say, 'Where are those you used to call upon beside God?' They say, 'They have gone astray from us,' and they bear witness against themselves that they were deniers.**"

### فَمَنْ أَظْلَمُ — the Qurʾān's pattern

**This pattern occurs in close to fifteen places in the Qurʾān and was treated in the index** (`018-kehf.md` 18/57, `032-secde.md` 32/22, `039-zumer.md` 39/32). **I rely on those treatments.**

**What belongs here is this: the verse counts two acts, and the two are joined by *aw* (or).**

| Verb | What | Direction |
|---|---|---|
| *Iftarā ʿala'llāhi kadhiban* | **To invent a lie against God** | **Addition** — to say what is not |
| *Kadhdhaba bi-āyātih* | **To call His signs lies** | **Subtraction** — to reject what is |

**I record this as an observation:** the two acts run in opposite directions — one adds, the other subtracts. **And the last item of the thirty-third verse (*an taqūlū ʿala'llāhi mā lā taʿlamūn*) corresponds to the first act.** **That is, there are four verses between the list and this verse, and the same act is named twice.**

### نَصِيبُهُم مِّنَ ٱلْكِتَٰبِ — a disagreement

**Two readings are transmitted of the expression *naṣībuhum mina'l-kitāb*:**

| Reading | What *kitāb* is | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **The preserved tablet / what is decreed** | "The share written for them reaches them" — lifespan, provision, term |
| 2 | **The Book sent down** | "The share announced for them in the Book reaches them" — the recompense promised |

**Both readings are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.**

**A point of word order:** *yanāluhum naṣībuhum* — the verb is *nāla* (ن-ي-ل), "to reach". **And the same root will return in the forty-ninth verse:** *lā **yanāluhumu**'llāhu bi-raḥma*. **Two occurrences: one the reaching of a share, the other the not reaching of mercy.**

### رُسُلُنَا يَتَوَفَّوْنَهُمْ

***Rusul* here are not messengers (prophets) but those charged with taking the soul** — this is understood from the context and from the verb (*yatawaffawnahum*). **The same usage is present at al-Anʿām 6/61 too** (*tawaffathu rusulunā*).

The root **و-ف-ي**: to be complete, to render in full. ***Tawaffā* is "to take a thing in full"** — hence "to take the soul". **The root was treated at 39/42 in `039-zumer.md` (*Allāhu yatawaffa'l-anfus*); I rely on that treatment.**

### وَشَهِدُوا۟ عَلَىٰٓ أَنفُسِهِمْ — the direction of the testimony

**The construction of the sentence must be recorded: *shahidū ʿalā anfusihim* — "they bore witness against themselves".**

**And the same structure will return in the hundred and seventy-second verse — but there the direction differs:**

| Verse | Sentence | When | For what |
|---|---|---|---|
| **7/37** | *Wa **shahidū ʿalā anfusihim** annahum kānū kāfirīn* | **At the moment of death** | **Against themselves** |
| **7/172** | *Wa **ashhadahum ʿalā anfusihim** a-lastu bi-rabbikum* | **At the beginning** | **An acknowledgement** |

**There are a hundred and thirty-five verses between the two, and both use the structure *shahida ʿalā nafsih*.**

**I record this as my own reading and it is not binding; its ground is the shared word order of the two verses:** **the sūra makes man a witness about himself twice** — once at the beginning (172), once at the end (37). **And the two testimonies face one another: the first an acknowledgement, the second a confession.** **This will be completed at 172-174.**

---

## 7/38-39 — قَالَ ٱدْخُلُوا۟ فِىٓ أُمَمٍ قَدْ خَلَتْ مِن قَبْلِكُم مِّنَ ٱلْجِنِّ وَٱلْإِنسِ فِى ٱلنَّارِ كُلَّمَا دَخَلَتْ أُمَّةٌ لَّعَنَتْ أُخْتَهَا حَتَّىٰٓ إِذَا ٱدَّارَكُوا۟ فِيهَا جَمِيعًا قَالَتْ أُخْرَىٰهُمْ لِأُولَىٰهُمْ رَبَّنَا هَٰٓؤُلَآءِ أَضَلُّونَا فَـَٔاتِهِمْ عَذَابًا ضِعْفًا مِّنَ ٱلنَّارِ قَالَ لِكُلٍّ ضِعْفٌ وَلَٰكِن لَّا تَعْلَمُونَ · وَقَالَتْ أُولَىٰهُمْ لِأُخْرَىٰهُمْ فَمَا كَانَ لَكُمْ عَلَيْنَا مِن فَضْلٍ فَذُوقُوا۟ ٱلْعَذَابَ بِمَا كُنتُمْ تَكْسِبُونَ

*Qāla'dkhulū fī umamin qad khalat min qablikum mina'l-jinni wa'l-insi fi'n-nār, kullamā dakhalat ummatun laʿanat ukhtahā, ḥattā idha'ddārakū fīhā jamīʿan qālat ukhrāhum li-ūlāhum rabbanā hāʾulāʾi aḍallūnā fa-ātihim ʿadhāban ḍiʿfan mina'n-nār, qāla li-kullin ḍiʿfun wa lākin lā taʿlamūn · Wa qālat ūlāhum li-ukhrāhum fa-mā kāna lakum ʿalaynā min faḍlin fa-dhūqu'l-ʿadhāba bimā kuntum taksibūn*

"**He says: 'Enter the Fire among communities of jinn and men that have passed away before you.' Every time a community enters, it curses its fellow. Then, when they have all caught up with one another there, the last of them will say of the first: 'Our Lord! These led us astray; give them a double punishment of the Fire.' He says: 'For each there is double, but you do not know.' · And the first of them will say to the last: 'You have no advantage over us; taste then the punishment for what you earned.'**"

### لَّعَنَتْ أُخْتَهَا — "her sister"

**A point of word order must be recorded: the verse does not say "it curses the one before it", it says "*ukhtahā*" — "its sister".**

**In Arabic a thing's like, one of the same kind, is called its *ukht*** (*hādhihi ukhtu hādhih* — "this is the like of this"). **The word states not kinship but likeness.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the choice of the word:** **the side cursed is the like of the side that curses.** **And the thirty-ninth verse will say this outright: *fa-mā kāna lakum ʿalaynā min faḍl* — "you have no advantage over us."**

### ٱدَّارَكُوا۟ — the root د-ر-ك

**The sense of the root: to catch up, to reach, to overtake.** ***Iddāraku* is originally *tadāraku* (Form VI); the *tāʾ* has been assimilated into the *dāl*.** **Form VI states reciprocity: "they caught up with one another".**

**And the same root was treated at 27/66 in `027-neml.md` (*bali'ddāraka ʿilmuhum fi'l-ākhira*); I rely on that treatment.**

### أُخْرَىٰهُمْ لِأُولَىٰهُمْ — two groups, two accusations

**The structure of the dialogue must be recorded, and it is symmetrical:**

| Who | To whom | What it says |
|---|---|---|
| **The last** (*ukhrāhum*) | **Of the first** (*ūlāhum*) | *Hāʾulāʾi **aḍallūnā*** — these led us astray; **give them double punishment** |
| **God** | — | *Li-kullin **ḍiʿfun*** — for each there is double |
| **The first** (*ūlāhum*) | **To the last** (*ukhrāhum*) | *Fa-mā kāna lakum ʿalaynā min **faḍl*** — you have no advantage |

**And the form of the answer must be recorded: the request was "give them double"; the answer becomes "for everyone there is double".**

***Wa lākin lā taʿlamūn* — "but you do not know."** **That is, the one who makes the request does not know that what he asks for takes him in as well.**

**I record this as an observation:** in the scene a **transfer of responsibility** is attempted and refused. **And the same attempt will be repeated in the hundred and seventy-third verse of the sūra:** *innamā ashraka **ābāʾunā** min qablu wa kunnā dhurriyyatan min baʿdihim, a-fa-tuhlikunā bimā faʿala'l-mubṭilūn*. **The two scenes do the same work: laying the load on the past.**

| Verse | Who | Sentence |
|---|---|---|
| **7/38** | **Those who came later** | *Hāʾulāʾi aḍallūnā* |
| **7/173** | **Those who came later** | *Innamā ashraka ābāʾunā min qablu* |

**This is an overlap that can be verified within the sūra, and it will be taken up again at 172-174.**

### ضِعْف — the indeterminacy of the word

The root **ض-ع-ف** carries both the sense "weakness" and the sense "fold, multiple". ***Ḍiʿf* means "as much again of a thing"; it is understood as "double" or as "many times over".**

**The lexicographers have disagreed over the exactness of the quantity; I build no claim about a number.** **What I record is that the answer corrects not the quantity but the distribution: *li-kullin* — "for each".**

---

## 7/40-41 — إِنَّ ٱلَّذِينَ كَذَّبُوا۟ بِـَٔايَٰتِنَا وَٱسْتَكْبَرُوا۟ عَنْهَا لَا تُفَتَّحُ لَهُمْ أَبْوَٰبُ ٱلسَّمَآءِ وَلَا يَدْخُلُونَ ٱلْجَنَّةَ حَتَّىٰ يَلِجَ ٱلْجَمَلُ فِى سَمِّ ٱلْخِيَاطِ وَكَذَٰلِكَ نَجْزِى ٱلْمُجْرِمِينَ · لَهُم مِّن جَهَنَّمَ مِهَادٌ وَمِن فَوْقِهِمْ غَوَاشٍ وَكَذَٰلِكَ نَجْزِى ٱلظَّٰلِمِينَ

*Inna'lladhīna kadhdhabū bi-āyātinā wa'stakbarū ʿanhā lā tufattaḥu lahum abwābu's-samāʾi wa lā yadkhulūna'l-jannata ḥattā yalija'l-jamalu fī sammi'l-khiyāṭ, wa kadhālika najzi'l-mujrimīn · Lahum min jahannama mihādun wa min fawqihim ghawāsh, wa kadhālika najzi'ẓ-ẓālimīn*

"**As for those who called Our signs lies and were too great for them: the gates of heaven will not be opened for them, and they will not enter the Garden until the camel passes through the eye of the needle. Thus do We recompense the guilty. · For them there is a bed of Hell and coverings above them. Thus do We recompense the wrongdoers.**"

### لَا تُفَتَّحُ لَهُمْ أَبْوَٰبُ ٱلسَّمَآءِ

**A point of word order must be recorded: the verb is the passive of Form II (*tufattaḥu*), not *tuftaḥu*.** **Form II here carries the sense of multiplicity and of one by one: "the gates are not opened one by one."**

**And the same pattern occurs in this sūra in the affirmative as well:**

| Verse | Sentence | What is opened |
|---|---|---|
| **7/40** | *Lā **tufattaḥu** lahum abwābu's-samāʾ* | **The gates of heaven — not opened** |
| **7/96** | *La-**fataḥnā** ʿalayhim barakātin mina's-samāʾi wa'l-arḍ* | **Blessings — opened** |

**The same root (ف-ت-ح), in two directions.** **And verse 96 is the counterpart of this passage: there, on the condition of faith and God-fearing, blessings will be opened from heaven and earth.** **I record this as an observation, and the two verses can be verified from the text.**

**Two readings are transmitted about what the expression "the gates of heaven are not opened" refers to:**

| Reading | What is not opened |
|---|---|
| 1 | **To their souls** — their ascent when their souls are taken |
| 2 | **To their deeds / their prayers** — their not being accepted |

**Both readings are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.** **And the Qurʾān gives no detail here; I invent no detail.**

### حَتَّىٰ يَلِجَ ٱلْجَمَلُ فِى سَمِّ ٱلْخِيَاطِ

**This is one of the best-known expressions of impossibility in the Qurʾān.**

***Ḥattā* here means "until", and in Arabic, used together with a condition impossible of fulfilment, it gives the sense "never".** This is a known pattern in the language (*taʿlīq bi'l-muḥāl*).

The root **و-ل-ج**: **to enter a narrow place.** ***Wulūj* is "to be thrust inside".** **The root was treated in `031-lokman.md` 31/29 and `057-hadid.md` 57/6 (*yūliju'l-layla fi'n-nahār*), where the sense "to pass one thing inside another" was recorded.** **I rely on those treatments.**

**I record this as an observation:** the choice of the verb is worth recording — not *yadkhulu* (to enter) but ***yaliju*** (to be thrust into a narrow place). **That is, the verb already carries narrowness, and it joins with the narrowness of the hole.**

### سَمِّ ٱلْخِيَاط — the eye of the needle

The root **س-م-م** was undone at 52/27 in `052-tur.md` (*ʿadhāba's-samūm*) and at 56/42 in `056-vakia.md`. **I rely on those treatments.** A summary of what was recorded there:

- **The root carries two branches: pore/hole, and poison.**
- ***Samūm* is "the scorching hot wind that works into the pores".**
- ***Masāmm* — the pores of the body;** the relation with *samm* (poison) was conveyed **as a relation drawn by the lexicographers**, not as a settled etymological ruling.

**What belongs here is this: the same root comes here with its first branch.** ***Sammu'l-khiyāṭ* — "the hole of the needle".**

| Place | Word | Which branch of the root |
|---|---|---|
| al-Ṭūr 52/27 · al-Wāqiʿa 56/42 | *Samūm* | **The heat that works into the pores** |
| **al-Aʿrāf 7/40** | ***Samm*** | **The hole — the eye of the needle** |

The root **خ-ي-ط**: to sew. ***Khiyāṭ* is the sewing needle; *khayṭ* is thread.** **And the same root occurs at al-Baqara 2/187: *al-**khayṭ**u'l-abyaḍu mina'l-**khayṭ**i'l-aswad* — "the white thread, the black thread".** **It was treated at 2/187 in `002-bakara.md`; I rely on that treatment.**

**I record this as an observation, and the two verses can be verified from the text:** the same root gives **the thread** in al-Baqara and **the needle** in al-Aʿrāf. **Both come from the picture of sewing.**

### ٱلْجَمَل — a variant reading

**There is a variant reading in this word that changes the meaning, and it must be given in a table.**

| Reading | Spelling | Meaning | Its root |
|---|---|---|---|
| ***al-jamal*** | ٱلْجَمَل | **Camel** — the male camel | ج-م-ل |
| ***al-jummal*** | ٱلْجُمَّل | **Ship's cable** — a thick twisted rope | ج-م-ل |
| ***al-jumal*** | ٱلْجُمَل | **The same** — cable (on another pattern) | ج-م-ل |
| ***al-juml*** | ٱلْجُمْل | **The same** — cable | ج-م-ل |

**The widespread reading, transmitted by mass transmission, is the first: *al-jamal* — camel.** **The other readings have been transmitted and are held to be anomalous (*shādhdh*).** **I do not impose a preference; both have been transmitted.**

**The effect of the two readings on the meaning must be recorded:**

| Reading | How the likeness works |
|---|---|
| ***Jamal* (camel)** | **Two extremes:** the largest animal and the smallest hole — **a difference of kind** |
| ***Jummal* (cable)** | **Of the same kind:** thread and cable, both belonging to sewing — **a difference of degree** |

**I record this as an observation:** the second reading has an inner consistency — **needle and cable are the two ends of one work (sewing);** thread passes through the needle, a cable does not. **But the first reading is both the widespread one and the one transmitted by mass transmission, and it builds the likeness more sharply.** **I do not impose a preference.**

**One further note:** the lexicographers record that the words *jamal* and *jummal* are **from the same root** (ج-م-ل: to gather together, to amass — *jumla*, *ijmāl* are from the same root). **A cable is threads twisted together; and the camel is the animal that gathers up a load and carries it.** **This is a relation drawn by the lexicographers; I do not give it as a settled etymological ruling.**

### مِهَاد and غَوَاشٍ — below and above

**The forty-first verse builds the picture of a bed with two words:**

| Word | Root | Meaning | Direction |
|---|---|---|---|
| ***Mihād*** | م-ه-د | **A mattress, a bed spread out** | **Below** |
| ***Ghawāsh*** | غ-ش-و | **Coverings, things drawn over** | **Above** |

The root **م-ه-د** was treated at 20/53 in `020-taha.md` (*jaʿala lakumu'l-arḍa mahdā*) and at 78/6 in `078-nebe.md` (*a-lam najʿali'l-arḍa mihādā*). **I rely on those treatments.** **And the difference must be recorded: there the *mihād* was the earth's being spread out for man; here it is Hell's being spread out.**

The root **غ-ش-و**: to cover, to envelop. **The root was treated in `088-gasiye.md` (*al-ghāshiya*) and at 45/23 in `045-casiye.md` (*ghishāwatan*).** **I rely on those treatments.** **And the same root will occur in the fifty-fourth verse of this sūra: *yughshi'l-layla'n-nahār*.**

**I record this as an observation:** the verse counts below and above — **and these were exactly the two directions Iblīs did not count in the seventeenth verse** (*fawq* and *taḥt*). **There is an overlap between the two verses, and I give it as a possible reading, not as a certainty.**

### Two closings: مُجْرِمِين and ظَٰلِمِين

**A point of word order must be recorded: both verses end with the same pattern but with a different word.**

| Verse | Closing |
|---|---|
| **7/40** | *Wa kadhālika najzi'l-**mujrimīn*** |
| **7/41** | *Wa kadhālika najzi'ẓ-**ẓālimīn*** |

The root **ج-ر-م**: to cut, to sever — hence *jurm*, "the evil act one earns". The root **ظ-ل-م**: to put a thing somewhere other than its place (treated at 8-9).

**The two words come one after another and name the same group by two different attributes.** **This accords with the sūra's manner, sustained from the beginning, of defining by attribute** (`STYLE.md`: "it is the attributes the verse describes").

---

## 7/42-43 — وَٱلَّذِينَ ءَامَنُوا۟ وَعَمِلُوا۟ ٱلصَّٰلِحَٰتِ لَا نُكَلِّفُ نَفْسًا إِلَّا وُسْعَهَآ أُو۟لَٰٓئِكَ أَصْحَٰبُ ٱلْجَنَّةِ هُمْ فِيهَا خَٰلِدُونَ · وَنَزَعْنَا مَا فِى صُدُورِهِم مِّنْ غِلٍّ تَجْرِى مِن تَحْتِهِمُ ٱلْأَنْهَٰرُ وَقَالُوا۟ ٱلْحَمْدُ لِلَّهِ ٱلَّذِى هَدَىٰنَا لِهَٰذَا وَمَا كُنَّا لِنَهْتَدِىَ لَوْلَآ أَنْ هَدَىٰنَا ٱللَّهُ لَقَدْ جَآءَتْ رُسُلُ رَبِّنَا بِٱلْحَقِّ وَنُودُوٓا۟ أَن تِلْكُمُ ٱلْجَنَّةُ أُورِثْتُمُوهَا بِمَا كُنتُمْ تَعْمَلُونَ

*Wa'lladhīna āmanū wa ʿamilu'ṣ-ṣāliḥāti lā nukallifu nafsan illā wusʿahā ulāʾika aṣḥābu'l-jannati hum fīhā khālidūn · Wa nazaʿnā mā fī ṣudūrihim min ghillin tajrī min taḥtihimu'l-anhār, wa qālu'l-ḥamdu li'llāhi'lladhī hadānā li-hādhā wa mā kunnā li-nahtadiya lawlā an hadāna'llāh, laqad jāʾat rusulu rabbinā bi'l-ḥaqq, wa nūdū an tilkumu'l-jannatu ūrithtumūhā bimā kuntum taʿmalūn*

"**Those who believe and do righteous deeds — We charge no soul beyond its capacity — those are the people of the Garden; they will remain in it. · We have pulled out whatever rancour was in their breasts. Rivers run beneath them. They say: 'Praise be to God, who guided us to this. We would never have found the way had God not guided us. The messengers of our Lord did come with the truth.' And they are called to: 'This is the Garden you have been made to inherit for what you used to do.'**"

### The intervening sentence

**A point of word order must be recorded, and it is striking: the sentence *lā nukallifu nafsan illā wusʿahā* comes in between the subject (*wa'lladhīna āmanū wa ʿamilu'ṣ-ṣāliḥāt*) and its predicate (*ulāʾika aṣḥābu'l-janna*).**

**This is a construction seen in the second verse too** (there *fa-lā yakun fī ṣadrika ḥarajun minh* had come in between two parts). **In both places the intervening sentence is an announcement that lightens a load.**

| Verse | The intervening sentence | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| **7/2** | *Fa-lā yakun fī ṣadrika ḥarajun minh* | **It lightens the messenger's load** |
| **7/42** | *Lā nukallifu nafsan illā wusʿahā* | **It limits the addressee's load** |

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is that the two sentences stand in the same position of word order.**

*Wusʿ* — the root **و-س-ع**: breadth. ***Wusʿ* is "a person's breadth", that is, his capacity.** **The same sentence occurs at al-Baqara 2/286** (*lā yukallifu'llāhu nafsan illā wusʿahā*) **and was treated in `002-bakara.md`; I rely on that treatment.**

**And the same root will occur twice more in this sūra, and both are striking:**

| Verse | Expression |
|---|---|
| **7/42** | *Illā **wusʿ**ahā* — capacity |
| **7/89** | ***Wasiʿa** rabbunā kulla shayʾin ʿilmā* — in Shuʿayb's mouth |
| **7/156** | *Wa raḥmatī **wasiʿat** kulla shayʾ* |

**Three occurrences: one the breadth of man, two the breadth of God (knowledge and mercy).**

### وَنَزَعْنَا مَا فِى صُدُورِهِم مِّنْ غِلٍّ

The root **ن-ز-ع** was treated at the twenty-seventh verse (*yanziʿu ʿanhumā libāsahumā*). **And the same verb comes back here.**

| Verse | What was pulled out | From whom |
|---|---|---|
| **7/27** | ***Libāsahumā*** — their clothing | **From Adam and his wife** — by Satan |
| **7/43** | ***Mā fī ṣudūrihim min ghill*** — the rancour in their breasts | **From the people of the Garden** — by God |

**I record this as my own reading and it is not binding; its ground is the two occurrences of the verb:** **the same verb is in one place the taking away of a covering, in the other the taking away of a burden.** **One a loss, the other a gain.**

The root **غ-ل-ل**: **a thing's seeping in between, entering secretly.** ***Ghill* is "rancour that has seeped into the breast";** from the same root come *ghull* (the ring or fetter put on the neck) and *ghulūl* (secretly filching from the spoils).

**And the other branch of the root will occur in the hundred and fifty-seventh verse of this sūra: *wa'l-**aghlāl**a'llatī kānat ʿalayhim* — "the fetters that were upon them".**

| Verse | Word | Branch of the root |
|---|---|---|
| **7/43** | *Ghill* | **Rancour** — what seeps inward |
| **7/157** | *Al-aghlāl* | **The fetter** — what is put on the neck |

**Two words from one root, and both carry the idea of a binding.** **And both are lifted: one in the Garden (*nazaʿnā*), the other by the messenger (*wa yaḍaʿu ʿanhum*).** This is an overlap that can be verified within the sūra.

### وَمَا كُنَّا لِنَهْتَدِىَ لَوْلَآ أَنْ هَدَىٰنَا ٱللَّهُ

**A point of word order must be recorded: the sentence contains the *lām* of denial (*lām al-juḥūd*) — *mā kunnā li-nahtadiya*.**

**In Arabic the pattern *mā kāna* + *li-* + imperfect states negation in its strongest form: "it was not a thing that could be, there was no possibility of it."** **This pattern was treated in `026-suara.md` and in the sections of `010-yunus.md`.**

**And it is akin to the pattern *mā yakūnu laka an tatakabbara* in the thirty-third verse — there too an impossibility was being stated.**

**I record this as an observation:** the first sentence of the people of the Garden is a **praise**, the second a **withdrawal of themselves**. **And the third is the confirmation of the messengers' truthfulness** (*laqad jāʾat rusulu rabbinā bi'l-ḥaqq*). **Three sentences: thanks, humility, confirmation.**

### أُورِثْتُمُوهَا — the verb of inheritance

The root **و-ر-ث**: to come into an inheritance. **The verb here is passive: "you have been made to inherit it."**

**And the same root occurs twice more in this sūra:**

| Verse | Expression | Who |
|---|---|---|
| **7/43** | *Ūrithtumūhā* | **The people of the Garden** |
| **7/100** | *Alladhīna **yarithūna'l-arḍa** min baʿdi ahlihā* | **Those who inherit the earth** |
| **7/128** | *Inna'l-arḍa li'llāhi **yūrithuhā** man yashāʾu min ʿibādih* | **Moses's word to his people** |
| **7/137** | *Wa **awrathna'l**-qawma'lladhīna kānū yustaḍʿafūna mashāriqa'l-arḍi wa maghāribahā* | **Those who had been made weak** |

**Four occurrences: one the Garden, three the earth.**

**And a point of word order must be recorded: the sentence ends with *bimā kuntum taʿmalūn* — "for what you used to do."** **In the same verse, "you were made to inherit" (a giving without return) and "for what you used to do" (a giving in return) stand side by side.** **I record this as an observation; the text joins the two and does not resolve the relation between them.**

---

## 7/44-45 — وَنَادَىٰٓ أَصْحَٰبُ ٱلْجَنَّةِ أَصْحَٰبَ ٱلنَّارِ أَن قَدْ وَجَدْنَا مَا وَعَدَنَا رَبُّنَا حَقًّا فَهَلْ وَجَدتُّم مَّا وَعَدَ رَبُّكُمْ حَقًّا قَالُوا۟ نَعَمْ فَأَذَّنَ مُؤَذِّنٌۢ بَيْنَهُمْ أَن لَّعْنَةُ ٱللَّهِ عَلَى ٱلظَّٰلِمِينَ · ٱلَّذِينَ يَصُدُّونَ عَن سَبِيلِ ٱللَّهِ وَيَبْغُونَهَا عِوَجًا وَهُم بِٱلْءَاخِرَةِ كَٰفِرُونَ

*Wa nādā aṣḥābu'l-jannati aṣḥāba'n-nāri an qad wajadnā mā waʿadanā rabbunā ḥaqqan fa-hal wajadtum mā waʿada rabbukum ḥaqqā, qālū naʿam, fa-adhdhana muʾadhdhinun baynahum an laʿnatu'llāhi ʿala'ẓ-ẓālimīn · Alladhīna yaṣuddūna ʿan sabīli'llāhi wa yabghūnahā ʿiwajan wa hum bi'l-ākhirati kāfirūn*

"**The people of the Garden call out to the people of the Fire: 'We have found what our Lord promised us to be true. Have you found what your Lord promised you to be true?' They say, 'Yes.' Then a crier calls out among them: 'The curse of God be upon the wrongdoers!' · Those who bar others from the way of God and seek to make it crooked, and who deny the hereafter.**"

### وَجَدْنَا … وَجَدتُّم — the same verb, two sides

**A point of word order must be recorded: the question is built so as to carry its own answer within it.**

> *Qad **wajadnā** mā waʿadanā rabbunā **ḥaqqā** · fa-hal **wajadtum** mā waʿada rabbukum **ḥaqqā***

**The same verb (و-ج-د), the same specifying accusative (*ḥaqqan*), two different subjects.** **And the answer is a single word: *naʿam* — "yes".**

**I record this as an observation:** there is no argument in the scene. **The other side does not object; it confirms.** **And this looks to the same place as *shahidū ʿalā anfusihim* in the thirty-seventh verse: the admission comes from the other side.**

**And it must be recorded that the verb *nādā* occurs four times in this block:**

| Verse | Who called | To whom |
|---|---|---|
| **7/44** | **The people of the Garden** | **To the people of the Fire** |
| **7/46** | **The people of *al-Aʿrāf*** | **To the people of the Garden** |
| **7/48** | **The people of *al-Aʿrāf*** | **To the men they recognised** |
| **7/50** | **The people of the Fire** | **To the people of the Garden** |

**Four calls, three different parties.** **And the people of *al-Aʿrāf* call out twice — the group that gives the sūra its name is the group that speaks most in the block.** **This is a countable datum.**

### فَأَذَّنَ مُؤَذِّنٌۢ بَيْنَهُمْ

The root **أ-ذ-ن**: **ear** (*udhun*), and from this **to announce, to proclaim** (*adhān*). ***Muʾadhdhin* is "one who announces".**

**A point of word order must be recorded: *muʾadhdhin* comes indefinite — "a crier".** **Who he is is not stated.**

**I record this as an observation, and I do not add the detail the Qurʾān does not give:** explanations of who this crier is are transmitted in the classical commentaries; **none of them can be verified from the text, and none is certain knowledge.** **I content myself with transmitting them.**

**And *baynahum* — "among them" — must be recorded: the crier is not on either of the two sides, but between them.** **And two verses later "a veil between them" (*wa baynahumā ḥijāb*) will be named.** **There is an overlap between the two expressions; but I give it not as a certainty, only as a possible reading.**

### يَبْغُونَهَا عِوَجًا — a repetition within the sūra

The root **ع-و-ج** was treated at 39/28 in `039-zumer.md` (*ghayra dhī ʿiwaj*) and at 18/1 in `018-kehf.md` (*wa lam yajʿal lahū ʿiwajā*). **I rely on those treatments.** The distinction recorded there: ***ʿiwaj* is crookedness that is not seen, *ʿawaj* crookedness that is seen.**

**And this sentence occurs twice in al-Aʿrāf:**

| Verse | Sentence | Who |
|---|---|---|
| **7/45** | *Yaṣuddūna ʿan sabīli'llāhi wa **yabghūnahā ʿiwajā*** | **The description of the wrongdoers** |
| **7/86** | *Wa taṣuddūna ʿan sabīli'llāhi man āmana bihī wa **tabghūnahā ʿiwajā*** | **The notables of Madyan** |

**The two sentences use exactly the same verbs: *ṣadda* and *baghā … ʿiwajan*.** **One a general description, the other a concrete community.**

**I record this as an observation, and the table can be verified from the text:** **the sūra applies in the eighty-sixth verse to a people the description it gave in the forty-fifth.** **And this is the third instance in al-Aʿrāf of the technique tabulated in `011-hud.md` Table 4** (the others: *lā tufsidū fi'l-arḍi baʿda iṣlāḥihā* 56→85, and *mā lam yunazzil bihī sulṭānan* 33→71).

**The sūra applies this technique four times. In a table:**

| Sentence | The general announcement | Its application in the sequence of prophets |
|---|---|---|
| *Mā (lam) yunazzil bihī sulṭān* | **7/33** — the list of what is forbidden | **7/71** — Hūd |
| *Lā tufsidū fi'l-arḍi baʿda iṣlāḥihā* | **7/56** — a direct command | **7/85** — Shuʿayb |
| *Yaṣuddūna ʿan sabīli'llāhi wa yabghūnahā ʿiwajā* | **7/45** — the description of the wrongdoers | **7/86** — Madyan |
| *La-aqʿudanna lahum ṣirāṭaka'l-mustaqīm* | **7/16** — Iblīs's threat | **7/86** — *lā taqʿudū bi-kulli ṣirāṭ* |

**Four rows.** **I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the eight verses themselves:** **al-Aʿrāf applies in the sequence of prophets the measures it set up in the first fifty-eight verses.** **And three of the four applications are in the story of Shuʿayb (85, 86, 86).**

---

## 7/46-49 — The people of al-Aʿrāf

## 7/46-47 — وَبَيْنَهُمَا حِجَابٌ وَعَلَى ٱلْأَعْرَافِ رِجَالٌ يَعْرِفُونَ كُلًّۢا بِسِيمَىٰهُمْ وَنَادَوْا۟ أَصْحَٰبَ ٱلْجَنَّةِ أَن سَلَٰمٌ عَلَيْكُمْ لَمْ يَدْخُلُوهَا وَهُمْ يَطْمَعُونَ · وَإِذَا صُرِفَتْ أَبْصَٰرُهُمْ تِلْقَآءَ أَصْحَٰبِ ٱلنَّارِ قَالُوا۟ رَبَّنَا لَا تَجْعَلْنَا مَعَ ٱلْقَوْمِ ٱلظَّٰلِمِينَ

*Wa baynahumā ḥijāb, wa ʿala'l-aʿrāfi rijālun yaʿrifūna kullan bi-sīmāhum, wa nādaw aṣḥāba'l-jannati an salāmun ʿalaykum, lam yadkhulūhā wa hum yaṭmaʿūn · Wa idhā ṣurifat abṣāruhum tilqāʾa aṣḥābi'n-nāri qālū rabbanā lā tajʿalnā maʿa'l-qawmi'ẓ-ẓālimīn*
