"**Between the two there is a veil. And upon al-Aʿrāf are men who recognise each by his mark. They call out to the people of the Garden: 'Peace be upon you.' They have not entered it, but they hope to. · And when their eyes are turned towards the people of the Fire they say: 'Our Lord! Do not place us with the wrongdoing people.'**"

### ٱلْأَعْرَاف — the word itself

The root **ع-ر-ف** carries two branches, and both must be recorded:

| Branch | Meaning | Derivatives |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | **Height, ridge** | *ʿUrf* (a horse's mane, a cockerel's comb), ***aʿrāf*** (plural) |
| **2** | **To know, to recognise** | *Maʿrifa*, *ʿārif*, ***yaʿrifūna***, *maʿrūf* |

**And the verse itself uses the two branches side by side:** *wa ʿala'l-**aʿrāf**i rijālun **yaʿrifūna** kullan bi-sīmāhum*.

**I record this as an observation, and its ground is the verse's own words:** **the same root occurs in the same sentence both as a place-name and as a verb.** **This is not a play on sounds or numbers but the presence of the root's two branches in one sentence** — and therefore it is not a reckoning of the kind `STYLE.md` forbids.

**And the word *maʿrūf* will occur twice in this sūra:** *wa'mur bi'l-**ʿurf*** (199) and *yaʾmuruhum bi'l-**maʿrūf*** (157). **The same root, in the name of the sūra and in two commands.**

### The disagreement — what is *al-Aʿrāf*?

**This is a place where the classical commentaries divide. I give the disagreement in a table and do not impose a preference.**

| View | What *al-Aʿrāf* is | Ground |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **The high part, the crest, of the wall between the Garden and the Fire** | The root sense of the word (*ʿurf* = ridge, height); its being read together with *wa baynahumā ḥijāb* in the verse |
| 2 | **A high place / a summit**, a station from which both sides are seen | The same root sense; the calling out to both sides in verses 46-49 |
| 3 | **Derived from *maʿrifa*, "the place of recognising"** | The second branch of the root; the sentence *yaʿrifūna kullan bi-sīmāhum* |

**The first and second views are close to one another, and the majority stands here.** **The third is also transmitted.** **I do not impose a preference.**

**A "wall" (*sūr*) is named in one other place in the Qurʾān: al-Ḥadīd 57/13** — *fa-ḍuriba baynahum bi-sūrin lahū bābun bāṭinuhū fīhi'r-raḥmatu wa ẓāhiruhū min qibalihi'l-ʿadhāb*. **It was treated at 57/13 in `057-hadid.md`.** **In the classical commentaries the two verses are read together; but that the two texts describe the same thing is not said by the Qurʾān.** **I give this as a possible link, not as a certainty.**

### The disagreement — who are those upon *al-Aʿrāf*?

**This is a more detailed disagreement, and at least five views are transmitted in the classical commentaries. I give them in a table and I DO NOT IMPOSE A PREFERENCE.**

| # | View | Ground (from the text) | Its difficulty |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Those whose good and evil come out equal** | *Lam yadkhulūhā wa hum yaṭmaʿūn* — they have not yet entered but they hope; their fear in 47 of being with the wrongdoers | The word "equality" (*istiwāʾ*) is not in the text; a link has to be made with the weighing in verses 8-9 |
| 2 | **Prophets, martyrs, or persons of high rank** | *Yaʿrifūna kullan bi-sīmāhum* — that they recognise everyone; their addressing the proud in 48 | On this view the sentence *lam yadkhulūhā wa hum yaṭmaʿūn* must be read not of "the people of *al-Aʿrāf*" but of the people of the Garden |
| 3 | **Angels** | That they recognise everyone; that they can address both sides | The word *rijāl* (men); and that a waiting (*yaṭmaʿūn*) is stated in 46 |
| 4 | **A group held back, on whom judgement has not yet been passed** | *Lam yadkhulūhā wa hum yaṭmaʿūn*; their being told *udkhulu'l-janna* in 49 | Being held back is not named as a verdict in the text |
| 5 | **A company on whom judgement has been passed but whose station is separate** | *ʿAla'l-aʿrāf* — that they stand in a place apart | The text does not say the reason for this separateness |

**And two separate grammatical questions feed the disagreement. Both must be recorded:**

**One — who is the subject of the sentence *lam yadkhulūhā wa hum yaṭmaʿūn*?**

| Reading | Subject | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| A | **The people of *al-Aʿrāf*** | "They (the people of al-Aʿrāf) have not entered the Garden, but they hope to" |
| B | **The people of the Garden** (named in 44) — the sentence, as a circumstantial clause, looks to the state before the words of the people of *al-Aʿrāf* | "While they (the people of the Garden) had not yet entered, the people of *al-Aʿrāf* greeted them" |

**The second reading is less widespread but has been voiced by the lexicographers.** **I do not impose a preference.**

**Two — who speaks the command *udkhulu'l-janna* in the forty-ninth verse, and to whom?**

| Reading | Speaker | Addressee |
|---|---|---|
| A | **The people of *al-Aʿrāf*** | **To the believers who had been made weak** — after addressing the proud in 48 |
| B | **God / a crier** | **To the people of *al-Aʿrāf*** — that is, their waiting comes to an end |

**Both readings are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.** **The second reading is the ground of the fourth view (those held back).**

**What I must record is this, and I write it plainly:** **the Qurʾān does not say who the people of *al-Aʿrāf* are.** **All the views above are readings drawn from the text.** **I make no preference, I invent no report, and I give no personal names** (`STYLE.md`).

### سِيمَا — the root

**The lexicographers disagree about the root of the word *sīmā*, and this was recorded at 48/29 in `048-fetih.md`:** **و-س-م** (to brand; *wasm*, *sima*) **or** **س-و-م**. **I rely on that treatment and issue no settled ruling on the root.**

**The word was treated twice in the index, and I rely on both:**

| Place | The note |
|---|---|
| `002-bakara.md` **2/273** | *Taʿrifuhum bi-sīmāhum* — "recognising them requires not an investigation but an **attentiveness**"; **the bearers of the mark are not aware that they carry it** |
| `048-fetih.md` **48/29** | A four-line table of the disagreement (light/gravity, humility, a physical trace, a sign of the resurrection), with no preference; ***sīmā* is what another reads, not what its bearer displays** |

**And at 55/41 in `055-rahman.md` the other direction of the word was recorded: *yuʿrafu'l-mujrimūna bi-sīmāhum* — the guilty too are recognised by their marks.** **That is, the word works in both directions.**

**What belongs here is this: al-Aʿrāf 7/46 and 7/48 are the only place where the word is used in both directions at once.**

| Verse | Sentence | Who is recognised |
|---|---|---|
| **7/46** | *Yaʿrifūna **kullan** bi-sīmāhum* | ***Everyone*** — both sides at once |
| **7/48** | *Yaʿrifūnahum bi-sīmāhum* | **Particular persons** — the proud |

**The *kullan* of the forty-sixth verse is indefinite and means "each one": both the people of the Garden and the people of the Fire.**

**I record this as my own reading and it is not binding; its ground is the word *kullan*:** **the distinguishing attribute of the people of al-Aʿrāf is that they can recognise both sides at once.** **And this accords with the picture of the place where they stand (a height):** one who stands on a height sees both flanks. **This is an explanation, not a ruling.**

### وَهُمْ يَطْمَعُونَ

The root **ط-م-ع**: **the desire to obtain a thing, hope.** **The word will occur again in the fifty-sixth verse in the form *ṭamaʿan*** (*wa'dʿūhu khawfan wa ṭamaʿā*).

| Verse | Expression | Who |
|---|---|---|
| **7/46** | *Wa hum **yaṭmaʿūn*** | **The people of *al-Aʿrāf*** |
| **7/56** | *Wa'dʿūhu khawfan wa **ṭamaʿā*** | **The addressee** |

**And there is an overlap between the two verses: in the forty-seventh verse, when the eyes of the people of al-Aʿrāf are turned towards the people of the Fire, they utter a sentence of fear** (*rabbanā lā tajʿalnā maʿa'l-qawmi'ẓ-ẓālimīn*), **and in the forty-sixth, while they look towards the Garden, they carry a hope** (*yaṭmaʿūn*).

**That is, verses forty-six and forty-seven together describe the state of *khawf* and *ṭamaʿ* — and the fifty-sixth verse gives those same two words as a command.**

**I record this as my own reading and it is not binding; its ground is the words of the two verses.** **The pair *khawfan wa ṭamaʿan* was treated at 30/24 in `030-rum.md` and at 32/16 in `032-secde.md`;** those treatments will be relied on at 7/56.

### وَإِذَا صُرِفَتْ أَبْصَٰرُهُمْ — the passive verb

**A point of word order must be recorded: the verb is passive — *ṣurifat* ("were turned"), not *ṣarafū* ("they turned").**

The root **ص-ر-ف**: to turn a thing from one direction to another. **And the same root will occur in the hundred and forty-sixth verse: *sa-aṣrifu ʿan āyātiya'lladhīna yatakabbarūn*.**

**I record this as an observation:** the gaze turns towards the people of the Fire not by their own will but by being turned — **and what they see makes them utter a prayer.** **The text does not say the subject of the turning.**

---

## 7/50-51 — وَنَادَىٰٓ أَصْحَٰبُ ٱلنَّارِ أَصْحَٰبَ ٱلْجَنَّةِ أَنْ أَفِيضُوا۟ عَلَيْنَا مِنَ ٱلْمَآءِ أَوْ مِمَّا رَزَقَكُمُ ٱللَّهُ قَالُوٓا۟ إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ حَرَّمَهُمَا عَلَى ٱلْكَٰفِرِينَ · ٱلَّذِينَ ٱتَّخَذُوا۟ دِينَهُمْ لَهْوًا وَلَعِبًا وَغَرَّتْهُمُ ٱلْحَيَوٰةُ ٱلدُّنْيَا فَٱلْيَوْمَ نَنسَىٰهُمْ كَمَا نَسُوا۟ لِقَآءَ يَوْمِهِمْ هَٰذَا

*Wa nādā aṣḥābu'n-nāri aṣḥāba'l-jannati an afīḍū ʿalaynā mina'l-māʾi aw mimmā razaqakumu'llāh, qālū inna'llāha ḥarramahumā ʿala'l-kāfirīn · Alladhīna'ttakhadhū dīnahum lahwan wa laʿiban wa gharrathumu'l-ḥayātu'd-dunyā, fa'l-yawma nansāhum kamā nasū liqāʾa yawmihim hādhā wa mā kānū bi-āyātinā yajḥadūn*

"**The people of the Fire call out to the people of the Garden: 'Pour down on us some water, or some of what God has provided you.' They say: 'God has forbidden both to the deniers.' · Those who took their religion as a diversion and a game, and whom the life of this world deluded. So today We forget them — just as they forgot the meeting of this day of theirs, and as they used knowingly to deny Our signs.**"

### The fourth call — a count of the scene

**In this section *nidāʾ* (calling out) occurs four times, and all four come from a different direction. This is a countable datum:**

| # | Verse | Who calls | Addressee | What is said |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **44** | **The people of the Garden** | **The people of the Fire** | *Qad wajadnā mā waʿadanā rabbunā ḥaqqā* — **a question of confirmation** |
| 2 | **46** | **The people of *al-Aʿrāf*** | **The people of the Garden** | *Salāmun ʿalaykum* — **a greeting** |
| 3 | **48** | **The people of *al-Aʿrāf*** | **Those who were proud** | *Mā aghnā ʿankum jamʿukum* — **a question of reckoning** |
| 4 | **50** | **The people of the Fire** | **The people of the Garden** | *Afīḍū ʿalaynā* — **a request** |

**Four calls, three different parties.** **And the direction of the sequence is worth recording: the first three come from above downwards or from the side, the fourth from below upwards.**

**I record this as my own reading and it is not binding; its ground is the subjects of the four verses:** through the section the speech is not **one-directional**. **But one thing never changes:** in no call does the addressee give the caller what he asks. **In the forty-fourth verse the answer is "yes", but a curse follows it; in the fiftieth the answer is a flat refusal.**

### أَفِيضُوا۟ — the picture in the root

The root **ف-ي-ض**: **a vessel filling and overflowing, the flowing of what overflows.** *Fāḍa'l-māʾu* — the water overflowed. *Afāḍa* (Form IV) — to make overflow, to make flow, to pour.

**The choice of the word is worth recording, and this is an observation about word order:** what is asked for is not "give us" (*aʿṭūnā*) but **"pour down on us"**. **That is, the request rests on the assumption that what is to be given is *abundant*** — a portion is asked of something that is overflowing.

**And the same root is used in the Qurʾān in the passage on the pilgrimage too** (*thumma afīḍū min ḥaythu afāḍa'n-nās* — al-Baqara 2/199) — **there the pouring of people from a place is described.** **The picture in the root is the same in both: a mass in flow.**

### إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ حَرَّمَهُمَا عَلَى ٱلْكَٰفِرِينَ

**The construction of the answer must be recorded: the people of the Garden do not say "we will not give".** **They are not the subject of the sentence:** *inna'llāha ḥarramahumā* — **the one who has forbidden is God.**

**I record this as an observation about word order:** the refusal is given **not as a personal stance but as the announcement of a ruling.** **And this accords with the forty-third verse:** there the *ghill* (rancour) had been pulled out of the breasts of the people of the Garden. **In the mouth of a company from whom rancour has been removed, a refusal can only be built this way.**

**And the pronoun *humā* (the two of them) must be recorded:** two things were asked for — **water** and **provision**; and the refusal covers both at once.

### لَهْوًا وَلَعِبًا — what was done with the religion

The root **ل-ه-و**: **to be diverted, to be occupied with what keeps one from the real business.** The root **ل-ع-ب**: **play, an unserious occupation.**

**The lexicographers give the difference between the two words thus:** *laʿib* is **an occupation aimless in itself**, while *lahw* is **what keeps a person from his real business**. **That is, one looks to the act itself, the other to its outcome.**

**And the word order must be recorded: *ittakhadhū dīnahum lahwan wa laʿibā* — what is abandoned is not the religion; *the religion itself has been turned into a game.***

| What could have been said | What is said |
|---|---|
| *Tarakū dīnahum* — they abandoned their religion | ***Ittakhadhū dīnahum lahwan wa laʿibā*** — they took their religion as a diversion and a game |

**I record this as my own reading and it is not binding; its ground is the object of the verb:** the verse describes not the **abandonment** of religion but its **being kept in hand and emptied of content.** **The religion stands where it was; its function has changed.**

**And this is the same pattern recorded in many places in `002-bakara.md`:** the form is preserved, the function is emptied. **I rely on those treatments.**

**The same phrase can be compared once more with the passage on *zīna* treated at the thirty-first verse:** there, what had been made lawful was held forbidden; **here, what is serious is turned into a game.** **Two errors, in opposite directions.**

### فَٱلْيَوْمَ نَنسَىٰهُمْ كَمَا نَسُوا۟ — requital, and a note of *tanzīh*

**The sentence is built with *kamā*: the requital is named in the language of the offence itself.**

**And in ascribing the verb to God a note of *tanzīh* is needed. This note was entered at 9/67 in `009-tevbe.md` (*nasu'llāha fa-nasiyahum*), and I rely on it:** the Qurʾān itself says *wa mā kāna rabbuka nasiyyā* (Maryam 19/64). **For this reason *nansāhum* here is explained, by the shared note of the lexicographers and the exegetes, by the second sense of the root — to leave aside, not to attend to, not to take into account.** **I do not repeat it here.**

**What belongs here is the name of what was forgotten:** *liqāʾa yawmihim hādhā* — **"the meeting of this day of theirs".** **That is, what was forgotten is not a piece of information but an appointment.**

---

## 7/52-53 — وَلَقَدْ جِئْنَٰهُم بِكِتَٰبٍ فَصَّلْنَٰهُ عَلَىٰ عِلْمٍ هُدًى وَرَحْمَةً لِّقَوْمٍ يُؤْمِنُونَ · هَلْ يَنظُرُونَ إِلَّا تَأْوِيلَهُۥ يَوْمَ يَأْتِى تَأْوِيلُهُۥ يَقُولُ ٱلَّذِينَ نَسُوهُ مِن قَبْلُ قَدْ جَآءَتْ رُسُلُ رَبِّنَا بِٱلْحَقِّ فَهَل لَّنَا مِن شُفَعَآءَ فَيَشْفَعُوا۟ لَنَآ أَوْ نُرَدُّ فَنَعْمَلَ غَيْرَ ٱلَّذِى كُنَّا نَعْمَلُ

*Wa laqad jiʾnāhum bi-kitābin faṣṣalnāhu ʿalā ʿilmin hudan wa raḥmatan li-qawmin yuʾminūn · Hal yanẓurūna illā taʾwīlah, yawma yaʾtī taʾwīluhū yaqūlu'lladhīna nasūhu min qablu qad jāʾat rusulu rabbinā bi'l-ḥaqq, fa-hal lanā min shufaʿāʾa fa-yashfaʿū lanā aw nuraddu fa-naʿmala ghayra'lladhī kunnā naʿmal, qad khasirū anfusahum wa ḍalla ʿanhum mā kānū yaftarūn*

"**We brought them a Book which We set out in detail upon knowledge — a guidance and a mercy for a people who believe. · Are they waiting for anything but its outcome? On the day its outcome comes, those who forgot it before will say: 'The messengers of our Lord did come with the truth. Have we now any intercessors to intercede for us? Or could we be sent back, that we might do other than what we used to do?' They have lost themselves, and what they used to invent has gone astray from them.**"

### فَصَّلْنَٰهُ عَلَىٰ عِلْمٍ

The root **ف-ص-ل**: **to separate two things from one another; to set a division.** *Faṣl* — separating. **Form II (*faṣṣala*) states multiplicity: to separate piece by piece, to set out in detail.**

**And the phrase is a pattern repeated in al-Aʿrāf. It is a countable datum:**

| Verse | Expression |
|---|---|
| **32** | *Kadhālika **nufaṣṣilu**'l-āyāti li-qawmin yaʿlamūn* |
| **52** | *Bi-kitābin **faṣṣalnāhu** ʿalā ʿilm* |
| **145** | *Wa katabnā lahū fi'l-alwāḥi min kulli shayʾin mawʿiẓatan wa **tafṣīlan** li-kulli shayʾ* |
| **174** | *Wa kadhālika **nufaṣṣilu**'l-āyāti wa laʿallahum yarjiʿūn* |

**Three of the four occurrences are for this Book, one for the tablets given to Moses.** **That is, the sūra ascribes the same verb to two Books.** **I record this as an observation.**

**عَلَىٰ عِلْمٍ** — "upon a knowledge". **The particle *ʿalā*: the ground on which the work of setting out in detail rests is stated.** **And this is the same word as *fa-la-naquṣṣanna ʿalayhim bi-ʿilmin* in the sixth and seventh verses. The sūra ties both the recounting and the setting out in detail to the same word.**

### تَأْوِيل — the root of the word

The root **أ-و-ل**: **the place to which a thing returns, the end it reaches.** *Āla ilayh* — it returned to it, it came to it. ***Taʾwīl* is *the place where a word or an event comes out in the end*** — that is, the announcement in its realised form.

**This differs from the sense "interpretation" that the word acquired later, and here the verse itself says which it means:** *yawma **yaʾtī** taʾwīluh* — **the *taʾwīl* "comes".** **What comes is not an interpretation but an event.**

**I give this as a note about the word, and its ground is the verb itself.**

### فَهَل لَّنَا مِن شُفَعَآءَ … أَوْ نُرَدُّ

**There are two requests in the sentence, and both are joined by *aw* (or):**

| # | Request | What is asked |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *Hal lanā min shufaʿāʾa fa-yashfaʿū lanā* | **An intermediary** — that the sentence be lightened from outside |
| 2 | *Aw nuraddu fa-naʿmala ghayra'lladhī kunnā naʿmal* | **A return** — that the work be done again from the start |

**And the order of the two requests is worth recording: first the intermediary, then their own hand.** **I record this as my own reading and it is not binding; its ground is the order:** what comes to mind first is not putting right what one did oneself, but **someone's stepping in between.**

**And *nuraddu* is passive: "if we were sent back".** **They do not speak of returning by their own power.**

**This second request is recorded elsewhere in the Qurʾān as well, and each time it goes unanswered** (al-Anʿām 6/27-28; al-Muʾminūn 23/99-100 — treated in `023-muminun.md`). **I rely on those treatments.**

---

## 7/54 — إِنَّ رَبَّكُمُ ٱللَّهُ ٱلَّذِى خَلَقَ ٱلسَّمَٰوَٰتِ وَٱلْأَرْضَ فِى سِتَّةِ أَيَّامٍ ثُمَّ ٱسْتَوَىٰ عَلَى ٱلْعَرْشِ يُغْشِى ٱلَّيْلَ ٱلنَّهَارَ يَطْلُبُهُۥ حَثِيثًا وَٱلشَّمْسَ وَٱلْقَمَرَ وَٱلنُّجُومَ مُسَخَّرَٰتٍۭ بِأَمْرِهِۦٓ أَلَا لَهُ ٱلْخَلْقُ وَٱلْأَمْرُ تَبَارَكَ ٱللَّهُ رَبُّ ٱلْعَٰلَمِينَ

*Inna rabbakumu'llāhu'lladhī khalaqa's-samāwāti wa'l-arḍa fī sittati ayyāmin thumma'stawā ʿala'l-ʿarsh, yughshi'l-layla'n-nahāra yaṭlubuhū ḥathīthā, wa'sh-shamsa wa'l-qamara wa'n-nujūma musakhkharātin bi-amrih, alā lahu'l-khalqu wa'l-amr, tabāraka'llāhu rabbu'l-ʿālamīn*

"**Your Lord is God, who created the heavens and the earth in six days, then mounted the Throne. He covers the day with the night — the night pursues it swiftly. And the sun, the moon and the stars are made subservient by His command. His alone are the creating and the command. Blessed is God, the Lord of all the worlds!**"

### The place of the verse in the sūra

**This verse opens the fifth block of the sūra (54-58), and what opens the block is a *change of address*.** **Up to the fifty-third verse the scene was in the hereafter; the fifty-fourth turns directly to the addressee:** *inna **rabbakum**.*

**I record this as an observation about word order:** the scene of the hereafter closes with *ḍalla ʿanhum mā kānū yaftarūn* — **what was invented disappears.** **Immediately afterwards comes the description of the real Lord.** **The place that has been emptied is filled.**

### فِى سِتَّةِ أَيَّامٍ — and the keeping of the limit

**This expression was treated in the index at 50/38 in `050-kaf.md` and at 41/9-12 in `041-fussilet.md`. I rely on those treatments and keep both notes here too:**

**One — the note at 50/38 in `050-kaf.md`:** the word **ي-و-م** in Arabic does not mean only "a twenty-four-hour day"; the primary sense of the root is **a stretch of time, a period.** **How long the six "days" lasted is not stated and is not known.**

**Two — the note at 41/9-12 in `041-fussilet.md`:** **modern cosmology is not dressed onto these verses;** the days are not equated with geological ages. **Such an equation would be to make the text say what it does not say** (`STYLE.md`).

**These two limits hold here as well, and I do not repeat them.**

**What belongs here is what follows the expression, and this differs in the three sūras:**

| Place | What comes after *sittati ayyām* |
|---|---|
| `050-kaf.md` **50/38** | *Wa mā massanā min lughūb* — **the negation of weariness** |
| `041-fussilet.md` **41/9-12** | **A detailed accounting of the days** and *dhālika taqdīru'l-ʿazīzi'l-ʿalīm* |
| **al-Aʿrāf 7/54** | ***Istiwāʾ*, and after it the scene of night and day** |

**Three sūras tie the same expression to three different places: one to power, one to measure, one to governance.** **I record this as an observation.**

### ٱسْتَوَىٰ عَلَى ٱلْعَرْشِ — a note of *tanzīh*, without opening the debate

**Per the method (`STYLE.md`) I enter a note about this phrase, and I do not open the debate.**

**At 40/7 in `040-gafir.md` the note kept consistently throughout the index for expressions concerning the Throne was entered, and I rely on it:** **these expressions cannot be understood so as to ascribe place, body or movement to God.** **Two chief attitudes are transmitted in the classical commentaries for verses of this kind:**

| Attitude | What is done |
|---|---|
| ***Tafwīḍ*** | The wording is accepted as it stands, **and its modality is referred to God** |
| ***Taʾwīl*** | It is interpreted in the sense of **sovereignty and disposal** |

**I convey both and impose no preference.** **The same note was entered at 32/4 in `032-secde.md` and at 20/5 in `020-taha.md`; the shared principle is al-Shūrā 42/11.** **I do not repeat it, and I enter into no polemic of *kalām*** (`STYLE.md`).

**What belongs here is only a datum of the text, and it was counted at 20/5 in `020-taha.md`: the phrase occurs in seven places in the Qurʾān** — **al-Aʿrāf 7/54**, Yūnus 10/3, al-Raʿd 13/2, Ṭāhā 20/5, al-Furqān 25/59, al-Sajda 32/4, al-Ḥadīd 57/4. **This verse is the first of those seven.**

### يُغْشِى ٱلَّيْلَ ٱلنَّهَارَ يَطْلُبُهُۥ حَثِيثًا — the third picture

**The Qurʾān tells of night and day following one another with several different pictures. Two of them were treated in the index; this is the third, and I compare the three in a table:**

| Place | Verb | Root | Picture |
|---|---|---|---|
| `031-lokman.md` **31/29** (and al-Ḥadīd 57/6) | *Yūliju'l-layla fi'n-nahāri wa yūliju'n-nahāra fi'l-layl* | **و-ل-ج** | **To pass one inside the other** — each is thrust into the other |
| `039-zumer.md` **39/5** | *Yukawwiru'l-layla ʿala'n-nahāri wa yukawwiru'n-nahāra ʿala'l-layl* | **ك-و-ر** | **To wind over** — as a turban is wound, each over the other |
| **al-Aʿrāf 7/54** | *Yughshi'l-layla'n-nahāra **yaṭlubuhū ḥathīthā*** | **غ-ش-و** + **ط-ل-ب** | **To cover and to pursue** — one covers the other over, running after it |

**The concrete senses of the three roots must be recorded separately:**

- **و-ل-ج** — to enter through a narrow place. *Wulūj* — entry. **Picture: passing one within the other.**
- **ك-و-ر** — to wind a thing by folding. *Kawr* — the winding of a turban. **Picture: winding over.**
- **غ-ش-و** — to cover a thing over, to overspread. *Ghishāwa* — the film that comes over the eye (it occurred at 45/23 in `045-casiye.md`). **Picture: closing over the top.**
- **ط-ل-ب** — to seek, to look for, to go after. **Picture: coming from behind.**

**حَثِيثًا** — the root **ح-ث-ث**: **to urge on, to go continuously and fast.** *Ḥaththa* — he drove without stopping, he urged on. ***Ḥathīth* means "without any halt and fast".** **The word in this pattern occurs only here in the Qurʾān; this is a countable datum.**

**And the difference of the three pictures is worth recording. I give this as my own reading and it is not binding; its ground is the concrete senses of the three roots:**

| | Luqmān 31/29 | al-Zumar 39/5 | **al-Aʿrāf 7/54** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Kind of relation** | **Interpenetration** | **Winding** | **Pursuit** |
| **Is it reciprocal** | **Yes** — two-directional | **Yes** — two-directional | **No** — one-directional |
| **Is speed stated** | No | No | **Yes** — *ḥathīthā* |

**The third row is what belongs to al-Aʿrāf alone: only here is there a note of *speed*, and only here is the movement one-directional.**

**And this accords with the rest of the sūra — I give this as a reading:** al-Aʿrāf is the sūra that tells of something that comes in haste. **In the fourth verse the destruction had come *bayātan aw hum qāʾilūn* (by night or in the noon sleep); in the thirty-fourth the term was not delayed by even an hour.** **The *ḥathīthā* of the fifty-fourth verse is the picture of the same line on the scale of the universe.**

**On the connection with natural science I draw the limit:** no conclusion about planetary motion, rotational speed or the terminator line is to be drawn from this. **The verse builds the picture of an observation: on the horizon, darkness coming swiftly after light.** **This is a fact everyone sees with his own eyes, and it is what the verse uses** (`STYLE.md`).

### مُسَخَّرَٰتٍۭ بِأَمْرِهِۦ and أَلَا لَهُ ٱلْخَلْقُ وَٱلْأَمْرُ

The root **س-خ-ر**: **to make a thing subservient to a work, to set it to service.**

And the closing of the verse must be recorded: *alā lahu'l-**khalqu** wa'l-**amr***. **Two words are set side by side, and both had occurred within the verse:**

| Word | Where it occurred in the verse |
|---|---|
| ***Khalq*** | *Khalaqa's-samāwāti wa'l-arḍ* — **at the beginning** |
| ***Amr*** | *Musakhkharātin bi-**amrih*** — **at the end** |

**That is, the closing sentence gathers the verse's own two halves.** **I record this as an observation about word order.**

**And the lexicographers give the difference of the two words thus:** *khalq* is **to bring into being**, *amr* is **to govern what has been brought into being.** **The sentence ties both at once to the same place** — **and this is the ground of what will be repeated in the later sections of the sūra: the one who creates is the one who lays down the ruling** (see 7/32, 7/33, 7/70).

**تَبَارَكَ** — the root **ب-ر-ك**: **a thing's being fixed and lasting; and from this, blessing, increasing good.** **The pattern is Form VI, and the lexicographers give here the sense "the fixedness of loftiness and abundance".** **The word will occur once more in this sūra, at the ninety-sixth verse, in the form *barakāt*.**

---

## 7/55-56 — ٱدْعُوا۟ رَبَّكُمْ تَضَرُّعًا وَخُفْيَةً إِنَّهُۥ لَا يُحِبُّ ٱلْمُعْتَدِينَ · وَلَا تُفْسِدُوا۟ فِى ٱلْأَرْضِ بَعْدَ إِصْلَٰحِهَا وَٱدْعُوهُ خَوْفًا وَطَمَعًا إِنَّ رَحْمَتَ ٱللَّهِ قَرِيبٌ مِّنَ ٱلْمُحْسِنِينَ

*Udʿū rabbakum taḍarruʿan wa khufya, innahū lā yuḥibbu'l-muʿtadīn · Wa lā tufsidū fi'l-arḍi baʿda iṣlāḥihā wa'dʿūhu khawfan wa ṭamaʿā, inna raḥmata'llāhi qarībun mina'l-muḥsinīn*

"**Call upon your Lord in humility and in secret. He does not love those who exceed the bounds. · Do not work corruption on the earth after it has been set in order. Call upon Him in fear and in hope. The mercy of God is near to those who do good.**"

### The place of the passage on supplication

**The fifty-fourth verse had stated who the Lord is; the fifty-fifth says how He is to be called upon.** **The order is worth recording: first the description, then the manner of address.**

### تَضَرُّعًا وَخُفْيَةً

The root **ض-ر-ع**: **weakness, submission, being brought low.** *Ḍariʿ* — weak, without strength. **Form V (*taḍarruʿ*) states a person's putting himself into that condition: to lower oneself, to plead.**

The root **خ-ف-ي**: **to hide, to conceal.** *Khufya* — secrecy.

**The relation of the two words must be recorded: one is a *bearing*, the other a *level of voice*.**

| Word | What it looks to |
|---|---|
| ***Taḍarruʿan*** | **The inward state** — holding oneself small |
| ***Khufyatan*** | **The outward state** — the voice not being heard |

**And this pair will overlap with one of the last verses of the sūra. This is a repetition that can be verified within the sūra:**

| Verse | Expression |
|---|---|
| **7/55** | *Udʿū rabbakum **taḍarruʿan wa khufyatan*** |
| **7/205** | *Wa'dhkur rabbaka fī nafsika **taḍarruʿan wa khīfatan** wa dūna'l-jahri mina'l-qawl* |

**In both verses the same word (*taḍarruʿ*), and in both a note about the voice.** **The difference: at 55 *khufya* (secrecy), at 205 *khīfa* (fear).** **That is, where the sūra opens the command to pray and where it closes it, it presses on the same two axes: lowering oneself, and voice.** **I record this as my own reading; its ground is the words of the two verses.**

### إِنَّهُۥ لَا يُحِبُّ ٱلْمُعْتَدِينَ — the place the sentence attaches to

The root **ع-د-و**: **to exceed the bound, to cross the limit.** *Iʿtidāʾ* — Form VIII.

**And a question of word order must be recorded: exceeding the bound in what does the sentence forbid?** **Two readings are transmitted in the classical commentaries, and I give the disagreement in a table:**

| Reading | Where the *iʿtidāʾ* is | Ground |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **In supplication** — exceeding the bound while praying (shouting, asking for what cannot be, demanding what is not one's own) | That the sentence comes immediately after the supplication; that the qualification *taḍarruʿan wa khufyatan* is setting a measure |
| 2 | **In general** — exceeding the bound in every field | That the sentence is left without an object; that the word *al-muʿtadīn* comes unrestricted |

**Both readings are transmitted. I do not impose a preference.** **And the two do not exclude each other: the next verse already moves out of supplication and on to the earth.**

### وَلَا تُفْسِدُوا۟ فِى ٱلْأَرْضِ بَعْدَ إِصْلَٰحِهَا

**This sentence occurs twice in the sūra, in word-for-word identical form** — **56 and 85** — **and its second occurrence is in the mouth of Shuʿayb.** **This repetition was tabulated in the section on the structure of the sūra; I rely on that treatment and do not repeat it here.**

**What belongs here is the link built between *supplication* and *corruption*:**

| Verse | Command | Field |
|---|---|---|
| **55** | *Udʿū rabbakum* | **Vertical** — the servant towards his Lord |
| **56a** | *Wa lā tufsidū fi'l-arḍ* | **Horizontal** — the servant towards the earth |
| **56b** | *Wa'dʿūhu khawfan wa ṭamaʿā* | **Vertical** — supplication again |

**That is, the command about the earth has been placed between two commands about supplication.** **I record this as an observation about word order, and its ground is the order of the three sentences.**

**بَعْدَ إِصْلَٰحِهَا** — "after it has been set in order". **What must be recorded is this: the sentence presupposes a *prior condition*.** **The earth is named not as a neutral ground but as a ground that has been set right.** **Corruption is described not as adding something to a void but as breaking an order that exists.**

**Who set it right is not stated**, and several explanations are transmitted in the classical commentaries for this gap (the order in creation; the order the messengers brought; both at once). **The text does not name it; nor do I make a preference.**

### خَوْفًا وَطَمَعًا — the third note in the index

**This pair was treated in the index, and I rely on those treatments:**

| Place | Expression | Whose state |
|---|---|---|
| `030-rum.md` **30/24** | *Yurīkumu'l-barqa **khawfan wa ṭamaʿā*** | **The state of one who looks at lightning** — fear of the bolt, hope of rain |
| `013-rad.md` **13/12** | *Yurīkumu'l-barqa **khawfan wa ṭamaʿā*** | **The same scene** — treated on the basis of `030-rum.md` |
| `032-secde.md` **32/16** | *Yadʿūna rabbahum **khawfan wa ṭamaʿā*** | **The state of one who prays** |
| **al-Aʿrāf 7/56** | *Wa'dʿūhu **khawfan wa ṭamaʿā*** | **A command** — the one who prays is commanded |

**And what al-Aʿrāf adds must be recorded, and it is a datum of the text:** in al-Rūm and al-Raʿd the pair is **the description of an observation**, in al-Sajda **the attribute of a company**, and in al-Aʿrāf **a direct command.**

**That is, the same pair occurs in the index first as a fact, then as an attribute, and here as a demand.** **I record this as an observation.**

**And within the sūra this pair has a precedent — it was treated at the forty-sixth and forty-seventh verses:** the people of *al-Aʿrāf*, looking towards the Garden, were *yaṭmaʿūn* (hoping), and looking towards the Fire were saying *rabbanā lā tajʿalnā maʿa'l-qawmi'ẓ-ẓālimīn* (fearing). **The fifty-sixth verse turns the state described there into a command.** **This link was recorded at 46-47; here it closes.**

### إِنَّ رَحْمَتَ ٱللَّهِ قَرِيبٌ — a grammatical point

**A point of word order must be recorded, and the lexicographers dwell on it: *raḥma* is a feminine word, but its predicate comes as *qarībun* — masculine.** **The expected form would be *qarībatun*.**

**The lexicographers give several explanations, and I convey all of them as transmitted, imposing no preference:**

| # | Explanation |
|---|---|
| 1 | *Raḥma* here is **grammatically feminine only** (it states no real femininity); the predicate of such words may come masculine |
| 2 | *Qarīb* is used here **not as an adjective but as an adverbial** — in the sense "in a near place"; adverbials do not take the feminine |
| 3 | *Raḥma* stands in place of a masculine word of like meaning such as ***raḥm*** or ***faḍl*** |

**This is a question of case (*iʿrāb*) that does not change the meaning and generates no ruling; I record it for the sake of the analysis of the word.**

**And it must be recorded to whom the mercy is near: *mina'l-**muḥsinīn***.** **It does not say those who pray, but those who do good.** **I record this as my own reading and it is not binding; its ground is the word itself:** for two verses supplication has been described, **and at the closing the measure set is not supplication itself but conduct.**

---

## 7/57-58 — وَهُوَ ٱلَّذِى يُرْسِلُ ٱلرِّيَٰحَ بُشْرًۢا بَيْنَ يَدَىْ رَحْمَتِهِۦ حَتَّىٰٓ إِذَآ أَقَلَّتْ سَحَابًا ثِقَالًا سُقْنَٰهُ لِبَلَدٍ مَّيِّتٍ فَأَنزَلْنَا بِهِ ٱلْمَآءَ فَأَخْرَجْنَا بِهِۦ مِن كُلِّ ٱلثَّمَرَٰتِ كَذَٰلِكَ نُخْرِجُ ٱلْمَوْتَىٰ لَعَلَّكُمْ تَذَكَّرُونَ · وَٱلْبَلَدُ ٱلطَّيِّبُ يَخْرُجُ نَبَاتُهُۥ بِإِذْنِ رَبِّهِۦ وَٱلَّذِى خَبُثَ لَا يَخْرُجُ إِلَّا نَكِدًا كَذَٰلِكَ نُصَرِّفُ ٱلْءَايَٰتِ لِقَوْمٍ يَشْكُرُونَ

*Wa huwa'lladhī yursilu'r-riyāḥa bushran bayna yaday raḥmatih, ḥattā idhā aqallat saḥāban thiqālan suqnāhu li-baladin mayyitin fa-anzalnā bihi'l-māʾa fa-akhrajnā bihī min kulli'th-thamarāt, kadhālika nukhriju'l-mawtā laʿallakum tadhakkarūn · Wa'l-baladu'ṭ-ṭayyibu yakhruju nabātuhū bi-idhni rabbih, wa'lladhī khabutha lā yakhruju illā nakidā, kadhālika nuṣarrifu'l-āyāti li-qawmin yashkurūn*

"**It is He who sends the winds as bearers of good news before His mercy. Then, when they have taken up heavy clouds, We drive them to a dead land and send down water by them, and by it bring forth every kind of fruit. So too do We bring forth the dead — perhaps you will take it to heart. · The good land brings forth its plants by the leave of its Lord; but that which is bad brings forth nothing but a stunted growth. Thus do We turn the signs about for a people who give thanks.**"

### بُشْرًۢا بَيْنَ يَدَىْ رَحْمَتِهِۦ

**بَيْنَ يَدَىْ** — word for word "between his two hands"; in Arabic it is an idiom meaning **"before him, ahead of him"**. **The rain is named *raḥma* (mercy), and the wind is the *good news that goes before it*.**

**A note on the variant reading:** for the word *bushran* the readings *nushran* (scattering), *nushuran* and *nashran* are also transmitted. **The difference affects the meaning:** on the first reading the wind is a **bearer of good news**, on the others a **scatterer, a disperser**. **Both senses accord with the picture of the verse; I do not impose a preference.**

### حَتَّىٰٓ إِذَآ أَقَلَّتْ سَحَابًا ثِقَالًا — shifts of pronoun and person

**There are two shifts in the verse, and both must be recorded.**

**One — the person changes:** the sentence begins with *huwa'lladhī **yursilu*** (third person), then becomes ***suqnāhu … fa-anzalnā … fa-akhrajnā*** (first person plural). **This is the technique called *iltifāt* (a shift in the direction of address) in Arabic, and it was treated in many places in the index.**

**I record this as an observation about word order:** the shift comes exactly at the point where **the verb turns into direct intervention** — the wind "is sent", but of the cloud it is said "We drove it".

**Two — the number changes:** *saḥāban **thiqālan*** (heavy clouds, plural adjective) is said, and immediately after it ***suqnā-hu*** (it, singular). **The lexicographers explain this by the fact that the word *saḥāb* can count both as a collective noun and as a plural.**

**أَقَلَّتْ** — the root **ق-ل-ل**: **fewness**; in Form IV it gives the sense **to lift a thing, to take up a load.** **The lexicographers tie this sense to the picture of "finding a load light and lifting it".** **That is, the wind carries the heavy cloud as though it were a light load.**

**I give this as a note about the word, and its ground is the meeting of the root's two senses in the same pattern:** the verse sets *thiqālan* (heavy) and *aqallat* (lifted lightly) side by side. **This is a contrast that can be read from the text.**

### كَذَٰلِكَ نُخْرِجُ ٱلْمَوْتَىٰ — the form of the proof

**The verse passes from an observation to a conclusion, and the particle of transition is *kadhālika* (thus).**

**And that the verb is the same must be recorded: *fa-akhrajnā bihī min kulli'th-thamarāt* → *kadhālika nukhriju'l-mawtā*.** **The same verb, two objects.**

| Object | From where | Verb |
|---|---|---|
| *Min kulli'th-thamarāt* | **A dead land** | *Akhrajnā* |
| *Al-mawtā* | (not stated) | *Nukhriju* |

**I record this as an observation about word order:** the proof is built not by a likeness but **by repeating the same verb.** **And this connects with the twenty-fifth verse of the sūra:** *wa minhā **tukhrajūn***. **The same root, the same work.**

### The likeness of the two soils — and a comparison with al-Raʿd 13/4

**The fifty-eighth verse is one of the densest likenesses in the Qurʾān, and it must be read together with the observation treated at 13/4 in `013-rad.md`.**

**What was recorded at 13/4 in `013-rad.md` was this, and I rely on it:**

| What is shared | What differs |
|---|---|
| *Yusqā bi-māʾin **wāḥid*** — **one water** | *Nufaḍḍilu baʿḍahā ʿalā baʿḍin fi'l-ukul* — **the fruits** |

**The conclusion recorded there was this:** the same soil, the same water, even the same root — and yet a different fruit; **that is, the difference does not lie in the means that come from outside.**

**Now I set the two verses side by side, and the difference can be read from the text:**

| | `013-rad.md` **13/4** | **al-Aʿrāf 7/58** |
|---|---|---|
| **What is shared** | **Water** — *yusqā bi-māʾin wāḥid* | **Water** — the rain that comes down in verse 57 |
| **The variable** | **The plants** — *ṣinwānun wa ghayru ṣinwān* | **The soil** — *al-baladu'ṭ-ṭayyib* / *alladhī khabutha* |
| **Where the difference stands** | **Among those that grow in the same soil** | **Between two different soils** |
| **The closing sentence** | *Li-qawmin yaʿqilūn* | *Li-qawmin **yashkurūn*** |

**This is a difference that can be verified from the two texts, and I record the following as my own reading:** **al-Raʿd shows the difference *within the same ground*; al-Aʿrāf shows the difference *in the ground itself*.** **Read together, two separate questions are answered:** why those who receive the same means diverge (al-Raʿd), and why what is received does not yield the same result (al-Aʿrāf). **This is an explanation, not a ruling.**

### طَيِّب and خَبُثَ — two roots

The root **ط-ي-ب**: **to be pleasant, clean, good; and to be fertile.** *Baladun ṭayyib* — according to the lexicographers, **a land both pleasant and fertile.**

The root **خ-ب-ث**: **to be bad, foul, corrupt.** **The two roots regularly form an opposed pair in the Qurʾān.**

**And the pattern of the terms is worth recording, and this is a point of word order:**

| Side | Expression | Pattern |
|---|---|---|
| **The good one** | *Al-baladu'ṭ-**ṭayyib*** | **A noun (adjective)** — a fixed attribute |
| **The bad one** | *Wa'lladhī **khabutha*** | **A verb (perfect)** — a condition that came about |

**The expected construction would be *wa'l-baladu'l-khabīth*; the verse does not build it that way.**

**I record this as my own reading and it is not binding; its ground is the difference of pattern:** **the good soil is named by an adjective, the bad soil by a verb.** **An adjective states a condition, a verb states a *becoming*.** **In the second a process is intimated.** **The text does not explain this; the difference of pattern, however, is a countable datum.**

**And on the second side the word *balad* is not repeated:** *wa'lladhī khabutha* — **"that which is bad".** **The naming is left incomplete.**

### نَكِدًا

The root **ن-ك-د**: **unfruitfulness, giving with difficulty and little.** The lexicographers explain the word as **"giving with hardship, in small amounts and grudgingly"**; *biʾrun nakidun* — a well whose water comes up with difficulty.

**The word occurs only here in the Qurʾān. This is a countable datum.**

**And there is a nicety to be recorded: the verse does not say "nothing comes forth".** *Lā yakhruju **illā** nakidā* — **with the particle of exception:** something does come forth, but it is **stunted.**

**I record this as an observation about word order:** in the likeness the second side is not drawn as **wholly barren.** **There is an output; its measure is low.**

### بِإِذْنِ رَبِّهِۦ — the position of the qualification

**A point of word order must be recorded: the qualification *bi-idhni rabbih* is placed only on the first side.** **On the second side there is no such qualification.**

**The text does not say the reason for this.** **Several explanations are transmitted in the classical commentaries** (that the qualification is placed on the good side to state that what comes forth is not of itself; or that a single qualification looks to both sides at once). **I impose no preference and build no ruling;** **that the qualification stands on only one side is a datum that can be read from the text, and I write that much.**

### كَذَٰلِكَ نُصَرِّفُ ٱلْءَايَٰتِ

The root **ص-ر-ف** had occurred at the forty-seventh verse (*ṣurifat abṣāruhum*); **here it comes in Form II, and as was recorded at 46/27 in `046-ahkaf.md`, it states repetition and variation:** the telling of the same thing by different routes and from different faces. **I rely on that treatment.**

**And the root's three occurrences in the sūra can be gathered in a table:**

| Verse | Expression | Who / what is turned |
|---|---|---|
| **47** | *Idhā **ṣurifat** abṣāruhum* | **Gazes** — passive |
| **58** | *Kadhālika **nuṣarrifu**'l-āyāt* | **The signs** — variation |
| **146** | *Sa-**aṣrifu** ʿan āyātiya'lladhīna yatakabbarūn* | **The proud** — being turned away from the signs |

**One root, three different works.** **This is a distribution that can be verified within the sūra.**

**And the closing of the fifty-eighth verse is *li-qawmin yashkurūn*.** **The tenth verse had ended with *qalīlan mā tashkurūn*.** **The sūra comes from where it stated the littleness of thanks to naming a people who give thanks.** **I record this as an observation.**

---

## 7/59-93 — The sequence of prophets: al-Aʿrāf's own pattern

**The sixth block of the sūra (59-102) tells of five prophets one after another: Noah, Hūd, Ṣāliḥ, Lot, Shuʿayb.** **The same five names occur in sequence in other sūras of the Qurʾān too, and two tables of pattern were drawn up in the index.** **I rely on those treatments and do not repeat them; I work out al-Aʿrāf's own pattern separately.**

### The two tables I rely on

| Source | What was recorded there |
|---|---|
| `026-suara.md` — **"The backbone of the sūra: seven stories, four patterns"** | **A four-part frame:** the announcement of denial (*kadhdhabat … al-mursalīn*), the opening (*idh qāla lahum akhūhum … alā tattaqūn*), the threefold entry (*innī lakum rasūlun amīn · fa'ttaqu'llāha wa aṭīʿūn · wa mā asʾalukum ʿalayhi min ajr*), the closing seal (*inna fī dhālika la-āyatan… · wa inna rabbaka la-huwa'l-ʿazīzu'r-raḥīm*). **Eight blocks, word for word the same seal.** |
| `011-hud.md` — **Table 3 and Table 4** | **No seal, but five scattered closing patterns** (*jāʾa amrunā*, *najjaynā*, *buʿdan li-*, *ka-an lam yaghnaw fīhā*, a closing of its own); **and the sūra distributes its own opening call (*istaghfirū rabbakum thumma tūbū ilayh*, 11/3) among the mouths of three prophets.** The address *yā qawmi* occurs sixteen times. |

**The difference between the three sūras can be gathered in a single sentence, and it is a countable datum:** **al-Shuʿarāʾ *likens* its stories to one another, Hūd *separates* them from one another, and al-Aʿrāf stands between the two** — **it has a shared opening sentence but no shared closing seal.**

### Al-Aʿrāf's own pattern — the opening

> **يَٰقَوْمِ ٱعْبُدُوا۟ ٱللَّهَ مَا لَكُم مِّنْ إِلَٰهٍ غَيْرُهُۥ**
>
> *Yā qawmi'budū'llāha mā lakum min ilāhin ghayruh*
>
> "O my people! Serve God; you have no god but Him."

**This sentence occurs four times in the sūra, in word-for-word identical form. This is the result of a count:**

| Prophet | Verse | What comes **before** the sentence | What comes **after** the sentence |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Noah** | **59** | *Laqad **arsalnā** Nūḥan ilā qawmihī fa-qāla* | *Innī akhāfu ʿalaykum ʿadhāba yawmin ʿaẓīm* — **a statement of fear** |
| **Hūd** | **65** | *Wa ilā ʿĀdin **akhāhum** Hūdā, qāla* | *A-fa-lā **tattaqūn*** — **a question** |
| **Ṣāliḥ** | **73** | *Wa ilā Thamūda **akhāhum** Ṣāliḥā, qāla* | *Qad jāʾatkum **bayyinatun** min rabbikum* — **a proof** |
| **Shuʿayb** | **85** | *Wa ilā Madyana **akhāhum** Shuʿaybā, qāla* | *Qad jāʾatkum **bayyinatun** min rabbikum* — **a proof** |
| **Lot** | **80** | *Wa Lūṭan **idh qāla li-qawmihī*** | ***No sentence*** — directly *a-taʾtūna'l-fāḥishata* |

**And four facts can be read straight off the table:**

**One — in the story of Lot there is no shared opening sentence.** **In four of the five stories *yā qawmi'budū'llāha mā lakum min ilāhin ghayruh* is present; in Lot's it is not.** **And as was recorded in the section on the structure of the sūra, the word *malaʾ* too is absent only from the story of Lot.** **Two absences gather in the same story; this is a countable datum.**

**Two — Noah's entry is built differently from the others.** **Only for Noah is the verb *laqad arsalnā* (We sent) used; for the others there is the pattern *wa ilā … akhāhum* (their brother), without a verb.** **That is, the first link of the sequence is built with a full sentence, the later ones by attachment.**

**Three — the qualification *akhāhum* (their brother) is present in three stories** (65, 73, 85), **and absent in Noah's and Lot's.** **In `026-suara.md` Table 2 the distribution of *akhūhum* in al-Shuʿarāʾ was worked out, and it was recorded there that it drops out in Shuʿayb's case, the reason being that the addressees are named by a *place*.** **In al-Aʿrāf the distribution is different: with Shuʿayb *akhāhum* is present, because al-Aʿrāf names the addressees as *Madyan*** — **at 26/176 in `026-suara.md` the addressees were *aṣḥābu'l-Ayka* (the people of a place).** **The two sūras name the addressees of the same prophet by two different names, and the qualification *akhūhum* changes accordingly.** **This is an overlap that can be verified from the two texts, and it supports the explanation given at 26/176 in `026-suara.md`.**

**Four — what follows the opening sentence is different in every story:** fear, question, proof, proof. **What is shared is the call; what changes is the ground placed behind the call.**

### Al-Aʿrāf's own pattern — the closing

**Al-Aʿrāf has no shared seal. The closings are scattered, and they can be tabulated:**

| Prophet | Closing | Pattern |
|---|---|---|
| **Noah** (64) | *Fa-kadhdhabūhu fa-**anjaynāhu** wa'lladhīna maʿahū fi'l-fulki wa **aghraqnā**'lladhīna kadhdhabū* | **Deliverance + drowning** |
| **Hūd** (72) | *Fa-**anjaynāhu** wa'lladhīna maʿahū bi-raḥmatin minnā wa **qaṭaʿnā dābira**'lladhīna kadhdhabū* | **Deliverance + cutting off the line** |
| **Ṣāliḥ** (78) | *Fa-**akhadhathumu'r-rajfatu** fa-aṣbaḥū fī dārihim jāthimīn* | **The quaking** |
| **Lot** (83-84) | *Fa-**anjaynāhu** wa ahlahū … wa **amṭarnā** ʿalayhim maṭarā* | **Deliverance + a raining down** |
| **Shuʿayb** (91) | *Fa-**akhadhathumu'r-rajfatu** fa-aṣbaḥū fī dārihim jāthimīn* | **The quaking** |

**And two data must be recorded:**

**One — the seventy-eighth and ninety-first verses are word for word identical.** **This repetition was counted in the section on the structure of the sūra; here it falls into place: al-Aʿrāf's only word-for-word repeated closing is between Thamūd and Madyan.**

**Two — in three stories *anjaynā* (We delivered) is present, in two it is absent** (Ṣāliḥ and Shuʿayb). **And in exactly those two stories the *rajfa* is present.** **That is, where deliverance is named the quaking is not, and where the quaking is named deliverance is not.** **I record this as the result of a count; the text does not say the reason, and I invent no reason.**

### A third shared element: *fa-tawallā ʿanhum wa qāla*

**Two stories end with the same sentence, and both are in a prophet's mouth:**

| Verse | Who | Sentence |
|---|---|---|
| **79** | **Ṣāliḥ** | *Fa-tawallā ʿanhum wa qāla yā qawmi laqad ablaghtukum **risālata** rabbī wa naṣaḥtu lakum wa lākin lā tuḥibbūna'n-nāṣiḥīn* |
| **93** | **Shuʿayb** | *Fa-tawallā ʿanhum wa qāla yā qawmi laqad ablaghtukum **risālāti** rabbī wa naṣaḥtu lakum, fa-kayfa āsā ʿalā qawmin kāfirīn* |

**The only difference is singular against plural: *risālata* (singular) and *risālāti* (plural).** **This is a difference that can be verified from the text, and the lexicographers explain *risālāt* by "the multiplicity of the messages sent".**

**And the second difference is at the end of the sentence: Ṣāliḥ says *lā tuḥibbūna'n-nāṣiḥīn* (you do not love those who counsel) — he describes the addressee; Shuʿayb says *fa-kayfa āsā ʿalā qawmin kāfirīn* (how then should I grieve?) — he states his own condition.**

**I record this as my own reading and it is not binding; its ground is the endings of the two sentences:** the same pattern is used twice, **and in both the final sentence is a note of *feeling*, and the two look in different directions.**

### The word peculiar to this block of the sūra: نُصْح

The root **ن-ص-ح**: **purity, freedom from admixture; and from this, to counsel out of a wish for another's good.** The lexicographers tie the root to the pictures of **the clarifying of honey** (*nāṣiḥu'l-ʿasal* — pure honey) and **the soundness of a seam** (*niṣāḥ* — sewing thread). **That is, counsel is both unmixed and something that joins parts together.**

**The root occurs four times in this block, and this is a countable datum:**

| Verse | Expression | Who |
|---|---|---|
| **62** | *Wa **anṣaḥu** lakum* | **Noah** |
| **68** | *Wa anā lakum **nāṣiḥun** amīn* | **Hūd** |
| **79** | *Wa **naṣaḥtu** lakum … lā tuḥibbūna'**n-nāṣiḥīn*** | **Ṣāliḥ** |
| **93** | *Wa **naṣaḥtu** lakum* | **Shuʿayb** |

**And the same root had occurred once more in the second block of the sūra — but in the opposite direction:** *wa qāsamahumā innī lakumā **la-mina'n-nāṣiḥīn*** (21) — **in the mouth of Iblīs.**

**This is a weave that can be verified within the sūra, and I record the following as my own reading:** **al-Aʿrāf puts the sentence "I am one who counsels you" first into the mouth of a liar, then into the mouths of four prophets.** **And the difference between them is given in the sūra's own words:** Iblīs's is built with an oath (*wa qāsamahumā*), the prophets' without one. **This is an explanation, not a ruling.**

---

## 7/59-64 — Noah

*Laqad arsalnā Nūḥan ilā qawmihī fa-qāla yā qawmi'budū'llāha mā lakum min ilāhin ghayruh, innī akhāfu ʿalaykum ʿadhāba yawmin ʿaẓīm · Qāla'l-malaʾu min qawmihī innā la-narāka fī ḍalālin mubīn · Qāla yā qawmi laysa bī ḍalālatun wa lākinnī rasūlun min rabbi'l-ʿālamīn · Uballighukum risālāti rabbī wa anṣaḥu lakum wa aʿlamu mina'llāhi mā lā taʿlamūn · A-wa ʿajibtum an jāʾakum dhikrun min rabbikum ʿalā rajulin minkum li-yundhirakum wa li-tattaqū wa laʿallakum turḥamūn · Fa-kadhdhabūhu fa-anjaynāhu wa'lladhīna maʿahū fi'l-fulki wa aghraqnā'lladhīna kadhdhabū bi-āyātinā innahum kānū qawman ʿamīn*

"**We sent Noah to his people. He said: 'O my people! Serve God; you have no god but Him. I fear for you the punishment of a mighty day.' · The notables of his people said: 'We see you in plain error.' · He said: 'O my people! There is no error in me; I am a messenger from the Lord of all the worlds. · I convey to you the messages of my Lord, and I counsel you, and I know from God what you do not know. · Do you find it strange that a reminder from your Lord should come to you through a man from among yourselves, to warn you, that you may guard yourselves and be shown mercy?' · They called him a liar; so We delivered him and those with him in the ark, and drowned those who called Our signs lies. They were indeed a blind people.**"

### إِنَّا لَنَرَىٰكَ فِى ضَلَٰلٍ مُّبِينٍ and the form of the answer

**The relation between the words of the other side and the words of the answer must be recorded, and this is a point of word order:**

| Who | Expression | Pattern of the word |
|---|---|---|
| **The notables** (60) | *Innā la-narāka fī **ḍalālin** mubīn* | ***Ḍalāl*** — verbal noun |
| **Noah** (61) | *Laysa bī **ḍalālatun*** | ***Ḍalāla*** — feminine verbal noun |

**The two words are from the same root (ض-ل-ل) but their patterns differ.** **The lexicographers explain this difference thus: the pattern *ḍalāla* lends itself, as against *ḍalāl*, to a negation that takes in even the smallest amount** — **that is, the answer means not "I am not in a great error" but "there is no straying in me at all".**

**I convey this explanation as it is transmitted, and the difference of pattern is a datum that can be read from the text.**

**And the construction of the answer must also be recorded: *laysa bī ḍalālatun wa lākinnī rasūlun min rabbi'l-ʿālamīn*.** **The objection is rejected and something is put in its place.** **I record this as an observation about word order:** the answer is not a defence but **a statement of identity.**

**And the same two-step answer will be repeated word for word in the story of Hūd** (66-67: the objection *safāha* → *laysa bī safāhatun wa lākinnī rasūlun min rabbi'l-ʿālamīn*). **The same structure in two stories, with two different words of objection.** **This is a countable datum and will be tabulated at Hūd.**

### وَأَعْلَمُ مِنَ ٱللَّهِ مَا لَا تَعْلَمُونَ

**The limit of the sentence must be recorded: *aʿlamu mina'llāhi mā lā taʿlamūn* — the source of the knowledge is stated.**

**That is, the sentence does not say "I know more than you"; it says "I know something that comes from God".** **I record this as an observation about word order, and its ground is the particle *min*.**

### أَوَعَجِبْتُمْ أَن جَآءَكُمْ ذِكْرٌ

**The sentence begins with a question, and the subject of the question is not what has come but through whom it has come:** *ʿalā **rajulin minkum***.

**And this question occurs twice in the sūra, in almost the same words:**

| Verse | Who | Sentence |
|---|---|---|
| **63** | **Noah** | *A-wa ʿajibtum an jāʾakum dhikrun min rabbikum ʿalā rajulin minkum **li-yundhirakum wa li-tattaqū wa laʿallakum turḥamūn*** |
| **69** | **Hūd** | *A-wa ʿajibtum an jāʾakum dhikrun min rabbikum ʿalā rajulin minkum **li-yundhirakum*** |

**The difference: with Noah three purposes are counted (warning, guarding, mercy), with Hūd only one (warning).** **This is a difference that can be verified from the text.**

**And the subject of the objection is worth recording:** what is objected to is not the message but **the messenger's being a human being.** **The same objection will be turned back on the addressee in the last block of the sūra** (7/184: *a-wa lam yatafakkarū mā bi-ṣāḥibihim min jinna*). **This link will close at verse 184.**

### كَانُوا۟ قَوْمًا عَمِينَ — the word of the closing

The root **ع-م-ي**: **blindness.** ***ʿAmīn* is the plural of *ʿamā*, and the lexicographers record a difference of emphasis between *aʿmā* (one whose eye does not see) and *ʿamī*:** *ʿamī* is used more for **blindness of heart**.

**I convey this explanation as it is transmitted.**

**And the place of the word in the sūra must be recorded: the theme of blindness will return in the last block of the sūra** — **7/179** (*lahum aʿyunun lā yubṣirūna bihā*) **and 7/198** (*wa tarāhum yanẓurūna ilayka wa hum lā yubṣirūn*). **The word the sūra uses to close the first story of the sequence it turns at the end into a general diagnosis.** **I record this as an observation.**

---

## 7/65-72 — Hūd

*Wa ilā ʿĀdin akhāhum Hūdā, qāla yā qawmi'budū'llāha mā lakum min ilāhin ghayruh, a-fa-lā tattaqūn · Qāla'l-malaʾu'lladhīna kafarū min qawmihī innā la-narāka fī safāhatin wa innā la-naẓunnuka mina'l-kādhibīn · Qāla yā qawmi laysa bī safāhatun wa lākinnī rasūlun min rabbi'l-ʿālamīn · Uballighukum risālāti rabbī wa anā lakum nāṣiḥun amīn*

"**And to ʿĀd [We sent] their brother Hūd. He said: 'O my people! Serve God; you have no god but Him. Will you not then guard yourselves?' · The notables of his people who denied said: 'We see you in folly, and we think you are one of the liars.' · He said: 'O my people! There is no folly in me; I am a messenger from the Lord of all the worlds. · I convey to you the messages of my Lord, and I am to you a trustworthy counsellor.'**"

### Two objections, one structure

**In the stories of Noah and Hūd the objection and the answer settle into the same pattern. This is a countable datum:**

| | **Noah** (60-61) | **Hūd** (66-67) |
|---|---|---|
| **The objector** | *Al-malaʾu min qawmih* — **without qualifier** | *Al-malaʾu'lladhīna **kafarū** min qawmih* |
| **The word of the objection** | ***Ḍalāl*** — straying from the way | ***Safāha*** — lightness of mind |
| **A second objection** | (none) | *Wa innā la-naẓunnuka mina'l-kādhibīn* — **the suspicion of lying** |
| **The answer** | *Laysa bī **ḍalālatun** wa lākinnī rasūlun min rabbi'l-ʿālamīn* | *Laysa bī **safāhatun** wa lākinnī rasūlun min rabbi'l-ʿālamīn* |
| **What follows** | *Wa **anṣaḥu** lakum wa aʿlamu mina'llāhi mā lā taʿlamūn* | *Wa anā lakum **nāṣiḥun amīn*** |

**The second half of the sentence (*wa lākinnī rasūlun min rabbi'l-ʿālamīn*) is word for word identical in the two stories; what changes is only the word that is rejected.**

**And the difference between the two objections is worth recording: *ḍalāl* is a fault of direction (on the wrong road), *safāha* a fault of capacity (light of mind).** The root **س-ف-ه**: **lightness, want of measure.** The lexicographers tie the root to **a garment or a rope being light and slack.**

**I record this as an observation:** the second objection is heavier than the first — **it aims not at the road but at the mind.** **And the third thing added to it is a statement of *supposition*:** *la-naẓunnuka* — "we think". **That is, the claim is offered not as proof but as opinion.**

### وَٱذْكُرُوٓا۟ إِذْ جَعَلَكُمْ خُلَفَآءَ — twice in the sūra

**The same pattern occurs in the sixty-ninth and seventy-fourth verses, and both are a *reminder*:**

| Verse | Who | Successors after whom | What was given |
|---|---|---|---|
| **69** | **Hūd → ʿĀd** | *Min baʿdi qawmi **Nūḥ*** | *Wa **zādakum fi'l-khalqi basṭatan*** — an increase in build |
| **74** | **Ṣāliḥ → Thamūd** | *Min baʿdi **ʿĀd*** | *Tattakhidhūna min suhūlihā **quṣūran** wa tanḥitūna'l-jibāla **buyūtā*** — palaces on the plain, houses hewn in the mountains |

**The two reminders build a chain: the people of Noah → ʿĀd → Thamūd.** **That is, the sūra lines up the stories not side by side but one after another, each link taking the place of the one before it.** **This is a weave that can be verified from the text.**

**And both are joined by the same sentence: *fa'dhkurū ālāʾa'llāh* — "so remember the bounties of God".**

**بَسْطَةً فِى ٱلْخَلْقِ** — the root **ب-س-ط**: **to spread, to extend.** **What it is an extension of is not named in the text.** **In the classical commentaries it is explained as stature and strength; I convey this explanation as it is transmitted and go into no further detail, because the verse does not** (`STYLE.md`).

### أَتُجَٰدِلُونَنِى فِىٓ أَسْمَآءٍ سَمَّيْتُمُوهَآ

**The seventy-first verse is one of the densest sentences to be recorded in this block:**

> *A-tujādilūnanī fī **asmāʾin sammaytumūhā antum wa ābāʾukum** mā nazzala'llāhu bihā min sulṭān*
>
> "Do you dispute with me about **names** that you and your forefathers have given? God has sent down no warrant for them."

**The work of the sentence must be recorded, and this is an observation about word order: the subject of the dispute is named not as an *entity* but as a *name*.**

| The sentence could have been built thus | What is built |
|---|---|
| "Do you dispute about the things you worship?" | ***Asmāʾin sammaytumūhā*** — "about names that you have given" |

**And a second note is entered: *mā nazzala'llāhu bihā min sulṭān* — there is no warrant sent down.**

**I record this as my own reading and it is not binding; its ground is two words (*asmāʾ* and *sulṭān*):** the verse brings what the other side holds down to the level of **naming**, and sets against it the measure of a **warrant sent down**. **That is, the dispute is taken off the axis of "your god or mine" and moved onto the axis of "what do you have in hand".**

**And the same sentence will return in the last block of the sūra:** at 7/194-195 what the things called upon can do will be counted one by one. **That link will close there.**

**And there is a limit to be recorded:** no general ruling about naming as such is to be drawn from this. **The subject of the verse is namings *for which there is no support whatever*, and the sentence enters that qualification itself.**

### كَذَٰلِكَ … وَقَطَعْنَا دَابِرَ

**دَابِر** — the root **د-ب-ر**: **the back, the end of a thing, what comes behind it.** ***Dābir* is the hindmost of a company, that is, the continuation of a line.** ***Qaṭaʿnā dābirahum* — "We cut off what was behind them", that is, We left them no continuation.**

**The expression occurs in al-Aʿrāf only in this verse (72), and it is the closing of only one of the five stories.** **This is a countable datum.**

**And the two halves of the closing must be recorded: *fa-anjaynāhu … bi-raḥmatin minnā* and *wa qaṭaʿnā dābira'lladhīna kadhdhabū*.** **The deliverance is given with a note of mercy, the destruction with a note of continuity:** one remains, the other's line is cut off.

---

## 7/73-79 — Ṣāliḥ

*Wa ilā Thamūda akhāhum Ṣāliḥā, qāla yā qawmi'budū'llāha mā lakum min ilāhin ghayruh, qad jāʾatkum bayyinatun min rabbikum, hādhihī nāqatu'llāhi lakum āyatan fa-dharūhā taʾkul fī arḍi'llāh wa lā tamassūhā bi-sūʾin fa-yaʾkhudhakum ʿadhābun alīm*

"**And to Thamūd [We sent] their brother Ṣāliḥ. He said: 'O my people! Serve God; you have no god but Him. A clear proof has come to you from your Lord: this is the she-camel of God — a sign for you. Let her be, to eat in God's earth; do not touch her with harm, or a painful punishment will seize you.'**"

### نَاقَة — and what is not told

The root **ن-و-ق**: **a she-camel.** **The Qurʾān speaks of this camel in several places** (al-Aʿrāf 7/73; Hūd 11/64; al-Isrāʾ 17/59; al-Qamar 54/27; al-Shams 91/13) **and it was treated in `054-kamer.md` and `091-sems.md`.** **I rely on those treatments.**

**And here there is a limit I must write out plainly, per the method:** **the Qurʾān says nothing about where the camel came from, how she appeared, her size, her colour, or her milk.** **There are detailed accounts transmitted in the classical sources; this commentary does not enter into them** — **because the verse does not, and their soundness cannot be established** (`STYLE.md`).

**What the text says is this, and that much I record:**

| What is said | Verse |
|---|---|
| The camel **is a sign** | *Hādhihī nāqatu'llāhi lakum **āyatan*** |
| She **is to be left alone** | *Fa-**dharūhā** taʾkul fī arḍi'llāh* |
| She **is not to be touched** | *Wa lā **tamassūhā bi-sūʾin*** |
| **The consequence** | *Fa-yaʾkhudhakum ʿadhābun alīm* |

**And a point of word order must be recorded: the sign given is not a thing to be done but a thing *not* to be done.** **Both commands are a withdrawal: let her be, and do not touch.**

**I record this as my own reading and it is not binding; its ground is the two verbs themselves:** **the test is built not with a demand for an act but with a demand for a *withholding of the hand*.** **And what was given to Adam in the second block of the sūra was of the same kind:** *wa lā taqrabā hādhihi'sh-shajarata* (19) — **a prohibition.** **In both scenes the subject of the test is that what is given not be taken.** **This is an overlap that can be verified within the sūra.**

### ٱلْمَلَأُ ٱلَّذِينَ ٱسْتَكْبَرُوا۟ and ٱلَّذِينَ ٱسْتُضْعِفُوا۟

**The seventy-fifth verse is the clearest instance of the "line of class" recorded in the section on the structure of the sūra:**

> *Qāla'l-malaʾu'lladhīna'**stakbarū** min qawmihī lilladhīna'**stuḍʿifū** li-man āmana minhum: a-taʿlamūna anna Ṣāliḥan mursalun min rabbih*

**Two patterns are set facing one another, and both are Form X:**

| Pattern | Root | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| ***Istakbarū*** | **ك-ب-ر** | **To reckon oneself great** — with a subject, what they themselves did |
| ***Ustuḍʿifū*** | **ض-ع-ف** | **To be made weak** — **passive**, what another did |

**The difference of voice must be recorded, and it is a datum of word order: the first is active, the second passive.** **That is, the verse gives weakness not as an attribute but as something that was done.**

**The same root was treated at 26/22 in `026-suara.md`**, where it was recorded: ***istiḍʿāf* — weakness is not something that comes of itself, it is something done.** **I rely on that treatment.**

**And there is an appositive note within the verse, and it must be recorded:** *lilladhīna'stuḍʿifū **li-man āmana minhum*** — "to those made weak, **to those of them who believed**". **The second expression narrows the first.**

**I record this as an observation about word order:** the verse does not **equate** "those made weak" with "those who believed"; it separates the second out from within the first. **That is, here too the sūra builds no blanket judgement about a class** (`STYLE.md`).

### فَعَقَرُوا۟ ٱلنَّاقَةَ وَعَتَوْا۟ عَنْ أَمْرِ رَبِّهِمْ

The root **ع-ق-ر**: **to bring an animal down by cutting its leg; and from this, to kill.** The lexicographers give the primary sense as **the cutting of the leg tendon.**

The root **ع-ت-و**: **to exceed the bound, to hold out in pride, to dry and harden.** ***ʿAtā ʿan amri rabbih* — "he went out from the command of his Lord".**

**And the order of the verbs is worth recording: first the act, then the naming of the act.** **The verse does not say "they went out from the command and hamstrung the camel"; it says the reverse.** **I record this as an observation about word order.**

**And the sentence that follows is in the same pattern as the one in the story of Hūd:**

| Verse | Who | Sentence |
|---|---|---|
| **70** | **ʿĀd** | *Fa'tinā bimā taʿidunā **in kunta mina'ṣ-ṣādiqīn*** |
| **77** | **Thamūd** | *Yā Ṣāliḥu'tinā bimā taʿidunā **in kunta mina'l-mursalīn*** |

**The only difference is in the last word: one *ṣādiqīn* (those who speak truly), the other *mursalīn* (those who are sent).** **This is a difference that can be verified from the text.**

**And as was recorded at 26/31 in `026-suara.md`, the conditional particle is *in*, not *idhā*** — **in Arabic *in* is used for the doubtful, *idhā* for the certain.** **I rely on that treatment.**

### ٱلرَّجْفَة and جَٰثِمِين

The root **ر-ج-ف**: **violent shaking, trembling.** *Rajfa* — a quaking.

The root **ج-ث-م**: **an animal's sinking down on its breast and knees, sticking fast to the ground.** The lexicographers use the word especially for **a bird's crouching to the ground.**

**And the choice of the word is worth recording: *jāthimīn* states a *posture*, not an annihilation.** **The scene is of bodies left collapsed where they were.**

**And precisely this sentence occurs twice in the sūra** (78 and 91) — **it was counted in the section on the structure of the sūra.** **It must also be recorded that for the same event the Qurʾān uses other words in other sūras** (*ṣāʿiqa* at Fuṣṣilat 41/13; *ṣayḥa* at al-Qamar 54/31). **They were treated in `041-fussilet.md` and `054-kamer.md`; I rely on those treatments.** **This variation is explained as the naming of different aspects of the same event; I convey it as transmitted and impose no preference.**

---

## 7/80-84 — Lot

*Wa Lūṭan idh qāla li-qawmihī a-taʾtūna'l-fāḥishata mā sabaqakum bihā min aḥadin mina'l-ʿālamīn · Innakum la-taʾtūna'r-rijāla shahwatan min dūni'n-nisāʾ, bal antum qawmun musrifūn · Wa mā kāna jawāba qawmihī illā an qālū akhrijūhum min qaryatikum innahum unāsun yataṭahharūn · Fa-anjaynāhu wa ahlahū illa'mraʾatahū kānat mina'l-ghābirīn · Wa amṭarnā ʿalayhim maṭarā, fa'nẓur kayfa kāna ʿāqibatu'l-mujrimīn*

"**And Lot too [We sent]. He said to his people: 'Do you commit that indecency which no one in all the worlds committed before you? · You approach men with desire instead of women. You are indeed a people who exceed all bounds.' · And the only answer of his people was to say: 'Drive them out of your town; they are people who would keep themselves pure.' · We delivered him and his household — except his wife; she was of those who stayed behind. · And We rained down upon them a rain. See, then, what was the end of the guilty!**"

### Where the pattern breaks

**This story is the link in the sūra's sequence of prophets where the pattern breaks most, and it carries three absences at once. All three are countable:**

| Shared element | In the other four stories | **In the story of Lot** |
|---|---|---|
| *Yā qawmi'budū'llāha mā lakum min ilāhin ghayruh* | **present** (59, 65, 73, 85) | **absent** |
| *Akhāhum* — "their brother" | **present** at 65, 73, 85 | **absent** |
| *Al-malaʾ* — the notables | **present** at 60, 66, 75, 88, 90 | **absent** — the addressee is directly *qawmuh* |

**And there is a fourth difference: the verb of entry.** **In the others there was *arsalnā* or the pattern *wa ilā … akhāhum*; here it is *wa Lūṭan idh qāla li-qawmihī* — the speech is entered directly.**

**I record this as my own reading and it is not binding; its ground is the four absences above:** **in al-Aʿrāf's story of Lot the call opens not as a call to *tawḥīd* but directly as a question about *conduct*.** **The text does not say the reason for this.** **And in the classical commentaries the explanation is transmitted that Lot's people were not of his own lineage — and that therefore *akhūhum* is not said; I convey this explanation as transmitted and claim no certainty for it.**

### فَٰحِشَة and مُسْرِفُون — where the words attach

**The root ف-ح-ش was treated at the thirty-third verse** (*innamā ḥarrama rabbiya'l-**fawāḥisha** mā ẓahara minhā wa mā baṭan*); **the sense of the root: an indecency that exceeds the measure.** **I rely on that treatment.**

The root **س-ر-ف** was treated at the thirty-first verse (*wa lā **tusrifū** innahū lā yuḥibbu'l-**musrifīn***); **the sense of the root: to exceed the bound in something, to put it out of its place.** **I rely on that treatment.**

**And the return of the two roots in this story is worth recording, and it is a weave that can be verified within the sūra:**

| Root | Where earlier in the sūra | Here |
|---|---|---|
| **ف-ح-ش** | **28** (*idhā faʿalū fāḥishatan*), **33** (*ḥarrama … al-fawāḥish*) | **80** — *a-taʾtūna'l-**fāḥishata*** |
| **س-ر-ف** | **31** (*wa lā tusrifū*) | **81** — *qawmun **musrifūn*** |

**That is, the sūra places in the sixth block, inside a story, the two measures it laid down in the third block.** **I record this as an observation, and its ground is the words themselves.**

**And I draw no legal ruling from this; I content myself with conveying the naming the verse sets down** (`STYLE.md`). **And this note is necessary:** the verse names **a behaviour**; it builds no blanket judgement about a community. **This is the sūra's own principle, and it was recorded in the section on the structure: what is described is an attribute, and whoever carries that attribute is included in it.**

### إِنَّهُمْ أُنَاسٌ يَتَطَهَّرُونَ — the form of the answer

**The answer of the other side is worth recording, and it is one of the most striking sentences in the block.**

The root **ط-ه-ر**: **cleanness, purification.** **Form V (*taṭahhara*) states "to keep oneself clean".**

**That is, what is counted as the ground for banishment is, in the other side's own mouth, a word of praise.**

| The expected ground | The ground given |
|---|---|
| An accusation | ***Innahum unāsun yataṭahharūn*** — "people who would keep themselves pure" |

**I record this as my own reading and it is not binding; its ground is the sentence itself:** **the decision to drive them out is grounded not on a fault of the one driven out but on his attribute.** **And it is recorded in the classical commentaries that there is a tone of mockery in the language of the sentence; I convey this explanation as transmitted.**

**And the sentence *akhrijūhum min qaryatikum* is not alone in the sūra:** **the same will be said to Shuʿayb in verse 88** — *la-**nukhrijannaka** yā Shuʿaybu … min qaryatinā*.

| Verse | Who is to be driven out | Who says it |
|---|---|---|
| **82** | **Lot and those with him** | **His people** |
| **88** | **Shuʿayb and those who believed** | ***Al-malaʾ*** |

**The same threat in two stories.** **And the same root had occurred elsewhere in the second block of the sūra:** *fa-**akhraja**humā mimmā kānā fīh* (the counterpart in al-Aʿrāf of al-Baqara 2/36 being 7/27: *kamā **akhraja** abawaykum mina'l-janna*). **In this sūra being driven out is a repeated verb.** **I record this as an observation.**

### ٱلْغَٰبِرِين and وَأَمْطَرْنَا

The root **غ-ب-ر**: **to remain behind, to be left over.** ***Ghābir* — the one who remains behind, who is left after those who go.** **The word is repeated several times in the Qurʾān for this event** (al-Aʿrāf 7/83; al-Ḥijr 15/60; al-Shuʿarāʾ 26/171) **and was treated in `026-suara.md`.** **I rely on that treatment.**

**And a point of word order must be recorded: *kānat mina'l-ghābirīn* — a feminine subject is ascribed to a masculine plural group.** **This is a common construction in Arabic for stating inclusion in a company, and it has occurred elsewhere in the index too.**

The root **م-ط-ر**: **rain.** **And the pattern of the verb must be recorded: Form IV, *amṭara*.** **The lexicographers transmit a distinction:** *maṭara* (Form I) is used for **mercy**, *amṭara* (Form IV) for **punishment**.

**This distinction is not certain and does not hold everywhere; but it is transmitted, and it accords with this verse.** **I convey it as transmitted and do not present it as a settled rule of the language.**

**And it must be recorded that the word *maṭarā* (rain) is left without a specification: what was rained down is not said here.** **In other places in the Qurʾān stones are named for this event** (al-Ḥijr 15/74; Hūd 11/82); **al-Aʿrāf does not say it.** **Where it does not say, neither do I.**

---

## 7/85-93 — Shuʿayb

*Wa ilā Madyana akhāhum Shuʿaybā, qāla yā qawmi'budū'llāha mā lakum min ilāhin ghayruh, qad jāʾatkum bayyinatun min rabbikum, fa-awfu'l-kayla wa'l-mīzāna wa lā tabkhasu'n-nāsa ashyāʾahum wa lā tufsidū fi'l-arḍi baʿda iṣlāḥihā, dhālikum khayrun lakum in kuntum muʾminīn*

"**And to Madyan [We sent] their brother Shuʿayb. He said: 'O my people! Serve God; you have no god but Him. A clear proof has come to you from your Lord. Give full measure and full weight; do not give people less than their due; do not work corruption on the earth after it has been set in order. That is better for you, if you are believers.'**"

### What is placed after the call

**In the four stories what came after the shared opening sentence was different** (it was given in the table of the pattern above). **With Shuʿayb that place is filled with three prohibitions, and all three concern measure:**

| # | Command/prohibition | Field |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *Fa-**awfu**'l-kayla wa'l-mīzān* | **Measure and weight** — an affirmative command |
| 2 | *Wa lā **tabkhasū**'n-nāsa ashyāʾahum* | **Giving short** — a prohibition |
| 3 | *Wa lā **tufsidū** fi'l-arḍi baʿda iṣlāḥihā* | **The earth** — a prohibition |

**The third is word for word identical with the fifty-sixth verse**, and this repetition was tabulated in the section on the structure of the sūra: **the sūra puts its own command into the mouth of a prophet.** **In `011-hud.md` Table 4 it was shown that Sūrat Hūd does the same thing four times** (*istaghfirū rabbakum thumma tūbū ilayh* → 11/3 and the mouths of three prophets). **I rely on that treatment: al-Aʿrāf applies the same technique once.**

The root **ب-خ-س**: **to give a thing less than its due, to lower its value.** **The verb occurs in Shuʿayb's mouth in other places in the Qurʾān too** (al-Shuʿarāʾ 26/183; Hūd 11/85) **and the subject of measure was treated in detail in `083-mutaffifin.md`.** **I rely on that treatment and do not repeat it here.**

### وَلَا تَقْعُدُوا۟ بِكُلِّ صِرَٰطٍ تُوعِدُونَ

**The eighty-sixth verse is the picture that belongs to this block:**

> *Wa lā **taqʿudū** bi-kulli ṣirāṭin **tūʿidūna** wa **taṣuddūna** ʿan sabīli'llāhi man āmana bihī wa **tabghūnahā ʿiwajā***
>
> "Do not sit on every road, threatening; do not bar from the way of God those who believe in Him, and do not seek to make that way crooked."
