**And this sentence has a precedent within the sūra. This is a verifiable overlap and must be recorded:**

| Verse | Who | Sentence |
|---|---|---|
| **16** | **Iblīs** | *La-**aqʿudanna** lahum **ṣirāṭaka'l-mustaqīm*** |
| **86** | **Madyan** | *Wa lā **taqʿudū** bi-kulli **ṣirāṭin** tūʿidūna* |

**The same verb (ق-ع-د — to sit) and the same word (*ṣirāṭ*).** **The first a statement of intent, the second a prohibition.**

**I record this as my own reading and it is not binding; its ground is the verb and object of the two verses:** **the sūra forbids to a human community, in the eighty-sixth verse, the work described in the sixteenth.** **That is, sitting at the head of the road is first told in the sūra as a stance, then forbidden as something a human being can do.** **This is an explanation, not a ruling.**

**And the sentence *wa tabghūnahā ʿiwajā* occurs twice in the sūra** (45 and 86) — **it was counted in the section on the structure.** **The first was the description of the wrongdoers in the scene of the hereafter; the second is a prohibition here.**

The root **ع-و-ج**: **crookedness.** **And the lexicographers record a distinction between *ʿiwaj* (with kasra) and *ʿawaj* (with fatḥa):** *ʿawaj* is used for crookedness **seen by the eye** (a stick, a wall), *ʿiwaj* for crookedness that is **not seen** (a word, a religion, a road). **This distinction is transmitted and accords with the verse; I convey it as transmitted.**

### Two *malaʾ*s, two different threats

**In the story of Shuʿayb *malaʾ* occurs twice, and this is the highest number for any one prophet in the sūra. It was counted in the section on the structure; here it is treated:**

| Verse | How it is qualified | To whom it speaks | What it says |
|---|---|---|---|
| **88** | *Al-malaʾu'lladhīna'**stakbarū*** | **To Shuʿayb and those who believed** | *La-nukhrijannaka … **aw la-taʿūdunna fī millatinā*** — banishment or return |
| **90** | *Al-malaʾu'lladhīna **kafarū*** | **To their own people** | *La-ini'ttabaʿtum Shuʿayban innakum idhan **la-khāsirūn*** — a warning of loss to whoever follows |

**The direction of the two addresses differs: one outward, the other inward.** **I record this as an observation about word order:** the other side speaks first with the messenger, then with those who might listen to the messenger.

**And in the ninety-second verse this second sentence is answered with the same word:**

| Verse | Sentence |
|---|---|
| **90** | *La-ini'ttabaʿtum Shuʿayban innakum idhan la-**khāsirūn*** |
| **92** | *Alladhīna kadhdhabū Shuʿayban kānū humu'l-**khāsirīn*** |

**The same word, with the subject reversed.** **Of those who said "if you follow him you will be losers" it is said "they were the losers themselves".** **This is a repetition that can be verified within the sūra.**

### The question of ٱلْعَوْد — a point of language

**The lexicographers dwell on *aw la-taʿūdunna fī millatinā* ("or you shall return to our creed") in the eighty-eighth verse and on the expression *in ʿudnā fī millatikum* in the eighty-ninth.**

**The question is this: does the verb *ʿawda* (to return) require having previously been in that state?** **Two explanations are transmitted in the classical commentaries, and I give them in a table:**

| Reading | What *ʿawd* is here | Ground |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **The verb has the sense "to enter, to pass into"**; it does not require having previously been in that state | The use of the verb *ʿāda* in Arabic in the sense of *ṣāra* (he became, he came to be) |
| 2 | **The word is spoken with regard to the majority of those who believed**; the condition of the many is ascribed to the company | That the addressees of the sentence are *Shuʿayb and those with him* — for the second group returning is meant in its literal sense |

**Both explanations are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.** **This is a question of language and is not used to build a ruling about prophets** (`STYLE.md`).

### كَأَن لَّمْ يَغْنَوْا۟ فِيهَا

The root **غ-ن-ي**: **to settle and remain in a place, to live there without want.** ***Maghnā* — a dwelling place.** **The pattern means "as though they had never dwelt there".**

**In `011-hud.md` Table 3 this pattern was counted as one of the five closing patterns of Sūrat Hūd** (11/68, 11/95). **I rely on that treatment.**

**And in al-Aʿrāf the pattern occurs only once, in the story of Shuʿayb.** **This is a countable datum.**

**And a point of word order must be recorded: in the ninety-second verse the expression *alladhīna kadhdhabū Shuʿayban* occurs twice** — **at the beginning of the sentence and in the middle.** **A pronoun could have been used; it was not.**

**I record this as an observation about word order:** the repetition fixes twice over who the subject is. **And in the second sentence the pronoun *hum* (they) is added as well:** *kānū **hum**u'l-khāsirīn* — **the pronoun of separation (*ḍamīr al-faṣl*), which states restriction: "they were the losers themselves".**

---

## 7/94-95 — وَمَآ أَرْسَلْنَا فِى قَرْيَةٍ مِّن نَّبِىٍّ إِلَّآ أَخَذْنَآ أَهْلَهَا بِٱلْبَأْسَآءِ وَٱلضَّرَّآءِ لَعَلَّهُمْ يَضَّرَّعُونَ · ثُمَّ بَدَّلْنَا مَكَانَ ٱلسَّيِّئَةِ ٱلْحَسَنَةَ حَتَّىٰ عَفَوا۟ وَّقَالُوا۟ قَدْ مَسَّ ءَابَآءَنَا ٱلضَّرَّآءُ وَٱلسَّرَّآءُ فَأَخَذْنَٰهُم بَغْتَةً وَهُمْ لَا يَشْعُرُونَ

*Wa mā arsalnā fī qaryatin min nabiyyin illā akhadhnā ahlahā bi'l-baʾsāʾi wa'ḍ-ḍarrāʾi laʿallahum yaḍḍarraʿūn · Thumma baddalnā makāna's-sayyiʾati'l-ḥasanata ḥattā ʿafaw wa qālū qad massa ābāʾana'ḍ-ḍarrāʾu wa's-sarrāʾu fa-akhadhnāhum baghtatan wa hum lā yashʿurūn*

"**Into whatever land We sent a prophet, We seized its people with hardship and affliction — that they might humble themselves. · Then We put good in the place of ill, until they grew many and said, 'Hardship and ease touched our forefathers too.' So We seized them suddenly, while they were unaware.**"

### The rule that follows the stories

**When the five stories are finished the sūra lays down a *rule*, and the form of the rule must be recorded: negation + exception** (*mā … illā*).

**This pattern is the strongest generalisation in Arabic: "We sent to no place without … coming about."**

### A two-stage test

**The verse counts two stages, and the two run in opposite directions:**

| Stage | What is done | What is expected | What happens |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1** (94) | *Baʾsāʾ* and *ḍarrāʾ* — **hardship** | *Laʿallahum **yaḍḍarraʿūn*** — pleading | (the text does not state the outcome) |
| **2** (95) | *Baddalnā makāna's-sayyiʾati'l-ḥasanata* — **ease** | — | *Ḥattā **ʿafaw*** and a sentence: *qad massa ābāʾana'ḍ-ḍarrāʾu wa's-sarrāʾu* |

**And the outcome of the second stage is a *sentence*, not a behaviour.** **I record this as my own reading and it is not binding; its ground is the verse itself:** what nullifies the test turns out to be an **explanation** — "such things happen anyway, they happened to our forefathers too."

**That is, the event is taken out of being a *sign* and turned into a *natural occurrence*.** **And this lies on the same line as the twenty-eighth verse of the sūra:** there too the ground of the behaviour was tied to the forefathers (*wajadnā ʿalayhā ābāʾanā*).

**عَفَوا۟** — the root **ع-ف-و**. **One of the primary senses of the root is to become many, to increase** (when hair grows long and thick it is said *ʿafā'sh-shaʿr*). **The lexicographers dwell on the meaning of the word in this verse, and two explanations are transmitted:**

| Reading | What *ʿafaw* means |
|---|---|
| 1 | **They grew many** — they increased in number and in goods |
| 2 | **They became well off, at ease** — their livelihood widened |

**Both explanations rest on the same root and are close to one another; I do not impose a preference.**

And this word will return in the last block of the sūra with another sense: *khudhi'l-**ʿafw*** (199). **The same root, two different branches of meaning.** **This link will be treated at verse 199.**

### بَغْتَةً وَهُمْ لَا يَشْعُرُونَ

The root **ب-غ-ت**: **to be seized suddenly, unprepared.**

The root **ش-ع-ر**: **to notice a fine thing, to sense it.** The root is akin to *shaʿr* (hair); **the lexicographers tie *shuʿūr* to the picture of "becoming aware of something as fine as a hair".**

**And the coming together of the two words is worth recording: *baghtatan* states the speed of the event, *lā yashʿurūn* the condition of the addressee.** **Together they overlap with the picture in the fourth verse of the sūra:** *fa-jāʾahā baʾsunā **bayātan aw hum qāʾilūn*** — in the sleep of night or in the sleep of noon. **The sūra says the same thing twice, with two different words.**

---

## 7/96-99 — وَلَوْ أَنَّ أَهْلَ ٱلْقُرَىٰٓ ءَامَنُوا۟ وَٱتَّقَوْا۟ لَفَتَحْنَا عَلَيْهِم بَرَكَٰتٍ مِّنَ ٱلسَّمَآءِ وَٱلْأَرْضِ وَلَٰكِن كَذَّبُوا۟ فَأَخَذْنَٰهُم بِمَا كَانُوا۟ يَكْسِبُونَ

*Wa law anna ahla'l-qurā āmanū wa'ttaqaw la-fataḥnā ʿalayhim barakātin mina's-samāʾi wa'l-arḍi wa lākin kadhdhabū fa-akhadhnāhum bimā kānū yaksibūn · A-fa-amina ahlu'l-qurā an yaʾtiyahum baʾsunā bayātan wa hum nāʾimūn · Aw amina ahlu'l-qurā an yaʾtiyahum baʾsunā ḍuḥan wa hum yalʿabūn · A-fa-aminū makra'llāh, fa-lā yaʾmanu makra'llāhi illa'l-qawmu'l-khāsirūn*

"**Had the people of those towns believed and guarded themselves, We would have opened upon them blessings from heaven and earth. But they called it a lie; so We seized them for what they had earned. · Did the people of those towns feel secure that Our violence would not come upon them by night, while they slept? · Or did the people of those towns feel secure that Our violence would not come upon them in the forenoon, while they played? · Did they feel secure from the devising of God? None feels secure from the devising of God but a people who are lost.**"

### The construction of the sentence: an unfulfilled condition

**The ninety-sixth verse is the most striking sentence in the sūra's section of rules, and its construction must be recorded.**

***Law*** — in Arabic the particle of the **unfulfilled condition**. **In English it answers to "had they … We would have": it states that the condition did not obtain, and therefore neither did the consequence.**

**That is, the verse is not a promise but the record of a possibility that did not come about.** **I record this as an observation about word order, and its ground is the particle.**

**And the second half of the sentence confirms it: *wa lākin kadhdhabū* — "but they called it a lie".** **The adversative particle (*lākin*) says outright that the condition did not obtain.**

### Two conditions, two verbs

**The condition is built with two verbs, and the order of the two is worth recording:**

| Order | Verb | What |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | ***Āmanū*** | **Faith** — belonging to the inward |
| 2 | ***Ittaqaw*** | **God-fearing** — belonging to conduct |

**The two are asked for together; neither is set as a condition on its own.**

### فَتَحْنَا عَلَيْهِم — the distribution of the pattern in the Qurʾān

**This pattern was treated at 23/77 in `023-muminun.md`, where a table of three links was drawn up. I rely on that treatment and carry the table over here:**

| Place | What is opened | What |
|---|---|---|
| al-Anʿām 6/44 | *Abwāba kulli shayʾ* — **the gates of everything** | **Bounty** |
| **al-Aʿrāf 7/96** | *Barakātin mina's-samāʾi wa'l-arḍ* | **Blessing** |
| `023-muminun.md` **23/77** | *Bāban dhā ʿadhābin shadīd* | **Punishment** |

**The conclusion recorded there was: the same pattern, the reverse content.** **And it was further recorded in `023-muminun.md` that at al-Anʿām 6/44, after the gates of bounty are opened, *akhadhnāhum baghtatan* follows.**

The difference that belongs here is this, and it must be recorded: in al-Aʿrāf the gate **is not opened.** **In al-Anʿām and al-Muʾminūn the act has taken place (*fataḥnā*), in al-Aʿrāf it is the apodosis of an unfulfilled condition (*la-fataḥnā*).**

**That is, the three verses hold three states of the same pattern: opened-bounty, opened-punishment, would-have-opened-did-not.** **I record this as an observation, and its ground is the verb forms of the three verses.**

### بَرَكَٰت — the root of the word and its plural

The root **ب-ر-ك**: **a thing's staying fixed, settling; and from this, increasing, lasting good.** The lexicographers tie the root to the picture of **a camel kneeling and settling** (*baraka'l-baʿīr*); and **a pool of water** (*birka*) is from the same root. **That is, blessing is what stays, not what flows away.**

**And the word occurs twice in this sūra:**

| Verse | Form | What |
|---|---|---|
| **54** | *Ta**bārak**a'llāhu rabbu'l-ʿālamīn* | **A verb** — the fixedness of loftiness |
| **96** | *Bara**kāt**in mina's-samāʾi wa'l-arḍ* | **A noun, plural** — what would have been opened |

**The same root, in the verse that opens the fifth block of the sūra and in the rule that closes the sixth.** **This is a repetition that can be verified within the sūra.**

**And the plural pattern must be recorded: *barakāt* — an indefinite plural.** **Not the singular (*baraka*).** **The lexicographers record that the indefinite plural here states multiplicity and variety.**

### مِّنَ ٱلسَّمَآءِ وَٱلْأَرْضِ — the extent of the pair

**And the source of the blessing is given in two directions: heaven and earth.**

**I record this as my own reading and it is not binding; its ground is the fifty-seventh verse:** there too the water came down from the sky (*fa-anzalnā bihi'l-māʾa*) and the produce came out of the earth (*fa-akhrajnā bihī min kulli'th-thamarāt*). **The pair in the ninety-sixth verse is the name of the two movements in the fifty-seventh.** **This is a reading, not a ruling.**

**And per the method (`STYLE.md`) I draw a limit:** no **mechanical law** between faith and economic prosperity is to be derived from this. **The sūra's own verses do not allow it:** the ninety-fourth verse says that in every place a messenger was sent the people were seized with **hardship**; the ninety-fifth says that abundance too is a test. **And the fifty-first verse records that the life of this world can delude.**

**That is, the sūra does not set up prosperity as a mark of reward.** **I record this as a datum of the text, and its ground is the sūra's own verses.**

### Three "did they feel secure" — and a note of *tanzīh*

**The ninety-seventh, ninety-eighth and ninety-ninth verses begin with the same verb: *amina* (he felt secure). Three questions, three different objects:**

| Verse | What they felt secure from | Time/state |
|---|---|---|
| **97** | *Baʾsunā* — the violence | *Bayātan wa hum **nāʾimūn*** — **by night, asleep** |
| **98** | *Baʾsunā* — the violence | *Ḍuḥan wa hum **yalʿabūn*** — **in the forenoon, at play** |
| **99** | ***Makra'llāh*** | (no time given) |

**The first two verses overlap with the fourth verse of the sūra** (*bayātan aw hum qāʾilūn*) **and both choose the slackest moments of daily life: sleep and play.**

**I record this as an observation:** the sūra places the danger not in the hour of work but in **the hour of rest**.

**And the verb *yalʿabūn* connects with the fifty-first verse:** there it was said that they took their religion as *lahwan wa **laʿibā***. **The same root, one an attribute and one a state.**

**For the word مَكْر I enter a note of *tanzīh*, per the method (`STYLE.md`):**

The root **م-ك-ر**: **to take a hidden measure, to act by a route the other side has not reckoned with.** **The word is not of itself pejorative in Arabic; the evil comes from against whom and for what it is done.**

**And when the word is ascribed to God in the Qurʾān, the shared note of the lexicographers and the exegetes is this:** **it is the requiting of the devising men have set up, by a route they have not reckoned with.** **It does not mean deception, trickery or injustice.**

**And the Qurʾān itself sets this out expressly in another place:** *wa yamkurūna wa yamkuru'llāh **wa'llāhu khayru'l-mākirīn*** (al-Anfāl 8/30). **That is, the word occurs in a context of requital.**

**I enter this note and close the discussion; I enter into no polemic of *kalām*** (`STYLE.md`).

**And the closing of the ninety-ninth verse must be recorded: *fa-lā yaʾmanu makra'llāhi illa'l-qawmu'l-**khāsirūn***.** **The same word had occurred in the ninetieth and ninety-second verses in the story of Shuʿayb.** **The sūra carries the word of the story over into the rule.**

---

## 7/100-102 — أَوَلَمْ يَهْدِ لِلَّذِينَ يَرِثُونَ ٱلْأَرْضَ مِنۢ بَعْدِ أَهْلِهَآ أَن لَّوْ نَشَآءُ أَصَبْنَٰهُم بِذُنُوبِهِمْ وَنَطْبَعُ عَلَىٰ قُلُوبِهِمْ فَهُمْ لَا يَسْمَعُونَ

*A-wa lam yahdi lilladhīna yarithūna'l-arḍa min baʿdi ahlihā an law nashāʾu aṣabnāhum bi-dhunūbihim wa naṭbaʿu ʿalā qulūbihim fa-hum lā yasmaʿūn · Tilka'l-qurā naquṣṣu ʿalayka min anbāʾihā, wa laqad jāʾathum rusuluhum bi'l-bayyināti fa-mā kānū li-yuʾminū bimā kadhdhabū min qabl, kadhālika yaṭbaʿu'llāhu ʿalā qulūbi'l-kāfirīn · Wa mā wajadnā li-aktharihim min ʿahd, wa in wajadnā aktharahum la-fāsiqīn*

"**Has it not been made plain to those who inherit a land after its former people that, if We willed, We could strike them for their sins and set a seal upon their hearts so that they would not hear? · Those are the towns — We recount to you something of their tidings. Their messengers did come to them with clear proofs; but they were not going to believe in what they had called a lie before. Thus does God set a seal upon the hearts of the deniers. · In most of them We found no keeping of a covenant; most of them We found to be transgressors.**"

### يَرِثُونَ ٱلْأَرْضَ مِنۢ بَعْدِ أَهْلِهَا — the addressee of the block

**The closing of the block turns from the stories to the present, and the word of the turn is *heir*.**

The root **و-ر-ث**: **to come into possession of what another has left behind him.**

**And this is the last link of the chain built up in the sūra. The chain is countable:**

| Verse | Who after whom | Expression |
|---|---|---|
| **69** | **ʿĀd**, after the people of Noah | *Jaʿalakum khulafāʾa min baʿdi qawmi Nūḥ* |
| **74** | **Thamūd**, after ʿĀd | *Jaʿalakum khulafāʾa min baʿdi ʿĀd* |
| **100** | **The addressee**, after all of them | *Yarithūna'l-arḍa min baʿdi ahlihā* |

**That is, the sūra ties to the reader, in a third link, the chain of succession it built in two stories.** **This is a weave that can be verified within the sūra, and I record it as an observation.**

**And the tense of the verb must be recorded: *yarithūna* — the imperfect, the present.** **The stories are built in the past tense, this sentence in the present.**

### ٱلطَّبْع — the seal

The root **ط-ب-ع**: **to press a stamp upon a thing, to seal it.** The lexicographers tie the root to the picture of **the stamping of coin and of wax.** ***Ṭabīʿa* (nature) is from the same root: what has been stamped on, a settled condition.**

**And the occurrences of the root in the sūra are countable: 100, 101 — and at 7/40 (in the context of *wa kadhālika najzi'l-mujrimīn*) other verbs of the same field will be used.**

**And per the method (`STYLE.md`) a note is required, and I write it plainly:** **the question of the seal is a field of *kalām* debate** (the relation between the servant's act and divine decree). **This commentary takes no side in that debate.**

**I record the one thing that can be verified from the text, and that is the order of the sentence:**

| Order | Verse | What |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **101** | *Fa-mā kānū li-yuʾminū **bimā kadhdhabū min qabl*** — **first the calling of a lie** |
| 2 | **101** | *Kadhālika **yaṭbaʿu'llāh** ʿalā qulūbi'l-kāfirīn* — **then the seal** |

**The verse places the seal after a prior calling of a lie.** **I record this as an observation about word order; I build no ruling beyond it.**

### عَهْد — the last word of the block

The root **ع-ه-د**: **to guard a thing, to watch over it, to keep one's word.** ***ʿAhd* is the word that is given, and within the root lies the idea of "watching over" — a word is given in order to be kept.**

**And the placing of the word here is worth recording, because the eighth block of the sūra (172-174) will be built precisely on this word:** there a **testimony** will be taken from man.

**I record this as my own reading and it is not binding; its ground is the words of the two blocks:** **the hundred and second verse says "in most of them We found no keeping of a covenant"; the hundred and seventy-second tells when that word was taken.** **There are seventy verses between the two, and the sūra sets down the claim first and gives its ground afterwards.** **This is an explanation, not a ruling.**

The root **ف-س-ق**: **a thing's coming out of its husk or out of its place.** The lexicographers tie the root to the picture of **a date coming out of its skin** (*fasaqa'r-ruṭabu*). **That is, a *fāsiq* is one who has come out of the place where he should be.**

**And this word closes the block: *wa in wajadnā aktharahum la-**fāsiqīn***.** **In the section on the structure of the sūra this was recorded as the closing sentence of the sixth block.**

---

## 7/103-108 — ثُمَّ بَعَثْنَا مِنۢ بَعْدِهِم مُّوسَىٰ بِـَٔايَٰتِنَآ إِلَىٰ فِرْعَوْنَ وَمَلَإِي۟هِۦ فَظَلَمُوا۟ بِهَا فَٱنظُرْ كَيْفَ كَانَ عَٰقِبَةُ ٱلْمُفْسِدِينَ

*Thumma baʿathnā min baʿdihim Mūsā bi-āyātinā ilā Firʿawna wa malaʾihī fa-ẓalamū bihā, fa'nẓur kayfa kāna ʿāqibatu'l-mufsidīn · Wa qāla Mūsā yā Firʿawnu innī rasūlun min rabbi'l-ʿālamīn · Ḥaqīqun ʿalā an lā aqūla ʿala'llāhi illa'l-ḥaqq, qad jiʾtukum bi-bayyinatin min rabbikum fa-arsil maʿiya banī Isrāʾīl · Qāla in kunta jiʾta bi-āyatin fa'ti bihā in kunta mina'ṣ-ṣādiqīn · Fa-alqā ʿaṣāhu fa-idhā hiya thuʿbānun mubīn · Wa nazaʿa yadahū fa-idhā hiya bayḍāʾu li'n-nāẓirīn*

"**Then, after them, We sent Moses with Our signs to Pharaoh and his notables. But they did wrong in the matter of those signs. See, then, what was the end of the corrupters! · Moses said: 'O Pharaoh! I am a messenger from the Lord of all the worlds. · It is incumbent on me to say nothing about God but the truth. I have brought you a clear proof from your Lord; so send the Children of Israel with me.' · He said: 'If you have brought a sign, produce it, if you are among the truthful.' · So he cast down his staff, and there it was, a plain serpent! · And he drew out his hand, and there it was, white to those who looked!**"

### The weight of the block in the sūra

**The Moses block runs from verse 103 to verse 171: sixty-nine verses.** **More than a third of the sūra.** **It was recorded in the section on the structure; here it is treated.**

**And the point at which the block departs from the earlier ones in respect of pattern must be recorded, and it is a countable datum:**

| Element | In the five stories | **In the Moses block** |
|---|---|---|
| *Yā qawmi'budū'llāha mā lakum min ilāhin ghayruh* | **4 times** | **absent** |
| *Akhāhum* | **3 times** | **absent** |
| *Al-malaʾ* | **5 times** | **present** (103, 109, 127) |
| The name of the addressee | **The name of a people** (ʿĀd, Thamūd, Madyan) | **A single person** (*Firʿawn*) |

**That is, the Moses block stands outside the shared opening.** **The same was recorded for al-Shuʿarāʾ in `026-suara.md` Table 2: there too the stories of Moses and Abraham stood outside the pattern of the opening.** **The two sūras make the same exception independently.** **This is an overlap that can be verified from the two texts, and I record it as an observation.**

### فَظَلَمُوا۟ بِهَا — the work of the preposition

**A point of word order must be recorded: the verb *ẓalama* is here made transitive with the preposition *bi-*.**

**The expected construction would be *fa-ẓalamūhā* ("they wronged them").** **The lexicographers explain the usage with *bi-* as either "they did wrong on account of them" or "they behaved wrongfully towards them"; both explanations are transmitted.**

**And the same construction had occurred in the ninth verse of the sūra:** *bimā kānū **bi-āyātinā yaẓlimūn***. **The same verb, the same preposition, the same object.** **This is a repetition that can be verified within the sūra.**

### حَقِيقٌ عَلَىٰٓ أَن لَّآ أَقُولَ — a variant reading

**Two readings are transmitted of the expression at the head of the hundred and fifth verse, and I record it because the difference affects the meaning:**

| Reading | Text | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *Ḥaqīqun **ʿalā** an lā aqūla* | "**It is incumbent to say** nothing about God but the truth" — this is what falls to me |
| 2 | *Ḥaqīqun **ʿalayya** an lā aqūla* | "It **falls to me** to say nothing about God but the truth" — the ascription is expressly to the first person |

**Both readings are transmitted and are close to one another in sense; I do not impose a preference.**

**And the work of the sentence must be recorded: the messenger sets out the truth of his word not with a proof but first with an obligation.** **The proof comes one sentence later:** *qad jiʾtukum bi-bayyinatin min rabbikum.*

### ثُعْبَان and بَيْضَآء — two signs

**ثُعْبَان** — the root **ث-ع-ب**: according to the lexicographers, **a great serpent.** **At 20/20 in `020-taha.md` and 26/32 in `026-suara.md` the difference between this word and the words *ḥayya* and *jānn* was tabulated** — **the Qurʾān uses three different words for the same event.** **I rely on those treatments and do not repeat them.**

The one thing that belongs here is which word al-Aʿrāf chooses: *thuʿbānun **mubīn*** — the same word as at al-Shuʿarāʾ 26/32.

**And a point of word order was recorded at 26/32 in `026-suara.md` and holds here too:** **the verse does not describe a transformation.** It does not say "the staff turned into a serpent"; it says ***"he cast it down — and there it was, a serpent"*** (*fa-idhā hiya*). **The process in between is not in the text.** **I rely on that treatment.**

**بَيْضَآءُ لِلنَّٰظِرِينَ** — "white to those who looked". **And the qualification must be recorded: *li'n-nāẓirīn* — the whiteness is described with reference to those who look.**

**I record this as an observation about word order:** the second sign is told not, like the first, by the condition of an object, but **by what the beholder sees.** **And at 20/22 in `020-taha.md` the same event occurs with the qualification *min ghayri sūʾin* (not from any defect).** **Al-Aʿrāf does not give that qualification; Ṭāhā does.** **This is a difference that can be verified from the two texts.**

---

## 7/109-116 — قَالَ ٱلْمَلَأُ مِن قَوْمِ فِرْعَوْنَ إِنَّ هَٰذَا لَسَٰحِرٌ عَلِيمٌ يُرِيدُ أَن يُخْرِجَكُم مِّنْ أَرْضِكُمْ فَمَاذَا تَأْمُرُونَ

*Qāla'l-malaʾu min qawmi Firʿawna inna hādhā la-sāḥirun ʿalīm · Yurīdu an yukhrijakum min arḍikum, fa-mādhā taʾmurūn · Qālū arjih wa akhāhu wa arsil fi'l-madāʾini ḥāshirīn · Yaʾtūka bi-kulli sāḥirin ʿalīm · Wa jāʾa's-saḥaratu Firʿawna qālū inna lanā la-ajran in kunnā naḥnu'l-ghālibīn · Qāla naʿam wa innakum la-mina'l-muqarrabīn · Qālū yā Mūsā immā an tulqiya wa immā an nakūna naḥnu'l-mulqīn · Qāla alqū, fa-lammā alqaw saḥarū aʿyuna'n-nāsi wa'starhabūhum wa jāʾū bi-siḥrin ʿaẓīm*

"**The notables of Pharaoh's people said: 'This is a sorcerer of knowledge; he wants to drive you out of your land. What then do you command?' · They said: 'Hold him and his brother back, and send gatherers into the cities · to bring you every sorcerer of knowledge.' · The sorcerers came to Pharaoh. They said: 'There is surely a reward for us if we are the victors?' · He said: 'Yes, and you shall certainly be among those brought near.' · They said: 'O Moses! Either you cast, or we shall be the ones who cast.' · He said: 'You cast.' And when they cast, they bewitched the eyes of the people, and struck fear into them, and produced a mighty sorcery.**"

### فَمَاذَا تَأْمُرُونَ — the same sentence in two sūras

**It was recorded at 26/35 in `026-suara.md`, and I rely on it: this sentence is word for word identical in two places in the Qurʾān** — **al-Aʿrāf 7/110 and al-Shuʿarāʾ 26/35.** **This is a datum that can be verified from the text.**

**And there is a difference of word order between the two sūras, and it must be recorded:**

| Sūra | Who speaks the sentence |
|---|---|
| `026-suara.md` **26/35** | **Pharaoh** — asking the *malaʾ* |
| **al-Aʿrāf 7/110** | ***Al-malaʾ*** — the sentence stands within the words of the notables |

**In the classical commentaries it is explained that the sentence in al-Aʿrāf may belong to Pharaoh too, the words of the notables ending in the first half of verse 110.** **Both readings are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.**

**But in both sūras the same fact is recorded, and it is beyond dispute: the decision is not taken alone, a body is consulted.** **And this accords with the *malaʾ* backbone of the sūra** (tabulated in the section on the structure).

### يُرِيدُ أَن يُخْرِجَكُم مِّنْ أَرْضِكُمْ — the third "driving out" in the sūra

**And this sentence is an accusation repeated in the sūra. It is a countable datum:**

| Verse | Who says it | Who is to be driven out |
|---|---|---|
| **82** | **The people of Lot** | *Akhrijūhum min qaryatikum* — **Lot and those with him** |
| **88** | ***Al-malaʾ*** (Madyan) | *La-nukhrijannaka yā Shuʿaybu* — **Shuʿayb and those who believed** |
| **110** | ***Al-malaʾ*** (Egypt) | *Yurīdu an yukhrijakum min arḍikum* — **the addressees themselves** |
| **123** | **Pharaoh** | *Li-tukhrijū minhā ahlahā* — **the people of the city** |

**In the first two of the four occurrences the messenger is driven out; in the last two it is claimed that the messenger will drive out the people.** **That is, the accusation changes direction.**

**I record this as my own reading and it is not binding; its ground is the subjects and objects of the four verses:** **the sūra uses the same verb both as a threat and as an accusation; and in the second case what one wants to do oneself is ascribed to the other side.** **This is an explanation, not a ruling.**

### إِنَّ لَنَا لَأَجْرًا — the record of the bargain

**A point of word order must be recorded: the first words of the sorcerers are a demand for a fee.**

**And this can be compared with the sūra's sequence of prophets. It was recorded in `026-suara.md` Table 3: in al-Shuʿarāʾ the third item of the shared threefold entry of five prophets was *wa mā asʾalukum ʿalayhi min ajrin* ("I ask you no fee for this").** **I rely on that treatment.**

| Who | Fee |
|---|---|
| **The messengers** (`026-suara.md`) | *Wa mā asʾalukum ʿalayhi min **ajr*** — **I do not ask** |
| **The sorcerers** (al-Aʿrāf 7/113) | *Inna lanā la-**ajran*** — **we do ask** |

**The same word, the reverse direction.** **I record this as an observation, and its ground is the word in the two texts.**

**And what the reward is must be recorded: *wa innakum la-mina'l-muqarrabīn* — "you shall be among those brought near".** **What is given is not goods but standing.**

### سَحَرُوٓا۟ أَعْيُنَ ٱلنَّاسِ — where the sorcery is described

**This sentence carries a description that must be recorded, and it is important in respect of word order:**

> *Saḥarū **aʿyuna'n-nās*** — **"they bewitched the eyes of the people"**

**The object is not the things cast but the eyes of the beholders.**

**I record this as an observation about word order, and its ground is the object of the sentence:** the verse does not say that a change took place in an object; **it says that the effect was upon the beholder.**

**And at 20/66 in `020-taha.md` the same event occurs in the form *yukhayyalu ilayhi min siḥrihim annahā tasʿā* ("through their sorcery it seemed to him that they were moving").** **The two sūras look the same way: al-Aʿrāf names the eye, Ṭāhā the appearance.** **This is an overlap that can be verified from the two texts.**

**وَٱسْتَرْهَبُوهُمْ** — the root **ر-ه-ب**: **fear, dread.** **Form X here states "to frighten, to strike fear".** **And the same root will return in verse 154:** *lilladhīna hum li-rabbihim **yarhabūn***. **The same root, one the frightening of people, the other awe before their Lord.**

### إِمَّآ أَن تُلْقِىَ وَإِمَّآ أَن نَّكُونَ نَحْنُ ٱلْمُلْقِينَ

**A point of word order must be recorded: the two halves of the offer are not equal.**

| Half | Construction |
|---|---|
| 1 | *An **tulqiya*** — **a verbal clause**, plain |
| 2 | *An nakūna **naḥnu**'l-mulqīn* — **a nominal clause + the pronoun of separation (*naḥnu*) + an active participle** |

**The second half is built more heavily than the first.** **The lexicographers explain such weighting as a statement of emphasis and of claim.**

**I record this as an observation about word order, and its ground is the difference of pattern between the two halves:** in the offer there is a formal courtesy, but the weight has been placed on their own side.

**And Moses's answer is a single word: *alqū* — "you cast".** **The same scene is given in three sūras in three different patterns, and this is a countable datum:**

| Sūra | The second half of the offer | Moses's answer |
|---|---|---|
| **al-Aʿrāf 7/115-116** | *An nakūna **naḥnu'l-mulqīn*** | ***Alqū*** |
| `020-taha.md` **20/65-66** | *An nakūna **awwala man alqā*** | ***Bal alqū*** |
| `026-suara.md` **26/43** | (the offer is not named) | ***Alqū mā antum mulqūn*** |

**In all three the messenger leaves the first move to the other side.** **The difference is in the addition: Ṭāhā has *bal* (rather), al-Shuʿarāʾ lengthens the sentence, al-Aʿrāf has a single word.**

---

## 7/117-126 — وَأَوْحَيْنَآ إِلَىٰ مُوسَىٰٓ أَنْ أَلْقِ عَصَاكَ فَإِذَا هِىَ تَلْقَفُ مَا يَأْفِكُونَ

*Wa awḥaynā ilā Mūsā an alqi ʿaṣāk, fa-idhā hiya talqafu mā yaʾfikūn · Fa-waqaʿa'l-ḥaqqu wa baṭala mā kānū yaʿmalūn · Fa-ghulibū hunālika wa'nqalabū ṣāghirīn · Wa ulqiya's-saḥaratu sājidīn · Qālū āmannā bi-rabbi'l-ʿālamīn · Rabbi Mūsā wa Hārūn*

"**We revealed to Moses: 'Cast down your staff.' And there it was, swallowing what they had fabricated! · So the truth came out, and what they had done came to nothing. · There they were defeated, and they turned back humbled. · And the sorcerers were cast down in prostration. · They said: 'We believe in the Lord of all the worlds — · the Lord of Moses and Aaron.'**"

### تَلْقَفُ مَا يَأْفِكُونَ

The root **ل-ق-ف**: **to snatch a thing up swiftly and swallow it.**

The root **أ-ف-ك**: **to turn a thing from its direction; and from this, a lie, a fabrication.** ***Ifk* — a word turned about, a fabrication.**

**And the choice of object must be recorded, and this is a point of word order: what is swallowed is not "what they cast" but ***what they fabricate***.**

| It could have been built thus | What is built |
|---|---|
| *Talqafu mā **alqaw*** — swallowing what they cast | ***Talqafu mā yaʾfikūn*** — swallowing **what they fabricate** |

**I record this as an observation about word order:** the verse names not the objects lying there but **what they are.** **And this accords with the description in the hundred and sixteenth verse: there too the effect was on the eye, not on the object.**

### وَأُلْقِىَ ٱلسَّحَرَةُ سَٰجِدِينَ — the passive voice

**This sentence was treated at 26/46 in `026-suara.md`, where it was recorded; I rely on that treatment:**

> It could have said *fa-sajada's-saḥaratu* ("the sorcerers prostrated themselves"); **it does not.** **The passive voice states that the prostration was not a planned decision but an irresistible response** — as though something set them down.

**And it was recorded there in addition that al-Aʿrāf 7/120 is in the same pattern.** **I do not repeat it.**

**What belongs here is that the sentence in al-Aʿrāf comes in two steps:**

| Verse | Sentence |
|---|---|
| **121** | *Āmannā bi-**rabbi'l-ʿālamīn*** |
| **122** | *Rabbi **Mūsā wa Hārūn*** |

**The second verse is an appositive to the first: the general expression is then made definite.**

**I record this as an observation about word order, and its ground is the separateness of the two verses:** **the sentence is divided; "the Lord of all the worlds" is said and there it stops, and then in a separate verse it is said whose Lord.** **The lexicographers record that this addition comes to remove every kind of obscurity from the expression.**

### Two threats, one answer

**Pharaoh's response is in three parts, and all three must be recorded:**

| Part | Verse | What |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **123** | *Āmantum bihī **qabla an ādhana lakum*** — the accusation of **believing without leave** |
| 2 | **123** | *Inna hādhā la-**makrun makartumūhu** fi'l-madīnati li-tukhrijū minhā ahlahā* — the claim of a **plot** |
| 3 | **124** | *La-uqaṭṭiʿanna aydiyakum wa arjulakum **min khilāfin** thumma la-uṣallibannakum ajmaʿīn* — the **punishment** |

**The first part is worth recording, and I give it as an observation:** the accusation is aimed not at the belief itself but at **its being without leave.** **That is, the matter is framed not as a question of belief but as a question of authority.**

**The word *makr* in the second part had occurred in the ninety-ninth verse** (*a-fa-aminū makra'llāh*). **The same word, in two different mouths.**

**مِنْ خِلَٰفٍ** — "crosswise": the cutting off of one hand and the opposite foot. **It was recorded at 26/49 in `026-suara.md`: the Qurʾān gives the same threat in the same words at al-Aʿrāf 7/124 and Ṭāhā 20/71 too.** **I rely on that treatment.**

### وَمَا تَنقِمُ مِنَّآ إِلَّآ أَنْ ءَامَنَّا

The root **ن-ق-م**: **to find a thing abhorrent and reject it; and from this, to punish, to take vengeance.** **The root was treated in `043-zuhruf.md`, where this verse was named.** **I rely on that treatment.**

**And the construction of the sentence must be recorded: negation + exception** (*mā … illā*). **In Arabic this is the most decisive way of reducing a cause to a single point.**

**That is, the answer does not put the ground of the punishment into the mouth of the other side; it leaves a single ground: faith.**

**And the word of the prayer must be recorded: *rabbanā afrigh ʿalaynā ṣabrā*.**

The root **ف-ر-غ**: **to empty a vessel, to pour out all that is in it.** **That is, what is asked for is not "give us patience" but ***"pour patience over us"*** — the picture of a vessel being emptied entire.**

**I give this as a note about the word, and its ground is the concrete sense of the root.** **And the second half of the request must also be recorded: *wa tawaffanā muslimīn* — the demand is not for deliverance but for the keeping of a state.**

**And the same scene was treated at 26/50-51 in `026-suara.md`. There the answer took the form *lā ḍayra innā ilā rabbinā munqalibūn*, and it was found that the pattern of the *lā* of generic negation (*lā al-nāfiya li'l-jins*) in the expression *lā ḍayra* removes the very notion of harm.** **I rely on that treatment.**

**The difference that belongs here is this, and it can be verified from the two texts:** **the second half of the sentence is word for word the same in the two sūras** (*innā ilā rabbinā munqalibūn*); **al-Aʿrāf does not give the first half (*lā ḍayra*).**

| | `026-suara.md` **26/50** | **al-Aʿrāf 7/125** |
|---|---|---|
| **First half** | *Lā ḍayr* — **"no harm"** | **absent** |
| **Second half** | *Innā ilā rabbinā munqalibūn* | *Innā ilā rabbinā munqalibūn* — **word for word the same** |
| **What follows** | *Innā **naṭmaʿu** an yaghfira lanā rabbunā* (26/51) — hope | *Wa mā **tanqimu** minnā illā an āmannā* (7/126) — a counter-question |

**And the word al-Aʿrāf uses is worth recording: *munqalibūn* — the root ق-ل-ب, "to turn, to be turned over".** **The same root had been used of the other side in the hundred and nineteenth verse:** *wa'**nqalabū** ṣāghirīn* — "they turned back humbled". **The same verb, two different turnings.** **This is a repetition that can be verified within the sūra.**

---

## 7/127-129 — وَقَالَ ٱلْمَلَأُ مِن قَوْمِ فِرْعَوْنَ أَتَذَرُ مُوسَىٰ وَقَوْمَهُۥ لِيُفْسِدُوا۟ فِى ٱلْأَرْضِ وَيَذَرَكَ وَءَالِهَتَكَ

*Wa qāla'l-malaʾu min qawmi Firʿawna a-tadharu Mūsā wa qawmahū li-yufsidū fi'l-arḍi wa yadharaka wa ālihatak, qāla sanuqattilu abnāʾahum wa nastaḥyī nisāʾahum wa innā fawqahum qāhirūn · Qāla Mūsā li-qawmihi'staʿīnū bi'llāhi wa'ṣbirū, inna'l-arḍa li'llāh yūrithuhā man yashāʾu min ʿibādih, wa'l-ʿāqibatu li'l-muttaqīn · Qālū ūdhīnā min qabli an taʾtiyanā wa min baʿdi mā jiʾtanā, qāla ʿasā rabbukum an yuhlika ʿaduwwakum wa yastakhlifakum fi'l-arḍi fa-yanẓura kayfa taʿmalūn*

"**The notables of Pharaoh's people said: 'Will you leave Moses and his people free to work corruption in the land and to forsake you and your gods?' He said: 'We shall slay their sons and spare their women. We hold overwhelming power above them.' · Moses said to his people: 'Seek help from God and be patient. The earth belongs to God; He gives it in inheritance to whom He wills of His servants. The outcome belongs to those who guard themselves.' · They said: 'We suffered harm before you came to us, and after you came to us.' He said: 'It may be that your Lord will destroy your enemy and make you successors in the land, and then see how you act.'**"

### On whom the word فَسَاد is laid

**This verse is a reversal that must be recorded in the sūra, and it is read entirely from the text:**

| Who | To whom *fasād* is ascribed |
|---|---|
| **The sūra's own command** (56) | *Wa lā **tufsidū** fi'l-arḍi baʿda iṣlāḥihā* — forbidden to the addressee |
| **Shuʿayb** (85) | The same sentence — forbidden to Madyan |
| **The verse's own verdict** (103) | *Fa'nẓur kayfa kāna ʿāqibatu'l-**mufsidīn*** — **of Pharaoh and his notables** |
| ***Al-malaʾ*** (127) | *Li-**yufsidū** fi'l-arḍ* — **of Moses and his people** |

**That is, in the hundred and third verse the sūra ascribes corruption to one side; in the hundred and twenty-seventh it lets the same word be heard in the mouth of the other side.**

**I record this as my own reading and it is not binding; its ground is that the two verses use the same root:** **the word is the same, the ascription reversed.** **The sūra does not interpret this; it leaves the two sentences side by side.**

**And a like attitude was recorded at 40/26 in `040-gafir.md`:** there too the decision to kill is presented **with a ground of protection**, and the word in the ground (*fasād*) was exactly the name of what was being done there. **I rely on that treatment; al-Aʿrāf builds the same structure once more.**

### وَيَذَرَكَ وَءَالِهَتَكَ — a variant reading

**Several readings are transmitted of this expression, and I record it because the difference affects the meaning:**

| Reading | Text | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *Wa **ālihatak*** | "you and **your gods**" — what Pharaoh worships |
| 2 | *Wa **ilāhatak*** | "you and **the worship paid to you**" — the service rendered to himself |

**Both readings are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.** **Both accord with the context of the sūra.**

### إِنَّ ٱلْأَرْضَ لِلَّهِ يُورِثُهَا مَن يَشَآءُ

**Moses's answer is in three parts, and the three do different work:**

| Part | Expression | What |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *Istaʿīnū bi'llāhi wa'ṣbirū* | **A command** — two verbs |
| 2 | *Inna'l-arḍa li'llāh yūrithuhā man yashāʾu min ʿibādih* | **A ruling** — to whom the dominion belongs |
| 3 | *Wa'l-ʿāqibatu li'l-muttaqīn* | **A note on the outcome** — qualified |

And the qualification of the third part must be recorded: *li'l-**muttaqīn*** — "those who guard themselves". **It does not say "yours".**

**I record this as an observation about word order, and its ground is the word itself:** **the promise is tied not to a community but to an attribute.** **And this is the in-text counterpart of the principle recorded in the section on the structure of the sūra: what is described is an attribute.**

**And the same qualification is built once more, and even more plainly, in the hundred and twenty-ninth verse:** *wa yastakhlifakum fi'l-arḍi **fa-yanẓura kayfa taʿmalūn*** — **"and then see how you act".**

**I record this as my own reading and it is not binding; its ground is the second half of the sentence:** **the promise is not a promise of unconditional supremacy.** **Succession is announced, and immediately after it a sentence of observation is placed.** **That is, deliverance is given not as the end of the test but as the beginning of a new one.**

**And the later verses of the sūra confirm this: at 138 the demand for a calf, at 148 the calf, at 163 the Sabbath, at 169 the condition of those who took over the Book.** **The sūra goes on telling what came after the deliverance.**

---

## 7/130-136 — وَلَقَدْ أَخَذْنَآ ءَالَ فِرْعَوْنَ بِٱلسِّنِينَ وَنَقْصٍ مِّنَ ٱلثَّمَرَٰتِ لَعَلَّهُمْ يَذَّكَّرُونَ

*Wa laqad akhadhnā āla Firʿawna bi's-sinīna wa naqṣin mina'th-thamarāti laʿallahum yadhdhakkarūn · Fa-idhā jāʾathumu'l-ḥasanatu qālū lanā hādhih, wa in tuṣibhum sayyiʾatun yaṭṭayyarū bi-Mūsā wa man maʿah, alā innamā ṭāʾiruhum ʿinda'llāhi wa lākinna aktharahum lā yaʿlamūn · Wa qālū mahmā taʾtinā bihī min āyatin li-tasḥaranā bihā fa-mā naḥnu laka bi-muʾminīn · Fa-arsalnā ʿalayhimu'ṭ-ṭūfāna wa'l-jarāda wa'l-qummala wa'ḍ-ḍafādiʿa wa'd-dama āyātin mufaṣṣalāt, fa'stakbarū wa kānū qawman mujrimīn*

"**We seized the people of Pharaoh with years of dearth and a shortfall of fruits — that they might take it to heart. · When good came to them they would say, 'This is ours'; and when an ill befell them they took it as an ill omen from Moses and those with him. Take note: their omen is with God; but most of them do not know. · They said: 'Whatever sign you bring us to bewitch us with, we are not going to believe in you.' · So We sent upon them the flood, the locusts, the lice, the frogs and the blood — signs set out one by one. But they were too great for them, and they were a guilty people.**"

### يَطَّيَّرُوا۟ — to take as an ill omen

The root **ط-ي-ر**: **to fly, a bird.** ***Ṭiyara* is the name of the custom of drawing a good or an ill omen from the direction in which a bird flies.** **That is, the word names the putting of an observation in the place of a decision.**

**The same root was treated at 27/47 in `027-neml.md`** (*iṭṭayyarnā bika wa bi-man maʿak* — Thamūd, of Ṣāliḥ), **where a table was drawn up; al-Aʿrāf 7/131 was named in that table.** **I rely on that treatment.**

**What belongs here is the answer the verse gives, and this is a point of word order:**

> *Alā innamā **ṭāʾiruhum ʿinda'llāh***

**The verse does not say "there is no such thing as an ill omen".** **It takes the word and changes its place:** *ṭāʾir* — "what flies, one's share of fortune" — **is with God.**

**I record this as my own reading and it is not binding; its ground is the construction of the sentence:** **what is objected to is not the search for a cause itself, but the placing of the cause in the wrong place.** **And *innamā* (the particle of restriction) fixes the cause at a single point.**

**And the first half of the sentence must also be recorded, and it is an observation about human behaviour:**

| What comes | What is said |
|---|---|
| *Al-**ḥasana*** (good) | *Lanā hādhih* — **"this is ours"** |
| *Sayyiʾa* (ill) | *Yaṭṭayyarū bi-Mūsā* — **it is attached to someone else** |

**What is good is ascribed to oneself, what is ill to the other side.** **This is a structure that can be verified from the text, and the verse gives it as a diagnosis.**

**I enter a note as a connection with the social sciences, and I give it with the qualification "this is one way of looking at it"** (`STYLE.md`): **the tendency to attribute success to oneself and failure to external factors is a pattern that has been observed and named in social psychology.** **It is an observation that overlaps with the attitude the verse describes; the verse does not set it up as a theory, it tells it as the behaviour of a community.** **I give this as a possible connection, not as a proof.**

### مَهْمَا تَأْتِنَا بِهِۦ مِنْ ءَايَةٍ — the closedness of the sentence

**The hundred and thirty-second verse is the plainest statement of closedness to be recorded in the sūra:**

> *Mahmā taʾtinā bihī min āyatin li-tasḥaranā bihā **fa-mā naḥnu laka bi-muʾminīn***

**The sentence is a conditional pattern, and the scope of the condition is left without limit: *mahmā* — "whatever you bring".**

**I record this as an observation about word order:** **the refusal is given before the proof that is to come.** **That is, the content of the proof is placed outside discussion.**

**And the same attitude was treated at 41/5 in `041-fussilet.md`** (*qulūbunā fī akinnatin … fa'mal innanā ʿāmilūn*), where the addressees' **declaring themselves closed** was recorded. **I rely on that treatment and record the difference in al-Aʿrāf:** **in Fuṣṣilat the closedness was stated as a *condition*, in al-Aʿrāf as a *decision*.**

### Five signs — and the limit of the count

**The hundred and thirty-third verse counts five things: *ṭūfān*, *jarād*, *qummal*, *ḍafādiʿ*, *dam*.**

| Word | Root | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| **ٱلطُّوفَان** | **ط-و-ف** | **What encircles and goes round** — the lexicographers generally say a flood; the explanation "a calamity that surrounds from every side" is also transmitted |
| **ٱلْجَرَاد** | **ج-ر-د** | **Locusts** — the root means "to strip a place and leave it bare" (*ajrad* — bare) |
| **ٱلْقُمَّل** | **ق-م-ل** | There is **disagreement** about it: it is explained as lice, ticks, small vermin, or grain weevils |
| **ٱلضَّفَادِع** | **ض-ف-د-ع** | **Frogs** |
| **ٱلدَّم** | **د-م-ي** | **Blood** |

**What the word *qummal* denotes is disputed, and I do not impose a preference;** **it is recorded that the root is tied to the idea of "what is small and numerous".**

**And the root of the word *jarād* is worth recording: from the same root comes *ajrad* (hairless, bare).** **That is, the name of the locust comes from the scene it leaves behind.** **This is an observation about the language.**

And the qualification the Qurʾān places on this list must be recorded: *āyātin **mufaṣṣalāt*** — **"signs set out one by one".**

The root **ف-ص-ل** was treated at the fifty-second verse; **I rely on that treatment.** **Here the word takes the five events out of being a single catastrophe and makes them signs distinguished from one another.**

**I record this as an observation about word order:** the verse stresses not the succession of the events but **their being told apart.**

**And per the method I draw a limit:** I build no explanation about the historical or natural causes of these five events; **the Qurʾān names the events and does not describe their mechanisms.** **And I do not go the way of comparing them with another religious text so as to equate or to separate them** (`STYLE.md`).

### The word given and its breaking

**The hundred and thirty-fourth and hundred and thirty-fifth verses build a *cycle*, and the steps of the cycle are countable:**

| Step | Verse | What |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **134** | *Waqaʿa ʿalayhimu'r-**rijz*** — the punishment falls |
| 2 | **134** | *Udʿu lanā rabbak* — **mediation is asked of the messenger** |
| 3 | **134** | *La-in kashafta … **la-nuʾminanna** laka wa **la-nursilanna** maʿaka banī Isrāʾīl* — **two promises are given** |
| 4 | **135** | *Fa-lammā **kashafnā** ʿanhumu'r-rijz* — **it is lifted** |
| 5 | **135** | *Idhā hum **yankuthūn*** — **the word is broken** |

The root **ن-ك-ث**: **to untwist a twisted rope, to undo it.** ***Nakatha'l-ḥabl* — he untwisted the rope.** **That is, the breaking of a word is described with the picture of something woven coming undone.**

**This is the concrete sense of the root and is worth recording:** **the verse pictures a word as a *binding*.**

**And a point of word order must be recorded: *idhā hum yankuthūn* — the *idhā* of suddenness (*idhā al-fujāʾiyya*).** **In Arabic this particle states the abrupt appearance of an unlooked-for state.** **That is, the breaking happens not at the end of a process but the moment the punishment lifts.**

And the qualification *ilā ajalin hum bālighūh* must be recorded: the lifting was **for a term.** **The verse gives the lifting not as a pardon but as a respite.**

### فَٱنتَقَمْنَا مِنْهُمْ فَأَغْرَقْنَٰهُمْ فِى ٱلْيَمِّ

The root **ي-م-م**: ***yamm*, according to the lexicographers, "the sea" or "a great body of water".** **It is also recorded that the word passed into Arabic from another language; I claim no certainty.**

**And the Qurʾān uses two different words for this event: *al-baḥr* (at 7/138, *jāwaznā bi-banī Isrāʾīla'l-baḥr*) and *al-yamm* (7/136).** **Two words two verses apart, for the same water.** **This is a countable datum.**

**And the ground of the drowning is given in the verse, and it is in two parts:** *bi-annahum **kadhdhabū** bi-āyātinā wa kānū ʿanhā **ghāfilīn***.

The root **غ-ف-ل**: **to remain unaware of a thing; and to leave a thing aside and take no interest in it.** **And the placing of the word here is worth recording: calling a lie is a *stance*, heedlessness a *condition*.** **The two are named together.**

---

## 7/137-141 — وَأَوْرَثْنَا ٱلْقَوْمَ ٱلَّذِينَ كَانُوا۟ يُسْتَضْعَفُونَ مَشَٰرِقَ ٱلْأَرْضِ وَمَغَٰرِبَهَا ٱلَّتِى بَٰرَكْنَا فِيهَا

*Wa awrathna'l-qawma'lladhīna kānū yustaḍʿafūna mashāriqa'l-arḍi wa maghāribaha'llatī bāraknā fīhā, wa tammat kalimatu rabbika'l-ḥusnā ʿalā banī Isrāʾīla bimā ṣabarū, wa dammarnā mā kāna yaṣnaʿu Firʿawnu wa qawmuhū wa mā kānū yaʿrishūn*

"**And We gave the people who had been made weak the easts and the wests of the land We had blessed as their inheritance. The good word of your Lord to the Children of Israel was fulfilled, because they had been patient. And We levelled what Pharaoh and his people had been making and what they had raised up.**"

### بَٰرَكْنَا فِيهَا — where the blessing is attached

**This expression was treated at 17/1 in `017-isra.md`, where it was recorded; I rely on that treatment:** **the blessing is attached not to the soil itself but to what is in it.** **The same expression is used in the Qurʾān for this region in other places too** (al-Anbiyāʾ 21/71, 21/81). **I do not repeat it.**

**And the note that belongs here is this:** **the verse gives no place-name.** *Mashāriqa'l-arḍi wa maghāribahā* — **"the easts of the land and its wests".** **No border is drawn.**

**I record this as an observation about word order:** **the text does not set up a geographical title deed; it states an outcome.** **And per the method I write plainly: no support for any present-day political claim is to be drawn from this verse.** **This commentary takes no side in current politics** (`STYLE.md`).

**And the qualification the verse itself lays down must be recorded: *bimā ṣabarū* — "because they had been patient".** **The inheritance is tied not to a lineage but to a conduct.** **And this lies on the same line as the note *wa'l-ʿāqibatu li'l-muttaqīn* in the hundred and twenty-eighth verse.**

**And the note *fa-yanẓura kayfa taʿmalūn* in the hundred and twenty-ninth verse is at work here too: the sūra will show the same community, a few verses later, asking for a calf** (138). **The text does not conceal it.**

### دَمَّرْنَا and يَعْرِشُونَ

The root **د-م-ر**: **to destroy, to level.**

The root **ع-ر-ش**: **to raise a thing up, to build a bower or a vault.** ***The word ʿarsh* is from the same root** — **and it occurred in the fifty-fourth verse.**

**This is a weave that can be verified within the sūra, and it must be recorded:**

| Verse | Word | Whose |
|---|---|---|
| **54** | *Thumma'stawā ʿala'l-**ʿarsh*** | **God** |
| **137** | *Wa mā kānū **yaʿrishūn*** | **Pharaoh and his people** |

**The same root, two different heights.** **And a note of *tanzīh* was entered concerning the first** (in the section on 54); **the second is levelled to the ground.**

**I record this as my own reading and it is not binding; its ground is the two occurrences of the root:** **the sūra uses a word from the same root both as the announcement of sovereignty and as the name of the structures that are brought down.** **This is an explanation, not a ruling.**

### ٱجْعَل لَّنَآ إِلَٰهًا كَمَا لَهُمْ ءَالِهَةٌ

**The hundred and thirty-eighth verse comes immediately after the crossing of the sea, and a point of word order must be recorded: there is no verse between the two events.**

| Verse | What happens |
|---|---|
| **138a** | *Wa **jāwaznā** bi-banī Isrāʾīla'l-baḥr* — the sea is crossed |
| **138b** | *Fa-**ataw** ʿalā qawmin yaʿkufūna ʿalā aṣnāmin lahum* — a people is come upon |
| **138c** | *Qālū yā Mūsa'**jʿal lanā ilāhan** kamā lahum āliha* — the demand comes |

**Three sentences, one verse.** **I record this as an observation about word order, and its ground is the verse itself:** **the ground of the demand is not a need but a *seeing*.** *Kamā lahum* — "like theirs".

**And the scene of the calf was treated at 20/88 in `020-taha.md`.** **The difference here must be recorded: at 138 there is as yet no calf; only a demand.** **The calf will come at verse 148.** **That is, the sūra puts the demand and its fulfilment in different places, and between them stands Moses's departure (142).**

The root **ع-ك-ف**: **to stand constantly by a thing, not to leave it.** **The root was treated at 20/91 in `020-taha.md` and at 26/71 in `026-suara.md`; I rely on those treatments.**

### مُتَبَّرٌ مَّا هُمْ فِيهِ

The root **ت-ب-ر**: **to be broken to pieces, to be reduced to fragments.** ***Tibr* — unworked, scattered gold.** **That is, the word carries the idea of "valuable but scattered, without wholeness".**

**And the construction of the answer must be recorded, and this is a point of word order: before rejecting what is asked for, Moses characterises what is being taken as a model.**

| Order | Sentence | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *Innakum qawmun **tajhalūn*** (138) | **A diagnosis** — the source of the demand |
| 2 | *Inna hāʾulāʾi **mutabbarun** mā hum fīhi wa **bāṭilun** mā kānū yaʿmalūn* (139) | **An assessment of the model** |
| 3 | *A-**ghayra'llāhi** abghīkum ilāhan wa huwa **faḍḍalakum** ʿala'l-ʿālamīn* (140) | **A question** — and a reminder |

**That is, the answer is in three steps, and the three do different work.** **I record this as an observation about word order, and its ground is the three verses themselves.**

**And the word in the third step must be recorded: *faḍḍalakum ʿala'l-ʿālamīn*.** **This expression occurs in several places in the Qurʾān of the Children of Israel** (al-Baqara 2/47, 2/122; al-Jāthiya 45/16) **and was treated at 2/47 in `002-bakara.md`.** **I rely on that treatment.**

**And the note entered there holds here too, and I repeat it, because it is required by the method:** **the expression does not establish a superiority of lineage; it states a means that was given.** **And the sūra itself will divide this expressly in verses 159 and 168: within the same community groups will be counted, separated out as *ummatun yahdūna bi'l-ḥaqq* (159) and *minhum dūna dhālik* (168).** **No blanket judgement is issued about an ethnic or religious group** (`STYLE.md`).

### 7/141 — the shift in the direction of address

> *Wa idh **anjaynākum** min āli Firʿawna yasūmūnakum sūʾa'l-ʿadhāb, yuqattilūna abnāʾakum wa yastaḥyūna nisāʾakum, wa fī dhālikum balāʾun min rabbikum ʿaẓīm*
>
> "**Remember when We delivered you from the people of Pharaoh, who were inflicting on you the worst of punishment: slaying your sons and sparing your women. In that there was a mighty test from your Lord.**"

**A point of word order must be recorded, and this is the most striking transition in the block: the verse passes from the third person to the second.**

| Verses | Person | Example |
|---|---|---|
| **137-140** | **Third person** | *Wa awrathna'l-qawma'lladhīna kānū yustaḍʿafūn* — "that people" |
| **141** | **Second person** | *Wa idh **anjaynākum*** — "We delivered you" |

**This is *iltifāt* (a shift in the direction of address), and it was treated in many places in the index.**

**And its work here is worth recording, and I give it as my own reading:** **the story being told is turned, in a single sentence, directly upon the addressee.** **And straight after this verse come Moses's departure (142) and the calf (148).** **That is, immediately after saying "this was done to you", the sūra tells "what you did".**

**And the word of the last sentence must be recorded: *wa fī dhālikum balāʾun min rabbikum ʿaẓīm*.**

The root **ب-ل-و**: **to try, to test; and to wear a thing out.** ***Balāʾ* in Arabic takes in both the good and the ill** — **"one is tested with bounty as well as with hardship".** **This was recorded in many places in the index.**

**And the object of the word here is worth recording: the sentence counts the deliverance itself as a test.** **This lies on the same line as the two-stage test in the ninety-fifth verse:** there hardship and ease, here affliction and deliverance.

---

## 7/142-143 — وَوَٰعَدْنَا مُوسَىٰ ثَلَٰثِينَ لَيْلَةً وَأَتْمَمْنَٰهَا بِعَشْرٍ فَتَمَّ مِيقَٰتُ رَبِّهِۦٓ أَرْبَعِينَ لَيْلَةً · وَلَمَّا جَآءَ مُوسَىٰ لِمِيقَٰتِنَا وَكَلَّمَهُۥ رَبُّهُۥ قَالَ رَبِّ أَرِنِىٓ أَنظُرْ إِلَيْكَ قَالَ لَن تَرَىٰنِى

*Wa wāʿadnā Mūsā thalāthīna laylatan wa atmamnāhā bi-ʿashrin fa-tamma mīqātu rabbihī arbaʿīna layla, wa qāla Mūsā li-akhīhi Hārūna'khlufnī fī qawmī wa aṣliḥ wa lā tattabiʿ sabīla'l-mufsidīn · Wa lammā jāʾa Mūsā li-mīqātinā wa kallamahū rabbuhū qāla rabbi arinī anẓur ilayk, qāla lan tarānī wa lākini'nẓur ila'l-jabali fa-ini'staqarra makānahū fa-sawfa tarānī, fa-lammā tajallā rabbuhū li'l-jabali jaʿalahū dakkan wa kharra Mūsā ṣaʿiqā, fa-lammā afāqa qāla subḥānaka tubtu ilayka wa anā awwalu'l-muʾminīn*

"**We appointed for Moses thirty nights and completed them with ten more; so the term set by his Lord was completed at forty nights. Moses said to his brother Aaron: 'Take my place among my people, set things right, and do not follow the way of the corrupters.' · And when Moses came at the time We appointed and his Lord spoke to him, he said: 'My Lord! Show me Yourself, that I may look at You.' He said: 'You will never see Me. But look at the mountain; if it stays firm in its place, you will see Me.' And when his Lord manifested Himself to the mountain He made it crumble to dust, and Moses fell down in a faint. When he came to, he said: 'Glory be to You! I turn to You in repentance; I am the first of the believers.'**"

### ثَلَٰثِينَ لَيْلَةً وَأَتْمَمْنَٰهَا بِعَشْرٍ — the way the number is given

**A point of word order must be recorded, and this is the most striking place in the verse: the term is not stated all at once.**

| It could have been built thus | What is built |
|---|---|
| *Wa wāʿadnā Mūsā **arbaʿīna laylatan*** | *Thalāthīna laylatan **wa atmamnāhā bi-ʿashrin** fa-tamma … arbaʿīna layla* |

**That is, the verse says thirty + ten = forty.** **And it states the sum itself.**

**I record this as an observation about word order, and its ground is the construction of the sentence:** **the telling gives the term not as a whole but as a *completing*.** **And the verb used confirms it: *atmamnāhā* — "We completed it"; and *fa-**tamma*** — "so it was completed".** **The same root (ت-م-م) twice.**

**And the choice of the word *layla* (night) is worth recording: it counts not "days" but "nights".** **This is one of the common ways of counting a span in Arabic, and the lexicographers record it separately.**

**Several explanations of the reason for the division between thirty and ten are transmitted in the classical commentaries.** **I do not convey them, because I cannot establish the soundness of any of them, and the Qurʾān states no reason** (`STYLE.md`). **The one thing that can be verified from the text is that the division is there.**

### ٱخْلُفْنِى فِى قَوْمِى — the three instructions given to Aaron

**What Moses says to his brother is in three parts, and all three must be recorded:**

| # | Command | Root | What |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | ***Ukhlufnī** fī qawmī* | **خ-ل-ف** | **Take my place** |
| 2 | *Wa **aṣliḥ*** | **ص-ل-ح** | **Set things right** |
| 3 | *Wa lā **tattabiʿ** sabīla'l-mufsidīn* | **ت-ب-ع** | **Do not follow the way of the corrupters** |

**And the opposition of two words must be recorded: *aṣliḥ* (set right) and *al-mufsidīn* (the corrupters) — the roots ص-ل-ح and ف-س-د.** **They are set facing each other in the same sentence.**

**And this pair had occurred twice earlier in the sūra** (56 and 85: *wa lā tufsidū fi'l-arḍi **baʿda iṣlāḥihā***). **That is, the sūra uses its own measure a third time, this time in the instruction a prophet gives to his brother.** **This is a repetition that can be verified within the sūra.**

**And the presence of the third command is worth recording, and I give it as an observation:** **the instruction is not only "take my place"; it carries a prohibition as well.** **The text does not say the reason for this prohibition, and I invent no reason.**

### رَبِّ أَرِنِىٓ أَنظُرْ إِلَيْكَ — the undoing of the words

**أَرِنِى** — the root **ر-أ-ي**, the imperative of Form IV: **"show me".** **No object is stated;** the sentence is not *arinī **nafsak*** ("show me Yourself") but only *arinī*. **That the object has been elided is understood from the second clause (*anẓur ilayk*).**

**أَنظُرْ إِلَيْكَ** — the root **ن-ظ-ر**: **to look, to turn the eye towards something.** **The verb is in the jussive: it comes as the answer to the request** ("show me **that** I may look").

**And the difference between the two roots must be recorded, and it is a datum of the language:**

| Root | Verb | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| **ر-أ-ي** | *Raʾā* | **To see** — it states the outcome |
| **ن-ظ-ر** | *Naẓara* | **To look** — it states the turning, and does not entail seeing |

**The request uses both at once: show (seeing) and let me look (turning).**

### The disagreement — the question of the vision (*ruʾya*)

**This verse is one of the most debated verses in the history of Islamic thought, and the debate is a debate of *kalām*.** **Per the method (`STYLE.md`) I impose no preference here, take no side, and enter into no polemic.** **I give the views, together with their grounds, in a table.**

**The subject of the debate is this: what does the sentence *lan tarānī* ("you will never see Me") negate?**

| # | Reading | Its ground | The difficulty raised against this reading |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Seeing is not possible in this world; the negation looks to this world.** The state in the hereafter is not the subject of this verse | The context in which the particle *lan* is used is this world; no distinction between this world and the hereafter is made in the verse, but the context is this world. And it is argued that there are other verses in the Qurʾān (al-Qiyāma 75/22-23) | The verse sets down no qualification of time; the qualification is brought from outside |
| 2 | **Seeing is absolutely impossible;** the negation covers every case | That the particle *lan* states certainty and perpetuity; and the sentence *lā tudrikuhu'l-abṣār* at al-Anʿām 6/103 | The view that the particle *lan* states eternal negation is disputed among the lexicographers |
| 3 | **What is negated is seeing in the form requested** — that is, an encompassing, comprehending vision | That the sentence comes as the answer to the request; and that the example of the mountain is built as a question of *withstanding* (*fa-ini'staqarra*) | There is no such qualification in the wording of the sentence |

**All three readings have been voiced in the classical sources, and each has its own chain of evidence.** **I make no preference and set no school's view above another's.**

**And I write this plainly: this verse has throughout history been a distinguishing question among the schools of *kalām*.** **In this commentary it is not said on which side of that division one stands.**

**I record separately the things that can be verified from the text, because these are not disputed:**

| # | Datum of the text |
|---|---|
| 1 | **The request comes from a prophet** and is refused; **but the request is not censured** — there is no sentence of rebuke in the verse |
| 2 | **The answer is not a plain "no":** *lan tarānī* is said and **immediately after it a direction** comes — *wa lākini'nẓur ila'l-jabal* |
| 3 | **The measure is tied to *istiqrār*:** *fa-ini'staqarra makānahū fa-sawfa tarānī* — the condition of being able to **stay** in place |
| 4 | **The mountain cannot stay:** *jaʿalahū dakkā* |
| 5 | **Moses's response is a fainting** (*kharra … ṣaʿiqā*), not an objection |
| 6 | **His words after coming to are a glorification and a repentance**, not a renewed request |

**And I enter a note of *tanzīh* about the word *tajallī*, and I do not open the discussion:**

The root **ج-ل-و**: **a thing's coming into the open, the lifting of a covering, brightness becoming visible.** **In ascribing the word to God, the note entered for the Throne at 40/7 in `040-gafir.md` holds here too:** **the expression cannot be understood so as to ascribe place, body or removal from one place to another to God.** **In the classical commentaries two attitudes are transmitted for expressions of this kind, *tafwīḍ* and *taʾwīl*; I convey both and impose no preference.**

And a point of word order must be recorded: *tajallā rabbuhū **li'l-jabal*** — the one addressed by the manifestation is **the mountain**, not Moses. **This is a datum that can be read from the text.**

**دَكًّا** — the root **د-ك-ك**: **to level a thing to the ground, to crumble it.** **A variant reading is transmitted: *dakkan* and *dakkāʾan*.** **The difference in sense is slight; both mean "shattered to pieces / levelled flat".**

**صَعِقًا** — the root **ص-ع-ق**: **to be struck by lightning; and from this, to faint, to lose consciousness.**

### أَنَا۠ أَوَّلُ ٱلْمُؤْمِنِينَ — the meaning of the sentence

**The lexicographers and the exegetes dwell on this sentence too, and I give the disagreement in a table:**

| Reading | What *awwalu'l-muʾminīn* means |
|---|---|
| 1 | **First in time** — the first to believe among his own people |
| 2 | **Foremost in rank** — at the head of faith |
| 3 | **First in this particular matter** — "I am the first of those who believe that You cannot be seen" |

**All three explanations are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.**

**And it must be recorded that the same expression occurs at 26/51 in `026-suara.md` in the mouth of the sorcerers:** *an kunnā **awwala'l-muʾminīn***. **The same pattern in two places, in two different mouths.** **This is an overlap that can be verified from the two texts.**

---

## 7/144-147 — قَالَ يَٰمُوسَىٰٓ إِنِّى ٱصْطَفَيْتُكَ عَلَى ٱلنَّاسِ بِرِسَٰلَٰتِى وَبِكَلَٰمِى فَخُذْ مَآ ءَاتَيْتُكَ وَكُن مِّنَ ٱلشَّٰكِرِينَ

*Qāla yā Mūsā innī'ṣṭafaytuka ʿala'n-nāsi bi-risālātī wa bi-kalāmī fa-khudh mā ātaytuka wa kun mina'sh-shākirīn · Wa katabnā lahū fi'l-alwāḥi min kulli shayʾin mawʿiẓatan wa tafṣīlan li-kulli shayʾin fa-khudhhā bi-quwwatin wa'mur qawmaka yaʾkhudhū bi-aḥsanihā, sa-urīkum dāra'l-fāsiqīn*

"**He said: 'O Moses! I have chosen you above mankind with My messages and with My speech to you. So take what I have given you and be among the thankful.' · And We wrote for him on the tablets an admonition about everything and a setting out in detail of everything: 'Take these with strength, and command your people to take the best of them. I shall show you the abode of the transgressors.'**"

### فَخُذْ مَآ ءَاتَيْتُكَ and فَخُذْهَا بِقُوَّةٍ — "to take hold"

**In these two verses the same verb (أ-خ-ذ) occurs three times, and each time it states a command to *take hold*:**

| Verse | Expression | To whom |
|---|---|---|
| **144** | *Fa-**khudh** mā ātaytuk* | **To Moses** |
| **145** | *Fa-**khudhhā** bi-quwwatin* | **To Moses** |
| **145** | *Wa'mur qawmaka **yaʾkhudhū** bi-aḥsanihā* | **To the people** |

**And the same pattern will come once more in the hundred and seventy-first verse of the sūra: *khudhū mā ātaynākum bi-quwwatin*.** **That is, the same command at the beginning and at the end of the block.**

**This command was treated at 2/63 in `002-bakara.md`, where it was recorded; I rely on that treatment:** **"It is said that to hold the Book in hand requires not a loose contact but a force of grip. What is held loosely does not stay in the hand."**

**And what belongs here is this, and it connects with the sūra as a whole — I record it as my own reading, and it is not binding:** **the backbone recorded in the section on the structure of the sūra was "the passing of what was given out of the hand".** **The command in the hundred and forty-fifth verse is exactly the reverse of that: *bi-quwwatin* — to hold fast.** **And in the hundred and seventy-fifth verse the sūra will tell the opposite: the man who slipped out of his signs.**

### بِأَحْسَنِهَا — a question of word order

**One expression in the hundred and forty-fifth verse draws notice: what the people are commanded is to take *the best* of the tablets.**

**The exegetes dwell on this expression, and I give the disagreement in a table:**

| Reading | What *aḥsanihā* means | Ground |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **All that is in them is good; *aḥsan* here states not a comparison but an attribute** | That in Arabic the elative is also used for an attribute without comparison |
| 2 | **Between dispensation (*rukhṣa*) and the stricter course (*ʿazīma*), to take the higher** | That there is a distinction between what the text permits and what it recommends |
| 3 | **Between what is commanded and what is merely allowed, to take what is commanded** | The application of the same distinction to kinds of ruling |

**All three explanations are transmitted; I do not impose a preference, and I draw no legal rule from this** (`STYLE.md`).

### سَأَصْرِفُ عَنْ ءَايَٰتِىَ ٱلَّذِينَ يَتَكَبَّرُونَ

The root **ص-ر-ف** occurs three times in the sūra, and its table was given at the fifty-eighth verse. **This is the third occurrence.**

**And the qualification of the sentence must be recorded: *alladhīna yatakabbarūna fi'l-arḍi bi-ghayri'l-ḥaqq*.**

**I record this as an observation about word order, and its ground is the relative clause:** **the turning away is tied not to a group as a name but to a behaviour.** **And the word connects with the second block of the sūra:** **the same root had been used of Iblīs too** — *fa-mā yakūnu laka an **tatakabbara** fīhā* (13).

**The same verb, two different subjects.** **This is a repetition that can be verified within the sūra.**

**And in the continuation of the hundred and forty-sixth verse a table of seeing and of way is built, and this table is read directly from the text:**

| What is seen | What is done |
|---|---|
| *Kulla āyatin* — **every sign** | *Lā yuʾminū bihā* |
| *Sabīla'r-rushd* — **the way of rightness** | *Lā yattakhidhūhu sabīlā* |
| *Sabīla'l-ghayy* — **the way of error** | *Yattakhidhūhu sabīlā* |

**Three things are seen, and to the three three different responses are given.** **And the verb is the same: *yattakhidhū* (to take up).** **That is, the verse speaks not of blindness but of choice.**

**I record this as my own reading and it is not binding; its ground is the threefold structure.** **And the same structure was recorded at 41/17 in `041-fussilet.md`** (*fa'staḥabbu'l-ʿamā ʿala'l-hudā* — they preferred blindness to guidance). **I rely on that treatment: the two sūras build the same fact with two different words.**

---

## 7/148-151 — وَٱتَّخَذَ قَوْمُ مُوسَىٰ مِنۢ بَعْدِهِۦ مِنْ حُلِيِّهِمْ عِجْلًا جَسَدًا لَّهُۥ خُوَارٌ

*Wa'ttakhadha qawmu Mūsā min baʿdihī min ḥuliyyihim ʿijlan jasadan lahū khuwār, a-lam yaraw annahū lā yukallimuhum wa lā yahdīhim sabīlā, ittakhadhūhu wa kānū ẓālimīn · Wa lammā suqiṭa fī aydīhim wa raʾaw annahum qad ḍallū qālū la-in lam yarḥamnā rabbunā wa yaghfir lanā la-nakūnanna mina'l-khāsirīn*

"**After Moses had gone, his people made out of their ornaments a calf, a body that lowed. Did they not see that it did not speak to them and did not guide them to any way? They took it, and they became wrongdoers. · And when it was brought home to them and they saw that they had gone astray, they said: 'If our Lord does not have mercy on us and forgive us, we shall surely be among the lost.'**"

### جَسَد and خُوَار

The root **ج-س-د**. **It was recorded in `021-enbiya.md`, and I rely on that treatment:** **the lexicographers record a distinction between *jasad* and *jism* — *jasad* is used of a body with no sign of life in it.**

The root **خ-و-ر**: **the lowing of cattle.** **It was recorded at 20/88 in `020-taha.md`: the word occurs in the Qurʾān only here and at Ṭāhā 20/88.** **I rely on that treatment.**

**And the coming together of the two words must be recorded, and this is a point of word order:**

| Word | What it states |
|---|---|
| ***Jasadan*** | **Lifelessness** |
| ***Lahū khuwār*** | **A sound** |

**The verse gives both in the same sentence: a lifeless body and a sound.** **I record this as an observation, and its ground is the verse's own words.**

**And how the sound came about is not stated in the verse.** **Detailed explanations are conveyed in the classical sources; this commentary does not enter into them, because the text does not, and their soundness cannot be established** (`STYLE.md`).

### أَلَمْ يَرَوْا۟ أَنَّهُۥ لَا يُكَلِّمُهُمْ وَلَا يَهْدِيهِمْ سَبِيلًا

**The proof the verse builds must be recorded, and it is one of the most concrete measures in the sūra: two lacks are counted.**

| # | Lack | Where its counterpart stands in the sūra |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *Lā **yukallimuhum*** — **it does not speak** | **143**: *wa **kallamahū** rabbuh* — his Lord spoke to Moses |
| 2 | *Lā **yahdīhim** sabīlā* — **it does not guide to a way** | **146**: *sabīla'r-rushd* / *sabīla'l-ghayy* — the question of the way |

**I record this as my own reading and it is not binding; its ground is the words of the two verses:** **the verse compares the calf with what was told five verses earlier.** **There there was speech; here there is none.** **And the measure is not the look of what is worshipped but what it can do.**

**And this measure will be repeated and widened in the last block of the sūra** (7/191-198): there what those called upon can do will be counted one by one. **That link will close there.**

### وَلَمَّا سُقِطَ فِىٓ أَيْدِيهِمْ — an idiom

**This expression is an idiom in Arabic, and the lexicographers dwell on it.** **Word for word: "it was dropped into their hands".** **As an idiom its sense is: severe regret and bewilderment.**

**And the verb's being passive must be recorded: *suqiṭa* — "it was dropped".** **Who dropped it is not stated.**

**This is the word's only occurrence in the Qurʾān. This is a countable datum.**

**I give this as a note about the language, and I convey as transmitted the explanation that renders the picture of the idiom thus:** **the regretful person biting his hand or striking hand upon hand — the bodily counterpart of regret.**

### 7/150-151 — the scene with Aaron

> *Wa lammā rajaʿa Mūsā ilā qawmihī **ghaḍbāna asifan** qāla biʾsamā khalaftumūnī min baʿdī, a-ʿajiltum amra rabbikum, **wa alqa'l-alwāḥa wa akhadha bi-raʾsi akhīhi yajurruhū ilayh**, qāla'bna umma inna'l-qawma'staḍʿafūnī wa kādū yaqtulūnanī fa-lā tushmit biya'l-aʿdāʾa wa lā tajʿalnī maʿa'l-qawmi'ẓ-ẓālimīn*
>
> "**When Moses returned to his people, angry and grieved, he said: 'How evil is what you have done after me! Were you in haste over the command of your Lord?' He put down the tablets and took hold of his brother's head, pulling him towards himself. [Aaron] said: 'Son of my mother! The people reckoned me weak and were about to kill me. Do not let the enemies gloat over me, and do not place me with the wrongdoing people.'**"

**This scene was treated in detail at 20/92-94 in `020-taha.md`, and I rely on that treatment. A summary of what was recorded there:**
