- **Ṭāhā 20/90, by saying *min qablu* (earlier), puts Aaron's warning back into the telling of the events; that is, when the reader hears Moses's question he already knows the answer.**
- **The two things Aaron asked of his people (*ittibāʿ* and *iṭāʿa*) are expressed in the same words as what Moses asked of Aaron.**
- **In Ṭāhā the address is *yabnaʾumma* ("son of my mother"), and it was treated there.**

**Now I work out the difference between the two sūras, and it can be verified from the two texts:**

| | `020-taha.md` **20/92-94** | **al-Aʿrāf 7/150** |
|---|---|---|
| **Moses's question** | *Yā Hārūnu **mā manaʿaka** idh raʾaytahum ḍallū, allā tattabiʿani, **a-fa-ʿaṣayta amrī*** — a questioning about **obedience** | ***Biʾsamā khalaftumūnī** min baʿdī, **a-ʿajiltum** amra rabbikum* — a **reproach** directed at the people |
| **The act** | (the taking hold is named: *akhadha bi-raʾsi akhīh* — before 20/94) | *Wa **alqa'l-alwāḥa** wa **akhadha bi-raʾsi akhīhi yajurruhū ilayh*** |
| **Aaron's answer** | *Innī **khashītu** an taqūla **farraqta bayna banī Isrāʾīla** wa lam tarqub qawlī* — a **fear of division** | *Inna'l-qawma'**staḍʿafūnī** wa kādū **yaqtulūnanī*** — a statement of **want of power** |
| **The final request** | (none) | *Fa-lā **tushmit** biya'l-aʿdāʾa wa lā tajʿalnī **maʿa'l-qawmi'ẓ-ẓālimīn*** |

**That is, the two sūras cut the same scene at two different places.** **I record this as an observation, and I give it not as a contradiction but as a choice of detail** — **the same method was recorded at 20/20 in `020-taha.md`.**

**ٱلْأَسَف** — **the root أ-س-ف.** **It was recorded at 12/84 in `012-yusuf.md` and at 43/55 in `043-zuhruf.md`, and I rely on those treatments:** **when the word is used of Jacob it means *grief*, and when it is used of Moses (*ghaḍbāna asifā* — al-Aʿrāf 7/150; Ṭāhā 20/86) it means grief mixed with anger. I do not repeat it.**

**And the coming together of two words in al-Aʿrāf must be recorded: *ghaḍbāna* (angry) and *asifan* (grieved).** **Two states are named together.**

**وَأَلْقَى ٱلْأَلْوَاحَ** — **and the verb must be recorded: *alqā*.** **This is a verb that has already occurred three times in the sūra** (7/107: *fa-**alqā** ʿaṣāh*; 7/115-116: *immā an **tulqiya*** / *alqū*; 7/117: *an **alqi** ʿaṣāk*). **The fourth occurrence is here, and its object is the tablets.**

**I record this as my own reading and it is not binding; its ground is the four occurrences of the verb:** **the same verb had three times been used for the showing of a sign; at the fourth what was given is set down.** **And it will be taken up again in the hundred and fifty-fourth verse: *akhadha'l-alwāḥ*.** **This is an explanation, not a ruling.**

**لَا تُشْمِتْ بِىَ ٱلْأَعْدَآءَ** — the root **ش-م-ت**: **to rejoice at the ill that befalls another.** **The word occurs only here in the Qurʾān. This is a countable datum.**

**And Aaron's last sentence must be recorded: *wa lā tajʿalnī maʿa'l-qawmi'ẓ-ẓālimīn*.** **This sentence had occurred word for word in the forty-seventh verse of the sūra** — **in the mouth of the people of *al-Aʿrāf*:** *rabbanā lā tajʿalnā **maʿa'l-qawmi'ẓ-ẓālimīn***.

| Verse | Who says it | To whom |
|---|---|---|
| **47** | **The people of *al-Aʿrāf*** | **To the Lord** |
| **150** | **Aaron** | **To Moses** |

**The same sentence, two different addressees.** **This is a repetition that can be verified within the sūra, and it is added to the repetitions counted in the section on the structure.**

### 7/151 — the scope of the prayer

> *Qāla rabbi'ghfir lī wa li-akhī wa adkhilnā fī raḥmatik, wa anta **arḥamu'r-rāḥimīn***

**A point of word order must be recorded: the prayer begins with himself.** *Ighfir **lī** wa li-akhī* — first himself, then his brother.

**I record this as an observation about word order and build no ruling on it:** **the order of the sentence is read from the text; its reason is not stated in the text.**

**And the verse-ending must be recorded: *arḥamu'r-rāḥimīn* — in the section on the structure of the sūra it was named as one of the short closings scattered among the long verses.** **The same expression will come once more at 7/155, in the form *khayru'l-ghāfirīn*, together with its fellow.**

---

## 7/152-154 — إِنَّ ٱلَّذِينَ ٱتَّخَذُوا۟ ٱلْعِجْلَ سَيَنَالُهُمْ غَضَبٌ مِّن رَّبِّهِمْ وَذِلَّةٌ فِى ٱلْحَيَوٰةِ ٱلدُّنْيَا وَكَذَٰلِكَ نَجْزِى ٱلْمُفْتَرِينَ

*Inna'lladhīna'ttakhadhu'l-ʿijla sa-yanāluhum ghaḍabun min rabbihim wa dhillatun fi'l-ḥayāti'd-dunyā, wa kadhālika najzi'l-muftarīn · Wa'lladhīna ʿamilu's-sayyiʾāti thumma tābū min baʿdihā wa āmanū inna rabbaka min baʿdihā la-ghafūrun raḥīm · Wa lammā sakata ʿan Mūsa'l-ghaḍabu akhadha'l-alwāḥ, wa fī nuskhatihā hudan wa raḥmatun lilladhīna hum li-rabbihim yarhabūn*

"**Those who took the calf: anger from their Lord and abasement in the life of this world will reach them. Thus do We recompense those who invent falsehood. · And as for those who do evil deeds and then repent afterwards and believe: your Lord, after that, is indeed forgiving and merciful. · And when the anger fell silent in Moses he took up the tablets. In their writing there was guidance and mercy for those who stand in awe of their Lord.**"

### The punishment and the door set side by side

**A point of word order must be recorded, and this is one of the most striking sequences in the sūra: the hundred and fifty-second verse announces a punishment, and the hundred and fifty-third opens a door immediately after it.**

| Verse | What is stated |
|---|---|
| **152** | *Sa-yanāluhum **ghaḍabun** … wa **dhillatun*** — **punishment** |
| **153** | *Thumma **tābū** … wa **āmanū** … la-**ghafūrun raḥīm*** — **the possibility of return** |

**I record this as my own reading and it is not binding; its ground is the order of the two verses:** **the sūra does not close the door after announcing the punishment.** **And the same structure was present in the second block of the sūra too: Adam broke the prohibition (22), then confessed (23), and the prayer was received.**

**And the sequence in the hundred and fifty-third verse must be recorded, and it is in three steps:**

| Step | Verb |
|---|---|
| 1 | *ʿAmilu's-sayyiʾāt* — **it was done** |
| 2 | *Thumma **tābū*** — **there was a turning back** |
| 3 | *Wa **āmanū*** — **there was belief** |

**And the particle *thumma* comes before the second step: the interval between them is stated.**

### سَكَتَ عَن مُّوسَى ٱلْغَضَبُ — a choice of word order

**The construction of the hundred and fifty-fourth verse is a place the lexicographers particularly dwell on, and it must be recorded.**

The root **س-ك-ت**: **to fall silent, to stop speaking.**

| It could have been built thus | What is built |
|---|---|
| *Wa lammā **sakana** ghaḍabu Mūsā* — when his anger subsided | ***Wa lammā sakata ʿan Mūsa'l-ghaḍab*** — **"the anger fell silent in Moses"** |

**There are two differences at once, and both are read from the text:**

**One — the verb used is not "to subside" but "to fall silent".** **That is, the anger is told of as something that speaks.**

**Two — the subject of the sentence is *al-ghaḍab*, not Moses.** **And the preposition is *ʿan* (from): the anger departs from Moses.**

**I record this as my own reading and it is not binding; its ground is the choice of verb and the preposition:** **the sentence pictures anger not as a state belonging to the person but as something that comes and goes.** **And the lexicographers explain this construction as *istiʿāra* (a borrowed expression); I convey this explanation as transmitted.**

**نُسْخَة** — the root **ن-س-خ**: **to put one thing in the place of another; and to transfer a text from one place to another (*istinsākh*).** **The word here means "what is written".**

**And the last sentence of the verse must be recorded: the guidance and the mercy are *lilladhīna hum li-rabbihim yarhabūn* — "for those who stand in awe of their Lord".**

**The root ر-ه-ب had occurred in the hundred and sixteenth verse:** *wa'**starhabū**hum* — the sorcerers had struck fear into the people. **The same root, two different fears.** **This is a repetition that can be verified within the sūra.**

---

## 7/155-157 — وَٱخْتَارَ مُوسَىٰ قَوْمَهُۥ سَبْعِينَ رَجُلًا لِّمِيقَٰتِنَا فَلَمَّآ أَخَذَتْهُمُ ٱلرَّجْفَةُ قَالَ رَبِّ لَوْ شِئْتَ أَهْلَكْتَهُم مِّن قَبْلُ وَإِيَّٰىَ

*Wa'khtāra Mūsā qawmahū sabʿīna rajulan li-mīqātinā, fa-lammā akhadhathumu'r-rajfatu qāla rabbi law shiʾta ahlaktahum min qablu wa iyyāya, a-tuhlikunā bimā faʿala's-sufahāʾu minnā, in hiya illā fitnatuk, tuḍillu bihā man tashāʾu wa tahdī man tashāʾ, anta waliyyunā fa'ghfir lanā wa'rḥamnā wa anta khayru'l-ghāfirīn · Wa'ktub lanā fī hādhihi'd-dunyā ḥasanatan wa fi'l-ākhirati innā hudnā ilayk, qāla ʿadhābī uṣību bihī man ashāʾu wa raḥmatī wasiʿat kulla shayʾ*

"**Moses chose seventy men of his people for the time We appointed. When the quaking seized them he said: 'My Lord! Had You willed, You could have destroyed them before, and me with them. Will You destroy us for what the foolish among us have done? This is nothing but Your test. By it You lead astray whom You will and guide whom You will. You are our guardian; forgive us and have mercy on us. You are the best of forgivers. · And write for us good in this world and in the hereafter. We have turned to You.' He said: 'My punishment — I visit it upon whom I will; and My mercy has embraced everything.'**"

### أَتُهْلِكُنَا بِمَا فَعَلَ ٱلسُّفَهَآءُ مِنَّا — the construction of the question

**This sentence is a place that must be recorded for the sūra's understanding of responsibility.**

**And a point of word order must be recorded: the sentence has the qualification *minnā* ("among us").** **That is, the guilty are not cast out; it says "among us".**

**And the word *sufahāʾ* had occurred in the sixty-sixth verse** — there it was the word of the objection put to Hūd (*innā la-narāka fī **safāhatin***). **The same root, two different directions: one an accusation thrown at a messenger, the other a diagnosis accepted for one's own community.** **This is a repetition that can be verified within the sūra.**

### إِنْ هِىَ إِلَّا فِتْنَتُكَ — and a limit in *kalām*

**The sentence is built with the pattern *in … illā*: the event is reduced to one thing — *fitna* (a test).**

The root **ف-ت-ن**: **to put gold into the fire to learn its purity.** **This concrete sense of the root was treated at 20/40 in `020-taha.md`;** I rely on that treatment.

**And the continuation of the sentence — *tuḍillu bihā man tashāʾu wa tahdī man tashāʾ* — opens onto a field of *kalām* debate.** **Per the method (`STYLE.md`) I do not enter that debate and take no side.**

**I record the things that can be verified from the text:**

| # | Datum of the text |
|---|---|
| 1 | **The sentence occurs within a prayer**, not as the announcement of a ruling |
| 2 | **A request follows it immediately:** *fa'ghfir lanā wa'rḥamnā* — that is, the speaker does not use the sentence as a statement of helplessness |
| 3 | **And the answer is one of the widest sentences in the sūra:** *wa raḥmatī **wasiʿat kulla shayʾ*** |

**And the note kept consistently in this field throughout the index holds here too:** **the expression "whom He wills", if read in the sense of arbitrariness, contradicts the Qurʾān's own language — because the same text describes again and again where mercy and guidance are directed.** **This note was entered in `002-bakara.md`; I rely on it and do not open the discussion here.**

### وَرَحْمَتِى وَسِعَتْ كُلَّ شَىْءٍ — the asymmetry of the two sentences

**The answer in the hundred and fifty-sixth verse is two sentences, and there is a difference of word order between them. This difference is read from the text:**

| Sentence | Qualification | Scope |
|---|---|---|
| *ʿAdhābī **uṣību bihī man ashāʾ*** | **Qualified** — a note of will | **Determinate** |
| *Wa raḥmatī **wasiʿat kulla shayʾ*** | **Unqualified** | ***Kulla shayʾ*** — **everything** |

**I record this as an observation about word order, and its ground is the construction of the two sentences:** **in the first there is a restriction, in the second there is none.**

**And the same expression had occurred at 40/7 in `040-gafir.md`, in the prayer of those who bear the Throne:** *rabbanā **wasiʿta kulla shayʾin raḥmatan wa ʿilmā***. **It was recorded there: the order puts mercy before knowledge.** **I rely on that treatment.**

**The difference between the two verses must be recorded: in Ghāfir the sentence is a *prayer* (servants speak it); in al-Aʿrāf it is an *answer*.**

### هُدْنَآ إِلَيْكَ

The root **ه-و-د**: **to turn gently, to incline; and to repent.** ***Hudnā ilayk* — "we have turned to You".**

**This root was treated at 2/62 in `002-bakara.md`, where it was recorded: the Qurʾān uses this root in verbal form, and *it occurs at al-Aʿrāf 7/156 in the sense of repentance*.** **I rely on that treatment.**

**And this is a datum to be recorded for the analysis of the word; no ruling about a community is to be drawn from it** (`STYLE.md`).

### 7/157 — the series of attributes

> *Alladhīna yattabiʿūna'r-rasūla'n-nabiyya'l-ummiyya'lladhī yajidūnahū maktūban ʿindahum fi't-Tawrāti wa'l-Injīli **yaʾmuruhum bi'l-maʿrūfi wa yanhāhum ʿani'l-munkari wa yuḥillu lahumu'ṭ-ṭayyibāti wa yuḥarrimu ʿalayhimu'l-khabāʾitha wa yaḍaʿu ʿanhum iṣrahum wa'l-aghlāla'llatī kānat ʿalayhim…***

**The verse counts a series of attributes, and the structure of the series must be recorded: five verbs, two opposed pairs and one single.**

| # | Verb | Its opposite |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *Yaʾmuruhum bi'l-**maʿrūf*** | 2 |
| 2 | *Wa yanhāhum ʿani'l-**munkar*** | 1 |
| 3 | *Wa yuḥillu lahumu'**ṭ-ṭayyibāt*** | 4 |
| 4 | *Wa yuḥarrimu ʿalayhimu'l-**khabāʾith*** | 3 |
| 5 | *Wa **yaḍaʿu** ʿanhum iṣrahum wa'l-aghlāl* | (single) |

**Four verbs in pairs, the fifth alone.** **This is a structure that can be read from the text.**

### ٱلْمَعْرُوف and ٱلطَّيِّب — links within the sūra

**And two words connect with other places in the sūra. These links are countable:**

| Word | Root | Where in the sūra |
|---|---|---|
| *Al-**maʿrūf*** | **ع-ر-ف** | **The name of the sūra** (*al-aʿrāf*, 46-48) and **199**: *wa'mur bi'l-**ʿurf*** |
| *Al-**ṭayyibāt*** / *al-**khabāʾith*** | **ط-ي-ب** / **خ-ب-ث** | **58**: *al-baladu'ṭ-**ṭayyib*** / *wa'lladhī **khabutha*** · **32**: *al-**ṭayyibāti** mina'r-rizq* |

**That is, the hundred and fifty-seventh verse uses together the root of the sūra's name and the words of the two soils in the fifty-eighth verse.** **This is a weave that can be verified within the sūra, and I record it as an observation.**

### إِصْر and أَغْلَال

The root **إ-ص-ر**: **the tie that holds a thing in place, a weight; and from this, a burden.** The lexicographers tie the root to the picture of **the rope by which a tent is fastened to the ground.**

The root **غ-ل-ل**: **the ring or chain put on the neck or the hand.** ***Aghlāl* — the plural of *ghull*.**

The root **ي-ض-ع**: **to put down, to lower; and from this, to set a load down on the ground.**

**And the choice of the verb is worth recording: *yaḍaʿu* — "he sets down, he puts off".** **Not to break or to snap; to set down on the ground.**

**I give this as a note about the word, and its ground is the concrete sense of the root.**

**And I build no legal ruling from this verse and do not name which burden is meant;** **the Qurʾān here describes an attribute and goes into no detail** (`STYLE.md`).

---

## 7/158 — قُلْ يَٰٓأَيُّهَا ٱلنَّاسُ إِنِّى رَسُولُ ٱللَّهِ إِلَيْكُمْ جَمِيعًا

*Qul yā ayyuha'n-nāsu innī rasūlu'llāhi ilaykum jamīʿan, alladhī lahū mulku's-samāwāti wa'l-arḍ, lā ilāha illā huwa yuḥyī wa yumīt, fa-āminū bi'llāhi wa rasūlihi'n-nabiyyi'l-ummiyyi'lladhī yuʾminu bi'llāhi wa kalimātihī wa'ttabiʿūhu laʿallakum tahtadūn*

"**Say: 'O people! I am the messenger of God to you all — of Him to whom belongs the dominion of the heavens and the earth. There is no god but He; He gives life and gives death. So believe in God and in His messenger, the unlettered prophet who believes in God and in His words, and follow him, that you may be guided.'**"

### The place of the verse in the block

**A point of word order must be recorded, and this is the sharpest transition in the Moses block: up to the end of the hundred and fifty-seventh verse the telling was in the third person; the hundred and fifty-eighth passes with a command *qul* (say) directly into address.**

And the scope of the address must be recorded: *yā ayyuha'n-**nās*** and *ilaykum **jamīʿā***.

| Earlier addresses in the sūra | Scope |
|---|---|
| *Yā banī Ādam* (26, 27, 31, 35) | **The human race** |
| *Yā qawmi* (59, 65, 73, 85, 79, 93) | **A particular people** |
| **158**: *Yā ayyuha'n-nāsu … ilaykum **jamīʿā*** | **Everyone, without distinction** |

**And the word *jamīʿan* reinforces this qualification further.** **This is a datum that can be read from the text.**

**And that this verse is set inside the Moses block is worth recording. I give this as my own reading and it is not binding; its ground is the place of the verse and the sentence before it:** **the hundred and fifty-seventh verse was speaking of a group within a community; the hundred and fifty-eighth opens the addressee out to all people.** **That is, the sūra passes from a narrow ring to a wide one, and does so in the middle of a story.**

### ٱلنَّبِىِّ ٱلْأُمِّىِّ

**This phrase occurs twice, in the hundred and fifty-seventh and hundred and fifty-eighth verses.**

The root **أ-م-م**: **mother; and from this, origin, source, community (*umma*).**

**The exegetes disagree about what the word means in this phrase, and I give the disagreement in a table:**

| Reading | What *ummī* means | Ground |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **One who cannot read or write** | The tying of the root to the sense "remaining in the state in which he was born"; and al-ʿAnkabūt 29/48, *wa mā kunta tatlū min qablihī min kitābin wa lā takhuṭṭuhū bi-yamīnik* |
| 2 | **One belonging to a community to which no Book had come before** | The use of the word *ummiyyīn* in the Qurʾān for those who are not people of a Book (Āl ʿImrān 3/20, 3/75) |
| 3 | **One of the people of Mecca** — with reference to the phrase *Ummu'l-qurā* (al-Anʿām 6/92) | That the name of the city is from the same root |

**All three explanations are transmitted; they do not exclude one another.** **I do not impose a preference.**

### يُؤْمِنُ بِٱللَّهِ وَكَلِمَٰتِهِۦ

**A point of word order must be recorded: the messenger who is to be followed is described as himself one who believes.**

**I record this as an observation, and its ground is the relative clause:** **the sentence does not put the messenger in a position apart from the addressee; the same act (belief) is ascribed to him too.**

**And this connects with the objection in the sixty-third and sixty-ninth verses of the sūra:** there it was thought strange that what came should come through *rajulun minkum* (a man from among yourselves). **The hundred and fifty-eighth verse describes the messenger in exactly that position.**

---

## 7/159-162 — وَمِن قَوْمِ مُوسَىٰٓ أُمَّةٌ يَهْدُونَ بِٱلْحَقِّ وَبِهِۦ يَعْدِلُونَ

*Wa min qawmi Mūsā ummatun yahdūna bi'l-ḥaqqi wa bihī yaʿdilūn · Wa qaṭṭaʿnāhumu'thnatay ʿashrata asbāṭan umamā, wa awḥaynā ilā Mūsā idhi'stasqāhu qawmuhū ani'ḍrib bi-ʿaṣāka'l-ḥajar, fa'nbajasat minhu'thnatā ʿashrata ʿaynā, qad ʿalima kullu unāsin mashrabahum, wa ẓallalnā ʿalayhimu'l-ghamāma wa anzalnā ʿalayhimu'l-manna wa's-salwā, kulū min ṭayyibāti mā razaqnākum, wa mā ẓalamūnā wa lākin kānū anfusahum yaẓlimūn*

"**And among the people of Moses there is a community who guide by the truth and by it do justice. · We divided them into twelve tribes, as communities. When his people asked him for water We revealed to Moses: 'Strike the rock with your staff.' Twelve springs gushed out from it. Each group knew its own drinking place. We shaded them with the cloud and sent down upon them the manna and the quails: 'Eat of the good things We have provided you.' They did not wrong Us; they were wronging themselves.**"

### أُمَّةٌ يَهْدُونَ بِٱلْحَقِّ — the sūra's dividing sentence

**This sentence occurs twice in the sūra, in word-for-word identical form, and it was counted in the section on the structure. Here it is treated:**

| Verse | Of whom |
|---|---|
| **159** | *Wa min **qawmi Mūsā** ummatun yahdūna bi'l-ḥaqqi wa bihī yaʿdilūn* |
| **181** | *Wa mimman **khalaqnā** ummatun yahdūna bi'l-ḥaqqi wa bihī yaʿdilūn* |

**The only difference is in the set to which the sentence is attached: one a particular people, the other all created beings.**

**I record this as an observation, and its ground is the two verses themselves:** **the sūra builds the same sentence first for a narrow set and then for the widest.**

**And the particle *min* must be recorded: *wa min qawmi Mūsā* — "some of his people".** **The sentence does not take in the whole; it separates out a part.**

**And this is the support that comes from within the text for the principle of `STYLE.md` that "no blanket judgement is issued".** **The sūra will do the same thing once more in the hundred and sixty-eighth verse:** *minhumu'ṣ-ṣāliḥūna wa **minhum dūna dhālik***.

### ٱثْنَتَىْ عَشْرَةَ — the number repeated in two places

**A point of word order must be recorded: the same number occurs twice in one verse, and it belongs to two different things.**

| What | Expression |
|---|---|
| **The tribes** | *Qaṭṭaʿnāhumu'**thnatay ʿashrata** asbāṭan umamā* |
| **The springs** | *Fa'nbajasat minhu'**thnatā ʿashrata** ʿaynā* |

**And the link of the sentence must be recorded: *qad ʿalima kullu unāsin mashrabahum* — "each group knew its own drinking place".** **That is, the equality of the two numbers has a consequence: there is no confusion.**

**The same event was treated at 2/60 in `002-bakara.md`** (*fa'nfajarat minhu'thnatā ʿashrata ʿaynā*), **and there al-Baqara's word was *infajarat*.** **In al-Aʿrāf it is *inbajasat*.**

**The difference between the two roots must be recorded, and it can be verified from the two texts:**

| Sūra | Verb | Root | The sense the lexicographers give |
|---|---|---|---|
| `002-bakara.md` **2/60** | *Infajarat* | **ف-ج-ر** | **To burst open and flow abundantly** — a wide issue |
| **al-Aʿrāf 7/160** | *Inbajasat* | **ب-ج-س** | **To split and seep, to begin to come out** — the first issue |

**The lexicographers record that these two verbs can be read in sequence: first the seeping, then the abundant flow.** **I convey this explanation as transmitted and claim no certainty.** **The difference of root, however, is a datum that can be verified from the text.**

**قَطَّعْنَا** — the root **ق-ط-ع**, **Form II: it states multiplicity — to divide piece by piece.** **And the same verb will occur once more in the same sūra, at the hundred and sixty-eighth verse:** *wa **qaṭṭaʿnāhum** fi'l-arḍi umamā*.

| Verse | Into what it is divided | Where |
|---|---|---|
| **160** | *Ithnatay ʿashrata **asbāṭan** umamā* — **twelve tribes** | (no place stated) |
| **168** | *Umamā* — **communities** | *Fi'l-**arḍ*** — **on the earth** |

**The same verb, two different divisions.** **I record this as an observation, and its ground is the verb of the two verses.**

### 7/161-162 — حِطَّة and the word that was changed

> *Wa idh qīla lahumu'skunū hādhihi'l-qaryata wa kulū minhā ḥaythu shiʾtum wa qūlū **ḥiṭṭatun** wa'dkhulu'l-bāba sujjadā naghfir lakum khaṭīʾātikum, sa-nazīdu'l-muḥsinīn · Fa-**baddala**'lladhīna ẓalamū minhum qawlan ghayra'lladhī qīla lahum fa-arsalnā ʿalayhim rijzan mina's-samāʾi bimā kānū yaẓlimūn*

**These two verses were treated at 2/58-59 in `002-bakara.md`, and I rely on that treatment. A summary of what was recorded there:** **another word was put in the place of the word that had been given; the form is preserved, the function emptied out.** **I do not repeat it.**

**What belongs here is the difference of word order between the two sūras, and it can be verified from the two texts:**

| | `002-bakara.md` **2/58-59** | **al-Aʿrāf 7/161-162** |
|---|---|---|
| **The order of the commands** | *Udkhulū … **wa'dkhulu'l-bāba sujjadā wa qūlū ḥiṭṭatun*** | *Uskunū … **wa qūlū ḥiṭṭatun wa'dkhulu'l-bāba sujjadā*** |
| **The verb of forgiveness** | *Naghfir lakum khaṭāyākum* | *Naghfir lakum **khaṭīʾātikum*** |
| **To whom it belongs** | (unqualified) | *Alladhīna **ẓalamū minhum*** — **"those of them who did wrong"** |

**The third row must particularly be recorded: al-Aʿrāf separates out those who changed the word with the qualification *minhum* ("of them").** **In the sentence in al-Baqara this qualification is absent.**

**I record this as an observation, and its ground is the words of the two verses:** **al-Aʿrāf, in telling the same event, enters a note of division** — **and this lies on the same line as the notes of division in the hundred and fifty-ninth and hundred and sixty-eighth verses.**

**رِجْز** — the root **ر-ج-ز**: **to tremble, to be shaken; and from this, punishment, filth.** **The word had occurred twice earlier in the sūra** (134, 135: *wa lammā waqaʿa ʿalayhimu'r-**rijz***). **There of the people of Pharaoh, here of this community.** **The same word, two different addressees.** **This is a repetition that can be verified within the sūra.**

---

## 7/163-166 — وَسْـَٔلْهُمْ عَنِ ٱلْقَرْيَةِ ٱلَّتِى كَانَتْ حَاضِرَةَ ٱلْبَحْرِ إِذْ يَعْدُونَ فِى ٱلسَّبْتِ

*Wa'salhum ʿani'l-qaryati'llatī kānat ḥāḍirata'l-baḥri idh yaʿdūna fi's-sabti idh taʾtīhim ḥītānuhum yawma sabtihim shurraʿan wa yawma lā yasbitūna lā taʾtīhim, kadhālika nablūhum bimā kānū yafsuqūn · Wa idh qālat ummatun minhum lima taʿiẓūna qawman Allāhu muhlikuhum aw muʿadhdhibuhum ʿadhāban shadīdā, qālū maʿdhiratan ilā rabbikum wa laʿallahum yattaqūn · Fa-lammā nasū mā dhukkirū bihī anjayna'lladhīna yanhawna ʿani's-sūʾi wa akhadhna'lladhīna ẓalamū bi-ʿadhābin baʾīsin bimā kānū yafsuqūn · Fa-lammā ʿataw ʿan mā nuhū ʿanhu qulnā lahum kūnū qiradatan khāsiʾīn*

"**Ask them about the town by the sea: how they broke the Sabbath. Their fish came to them in shoals on the day of their Sabbath, and on the days when they did not keep the Sabbath they did not come. So We were testing them for their transgressing. · When a company among them said, 'Why do you admonish a people whom God is going to destroy or to punish with a severe punishment?', they said: 'As a plea before your Lord, and that they may perhaps guard themselves.' · When they forgot what they had been reminded of, We delivered those who forbade evil, and seized those who did wrong with a grievous punishment for their transgressing. · And when they exceeded the bound in what they had been forbidden, We said to them: 'Be apes, driven away.'**"

### The note I rely on

**This event was treated at 2/63-66 in `002-bakara.md`, and I rely on that treatment. A summary of what was recorded there:**

> **"The solution that is transmitted is this: on the Sabbath nets and pools are made ready, the fish go into them, and on Sunday they are gathered. On the Sabbath there was no fishing — technically."** **"The wording of the prohibition given is preserved while its purpose is cancelled. The prohibition appears to have been kept; the prohibition does not work."** **"The Qurʾān marks this behaviour in three separate places with three different examples — which means that it is not a single event but a *kind* that is being described."**

**I do not repeat it.** **And the limit recorded at 16/124 in `016-nahl.md` also holds:** **the Qurʾān touches on this matter in several places** (al-Baqara 2/65, al-Aʿrāf 7/163, al-Nisāʾ 4/154) **and goes into no detail.** **I rely on that treatment.**

### What al-Aʿrāf adds — three groups

**Al-Aʿrāf gives a piece of information al-Baqara does not, and this is a difference that can be verified from the two texts: in the event there are three groups, not two.**

| Group | Verse | What it does | Its end |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | 163 | *Yaʿdūna fi's-sabt* — **those who broke the prohibition** | *Akhadhna'lladhīna **ẓalamū*** (165) |
| **2** | 165 | *Yanhawna ʿani's-sūʾ* — **those who forbade evil** | *Anjaynā* (165) — **they were delivered** |
| **3** | 164 | *Lima **taʿiẓūna** qawman…* — **those who found admonition needless** | **The text does not say** |

**The outcome of the third group is not stated in the verse.** **This is a gap that can be verified from the text, and it has been much debated in the classical commentaries.**

**I give the disagreement in a table and impose no preference:**

| View | What became of the third group | Ground |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **They are included among the delivered** — they did not break the prohibition | Only two groups are named in verse 165: *anjaynā* and *akhadhnā*; the third group is not counted among those who broke the prohibition |
| 2 | **They are included among those seized** — they chose silence | In verse 165 those delivered are named *alladhīna yanhawna ʿani's-sūʾ*; the third group does not fall under that description |
| 3 | **The text is deliberately silent** — the outcome is not stated | That the verse counts two groups and does not include the third in the count |

**This disagreement is transmitted from the earliest period, and no preference is made in this commentary.**

### The ground of the admonition

**And the answer of the second group must be recorded, and it is one of the most concrete sentences in the sūra:**

> *Qālū ***maʿdhiratan ilā rabbikum wa laʿallahum yattaqūn***

**Two grounds are counted, and the two look in different directions:**

| # | Ground | What it looks to |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *Maʿ**dhiratan ilā rabbikum*** | **The one who admonishes** — his own account |
| 2 | *Wa laʿallahum **yattaqūn*** | **The one addressed** — the possibility of a result |

**I record this as my own reading and it is not binding; its ground is the order of the two grounds:** **the first ground remains valid even if the result does not come about.** **That is, admonition is given as an obligation not contingent on the result.** **And this is the answer to the objection itself: the objection was saying "they are going to be destroyed anyway".**

**م-ع-ذ-ر** — the root **ع-ذ-ر**: **to remove a fault, to offer an excuse.** ***Maʿdhira* means "the setting out of an excuse".**

### كُونُوا۟ قِرَدَةً خَٰسِـِٔينَ — likeness or reality

**The disagreement about this expression was tabulated at 2/65 in `002-bakara.md`, and I rely on that treatment.** **What was recorded there will not be repeated; I content myself with carrying the disagreement over here and tabulating it with its grounds:**

| View | What is understood | Ground | Its difficulty |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **A real transformation** | The plain sense of the expression; that the command *kūnū* states a becoming; that it is the view of the majority | The text does not give the detail of the process |
| 2 | **A likeness** — the becoming ape-like of hearts and conduct, falling into imitation and cunning | The frequency of such expression in Arabic; and that it was voiced in the early period too | That the plain sense of the command *kūnū* states a becoming |

**Both views are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.**

**And the finding recorded at 2/65 in `002-bakara.md`, which holds independently of the two views, holds here too; I rely on it:** **the animal chosen is not accidental.** **The ape is the animal that imitates — it copies the appearance and does not understand the function.** **And that is exactly what was done: the appearance of the prohibition was preserved, its meaning imitated.** **The punishment is named in the language of the offence itself.**

**The one new thing that belongs here is the pattern of the verb in al-Aʿrāf, and it is a datum of word order:**

| Place | Expression |
|---|---|
| `002-bakara.md` **2/65** | *Fa-**qulnā** lahum kūnū qiradatan khāsiʾīn* |
| **al-Aʿrāf 7/166** | *Fa-lammā **ʿataw** ʿan mā nuhū ʿanhu **qulnā** lahum kūnū qiradatan khāsiʾīn* |

**Al-Aʿrāf adds a conditional clause at the head of the same sentence: *fa-lammā ʿataw ʿan mā nuhū ʿanh* — "when they exceeded the bound in what they had been forbidden".** **In al-Baqara this qualification is absent.**

**The root ع-ت-و had occurred in the seventy-seventh verse** (*wa **ʿataw** ʿan amri rabbihim* — Thamūd). **The same root, two different communities.** **This is a repetition that can be verified within the sūra.**

**خَٰسِـِٔين** — the root **خ-س-أ**: **to be driven off, to be sent away;** the lexicographers tie the root to the sound used **to shoo an animal away.** **It was treated at 2/65 in `002-bakara.md`.**

---

## 7/167-171 — وَإِذْ تَأَذَّنَ رَبُّكَ لَيَبْعَثَنَّ عَلَيْهِمْ إِلَىٰ يَوْمِ ٱلْقِيَٰمَةِ مَن يَسُومُهُمْ سُوٓءَ ٱلْعَذَابِ

*Wa idh taʾadhdhana rabbuka la-yabʿathanna ʿalayhim ilā yawmi'l-qiyāmati man yasūmuhum sūʾa'l-ʿadhāb, inna rabbaka la-sarīʿu'l-ʿiqāb wa innahū la-ghafūrun raḥīm · Wa qaṭṭaʿnāhum fi'l-arḍi umamā, minhumu'ṣ-ṣāliḥūna wa minhum dūna dhālik, wa balawnāhum bi'l-ḥasanāti wa's-sayyiʾāti laʿallahum yarjiʿūn*

"**Remember when your Lord announced that He would surely send against them, until the Day of Resurrection, those who would inflict on them the worst of punishment. Your Lord is swift in retribution; and He is forgiving and merciful. · We divided them into communities on the earth. Among them are the righteous, and among them are others than that. We tested them with good things and with ill, that they might return.**"

### A limit in reading this verse, per the method (`STYLE.md`)

**This verse has throughout history been made the ground for a great many political and social claims.** **No such use is made of it in this commentary, and I write that plainly** (`STYLE.md`).

**The text itself enters a note of division, and that note of division is the verse's own sentence:**

> ***Minhumu'ṣ-ṣāliḥūna wa minhum dūna dhālik*** — "among them are the righteous, and among them are others than that."

**That is, the verse itself does not allow a blanket judgement to be built about a community.** **And this note lies on the same line as the note in the hundred and fifty-ninth verse (*wa min qawmi Mūsā ummatun yahdūna bi'l-ḥaqq*).**

**Within the sūra this note of division occurs three times, and it is a countable datum:**

| Verse | Note of division |
|---|---|
| **159** | *Wa **min** qawmi Mūsā ummatun yahdūna bi'l-ḥaqq* |
| **162** | *Fa-baddala'lladhīna ẓalamū **minhum*** |
| **168** | *Minhumu'ṣ-ṣāliḥūna wa **minhum dūna dhālik*** |

**I record this as an observation, and its ground is the particles of the three verses (*min*, *minhum*).** **The sūra divides three times within the same block.**

### تَأَذَّنَ

The root **أ-ذ-ن**: **ear; and from this, to hear, to give leave, to proclaim.** **Form V (*taʾadhdhana*) here has the sense of a decisive announcement: "to proclaim something upon oneself".**

**And the verb had occurred once more in the sūra, in the forty-fourth verse:** *fa-**adhdhana** muʾadhdhinun baynahum*. **The same root, one a proclamation in the scene of the hereafter, the other an announcement here.**

### Two attributes standing side by side

**The end of the verse must be recorded, and this is a point of word order:**

> *Inna rabbaka la-**sarīʿu'l-ʿiqāb** wa innahū la-**ghafūrun raḥīm***

**Two statements side by side, and in opposite directions.**

**And the difference in the construction of the two sentences must be recorded:**

| Sentence | Construction |
|---|---|
| *La-sarīʿu'l-ʿiqāb* | *Inna rabbaka* — **"your Lord"** |
| *La-ghafūrun raḥīm* | *Wa **innahū*** — **with a fresh *inna*, a separate sentence** |

**The second attribute is given not by being coordinated with the first but in a self-standing sentence.** **I record this as an observation about word order, and its ground is the presence of the second *inna*.**

### بَلَوْنَٰهُم بِٱلْحَسَنَٰتِ وَٱلسَّيِّـَٔاتِ — the third note in the sūra

**The sūra builds the two-directional test in three separate places. This is a countable datum:**

| Verse | Who is tested | How |
|---|---|---|
| **94-95** | **Every land to which a messenger was sent** | *Baʾsāʾ wa ḍarrāʾ* → *baddalnā makāna's-sayyiʾati'l-ḥasanata* |
| **130-131** | **The people of Pharaoh** | *Sinīn wa naqṣ* → *jāʾathumu'l-ḥasana* |
| **168** | **This community** | *Bi'l-ḥasanāti wa's-sayyiʾāt* |

**In all three the same structure: good and ill together are counted as a test.** **And in all three the purpose of the test is stated with the same kind of verb:** *laʿallahum **yadhdhakkarūn*** (130), *laʿallahum **yarjiʿūn*** (168).

**I record this as an observation, and its ground is the words of the three verses.**

### 7/169 — عَرَض هَٰذَا ٱلْأَدْنَىٰ

> *Fa-khalafa min baʿdihim **khalfun** warithu'l-kitāba yaʾkhudhūna **ʿaraḍa hādha'l-adnā** wa yaqūlūna **sa-yughfaru lanā**, wa in yaʾtihim ʿaraḍun mithluhū yaʾkhudhūh, a-lam yuʾkhadh ʿalayhim mīthāqu'l-kitābi an lā yaqūlū ʿala'llāhi illa'l-ḥaqqa wa darasū mā fīh*
>
> "**After them came a generation who inherited the Book. They take the goods of this lower world and say, 'We shall be forgiven.' And if the like of those goods comes to them again, they take it too. Was the covenant of the Book not taken from them, that they should say nothing about God but the truth? And they had studied what is in it.**"

**This verse is the place that prepares the passage into the eighth block of the sūra, and it carries three things to be recorded.**

**One — the word *khalf*.** The root **خ-ل-ف**: **to come behind.** **The lexicographers record a distinction between *khalf* (with fatḥa) and *khalaf*:** *khalaf* is generally used of **a good successor**, *khalf* of **a bad one**. **This distinction is not certain and does not hold everywhere; I convey it as transmitted.**

**And the same root had occurred twice more in the sūra:** *jaʿalakum **khulafāʾa*** (69, 74) — **there in a positive sense.** **The same root, two different directions.**

**Two — *ʿaraḍa hādha'l-adnā*.**

The root **ع-ر-ض**: **breadth; and from this, what comes past from one side.** ***ʿAraḍ* is what does not last, what is passing.** **And the lexicographers use the word as the opposite of *jawhar* (what abides).**

The root **أ-د-ن-و**: **nearness; and from this, lowness, baseness.** ***Adnā* means both "nearest" and "lowest"; and the word *dunyā* is from the same root.**

**That is, the phrase states transience twice over: *ʿaraḍ* (what passes by) and *adnā* (what is near, what stays low).**

**I give this as a note about the words, and its ground is the concrete senses of the two roots.**

**Three — the sentence *sa-yughfaru lanā*.**

**A point of word order must be recorded: the verb is passive and in the future.** **It is not said who will forgive; forgiveness is voiced as an expectation.**

**And the verse sets beside this expectation a note of *persistence*:** *wa in yaʾtihim ʿaraḍun mithluhū **yaʾkhudhūh*** — "if it comes again they take it again".

**I record this as my own reading and it is not binding; its ground is the two sentences standing side by side:** **the verse names in the same place the expectation of forgiveness and the continuation of the behaviour.** **And then a question comes:** *a-lam yuʾkhadh ʿalayhim **mīthāqu'l-kitāb***.

**And this question is the door of the eighth block of the sūra: in the hundred and seventy-second verse a far older covenant will be told.**

**And the verb *darasū* must be recorded: the root د-ر-س — to read a text over and over and to work on it; and for a trace to be effaced and vanish.** **The root has two branches, and both are transmitted.**

**I record this as an observation:** **the verse does not leave ignorance as a ground** — it says *wa darasū mā fīh*. **The knowledge was there.**

### 7/170-171 — those who hold the Book, and the mountain

> *Wa'lladhīna **yumassikūna** bi'l-kitābi wa aqāmu'ṣ-ṣalāta innā lā nuḍīʿu ajra'l-muṣliḥīn · Wa idh **nataqna'l-jabala** fawqahum ka-annahū **ẓullatun** wa ẓannū annahū wāqiʿun bihim, **khudhū mā ātaynākum bi-quwwatin** wa'dhkurū mā fīhi laʿallakum tattaqūn*

**The hundred and seventieth verse is set against the hundred and sixty-ninth, and the contrast is in the verb:**

| Verse | Verb | Object |
|---|---|---|
| **169** | *Yaʾkhudhūna* — **they take** | *ʿAraḍa hādha'l-adnā* — **passing goods** |
| **170** | *Yumassikūna* — **they hold fast** | *Bi'l-**kitāb*** |

The root **م-س-ك**: **to hold, to keep in hand.** **Form II (*tamassuk*) here states a firm grip; and a variant reading is transmitted: *yumsikūna* (Form IV).** **The difference in sense is slight.**

**I record this as an observation about word order:** **the two verses use two verbs from the same field, and their objects differ.**

The root **ن-ت-ق**: **to pull a thing from its place and lift it with a shaking.** The lexicographers tie the word to the picture of **a sack being shaken out.**

**ظُلَّة** — the root **ظ-ل-ل**: **what gives shade, a canopy.**

**And this scene was treated at 2/63 in `002-bakara.md`, and I rely on that treatment.** **What belongs here is what al-Aʿrāf adds, and this is a difference that can be verified from the two texts:**

| Sūra | Expression | What is added |
|---|---|---|
| `002-bakara.md` **2/63** | *Wa **rafaʿnā** fawqakumu'ṭ-Ṭūr* | — |
| **al-Aʿrāf 7/171** | *Wa idh **nataqna'l-jabala** fawqahum **ka-annahū ẓullatun** wa **ẓannū annahū wāqiʿun bihim*** | **A likeness** (*ẓulla*) and **a note of inner state** (*ẓannū*) |

**Al-Aʿrāf widens the scene in respect of image and of response.** **I record this as an observation.**

**And the command is the same: *khudhū mā ātaynākum bi-quwwatin*.** **These are the same words as the command spoken to Moses in the hundred and forty-fifth verse.**

| Verse | To whom |
|---|---|
| **145** | **To Moses** — *fa-khudhhā bi-quwwatin* |
| **171** | **To the community** — *khudhū mā ātaynākum bi-quwwatin* |

**The same command, two different addressees.** **And the Moses block closes with this command.** **In the section on the structure of the sūra, *laʿallakum tattaqūn* was recorded as the block's closing sentence.**

---

## 7/172-174 — وَإِذْ أَخَذَ رَبُّكَ مِنۢ بَنِىٓ ءَادَمَ مِن ظُهُورِهِمْ ذُرِّيَّتَهُمْ وَأَشْهَدَهُمْ عَلَىٰٓ أَنفُسِهِمْ أَلَسْتُ بِرَبِّكُمْ قَالُوا۟ بَلَىٰ شَهِدْنَآ

*Wa idh akhadha rabbuka min banī Ādama min ẓuhūrihim dhurriyyatahum wa ashhadahum ʿalā anfusihim, a-lastu bi-rabbikum, qālū balā shahidnā, an taqūlū yawma'l-qiyāmati innā kunnā ʿan hādhā ghāfilīn · Aw taqūlū innamā ashraka ābāʾunā min qablu wa kunnā dhurriyyatan min baʿdihim, a-fa-tuhlikunā bimā faʿala'l-mubṭilūn · Wa kadhālika nufaṣṣilu'l-āyāti wa laʿallahum yarjiʿūn*

"**Remember when your Lord took from the children of Adam — from their backs — their offspring, and made them witnesses against themselves: 'Am I not your Lord?' They said, 'Yes, we bear witness.' Lest you should say on the Day of Resurrection, 'We were unaware of this.' · Or should say, 'Our forefathers associated others with God before us, and we were a generation coming after them. Will You then destroy us for what the followers of falsehood did?' · Thus do We set out the signs in detail, that they may return.**"

### The place of the verse in the sūra

**This verse is the second of the three cornerstones recorded in the section on the structure of the sūra** (11-25 Adam, 172-174 *alast*, 175-177 the man who slipped out of the signs). **I rely on that treatment.**

**And the sentence immediately before the verse must be recorded: in the hundred and sixty-ninth verse a covenant was being asked about** — *a-lam yuʾkhadh ʿalayhim **mīthāqu'l-kitāb***. **The hundred and seventy-second verse tells of a far older covenant, and it begins with the same verb: *wa idh akhadha rabbuk*.**

**The same root (أ-خ-ذ), two verses apart, for two different covenants.** **This is a weave that can be verified within the sūra.**

### The words

**ظُهُور** — the root **ظ-ه-ر**: **the back; and from this, the visible, outer face of a thing.** **The plural of *ẓahr*.**

**ذُرِّيَّة** — the lexicographers disagree about its root: **ذ-ر-أ** (to scatter as one scatters seed) or **ذ-ر-ر** (to divide into small parts) or **ذ-ر-و** (to winnow). **All three are transmitted; I issue no settled ruling on the root.** **In sense they all come to the same place: what issues from one origin and spreads.**

**And the root ذ-ر-أ will occur expressly in the hundred and seventy-ninth verse of the sūra:** *wa laqad **dharaʾnā** li-jahannam*. **This root was undone at 23/79 in `023-muminun.md`, where al-Aʿrāf 7/179 was named.** **I rely on that treatment.**

**أَشْهَدَهُمْ عَلَىٰٓ أَنفُسِهِمْ** — the root **ش-ه-د**: **to be present; and from this, to know by seeing, to bear witness.** **Form IV (*ashhada*) — "to call to witness".**

**And a point of word order must be recorded: the testimony is taken about themselves** — *ʿalā **anfusihim***. **That is, the person is made a witness not about another but about himself.**

**أَلَسْتُ بِرَبِّكُمْ** — **the question is built on a negation:** *a-lastu* — "am I not?" **In Arabic this pattern is a question of confirmation whose answer is affirmative.**

**بَلَىٰ** — **and the word of the answer must be recorded: *balā*, not *naʿam*.** **In Arabic *balā* is the answer that removes the negation of a negative question.** **Had *naʿam* been said, then according to the majority of the lexicographers the negation would have been affirmed.**

**This is a datum of the language and must be recorded; the difference between the two words is settled in Arabic.**

### The disagreement — what "*alast*" tells

**There are two great readings of this verse, and both have been voiced in the classical sources.** **Per the method (`STYLE.md`) I give the disagreement with its grounds in a table and I DO NOT IMPOSE A PREFERENCE.** **This is a field of *kalām* debate, and no side is taken here.**

| | **Reading 1 — a real address** | **Reading 2 — a description of the *fiṭra*** |
|---|---|---|
| **What it says** | The scene is an event that **actually took place**: the offspring were brought out, the question was put, the answer was given | The scene is a likeness telling of the **manner of man's creation**: the question and the answer are the putting into language of the structure man carries |
| **Its ground from the text** | That the verbs are in the perfect (*akhadha*, *ashhada*, *qālū*); that the particle *idh* states a past time; that the question and the answer are **reported directly** | That the purpose of the sentence is given as *an taqūlū yawma'l-qiyāmati innā kunnā ʿan hādhā ghāfilīn* — that is, a **closing off of an excuse**; and that no human being has any such memory |
| **Support from within the Qurʾān** | That the scene is given with a detailed telling; that it is nowhere characterised as a likeness | `030-rum.md` **30/30**: *fiṭrata'llāhi'llatī faṭara'n-nāsa ʿalayhā* — that there is a ground **upon which** man was created |
| **The difficulty raised against this reading** | The text does not say where or how the event took place; and the question is asked how a forgotten testimony can serve as proof | Reducing the **speech** in the sentence *qālū balā shahidnā* to a likeness counts as departing from the plain sense of the wording |

**Both readings are transmitted. I do not impose a preference, and I do not use the evidence of one side against the other.**

**And no report at all is transmitted about this verse in this commentary, because their soundness cannot be established.** **The detail the Qurʾān does not give is not invented: I write nothing about the place, the time, the manner of the scene, or who was brought out and how** (`STYLE.md`).

### The link with فِطْرَة — 30/30 in `030-rum.md`

**The within-the-Qurʾān ground of the second reading is 30/30 in `030-rum.md`, and I rely on that treatment. A summary of what was recorded there:**

> ***Fiṭra* is not "creation" but "the manner of creation".** **The word names not man's having been brought into being but the fashion upon which he was brought into being.** **And the verse explains this itself: *allatī faṭara'n-nāsa ʿalayhā* — "upon which He created mankind".** **The particle *ʿalā* — upon: it states a ground.**

**And the overlap between the two verses must be recorded, and it can be verified from the two texts:**

| | `030-rum.md` **30/30** | **al-Aʿrāf 7/172** |
|---|---|---|
| **What is stated** | A ground **upon which** man was created | A testimony **taken** from man |
| **Particle / pattern** | *Faṭara'n-nāsa **ʿalayhā*** — upon it | *Ashhadahum **ʿalā anfusihim*** — against themselves |
| **Its permanence** | *Lā tabdīla li-khalqi'llāh* | *An taqūlū yawma'l-qiyāmati innā kunnā ʿan hādhā ghāfilīn* — the excuse is closed |

**Both verses state a *ground*, and in both the particle is *ʿalā*.**

**I record this as an observation, and its ground is the patterns of the two verses.** **This observation appears to support the second reading; but I do not present it as a preference** — **those who hold the first reading also draw a link between this verse and *alast*, and explain the *fiṭra* as the trace the testimony taken has left in man.** **Both readings can make use of this verse.**

### The purpose the verse itself gives — two excuses

**And independently of the debate, the purpose the verse itself states is read from the text and is not disputed. This is the main thing to be recorded:**

| # | The excuse that is closed | Verse |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | ***We were unaware*** — *innā kunnā ʿan hādhā ghāfilīn* | **172** |
| 2 | ***Our forefathers did it*** — *innamā ashraka ābāʾunā min qablu wa kunnā dhurriyyatan min baʿdihim* | **173** |

**The second excuse had occurred twice earlier in the sūra:**

| Verse | Expression |
|---|---|
| **28** | *Qālū **wajadnā ʿalayhā ābāʾanā** wa'llāhu amaranā bihā* |
| **70** | *Wa nadhara mā kāna yaʿbudu **ābāʾunā*** |
| **173** | *Innamā **ashraka ābāʾunā** min qablu* |

**Three occurrences.** **And the hundred and seventy-third verse carries this excuse into the scene of the hereafter and closes it there.**

**I record this as my own reading and it is not binding; its ground is the word shared by the three verses (*ābāʾ*):** **the ground "our forefathers used to do it", repeated throughout the sūra, is declared invalid in advance in the hundred and seventy-third verse.** **And the ground is tied to the sūra's own backbone: knowledge was given, no excuse remains.**

**And the closing of the hundred and seventy-fourth verse must be recorded: *wa kadhālika nufaṣṣilu'l-āyāti wa laʿallahum yarjiʿūn*.** **The same verb had occurred in the thirty-second verse of the sūra too** (*kadhālika nufaṣṣilu'l-āyāti li-qawmin yaʿlamūn*). **The table at the fifty-second verse is completed here.**

---

## 7/175-177 — وَٱتْلُ عَلَيْهِمْ نَبَأَ ٱلَّذِىٓ ءَاتَيْنَٰهُ ءَايَٰتِنَا فَٱنسَلَخَ مِنْهَا فَأَتْبَعَهُ ٱلشَّيْطَٰنُ فَكَانَ مِنَ ٱلْغَاوِينَ

*Wa'tlu ʿalayhim nabaʾa'lladhī ātaynāhu āyātinā fa'nsalakha minhā fa-atbaʿahu'sh-shayṭānu fa-kāna mina'l-ghāwīn · Wa law shiʾnā la-rafaʿnāhu bihā wa lākinnahū akhlada ila'l-arḍi wa'ttabaʿa hawāh, fa-mathaluhū ka-mathali'l-kalbi in taḥmil ʿalayhi yalhath aw tatrukhu yalhath, dhālika mathalu'l-qawmi'lladhīna kadhdhabū bi-āyātinā, fa'qṣuṣi'l-qaṣaṣa laʿallahum yatafakkarūn · Sāʾa mathalan i'l-qawmu'lladhīna kadhdhabū bi-āyātinā wa anfusahum kānū yaẓlimūn*

"**Recite to them the tidings of the man to whom We gave Our signs: he slipped out of them, and Satan took him in tow, and he became one of those who go astray. · Had We willed, We would have raised him by them; but he clung to the earth and followed his own craving. His likeness is the likeness of the dog: if you set upon it, it lolls out its tongue and pants; and if you leave it alone, it lolls out its tongue and pants. That is the likeness of the people who called Our signs lies. So tell the story — perhaps they will think. · Evil is the likeness of the people who called Our signs lies and were wronging themselves.**"

### A limit per the method (`STYLE.md`) — no name is given

**A great many mutually inconsistent reports and names are conveyed about this verse in the classical sources.** **In this commentary no name is given and no report is transmitted**, because:

**One — the Qurʾān gives no name.** **The verse says *alladhī* (the man); that is all.**

**Two — the names transmitted do not agree with one another, and their soundness cannot be established** (`STYLE.md`).

**Three — and the verse itself says that the matter does not remain particular to one man:** *dhālika **mathalu'l-qawmi**'lladhīna kadhdhabū bi-āyātinā* — **"that is the likeness of the people who called Our signs lies."**

**That is, the verse draws a kind out of a single story.** **This is a datum that can be verified from the text, and the text itself states that looking for a name is unnecessary.**

### ٱنسَلَخَ — the concrete sense of the root

The root **س-ل-خ**: **to separate the skin from the body, to flay.** *Salakha'sh-shāt* — he flayed the sheep. **Form VII (*insalakha*) is reflexive: to slip out, to come out of itself.**

**And the root's other use in the Qurʾān must be recorded: *fa-idha'nsalakha'l-ashhuru'l-ḥurum* (al-Tawba 9/5) — "when the sacred months have passed".** **It was treated in `009-tevbe.md`.** **There the passing away of time, here a man's coming out of the signs.**

**And the direction of the picture must be recorded, and this is the most striking place in the verse:**

| It could have been built thus | What is built |
|---|---|
| *Fa-taraka'l-āyāt* — he left the signs | ***Fa'nsalakha minhā*** — **he slipped out of the signs** |

**That is, the signs are pictured as something worn upon him; and the man comes out of them like a skin.**

**I record this as my own reading and it is not binding; its ground is the concrete sense of the root:** **a skin is not a thing taken off like a garment — it belongs to the body itself.** **The picture the root chooses intimates that what is parted with was not something fastened on from outside.**

**And this connects with the second and third blocks of the sūra. This link is read from the text:**

| Verse | Verb | What is taken off |
|---|---|---|
| **22** | *Badat lahumā **sawʾātuhumā*** | The covering went |
| **27** | *Yanziʿu ʿanhumā **libāsahumā*** | *Libās* — **the clothing is stripped off** |
| **26** | *Wa **libāsu't-taqwā** dhālika khayr* | *Libās* — **what is to be worn** |
| **175** | *Fa'n**salakha** minhā* | **The signs — slipped off like a skin** |

**The sūra uses the picture of putting on and taking off in four separate places.** **I record this as an observation, and its ground is the words of the four verses.**

### أَخْلَدَ إِلَى ٱلْأَرْضِ

The root **خ-ل-د**: **permanence, remaining for ever.** **Form IV (*akhlada*) here means "to cling to a place, to incline towards it and settle there".**

**And the particle *ilā* must be recorded: the verb is tied to a direction.**

**I give this as a note about the word:** **the word comes from the root of everlastingness and describes clinging to a place that does not last.** **That is, a search for permanence is directed at a place that is not permanent.**

**And the first half of the verse must be recorded: *wa law shiʾnā la-rafaʿnāhu bihā*.** **The raising too would have been by those same signs** (*bihā*). **The same thing can both raise and pass out of the hand.**

### The likeness of the dog — at what point it works

**This likeness is a place that must be undone with care, and it is the point most open to being misunderstood.**

**The sentence of the verse is this:**

> *Fa-mathaluhū ka-mathali'l-kalbi **in taḥmil ʿalayhi yalhath aw tatrukhu yalhath***

The root **ل-ه-ث**: **to loll out the tongue and pant** — the breathing of a dog with its mouth open and its tongue hanging out.

**And the point at which the likeness works is read from the structure of the sentence itself. This is an indisputable datum of the text:**

| Condition | Result |
|---|---|
| *In **taḥmil ʿalayhi*** — if you set upon it | *Yalhath* |
| *Aw **tatrukhu*** — if you leave it | *Yalhath* |

**Two opposite cases, one result.**

**That is, the point on which the likeness rests is not the dog itself but the fact that the condition does not change.** **And the structure of the verse says so: two conditional clauses are built and the apodosis of both is given with the same word.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the structure of the sentence:** **the likeness is not a debasement but a description of *unresponsiveness*.** **Whatever is done to the man — whether he is set upon or left alone — his behaviour does not change.** **And this is exactly the description of the signs becoming useless: warning and letting alone give the same result.**

**And this reading overlaps directly with another verse of the sūra:**

| Verse | Expression |
|---|---|
| **132** | *Mahmā taʾtinā bihī min āyatin … **fa-mā naḥnu laka bi-muʾminīn*** — whatever you bring |
| **176** | *In taḥmil ʿalayhi yalhath **aw tatrukhu yalhath*** — whatever is done |

**The two verses build the same structure: whatever the input, the output does not change.** **This is an overlap that can be verified within the sūra.**

**And per the method (`STYLE.md`) I enter one further note, because this likeness can be used with wounding language:** **the verse does not turn a group of human beings into animals;** **it describes a *manner of behaving*.** **And the verse itself says who is described: *alladhīna kadhdhabū bi-āyātinā* — an attribute.** **Whoever carries that attribute is included in it; this is the sūra's own principle.**

**And a point of word order must be recorded: the likeness is made not to the man but to the case** — *fa-**mathaluhū** ka-**mathali**'l-kalb*: "his **case** is like the **case** of the dog". **On both sides stands the word *mathal*.**

**This is the same choice of word order recorded at 47/12 in `047-muhammed.md`** (*yaʾkulūna **kamā taʾkulu**'l-anʿām* — the likeness is made not to the person but to the act). **I rely on that treatment.**

### فَٱقْصُصِ ٱلْقَصَصَ

**And the command at the end of the verse must be recorded: *fa'qṣuṣi'l-qaṣaṣa laʿallahum yatafakkarūn*.**

The root **ق-ص-ص**: **to follow a trace.** ***Qaṣaṣ* is what is told by following its trace.** **The root had occurred in the seventh verse of the sūra** (*fa-la-**naquṣṣanna** ʿalayhim bi-ʿilm*) **and in the thirty-fifth as well** (*yaquṣṣūna ʿalaykum āyātī*).

**That is, in the sūra the same root is ascribed to three different subjects:**

| Verse | Who tells |
|---|---|
| **7** | **God** — *fa-la-naquṣṣanna ʿalayhim bi-ʿilm* |
| **35** | **The messengers** — *yaquṣṣūna ʿalaykum āyātī* |
| **176** | **The addressee** — *fa'qṣuṣi'l-qaṣaṣ* |

**Three links.** **This is a weave that can be verified within the sūra, and I record it as an observation.**

---

## 7/178-179 — مَن يَهْدِ ٱللَّهُ فَهُوَ ٱلْمُهْتَدِى وَمَن يُضْلِلْ فَأُو۟لَٰٓئِكَ هُمُ ٱلْخَٰسِرُونَ · وَلَقَدْ ذَرَأْنَا لِجَهَنَّمَ كَثِيرًا مِّنَ ٱلْجِنِّ وَٱلْإِنسِ لَهُمْ قُلُوبٌ لَّا يَفْقَهُونَ بِهَا وَلَهُمْ أَعْيُنٌ لَّا يُبْصِرُونَ بِهَا وَلَهُمْ ءَاذَانٌ لَّا يَسْمَعُونَ بِهَآ أُو۟لَٰٓئِكَ كَٱلْأَنْعَٰمِ بَلْ هُمْ أَضَلُّ أُو۟لَٰٓئِكَ هُمُ ٱلْغَٰفِلُونَ

*Man yahdi'llāhu fa-huwa'l-muhtadī, wa man yuḍlil fa-ulāʾika humu'l-khāsirūn · Wa laqad dharaʾnā li-jahannama kathīran mina'l-jinni wa'l-ins, lahum qulūbun lā yafqahūna bihā wa lahum aʿyunun lā yubṣirūna bihā wa lahum ādhānun lā yasmaʿūna bihā, ulāʾika ka'l-anʿāmi bal hum aḍall, ulāʾika humu'l-ghāfilūn*

"**Whomever God guides, he is the one guided. And whomever He leaves astray, those are the losers. · We have brought into being for Hell many of the jinn and of mankind: they have hearts with which they do not understand, and they have eyes with which they do not see, and they have ears with which they do not hear. Those are like cattle; rather, they are further astray. Those are the heedless.**"

### ذَرَأْنَا لِجَهَنَّمَ — the function of the *lām*, and a limit in *kalām*

The root **ذ-ر-أ** was **undone at 23/79 in `023-muminun.md`, where al-Aʿrāf 7/179 was named.** **I rely on that treatment. What was recorded there: the concrete sense of the root is to scatter as one scatters seed, to multiply; the lexicographers describe the root as lying somewhere between creating and spreading.** **I do not repeat it.**

**What belongs here is the function of the *lām* in the phrase *li-jahannama*, and this opens onto a debate of *kalām*.** **Per the method (`STYLE.md`) I take no side in the debate; I convey the two explanations in a table:**

| Reading | What the *lām* states | Ground |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | ***Lām al-ʿāqiba*** — **the *lām* of outcome**: "those who will end up in Hell" | That there are instances in Arabic where this *lām* states an outcome (*fa'ltaqaṭahū ālu Firʿawna li-yakūna lahum ʿaduwwan* — al-Qaṣaṣ 28/8: the purpose of the taking in was not enmity) |
| 2 | ***Lām al-taʿlīl*** — **the *lām* of purpose** | That the primary function of the *lām* is to state purpose |

**Both explanations are transmitted; I do not impose a preference, and I do not enter the debate about the decree** (`STYLE.md`).

**I record the one thing that can be verified from the text, and that is the verse's own continuation:** **the verse describes these people by a series of attributes** — three organs and the non-use of all three. **That is, the description is made not by the name of a lineage or a group but by a failure to act.**

### The triad heart-eye-ear — a new link

**This triad was treated in eight places in the index, and an eight-link table was drawn up at 16/78 in `016-nahl.md`.** **I rely on that treatment and widen the table from here:**

| Place | Sentence | The work of the triad |
|---|---|---|
| `046-ahkaf.md` **46/26** | *Wa jaʿalnā **lahum** samʿan wa abṣāran wa afʾidatan **fa-mā aghnā ʿanhum…*** | **Given — of no avail** |
| `045-casiye.md` **45/23** | *Wa **khatama** ʿalā samʿihī wa qalbihī wa jaʿala ʿalā baṣarihī ghishāwatan* | **Closed** — a seal and a film |
| `041-fussilet.md` **41/20** | *Shahida ʿalayhim samʿuhum wa abṣāruhum wa **julūduhum*** | **They bore witness** — the third is *the skins* |
| `032-secde.md` **32/9** | *Wa **jaʿala lakum** … qalīlan mā tashkurūn* | **Given — no thanks given** |
| `023-muminun.md` **23/78** | *Wa huwa'lladhī **anshaʾa** lakumu's-samʿa wa'l-abṣāra wa'l-afʾida* | **Brought into being** |
| `067-mulk.md` **67/23** | *Qul huwa'lladhī **anshaʾakum** wa jaʿala lakum … qalīlan mā tashkurūn* | **Given — no thanks given** |
| `017-isra.md` **17/36** | *Inna's-samʿa wa'l-baṣara wa'l-fuʾāda **kullu ulāʾika kāna ʿanhu masʾūlā*** | **Held answerable** |
| `016-nahl.md` **16/78** | *Wa jaʿala lakum … **laʿallakum tashkurūn*** | **Given — thanks hoped for** |
| **al-Aʿrāf 7/179** | *Lahum **qulūbun** lā yafqahūna bihā wa lahum **aʿyunun** lā yubṣirūna bihā wa lahum **ādhānun** lā yasmaʿūna bihā* | **Present — not used** |
| `006-enam.md` **6/46** | *In akhadha'llāhu samʿakum wa abṣārakum wa **khatama** ʿalā qulūbikum* | **The possibility of their being taken away** |

**And what al-Aʿrāf adds lies at two points. Both can be verified from the text, and both set this verse apart from all the other links.**

**One — the order is reversed.**

| | The other links | **al-Aʿrāf 7/179** |
|---|---|---|
| **Order** | *Samʿ* → *abṣār* → *afʾida* — **from outside inward** | ***Qulūb*** → ***aʿyun*** → ***ādhān*** — **from inside outward** |

**At 46/26 in `046-ahkaf.md` the meaning of the order was recorded: it goes from outside inward — what is heard → what is seen → what is weighed within.** **In al-Aʿrāf this direction is reversed: the heart comes first.**

**I record this as my own reading and it is not binding; its ground is the order itself:** **the other verses were telling of the *giving* of the organs, and the order of giving was from outside inward.** **Al-Aʿrāf tells of their *not being used*, and it begins the matter at the heart.** **That is, the lack is pictured as beginning not at the outer gates but within.** **This is an explanation, not a ruling.**

**Two — the words have changed.**

| Organ | The other links | **al-Aʿrāf 7/179** |
|---|---|---|
| **The inward** | *Afʾida* / *fuʾād* / *qalb* | ***Qulūb*** |
| **The eye** | *Abṣār* / *baṣar* | ***Aʿyun*** |
| **The ear** | *Samʿ* (a verbal noun, singular) | ***Ādhān*** (the plural of *udhun*) |

**And the difference must be recorded: in the other verses *samʿ* is a verbal noun** — that is, the *faculty* of hearing. **In al-Aʿrāf it is *ādhān*: the organ itself.**

**I record this as a datum of the language:** **in all three places al-Aʿrāf names the organ and does not use the name of the faculty.** **And this accords with the work of the sentence: the verse says the organs are there** (*lahum* — "they have") **and that they do not work.** **What is stressed is the presence not of the faculty but of the instrument.**

**And the choice of the verbs must also be recorded:**

| Organ | Verb | Root | What it states |
|---|---|---|---|
| *Qulūb* | *Lā **yafqahūna*** | **ف-ق-ه** | **To grasp in depth** — not to know superficially |
| *Aʿyun* | *Lā **yubṣirūna*** | **ب-ص-ر** | **To see** — different from *naẓar*: it states the outcome |
| *Ādhān* | *Lā **yasmaʿūna*** | **س-م-ع** | **To hear** |

That the root **ف-ق-ه** is chosen is worth recording: the root means **to go down into the depth of a thing and understand it**; the lexicographers point to a narrower and deeper understanding than *ʿilm*.

**And it was recorded at 26/194 in `026-suara.md` (*ʿalā qalbik*) that the Qurʾān ascribes reasoning and understanding to the heart, and this very sentence was named as the example** (*lahum qulūbun lā yafqahūna bihā* — al-Aʿrāf 7/179). **I rely on that treatment.**

### كَٱلْأَنْعَٰمِ بَلْ هُمْ أَضَلُّ

**The likeness was treated in two places in the index, and I rely on both:**

| Place | Sentence | What was recorded there |
|---|---|---|
| `047-muhammed.md` **47/12** | *Yaʾkulūna **kamā taʾkulu'l-anʿām*** | **The eating of the animal is not censured; what is described is man's contenting himself with that much alone.** **And the likeness is made not to the person but to the *act*.** |
| `025-furkan.md` **25/44** | *In hum illā **ka'l-anʿāmi bal hum aḍallu sabīlā*** | **The ground of the addition *bal hum aḍallu*: the animal has no way from which to stray.** **The comparison looks to the non-use of a means that was given.** |

**I do not repeat them and I rely on both notes.**

**And one thing recorded in `047-muhammed.md` carries particular weight here, and I need to repeat it, because a reading of the verse that does not wound depends on it:** **the Qurʾān counts *anʿām* (grazing livestock) as a bounty in other places, and there is a sūra named for them** (al-Anʿām, 6). **At al-Naḥl 16/5-8 the creation of these animals is told as a favour.**

**That is, the likeness is not built in order to debase the animal.**

**And the difference between al-Aʿrāf and al-Furqān must be recorded, and it can be verified from the two texts:**

| | `025-furkan.md` **25/44** | **al-Aʿrāf 7/179** |
|---|---|---|
| **What precedes** | *Am taḥsabu anna **aktharahum yasmaʿūna aw yaʿqilūn*** — two verbs | **Three organs, three verbs** — a detailed count |
| **The likeness** | *Ka'l-anʿāmi **bal hum aḍallu sabīlā*** | *Ka'l-anʿāmi **bal hum aḍall*** |
| **Difference** | The qualification *sabīlā* is **present** | The qualification *sabīlā* is **absent** |
| **Closing** | (it passes to the next verse) | *Ulāʾika humu'l-**ghāfilūn*** |

**Al-Aʿrāf leaves the word *aḍall* unqualified and puts a closing sentence in its place: *ulāʾika humu'l-ghāfilūn*.**

The root **غ-ف-ل** had occurred in the hundred and thirty-sixth verse (*wa kānū ʿanhā **ghāfilīn***) and had been named in the hundred and seventy-second verse as an excuse (*innā kunnā ʿan hādhā **ghāfilīn***).

**And this is a weave that can be verified within the sūra, and I record it as my own reading:** **in the hundred and seventy-second verse heedlessness was being put forward as an *excuse*; the hundred and seventy-ninth gives the same word as a *diagnosis*.** **There are seven verses between the two.** **That is, the sūra brings back, a few verses later, the name of the excuse it closed, now as an attribute.** **This is an explanation, not a ruling.**

---

## 7/180-181 — وَلِلَّهِ ٱلْأَسْمَآءُ ٱلْحُسْنَىٰ فَٱدْعُوهُ بِهَا وَذَرُوا۟ ٱلَّذِينَ يُلْحِدُونَ فِىٓ أَسْمَٰٓئِهِۦ سَيُجْزَوْنَ مَا كَانُوا۟ يَعْمَلُونَ

*Wa li'llāhi'l-asmāʾu'l-ḥusnā fa'dʿūhu bihā, wa dharu'lladhīna yulḥidūna fī asmāʾih, sa-yujzawna mā kānū yaʿmalūn · Wa mimman khalaqnā ummatun yahdūna bi'l-ḥaqqi wa bihī yaʿdilūn*

"**To God belong the most beautiful names; so call upon Him by them. And leave those who twist His names aside. They will be recompensed for what they used to do. · And among those We created there is a community who guide by the truth and by it do justice.**"

### ٱلْأَسْمَآءُ ٱلْحُسْنَىٰ

**The phrase was treated at 59/24 in `059-hasr.md` and named again at 20/8 in `020-taha.md`. I rely on those treatments. A summary of what was recorded there:**

> ***Lahū* has been brought forward, and in Arabic the fronting of the predicate states *ḥaṣr* (restriction)** — "the most beautiful names belong **to Him alone**". **And *ḥusnā* is the feminine elative of *aḥsan*: not "beautiful names" but "the most beautiful names".**

**And it was counted at 20/8 in `020-taha.md`: the phrase occurs in four places in the Qurʾān** — **al-Aʿrāf 7/180**, al-Isrāʾ 17/110, Ṭāhā 20/8, al-Ḥashr 59/24. **This verse is one of those four.**

**Two things belong here.**

**One — al-Aʿrāf is the only one of the four occurrences that carries a command.**

| Place | The kind of sentence |
|---|---|
| `059-hasr.md` **59/24** | **A statement** — the closing of the sūra |
| `020-taha.md` **20/8** | **A statement** — the sentence closing the opening block |
| `017-isra.md` **17/110** | **A ground** — *ayyan mā tadʿū **fa-lahu'l-asmāʾu'l-ḥusnā*** |
| **al-Aʿrāf 7/180** | ***A command*** — *fa'dʿūhu bihā* |

**This is a countable datum and is read from the construction of the four verses.**

**Two — the relation between 17/110 in `017-isra.md` and al-Aʿrāf 7/180 must be recorded. It was recorded in `017-isra.md`, and I rely on that treatment:**

> **In al-Isrāʾ a dispute is being closed** — *ayyan mā tadʿū fa-lahu'l-asmāʾu'l-ḥusnā*: **the verse does not equate two names, it says that both go to the same place.** **And the ground is kept wide: the names are not two but many.**

**In al-Aʿrāf, however, the same sentence does not close a dispute; it gives a command for use: *fa'dʿūhu bihā* — "call upon Him by them".**

**And the place of the command in the sūra must be recorded: the sūra had given a command to pray in the fifty-fifth and fifty-sixth verses** (*udʿū rabbakum taḍarruʿan wa khufyatan*; *wa'dʿūhu khawfan wa ṭamaʿā*). **The hundred and eightieth is the third command to pray, and this time it says by what one is to pray.**

| Verse | Command | What it determines |
|---|---|---|
| **55** | *Udʿū rabbakum **taḍarruʿan wa khufyatan*** | **Bearing and voice** |
| **56** | *Wa'dʿūhu **khawfan wa ṭamaʿā*** | **The inner state** |
| **180** | *Fa'dʿūhu **bihā*** | **By which names** |

**Three commands, three different determinations.** **This is a weave that can be verified within the sūra, and I record it as an observation.**

### يُلْحِدُونَ فِىٓ أَسْمَٰٓئِهِۦ

The root **ل-ح-د**: **to swerve to one side, to leave the straight and slide sideways.** **And *laḥd* (the niche hollowed out in the side of a grave) is from the same root** — **the picture in the root: not going straight but opening out to the side.**

**A variant reading is transmitted: *yulḥidūna* (Form IV) and *yalḥadūna* (Form I).** **The difference in sense is slight.**

**And the particle *fī* must be recorded: the swerving takes place within the names.** **That is, the names are not abandoned; a slippage in the use of the names is stated.**

**I record this as an observation about word order, and its ground is the particle.** **This connects with the seventy-first verse of the sūra:** there Hūd had named what the other side held as *asmāʾin sammaytumūhā antum wa ābāʾukum*. **The same word (*asmāʾ*), in two different directions: one names that are given by men, the other names that are given by God.**

**And the command *dharū* must be recorded: *wa dharu'lladhīna yulḥidūna fī asmāʾih* — "leave them".** **The command is not a command to dispute but a command to withdraw.** **And this lies on the same line as the command *wa aʿriḍ ʿani'l-jāhilīn* in the hundred and ninety-ninth verse of the sūra.**

### 7/181 — the sūra's widening sentence

**This verse is word for word identical with the hundred and fifty-ninth; the only difference is in the set to which the sentence is attached.** **It was tabulated in the section on the structure and treated at verse 159.** **I do not repeat it.**

**The one thing that belongs here is the verse's place in this block, and I record it as my own reading, and it is not binding; its ground is the order of the two verses:** **the hundred and eightieth verse names those who twist the names; the hundred and eighty-first names, immediately afterwards, a community that guides by the truth.** **Every time, the sūra sets a note of division beside a negative finding.** **And this has been done four times through the sūra** (159, 162, 168, 181).

---

## 7/182-186 — وَٱلَّذِينَ كَذَّبُوا۟ بِـَٔايَٰتِنَا سَنَسْتَدْرِجُهُم مِّنْ حَيْثُ لَا يَعْلَمُونَ

*Wa'lladhīna kadhdhabū bi-āyātinā sa-nastadrijuhum min ḥaythu lā yaʿlamūn · Wa umlī lahum, inna kaydī matīn · A-wa lam yatafakkarū, mā bi-ṣāḥibihim min jinna, in huwa illā nadhīrun mubīn · A-wa lam yanẓurū fī malakūti's-samāwāti wa'l-arḍi wa mā khalaqa'llāhu min shayʾin wa an ʿasā an yakūna qadi'qtaraba ajaluhum, fa-bi-ayyi ḥadīthin baʿdahū yuʾminūn · Man yuḍlili'llāhu fa-lā hādiya lah, wa yadharuhum fī ṭughyānihim yaʿmahūn*

"**Those who called Our signs lies — We shall draw them on step by step from where they do not know. · I grant them respite. My devising is firm. · Have they not thought? There is no madness in their companion. He is only a plain warner. · Have they not looked into the dominion of the heavens and the earth, and into whatever God has created, and considered that their term may have drawn near? In what account after this will they believe? · Whomever God leaves astray, there is no guide for him; He leaves them in their overstepping, wandering blindly.**"

### ٱلِٱسْتِدْرَاج — the picture in the word

The root **د-ر-ج**: **a step, a degree; and to advance step by step.** ***Daraja* is from the same root.** **Form X (*istadraja*) — to bring someone to a place step by step, without his noticing.**

**And the qualification set beside the word must be recorded: *min ḥaythu lā yaʿlamūn* — "from where they do not know".**

**I give this as a note about the word, and its ground is the concrete sense of the root:** **the word builds the picture not of a sudden seizure but of an unnoticed approach.** **And this accords with the ninety-fifth verse of the sūra:** there abundance had been given, and then they were seized suddenly.

**وَأُمْلِى لَهُمْ** — the root **م-ل-و/م-ل-ي**: **to lengthen a span, to grant respite.** ***Malā* — a long time.**

**كَيْدِى مَتِينٌ** — the root **م-ت-ن**: **the strong muscle of the back and loins; and from this, firmness, endurance.**

**And for the word *kayd*, the note of *tanzīh* entered for *makr* at the ninety-ninth verse holds here too.** **I do not repeat it.**

### مَا بِصَاحِبِهِم مِّن جِنَّةٍ — the closing of the objection

**This verse closes the ring opened in the sixty-third and sixty-ninth verses of the sūra. This link is read from the text:**

| Verse | Who | Objection / answer |
|---|---|---|
| **63** | **Noah** | *A-wa **ʿajibtum** an jāʾakum dhikrun min rabbikum ʿalā **rajulin minkum*** |
| **69** | **Hūd** | The same sentence |
| **184** | **To the addressee** | *A-wa lam **yatafakkarū**, mā bi-**ṣāḥibihim** min jinna* |

**And the choice of the word must be recorded: *ṣāḥibihim* — "their companion".**

The root **ص-ح-ب**: **to be together, to accompany.** ***Ṣāḥib* is the one in whose company one is, the one who is known.**

**I record this as my own reading and it is not binding; its ground is the word itself:** **the answer is given not by a lining up of proofs but by a reminder of *acquaintance*.** **The verse says "you know him".** **And this is the answer to the objection *rajulun minkum* (a man from among yourselves) in the stories: nearness is counted not as a reason for objection but as evidence.**

### مَلَكُوت

The root **م-ل-ك**: **to own, to hold command.** **The pattern *malakūt* in Arabic states intensity: dominion in its widest and most encompassing form.**

**It was recorded at 23/88 in `023-muminun.md`: the word occurs in four places in the Qurʾān** — al-Anʿām 6/75, **al-Aʿrāf 7/185**, al-Muʾminūn 23/88, Yā Sīn 36/83. **I rely on that treatment.**

**And the choice of construction must be recorded: *a-wa lam yanẓurū fī malakūt* — the particle *fī* (into).** **Not "to look at" but "to look into".**

**I record this as an observation about word order, and its ground is the particle.**

### فِى طُغْيَٰنِهِمْ يَعْمَهُونَ

**The phrase was tabulated in `023-muminun.md`, where its regular occurrences in the Qurʾān were counted** — al-Baqara 2/15, al-Anʿām 6/110, **al-Aʿrāf 7/186**, Yūnus 10/11. **I rely on that treatment and do not repeat it.**

**And in the section on the structure of the sūra this sentence was recorded as the closing sentence of the eighth block.**

---

## 7/187-188 — يَسْـَٔلُونَكَ عَنِ ٱلسَّاعَةِ أَيَّانَ مُرْسَىٰهَا قُلْ إِنَّمَا عِلْمُهَا عِندَ رَبِّى لَا يُجَلِّيهَا لِوَقْتِهَآ إِلَّا هُوَ

*Yasʾalūnaka ʿani's-sāʿati ayyāna mursāhā, qul innamā ʿilmuhā ʿinda rabbī, lā yujallīhā li-waqtihā illā hū, thaqulat fi's-samāwāti wa'l-arḍ, lā taʾtīkum illā baghtatan, yasʾalūnaka ka-annaka ḥafiyyun ʿanhā, qul innamā ʿilmuhā ʿinda'llāhi wa lākinna akthara'n-nāsi lā yaʿlamūn · Qul lā amliku li-nafsī nafʿan wa lā ḍarran illā mā shāʾa'llāh, wa law kuntu aʿlamu'l-ghayba la'stakthartu mina'l-khayri wa mā massaniya's-sūʾ, in ana illā nadhīrun wa bashīrun li-qawmin yuʾminūn*

"**They ask you about the Hour: when will it come to anchor? Say: 'Its knowledge is with my Lord alone. Only He will bring it out at its time. It weighs heavy on the heavens and the earth. It will not come upon you but suddenly.' They ask you as though you had searched it out. Say: 'Its knowledge is with God alone; but most people do not know.' · Say: 'I hold for myself neither benefit nor harm, except what God wills. Had I known the unseen, I would have gained much good, and no ill would have touched me. I am only a warner and a bringer of good news to a people who believe.'**"

### مُرْسَىٰهَا — the picture in the word

The root **ر-س-و**: **to be fixed, to settle into place.** **And the lexicographers use the root for a ship's dropping anchor: *arsati's-safīnatu* — the ship came to anchor.** **From the same root comes *rawāsī* (the firm mountains).**

**And at 11/41 in `011-hud.md` the same root had occurred of a ship** (*bismi'llāhi majrāhā wa **mursāhā***). **I rely on that treatment.**

**I give this as a note about the word, and its ground is the concrete sense of the root:** **the question asks about the Hour not as something that "comes" but as something that drops anchor.** **That is, what is asked about is the moment at which the event will *settle*.**

### The limit of knowledge — the notes in the index

**This verse is part of a limit kept consistently throughout the index. I rely on two places:**

| Place | What was recorded |
