|---|---|
| `031-lokman.md` **31/34** | *Inna'llāha ʿindahū ʿilmu's-sāʿa* — and five things are counted; **the limit of knowledge** |
| `041-fussilet.md` **41/47** | *Ilayhi yuraddu ʿilmu's-sāʿa* — the knowledge **is referred back to Him** |

**And what al-Aʿrāf adds must be recorded, and it is a datum of the text: al-Aʿrāf builds the sentence twice, and there is a difference between the two:**

| Place | Sentence |
|---|---|
| **187a** | *Qul innamā ʿilmuhā ʿinda **rabbī*** |
| **187b** | *Qul innamā ʿilmuhā ʿinda'**llāh*** |

**The same sentence twice in the same verse; in the first *rabbī*, in the second *Allāh*.**

**I record this as an observation about word order, and its ground is the two sentences themselves.** **The first answer is given with the messenger's own relation, the second with the name itself.**

**And between them stands a sentence: *yasʾalūnaka ka-annaka ḥafiyyun ʿanhā*.**

The root **ح-ف-ي**: **to search a thing out thoroughly, to press on it and come to know it well.** ***Ḥafiyy* means "one who knows a thing well by inquiring into it".** **I record this so that it is not confused with the sense "hidden": the sense given by the majority of the lexicographers here is "one who knows well".**

**And the sentence *thaqulat fi's-samāwāti wa'l-arḍ* must be recorded.** The root **ث-ق-ل**: weight. **Several explanations are transmitted of the sense of the sentence** (that its knowledge weighs heavy in the heavens and the earth; that the event itself weighs heavy upon the heavens and the earth). **I convey them as transmitted and impose no preference.**

### 7/188 — the messenger's own limit

**A point of word order must be recorded, and this is the work of the verse: the limit is set first for the messenger himself.**

> *Qul lā amliku **li-nafsī** nafʿan wa lā ḍarran*

**That is, the sentence does not say "I can bring you no benefit"; it says "I can bring myself none".** **The widening comes afterwards.**

**And the second sentence is a conditional pattern, and it counts two results:**

| Condition | Result 1 | Result 2 |
|---|---|---|
| *Law kuntu aʿlamu'l-ghayb* | *La'stakthartu mina'l-**khayr*** — **I would have gained much good** | *Wa mā massaniya's-**sūʾ*** — **no ill would have touched me** |

**The two results run in opposite directions: one gaining, the other avoiding.** **And the particle *law* states an unfulfilled condition** — **it was treated at the ninety-sixth verse.**

**I record this as an observation about word order, and its ground is the particle:** **the sentence shows that the unseen is not known by way of two concrete results.**

---

## 7/189-193 — هُوَ ٱلَّذِى خَلَقَكُم مِّن نَّفْسٍ وَٰحِدَةٍ وَجَعَلَ مِنْهَا زَوْجَهَا لِيَسْكُنَ إِلَيْهَا

*Huwa'lladhī khalaqakum min nafsin wāḥidatin wa jaʿala minhā zawjahā li-yaskuna ilayhā, fa-lammā taghashshāhā ḥamalat ḥamlan khafīfan fa-marrat bih, fa-lammā athqalat daʿawa'llāha rabbahumā la-in ātaytanā ṣāliḥan la-nakūnanna mina'sh-shākirīn · Fa-lammā ātāhumā ṣāliḥan jaʿalā lahū shurakāʾa fīmā ātāhumā, fa-taʿāla'llāhu ʿammā yushrikūn*

"**It is He who created you from a single soul and made from it its mate, that he might find rest in her. When he covered her she bore a light burden and went about with it. Then, when she grew heavy, the two of them called upon God their Lord: 'If You give us a sound child, we shall surely be among the thankful.' · But when He gave them a sound child they set up partners to Him in what He had given them. Exalted is God above what they associate with Him.**"

### The place of the sentence in the sūra

**This verse begins with a sentence that returns to the second block of the sūra (Adam): *khalaqakum min nafsin wāḥidatin*.**

**And a point of word order must be recorded: the sentence begins with a singular scene, then passes to the dual** (*rabbahumā*, *ātāhumā*, *jaʿalā*), **and then in the hundred and ninety-third verse turns to the plural** (*yushrikūn*, *tattabiʿūkum*).

| Verse | Person |
|---|---|
| **189a** | **Singular** — *nafsin wāḥidatin* and its mate |
| **189b-190a** | **Dual** — *rabbahumā*, *jaʿalā* |
| **190b-193** | **Plural** — *ʿammā **yushrikūn*** |

**This transition is one of the places most debated in the classical commentaries, and I give the disagreement in a table. I DO NOT IMPOSE A PREFERENCE.**

| Reading | Who associates partners | Ground | Its difficulty |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Man as a kind** — the scene tells not the behaviour of a single couple but of the human species | The verse's passing to the plural (*yushrikūn*); and that the expression *nafsin wāḥidatin* can state a kind | The passage from the dual pattern to the plural requires explanation in respect of word order |
| 2 | **Later generations** — the couple who prayed and those who associate partners are different people | At the end of verse 190 *fa-taʿāla'llāhu ʿammā **yushrikūn*** is plural; from 191 on the addressees are plainly others | There is no note of a change of subject in the flow of the sentence |
| 3 | **A particular event** — it rests on a transmitted account | Reports conveyed in the classical sources | **No report is transmitted in this commentary;** and the accounts conveyed do not agree with one another |

**The first and second readings can be carried through on the text; I do not impose a preference.**

**And for the third reading I write plainly, per the method: accounts have been conveyed about this verse that ascribe a fault to prophets.** **This commentary does not enter into those accounts**, because their soundness cannot be established and **the detail the Qurʾān does not give is not invented** (`STYLE.md`).

### لِيَسْكُنَ إِلَيْهَا

The root **س-ك-ن**: **to become still, for motion to subside; and from this, to find rest, to settle.** ***Sakīna* and *maskan* are from the same root.**

**And the same verb was treated at 30/21 in `030-rum.md`** (*li-taskunū ilayhā*). **I rely on that treatment.**

**And the particle *ilā* must be recorded: the verb is tied to a direction — "to come to rest towards her".**

**فَمَرَّتْ بِهِۦ** — the root **م-ر-ر**: to pass, to go to and fro. **The sense of the sentence: that while the burden was light she went on with her ordinary life.**

**وَٱلَّذِينَ تَدْعُونَ مِن دُونِهِۦ لَا يَسْتَطِيعُونَ نَصْرَكُمْ** (192-193) — **these two verses are the entry to the long enumeration that will come from the hundred and ninety-fourth verse on, and they will be treated together there.**

---

## 7/194-198 — إِنَّ ٱلَّذِينَ تَدْعُونَ مِن دُونِ ٱللَّهِ عِبَادٌ أَمْثَالُكُمْ فَٱدْعُوهُمْ فَلْيَسْتَجِيبُوا۟ لَكُمْ إِن كُنتُمْ صَٰدِقِينَ

*Inna'lladhīna tadʿūna min dūni'llāhi ʿibādun amthālukum, fa'dʿūhum fa'l-yastajībū lakum in kuntum ṣādiqīn · A-lahum arjulun yamshūna bihā, am lahum aydin yabṭishūna bihā, am lahum aʿyunun yubṣirūna bihā, am lahum ādhānun yasmaʿūna bihā, quli'dʿū shurakāʾakum thumma kīdūni fa-lā tunẓirūn*

"**Those you call upon beside God are servants like yourselves. Call upon them, then, and let them answer you — if you are truthful! · Have they feet with which to walk? Or hands with which to grasp? Or eyes with which to see? Or ears with which to hear? Say: 'Call your partners, then devise against me and give me no respite.'**"

### The structure of the enumeration — and the link with 7/179

**The hundred and ninety-fifth verse counts four organs, and the structure of the enumeration must be compared directly with the hundred and seventy-ninth verse. This is a weave that can be verified within the sūra, and one of the most striking overlaps to be recorded:**

| | **7/179 — of man** | **7/195 — of those called upon** |
|---|---|---|
| **Construction** | *Lahum … **lā** yafqahūna bihā* — **the organ is there, it does not work** | *A-**lahum** … yamshūna bihā* — **the question whether the organ is there** |
| **Heart** | ***Qulūbun** lā yafqahūna bihā* | (absent) |
| **Eye** | ***Aʿyunun** lā yubṣirūna bihā* | ***Aʿyunun** yubṣirūna bihā* |
| **Ear** | ***Ādhānun** lā yasmaʿūna bihā* | ***Ādhānun** yasmaʿūna bihā* |
| **Foot** | (absent) | ***Arjulun** yamshūna bihā* |
| **Hand** | (absent) | ***Aydin** yabṭishūna bihā* |

**And the difference between the two verses is exactly this, and it is read from the text:**

**One — the two verses share the same two words: *aʿyun* and *ādhān*; and the same verbs: *yubṣirūna bihā*, *yasmaʿūna bihā*.**

**Two — in 179 the organ is there but the act is not; in 195 the organ itself is asked about.**

**I record this as my own reading and it is not binding; its ground is the words shared by the two verses:** **the sūra applies the same measure to both sides.** **First to the one who calls: "you have the organs, you do not use them." Then to the one called upon: "do you have organs?"** **And the measure is one: not what a thing is, but what it can do.**

**And this measure had already been used in the sūra — at the hundred and forty-eighth verse, of the calf:** *a-lam yaraw annahū **lā yukallimuhum wa lā yahdīhim sabīlā***. **That is, the sūra builds the same measure in three separate places** (148, 179, 195). **This is a countable datum.**

### عِبَادٌ أَمْثَالُكُمْ

**A point of word order must be recorded: the sentence does not deny the existence of those called upon; it states their standing.** *ʿIbādun **amthālukum*** — "servants like yourselves".

**I record this as an observation about word order, and its ground is the word:** **what is objected to is not their existence but the work attributed to their standing.** **And this lies on the same line as the sentence *asmāʾin sammaytumūhā* in the seventy-first verse: the matter is one of naming.**

The root **ب-ط-ش**: **to seize a thing with force, to hold it violently.** **The choice of the word is worth recording: for "to hold" not a plain verb but a verb stating force has been chosen.**

### ثُمَّ كِيدُونِ فَلَا تُنظِرُونِ

**It was recorded at 11/55 in `011-hud.md`, and I rely on that treatment: this pattern occurs in other places in the Qurʾān too** — *thumma kīdūni fa-lā tunẓirūn* (**al-Aʿrāf 7/195**); *fa-ajmiʿū amrakum wa shurakāʾakum* (Yūnus 10/71). **These belong to the same family.**

The root **ن-ظ-ر** here means **"to grant respite"**, and it had occurred in the fifteenth verse of the sūra: *innaka mina'l-**munẓarīn***.

**The same root in two different places:** **in the fifteenth verse respite is granted to Iblīs; in the hundred and ninety-fifth the messenger asks for none.** **This is a repetition that can be verified within the sūra, and I record it as an observation.**

**And from the hundred and ninety-sixth verse to the hundred and ninety-eighth the enumeration is completed:**

| Verse | What is stated |
|---|---|
| **196** | *Inna waliyyiya'llāhu'lladhī nazzala'l-kitāba wa huwa yatawalla'ṣ-ṣāliḥīn* — **who the guardian is** |
| **197** | *Wa'lladhīna tadʿūna min dūnihī lā yastaṭīʿūna naṣrakum wa lā anfusahum yanṣurūn* — **they cannot even help themselves** |
| **198** | *Wa in tadʿūhum ila'l-hudā lā yasmaʿū, wa tarāhum yanẓurūna ilayka wa hum lā yubṣirūn* — **they look but do not see** |

**And the last sentence in the hundred and ninety-eighth verse must be recorded: *wa tarāhum yanẓurūna ilayka wa hum lā yubṣirūn*.**

**This is the coming together in one sentence of the two roots undone at the hundred and forty-third verse (ن-ظ-ر and ب-ص-ر):** **there is looking, there is no seeing.**

**I record this as an observation, and its ground is the difference between the two roots:** **the verse shows within a single sentence that looking and seeing are not the same thing.**

---

## 7/199-200 — خُذِ ٱلْعَفْوَ وَأْمُرْ بِٱلْعُرْفِ وَأَعْرِضْ عَنِ ٱلْجَٰهِلِينَ · وَإِمَّا يَنزَغَنَّكَ مِنَ ٱلشَّيْطَٰنِ نَزْغٌ فَٱسْتَعِذْ بِٱللَّهِ إِنَّهُۥ سَمِيعٌ عَلِيمٌ

*Khudhi'l-ʿafwa wa'mur bi'l-ʿurfi wa aʿriḍ ʿani'l-jāhilīn · Wa immā yanzaghannaka mina'sh-shayṭāni nazghun fa'staʿidh bi'llāh, innahū samīʿun ʿalīm*

"**Take what is easily borne, command what is recognised as good, and turn away from the ignorant. · And if a prompting from Satan should prompt you, take refuge with God. He is hearing, knowing.**"

### Three commands, one verse

**At the centre of the ninth block of the sūra stands a series of three commands, and all three look to a different field:**

| # | Command | Field | Direction |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | *Khudhi'l-**ʿafw*** | **His own bearing** | **Inward** |
| 2 | *Wa'mur bi'l-**ʿurf*** | **What is said to the other side** | **Outward** |
| 3 | *Wa **aʿriḍ** ʿani'l-jāhilīn* | **What is not answered** | **Withdrawal** |

**The three commands set up a balance: take, say, withdraw.** **I record this as an observation about word order, and its ground is the directions of the three verbs.**

### ٱلْعَفْو — two branches of meaning, and the difference from 2/219 in `002-bakara.md`

**The root ع-ف-و carries two branches, and both are used in the Qurʾān:**

| Branch | Meaning | Where |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | **To wipe away, to pardon, to remit a penalty** | Widespread in the Qurʾān |
| **2** | **What is over and above; the surplus beyond need** | `002-bakara.md` **2/219** |

**It was recorded at 2/219 in `002-bakara.md`, and I rely on that treatment:** *wa yasʾalūnaka mādhā yunfiqūna quli'l-**ʿafw*** — **"they ask you what they should spend; say: al-ʿafw."** **The sense the lexicographers gave there was the second branch: what remains over from one's own need.** **And it was recorded there: "the same question was asked in verse 215, and the answer was given as a list of recipients. Here the same question comes again, and this time a measure is given."**

**And at al-Aʿrāf 7/199 the exegetes disagree about the meaning of the same word. I give the disagreement in a table and impose no preference:**

| Reading | What *al-ʿafw* is here | Ground |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **To pardon, to forgive** — to pass over people's faults | The first branch of the root; and that *aʿriḍ ʿani'l-jāhilīn* follows the command |
| 2 | **To take what is easy** — to ask of people what they can do, not to press them | The "surplus/ease" branch of the root; and that the verb *khudh* (take) states a **taking of a measure** |
| 3 | **To accept what comes easily from people's dispositions** | Another expression of the second reading |

**Both of the two great readings are transmitted, and they do not exclude each other.** **I do not impose a preference.**

**And the difference of sense between the two verses must be recorded, and it can be verified from the two texts:**

| | `002-bakara.md` **2/219** | **al-Aʿrāf 7/199** |
|---|---|---|
| **Subject** | **Spending** — how much is to be given | **Dealing** — how one is to behave with people |
| **Verb** | *Quli'l-ʿafw* — **an answer** | *Khudhi'l-ʿafw* — **a command** |
| **Object** | **What is over from one's goods** | **What is easy/pardoned in conduct** |

**The same word, in two different fields.** **And in `002-bakara.md` the word is given as a material measure, in al-Aʿrāf as a moral one.**

**I record this as an observation, and its ground is the context of the two verses.**

**And the same root had occurred once more within the sūra: at the ninety-fifth verse, *ḥattā ʿafaw* — there in the sense "they grew many".** **That is, the root occurs twice in al-Aʿrāf, with two different branches.** **This is a datum that can be verified within the sūra.**

### ٱلْعُرْف — a return to the name of the sūra

**The root ع-ر-ف is the root of the sūra's name, and its two branches were tabulated at 46-47.** **I rely on that treatment.**

**And the distribution of the word in the sūra is completed here. This is a countable datum:**

| Verse | Word | Branch |
|---|---|---|
| **46** | *ʿAla'l-**aʿrāf*** | **Height** |
| **46** | *Yaʿ**rifūna** kullan bi-sīmāhum* | **To recognise** |
| **48** | *Yaʿ**rifūna**hum bi-sīmāhum* | **To recognise** |
| **157** | *Yaʾmuruhum bi'l-**maʿrūf*** | **The good that is known and accepted** |
| **199** | *Wa'mur bi'l-**ʿurf*** | **The good that is known and accepted** |

**Five occurrences, two branches.** **And the name of the sūra and the command in its last block come from the same root.**

**I record this as my own reading and it is not binding; its ground is the five occurrences:** **the sūra turns the root from which it takes its name into a *command* at the end.** **And the "recognising" branch of the root becomes, in the word *ʿurf*, "the good that everyone recognises and accepts".**

**And the difference between *ʿurf* and *maʿrūf* must be recorded: both are from the same root and the same field; *maʿrūf* is a passive participle (what is recognised), while *ʿurf* is on the pattern of a verbal noun.** **The hundred and fifty-seventh verse uses *maʿrūf*, the hundred and ninety-ninth *ʿurf*.** **This is a difference that can be verified from the text.**

### وَأَعْرِضْ عَنِ ٱلْجَٰهِلِينَ — the third link in the index

**This bearing was treated in two places in the index, and I rely on both:**

| Place | Sentence | What was recorded there |
|---|---|---|
| `028-kasas.md` **28/55** | *Wa idhā samiʿu'l-laghwa **aʿraḍū ʿanhu** … salāmun ʿalaykum **lā nabtaghi'l-jāhilīn*** | The answer is in **four parts**; and the bearing is given in the context of a *response* |
| `025-furkan.md` **25/63** | *Wa idhā khāṭabahumu'l-**jāhilūna** qālū **salāmā*** | **The answer has come down to a single word**; in al-Qaṣaṣ there were three sentences, in al-Furqān only *salāmā* |

**And what al-Aʿrāf adds must be recorded, and it can be verified from the three texts:**

| Place | The kind of sentence | What is given |
|---|---|---|
| `028-kasas.md` **28/55** | **A statement** — the behaviour of a company | *Aʿraḍū* — **the past tense** |
| `025-furkan.md` **25/63** | **A statement** — an attribute | *Qālū salāmā* — **a word** |
| **al-Aʿrāf 7/199** | ***A command*** | *Aʿ**riḍ*** — **a direct command** |

**That is, the same bearing occurs in the index first as a behaviour, then as an attribute, and here as a command.** **I record this as an observation, and its ground is the moods of the three verses.**

**And the word *jāhil* must be recorded: the root ج-ه-ل, according to the lexicographers' note, carries not only "ignorance" but also the sense of angry and unmeasured conduct.** **In this second sense the word's opposite is *ḥilm* (composure), not *ʿilm*.** **This distinction is transmitted and accords with the context of the verse: what is commanded is not a dispute about knowledge but a withdrawal.**

And the root had occurred earlier in the sūra: *innakum qawmun **tajhalūn*** (138) — Moses's diagnosis in the face of the demand for a calf. **The same root, in two different places.**

### نَزْغ — the picture in the word

The root **ن-ز-غ** was **undone at 41/36 in `041-fussilet.md`, and I rely on that treatment. What was recorded there:** **to prod, to prick, to come in between and stir up;** *nazagha baynahum* — he set them at odds.

**And one thing recorded at 41/36 in `041-fussilet.md` belongs directly here, and it was named at 16/98 in `016-nahl.md` too:** **the command came immediately after the hardest counsel** — there after the command *idfaʿ bi'llatī hiya aḥsan* (repel evil with what is better). **That is, it was saying where the counsel is most likely to crack.**

**And in al-Aʿrāf the same placement holds, and it can be verified from the two texts:**

| Sūra | The command before it | What follows |
|---|---|---|
| `041-fussilet.md` **41/34-36** | *Idfaʿ bi'llatī hiya **aḥsan*** — repel evil with what is better | *Wa immā yanzaghannaka … **fa'staʿidh bi'llāh*** |
| **al-Aʿrāf 7/199-200** | *Khudhi'l-ʿafwa … wa **aʿriḍ** ʿani'l-jāhilīn* — withdraw | *Wa immā yanzaghannaka … **fa'staʿidh bi'llāh*** |

**In both sūras the same sentence in the same place: immediately after the hardest command.** **I record this as an observation, and its ground is the order of the two verses.**

**And the difference recorded at 16/98 in `016-nahl.md` is at work here too:** in al-Naḥl the time of taking refuge was *idhā qaraʾta'l-qurʾān* (when you recite); **in Fuṣṣilat and al-Aʿrāf it is *immā yanzaghannaka* — when the prompting comes.** **I rely on that treatment.**

**And the pattern of the verb must be recorded: *yanzagha**nna**ka* — it carries the emphatic *nūn*.** **That is, what is stated is not a possibility but a case assumed to occur.**

**And the answer to the command must be recorded: *fa'staʿidh bi'llāh* — a single step.** **The verse describes no method of struggle; it gives a command to turn.**

---

## 7/201-203 — إِنَّ ٱلَّذِينَ ٱتَّقَوْا۟ إِذَا مَسَّهُمْ طَٰٓئِفٌ مِّنَ ٱلشَّيْطَٰنِ تَذَكَّرُوا۟ فَإِذَا هُم مُّبْصِرُونَ

*Inna'lladhīna'ttaqaw idhā massahum ṭāʾifun mina'sh-shayṭāni tadhakkarū fa-idhā hum mubṣirūn · Wa ikhwānuhum yamuddūnahum fi'l-ghayyi thumma lā yuqṣirūn · Wa idhā lam taʾtihim bi-āyatin qālū law la'jtabaytahā, qul innamā attabiʿu mā yūḥā ilayya min rabbī, hādhā baṣāʾiru min rabbikum wa hudan wa raḥmatun li-qawmin yuʾminūn*

"**When a visitation from Satan touches those who guard themselves, they at once take thought and remember, and then they see. · But their brothers draw them on into error, and never leave off. · And when you do not bring them a sign, they say, 'Why have you not gathered one up yourself?' Say: 'I follow only what is revealed to me from my Lord. This is clear insight from your Lord, and guidance and mercy for a people who believe.'**"

### طَٰٓئِف — the word and the variant reading

The root **ط-و-ف**: **to go round a thing, to circle it.** ***Ṭāʾif* means "one who goes round".**

**A variant reading is transmitted, and I record it because it affects the meaning:**

| Reading | Text | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | ***Ṭāʾif*** | **One who circles** — who goes round a person watching for an opening |
| 2 | ***Ṭayf*** | **A passing appearance** — what shows itself in sleep or waking |

**Both readings are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.** **Both accord with the main picture of the word: something not lasting, but passing by or circling about.**

**And the measure of the picture is worth recording, and I give it as an observation:** **in the two hundredth verse there was *nazgh* (a prod) — a short, sharp contact.** **Between the hundred and ninety-ninth and the two hundred and first verses the same fact is given in two different pictures: one a prodding, the other a circling about.**

### فَإِذَا هُم مُّبْصِرُونَ — the sūra's axis of seeing closes

**A point of word order must be recorded: the *idhā* of suddenness (*idhā al-fujāʾiyya*) is used.** **That is, the seeing comes not at the end of a process but immediately after the remembering.**

**And the word *mubṣirūn* is the last link of the sūra's axis of seeing. This axis is countable:**

| Verse | Expression | State |
|---|---|---|
| **64** | *Kānū qawman **ʿamīn*** | **Blindness** |
| **179** | *Aʿyunun lā **yubṣirūna** bihā* | **The eye is there, the seeing is not** |
| **195** | *A-lahum aʿyunun **yubṣirūna** bihā* | **Is the eye there?** |
| **198** | *Yanẓurūna ilayka wa hum lā **yubṣirūn*** | **There is looking, there is no seeing** |
| **201** | *Fa-idhā hum **mubṣirūn*** | **There is seeing** |

**Five occurrences.** **And four are negative, the fifth positive.**

**I record this as my own reading and it is not binding; its ground is the root shared by the five verses (ب-ص-ر):** **after describing the state of not seeing four times, the sūra says once how seeing comes.** **And the way given is *tadhakkur* (taking thought and remembering), not a proof.**

**And in the two hundred and third verse the word *baṣāʾir* comes — from the same root: *hādhā baṣāʾiru min rabbikum*.** **That is, the root occurs a sixth time at the closing of the sūra, and this time as the name of the Book.**

The root **ب-ص-ر**: **to see; and from this *baṣīra* — to see the inner face of a thing.** ***Baṣāʾir* is the plural of *baṣīra*.**

### لَوْلَا ٱجْتَبَيْتَهَا — the word of the objection

The root **ج-ب-ي**: **to gather a thing together, to pick out and collect.** *Jabā'l-māʾa* — he gathered the water in a pool.

**And what the objection is must be recorded: the other side assumes that a sign is something that can be gathered up.**

**And the answer removes exactly that assumption: *innamā attabiʿu mā yūḥā ilayya min rabbī* — "I follow only what is revealed to me".**

**I record this as an observation about word order, and its ground is the opposition of the two verbs:** **in the objection *ijtabā* (to gather and select), in the answer *ittabaʿa* (to follow).** **One states choosing, the other following.**

---

## 7/204-206 — وَإِذَا قُرِئَ ٱلْقُرْءَانُ فَٱسْتَمِعُوا۟ لَهُۥ وَأَنصِتُوا۟ لَعَلَّكُمْ تُرْحَمُونَ

*Wa idhā quriʾa'l-qurʾānu fa'stamiʿū lahū wa anṣitū laʿallakum turḥamūn · Wa'dhkur rabbaka fī nafsika taḍarruʿan wa khīfatan wa dūna'l-jahri mina'l-qawli bi'l-ghuduwwi wa'l-āṣāli wa lā takun mina'l-ghāfilīn · Inna'lladhīna ʿinda rabbika lā yastakbirūna ʿan ʿibādatihī wa yusabbiḥūnahū wa lahū yasjudūn*

"**When the Qurʾān is recited, give ear to it and be silent, that you may be shown mercy. · Remember your Lord within yourself, in humility and in fear, and without loudness of speech, in the morning and in the evening; and do not be of the heedless. · Those who are with your Lord are not too proud to serve Him; they glorify Him, and to Him alone they prostrate themselves.**"

### فَٱسْتَمِعُوا۟ لَهُۥ وَأَنصِتُوا۟ — two commands

The root **س-م-ع**: to hear. **Form VIII (*istamaʿa*) — to give ear, to listen attentively.** **That is, the command is not "hear" but "give ear".**

The root **ن-ص-ت**: **to fall silent, to stop one's voice and listen.** ***Inṣāt* — to leave off speaking and settle to listening.**

**And the difference between the two commands must be recorded, and this is a point of word order:**

| Command | What is asked | Direction |
|---|---|---|
| *Fa'stamiʿū lahū* | **Giving ear** | **Positive** — something is done |
| *Wa anṣitū* | **Falling silent** | **Negative** — something is left off |

**Together the two describe a state.** **And the particle must be recorded: *istamiʿū lahū* — made transitive with the *lām*: the place to which the attention is directed is stated.**

**And per the method I draw a limit: I build no legal ruling from this verse.** **Which cases the verse covers (within the prayer, outside it, in which circumstances) has been debated among the schools of law, and no side is taken in that debate** (`STYLE.md`).

### 7/205 — the measure of voice

**This verse connects directly with the fifty-fifth verse of the sūra, and that link was recorded at verse 55. Here it closes:**

| Verse | Expression |
|---|---|
| **55** | *Udʿū rabbakum **taḍarruʿan wa khufyatan*** |
| **205** | *Wa'dhkur rabbaka fī nafsika **taḍarruʿan wa khīfatan** wa **dūna'l-jahri mina'l-qawl*** |

**And two differences must be recorded:**

**One — at 55 *khufya* (secrecy), at 205 *khīfa* (fear).** **The two words resemble one another but their roots are different: خ-ف-ي (to conceal) and خ-و-ف (to fear).** **This is a difference that can be verified from the text.**

**Two — at 205 a note about the voice is added: *dūna'l-jahri mina'l-qawl* — "without loudness of speech".**

### The measure of voice in the index — three links

**The places in the Qurʾān where the voice is tied to a measure were treated in the index, and I rely on two of them:**

| Place | Expression | What was recorded there |
|---|---|---|
| `031-lokman.md` **31/19** | *Wa'**ghḍuḍ min** ṣawtik* | **The particle *min* states a partitive** — the verse asks not for silence but for **lowering part of the voice** |
| `019-meryem.md` **19/3** | *Idh nādā rabbahū **nidāʾan khafiyyā*** | **The opening of the sūra's axis of voice** — the prayer was made **in a hidden voice** |
| `017-isra.md` **17/110** | *Wa lā **tajhar** bi-ṣalātika wa lā **tukhāfit** bihā wa'btaghi **bayna dhālika sabīlā*** | **Between two extremes** — a measure |

**And al-Aʿrāf 7/205 is the fourth of this chain.** **What the four verses share and how they differ can be tabulated:**

| Place | How the measure is set |
|---|---|
| `019-meryem.md` **19/3** | **As an example** — Zechariah's prayer is told |
| `031-lokman.md` **31/19** | **As a command** — to lower part of the voice |
| `017-isra.md` **17/110** | **As the mean between two extremes** — neither shout nor whisper |
| **al-Aʿrāf 7/205** | **As a limit** — *dūna'l-jahr*: below a raised voice |

**All four look the same way, and none of them commands silence.** **I record this as an observation, and its ground is the words of the four verses.**

**And the qualification *fī nafsika* must be recorded: "within yourself".** **The command determines both a place (within) and a level of voice (*dūna'l-jahr*).**

**بِٱلْغُدُوِّ وَٱلْءَاصَالِ** — the root **غ-د-و**: the early time of morning. The root **أ-ص-ل**: the end of the day, the time from mid-afternoon to evening; the plural of *aṣīl*.

**And these two times are given not as a further ruling of the sūra but as an expression of continuity: the two ends of the day.**

**And the end of the command must be recorded: *wa lā takun mina'l-**ghāfilīn***.**

**The root غ-ف-ل had occurred three times in the sūra** (136, 172, 179) **and the fourth is here.** **And the fourth comes as a *prohibition*.**

| Verse | Of whom | Mood |
|---|---|---|
| **136** | The people of Pharaoh | **Statement** — *wa kānū ʿanhā ghāfilīn* |
| **172** | The excuse to be pleaded at the resurrection | **Quotation** — *innā kunnā ʿan hādhā ghāfilīn* |
| **179** | Those of whom *dharaʾnā li-jahannam* is said | **Diagnosis** — *ulāʾika humu'l-ghāfilūn* |
| **205** | **The addressee** | ***Prohibition*** — *wa lā takun mina'l-ghāfilīn* |

**Four occurrences, four different moods.** **This is a weave that can be verified within the sūra, and I record it as an observation.**

### 7/206 — the sūra closing with prostration

> *Inna'lladhīna **ʿinda rabbika** lā **yastakbirūna** ʿan ʿibādatihī wa **yusabbiḥūnahū** wa lahū **yasjudūn***

**This verse is one of the verses of prostration in the Qurʾān.** **The number of the verses of prostration and which verses fall within them differ among the schools of law; no side is taken in that debate in this commentary and no legal ruling is issued** (`STYLE.md`). **What is recorded is that this verse is transmitted as one of the verses of prostration, and that note is not disputed.**

**And the verse's place within the sūra must be recorded, and this is the sūra's most striking closing.**

**The sūra had opened, in the eleventh verse, with a command to prostrate** (*usjudū li-Ādama fa-sajadū illā Iblīs*) **and there there was a making of oneself great** (*fa-mā yakūnu laka an **tatakabbara** fīhā* — 13).

**The two hundred and sixth verse brings back the same two words:**

| Verse | Prostration | Pride |
|---|---|---|
| **11-13** | *Us**judū** li-Ādam* — **commanded; one did not do it** | *An **tatakabbara** fīhā* — **pride** |
| **206** | *Wa lahū **yasjudūn*** — **it is done** | *Lā **yastakbirūna** ʿan ʿibādatih* — **no pride** |

**The same two roots (س-ج-د and ك-ب-ر), at the beginning and at the end of the sūra; at the beginning in the negative, at the end in the affirmative.**

**I record this as my own reading and it is not binding; its ground is the roots shared by the two verses:** **the sūra opens with the telling of a refusal to prostrate and closes with the telling of those who prostrate.** **And in between, for two hundred verses, the same matter is worked: the knowledge given, and the response given to it.**

**And the qualification *ʿinda rabbika* must be recorded: who is meant is not named.** **In the classical commentaries it is explained as the angels; I convey this explanation as transmitted and do not name what the text does not name** (`STYLE.md`).

**And three verbs are counted, and their order is worth recording:**

| Order | Verb | What |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *Lā yastakbirūna ʿan ʿibādatih* | **Negative** — no pride |
| 2 | *Wa yusabbiḥūnahū* | **Positive** — glorification |
| 3 | *Wa lahū yasjudūn* | **Positive** — prostration |

**And in the third verb there is a fronting: *wa lahū yasjudūn* — the object has been brought forward.** **In Arabic this states *ḥaṣr* (restriction): to Him alone.**

**The last word of the sūra is a verb, and that verb is prostration.**

---

## The sūra as a whole — looking back

**This section gathers the data recorded through the sūra. I give two tables: the first, repetitions countable and verifiable within the sūra; the second, disagreements left without a preference.** **The first table is a count, not an interpretation; the places that are interpretation are marked separately.**

### Table A — repetitions verifiable within the sūra

**A first count was given in the section on the structure of the sūra; this table completes it and adds the repetitions that emerged from the sections treated.**

| # | Repetition | Verses where it occurs | How many | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | ***Yā qawmi'budū'llāha mā lakum min ilāhin ghayruh*** | 59, 65, 73, 85 | **4** | The shared opening of the sequence of prophets; **absent in Lot's** |
| 2 | ***Yā banī Ādam*** | 26, 27, 31, 35 | **4** | The address of the third block |
| 3 | ***Fa-akhadhathumu'r-rajfatu fa-aṣbaḥū fī dārihim jāthimīn*** | 78, 91 | **2** | The word-for-word identical closing of Thamūd and Madyan |
| 4 | ***Fa-tawallā ʿanhum wa qāla yā qawmi laqad ablaghtukum … wa naṣaḥtu lakum*** | 79, 93 | **2** | Ṣāliḥ and Shuʿayb; the only difference *risālata* / *risālāt* |
| 5 | ***Wa lā tufsidū fi'l-arḍi baʿda iṣlāḥihā*** | 56, 85 | **2** | The sūra's own command in Shuʿayb's mouth |
| 6 | ***Wa yabghūnahā ʿiwajā*** | 45, 86 | **2** | An attribute in the scene of the hereafter, a prohibition in Madyan |
| 7 | ***Ummatun yahdūna bi'l-ḥaqqi wa bihī yaʿdilūn*** | 159, 181 | **2** | For a people, then for all created beings |
| 8 | ***Lā tajʿalnā / tajʿalnī maʿa'l-qawmi'ẓ-ẓālimīn*** | 47, 150 | **2** | In the mouths of the people of *al-Aʿrāf* and of Aaron |
| 9 | ***A-wa ʿajibtum an jāʾakum dhikrun … ʿalā rajulin minkum*** | 63, 69 | **2** | Noah and Hūd; at 184 it is turned upon the addressee |
| 10 | ***Khudhū / khudh … bi-quwwatin*** | 145, 171 | **2** | The same command to Moses and to the community |
| 11 | ***Fa'tinā bimā taʿidunā in kunta …*** | 70, 77 | **2** | ʿĀd and Thamūd; *ṣādiqīn* / *mursalīn* |
| 12 | ***Akhrijūhum / la-nukhrijannaka / yukhrijakum / li-tukhrijū*** (**خ-ر-ج**) | 82, 88, 110, 123 | **4** | The threat and the accusation of driving out |
| 13 | ***Jaʿalakum khulafāʾa*** and ***yarithūna'l-arḍ*** | 69, 74, 100 | **3** | The chain of succession: ʿĀd → Thamūd → the addressee |
| 14 | ***Nufaṣṣilu'l-āyāt / faṣṣalnāhu / tafṣīl / mufaṣṣalāt*** (**ف-ص-ل**) | 32, 52, 133, 145, 174 | **5** | Setting out in detail |
| 15 | ***Ṣurifat / nuṣarrifu / sa-aṣrifu*** (**ص-ر-ف**) | 47, 58, 146 | **3** | Gazes, signs, the proud |
| 16 | ***Ghāfilīn / ghāfilūn*** (**غ-ف-ل**) | 136, 172, 179, 205 | **4** | Statement → excuse → diagnosis → **prohibition** |
| 17 | ***Yubṣirūn / mubṣirūn / baṣāʾir*** (**ب-ص-ر**) | 179, 195, 198, 201, 203 | **5** | The axis of seeing; four negative, one positive |
| 18 | ***Nāṣiḥ / anṣaḥu / naṣaḥtu*** (**ن-ص-ح**) | 21, 62, 68, 79, 93 | **5** | One in the mouth of Iblīs, four in those of prophets |
| 19 | ***Aqʿudanna / lā taqʿudū*** (**ق-ع-د**) + *ṣirāṭ* | 16, 86 | **2** | Iblīs's intent, a prohibition to Madyan |
| 20 | ***Rijz*** | 134, 135, 162 | **3** | The people of Pharaoh and this community |
| 21 | ***ʿArsh / yaʿrishūn*** (**ع-ر-ش**) | 54, 137 | **2** | Sovereignty and the structures brought down |
| 22 | ***Tabāraka / barakāt*** (**ب-ر-ك**) | 54, 96, 137 | **3** | Loftiness, blessing, a blessed land |
| 23 | ***Aʿrāf / yaʿrifūna / maʿrūf / ʿurf*** (**ع-ر-ف**) | 46, 46, 48, 157, 199 | **5** | The name of the sūra turns at the end into a command |
| 24 | ***ʿAfaw / al-ʿafw*** (**ع-ف-و**) | 95, 199 | **2** | "They grew many" and "take the ʿafw" — two branches |
| 25 | ***Naquṣṣanna / yaquṣṣūna / fa'qṣuṣ*** (**ق-ص-ص**) | 7, 35, 176 | **3** | God, the messengers, the addressee |
| 26 | ***Munẓarīn / lā tunẓirūn*** (**ن-ظ-ر**, respite) | 15, 195 | **2** | What was given to Iblīs, what the messenger does not ask |
| 27 | ***Sufahāʾ / tajhalūn / jāhilīn*** | 66, 138, 155, 199 | **4** | The same field, four different directions |
| 28 | ***Anjaynā*** | 64, 72, 83, 165 | **4** | The announcement of deliverance |
| 29 | ***Wa'dhkurū ālāʾa'llāh*** | 69, 74 | **2** | The shared sentence of Hūd and Ṣāliḥ |
| 30 | ***Udʿū / wa'dʿūhu / fa'dʿūhu*** — the command to pray | 55, 56, 180 | **3** | Bearing, inner state, by which names |
| 31 | **Prostration and pride** (**س-ج-د** + **ك-ب-ر**) | 11-13, 206 | **2** | The opening and the closing of the sūra |
| 32 | **The note of division** (*min*, *minhum*) | 159, 162, 168, 181 | **4** | That no blanket judgement is built |
| 33 | **The two-directional test** (*ḥasana* + *sayyiʾa*) | 94-95, 130-131, 168 | **3** | Hardship and abundance together |
| 34 | ***Alqā / alqū / tulqiya*** (**ل-ق-ي**) | 107, 115, 116, 117, 150 | **5** | Four of them a sign, one the tablets |
| 35 | **The measure "what can it do?"** | 148, 179, 195 | **3** | The calf, man, those called upon — **the same measure** |
| 36 | ***Bayātan / ḍuḥan / baghtatan*** — suddenness | 4, 95, 97, 98 | **4** | The hour of sleep and of play |

**And two lines must be recorded separately; both are the result of the count above, and I give them as my own reading, and they are not binding:**

**One — line 35 is the sūra's most consistent measure.** **In three separate places, for three different addressees, the same question is asked: what are you doing with what you have in hand / what can it do?** **Of the calf (148: it does not speak, it does not guide), of man (179: the organs are there, they do not work), of those called upon (195: are the organs there?).** **The measure changes nowhere.**

**Two — line 32 is the support that comes from within the text for the principle of `STYLE.md` that "no blanket judgement is issued".** **Even in the places where it says the harshest things about them, the sūra enters a note of division four times.** **This is a countable datum and is not an interpretation; only the conclusion I draw from it is interpretation.**

**The count: in forty-three places no preference has been made.** **Seven of them fall directly within a field of *kalām* debate** (7, 15, 16, 22, 26, 33, 35), **seven are variant readings** (10, 17, 19, 23, 32, 36, 40), **three fall within the field of legal ruling** (25, 41, 42); **the rest are questions of word, of word order and of meaning.**

### The sūra's own answer

**A reading was recorded in the section on the structure of the sūra: the three cornerstones — 11-25 (Adam), 172-174 (*alast*), 175-177 (the man who slipped out of the signs) — are three moments of the same matter; in all three there is first a giving, and then the passing of what was given out of the hand.**

**Now, with the whole sūra treated, two things can be added to that reading, and both can be verified from the text:**

**One — the sūra tells of "what was given passing out of the hand" not only in the three cornerstones but in every block:**

| Block | What was given | What happened |
|---|---|---|
| **II** (11-25) | A **prohibition** | It was broken — then confessed |
| **III** (26-36) | **Clothing and adornment** | Held forbidden, or the measure exceeded |
| **V** (54-58) | **Rain and soil** | Two soils, one water — the result diverged |
| **VI** (59-102) | **Messengers and bounties** | *Wa in wajadnā aktharahum la-fāsiqīn* (102) |
| **VII** (103-171) | **Deliverance and a Book** | The calf (148), the Sabbath (163), *ʿaraḍa hādha'l-adnā* (169) |
| **VIII** (172-186) | **A testimony and signs** | It was forgotten (172); **it was slipped off** (175) |
| **IX** (187-206) | **A Book, names, commands** | *Wa lā takun mina'l-ghāfilīn* (205) — **as a prohibition** |

**Two — and the one counter-command the sūra gives is the reverse of the same picture: *to hold*.**

| Verse | Command |
|---|---|
| **145** | *Fa-khudhhā **bi-quwwatin*** |
| **170** | *Wa'lladhīna **yumassikūna** bi'l-kitāb* |
| **171** | *Khudhū mā ātaynākum **bi-quwwatin*** |
| **199** | *Khudhi'l-ʿafw* |
| **205** | *Wa'**dhkur** rabbaka fī nafsik* |

**In five places verbs from the same field: take, hold fast, remember.**

**I record this as my own reading and it is not binding; its ground is the two tables above.** **The sūra works one question through two hundred and six verses — what does man do with the knowledge given to him? — and gives its answer with a command: hold it in your hand.**

**And the last verse shows what answers to this: *lā yastakbirūna ʿan ʿibādatihī wa yusabbiḥūnahū wa lahū yasjudūn.***

**The sūra closes with prostration.**
